Scattering map for the Vlasov-Maxwell system around source-free electromagnetic fields
Léo Bigorgne

Abstract
We construct an isometric modified scattering operator, mapping any sufficiently regular past scattering state, with a small distribution function, to the future one corresponding to forward evolution by the Vlasov-Maxwell system. The main part of this work is devoted to the construction of a modified wave operator, which relates the future asymptotic dynamics of the solutions to their initial data. Then, by applying our previous results on modified scattering [ 8 ] , we are able to construct a small data scattering map for the Vlasov-Maxwell system. Our analysis relies in particular on the study of the asymptotic Maxwell equations . Its solution captures not only the large time behavior of the electromagnetic field of the plasma near future timelike infinity as well as future null infinity, but also the one of its weighted derivatives. The wave operator provides as well a class of global solutions to the Vlasov-Maxwell system which are large in L x , v p × L x 2 L^{p}_{x,v}\times L^{2}_{x} but initially dispersed.



1
Introduction

This paper is concerned with the Vlasov-Maxwell system on
\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
in the perturbative regime. This set of equations are used to describe the evolution of collisionless plasma and can be written as

\displaystyle\partial_{t}f+\widehat{v}\cdot\nabla_{x}f+(E+\widehat{v}\times B)

\displaystyle\cdot\nabla_{v}f=0,

(1)

\displaystyle\nabla_{x}\cdot E=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f\mathrm{d}v,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}E=\nabla_{x}\times B-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\widehat{v}f\mathrm{d}v,

(2)

\displaystyle\nabla_{x}\cdot B=0,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}B=-\nabla_{x}\times E,

(3)

where

•

f:\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}_{+}
is the density distribution function of the particles.

•

\widehat{v}=\frac{v}{v^{0}}
, where
v^{0}=\langle v\rangle:=\sqrt{1+|v|^{2}}
, is the relativistic speed of a particle of momentum
v\in\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
.

•

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f\mathrm{d}v
and
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\widehat{v}f\mathrm{d}v
are respectively the total charge density and the total current density.

•

E,B:\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{3}
are respectively the electric and the magnetic field.

Although we consider, as it is usually done, plasmas composed by one species of particles of charge
q=1
and mass
m=1
, all the results of this paper can be extended without any additional difficulty to the multispecies case, with particles of different charges and strictly positive masses
1
1

1

In contrast, in view of the analysis performed in
[
7
]
, linear scattering is expected to hold for massless particles.
. For convenience, we set in this article the speed of light
c
to one,
c=1
, but in certain context it is important to keep it as a parameter in (
1
)–(
3
). For instance, it is well known from
[
2
,
20
,
62
,
14
]
that in the non-relativistic limit
c\to+\infty
, the solutions to the Vlasov-Maxwell equations converge to the ones of the repulsive Vlasov-Poisson system

\partial_{t}f+v\cdot\nabla_{x}f+\nabla_{x}\phi\cdot\nabla_{v}f=0,\qquad\qquad\Delta\phi=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f\mathrm{d}v.

(4)

A detailed introduction to kinetic equations, including these two systems, can be found in
[
28
]
.

1.1 Local and global well-posedness for the Vlasov-Maxwell system

An initial data set
(f_{0},E_{0},B_{0})
for (
1
)–(
3
) is composed by a function
f_{0}:\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}_{+}
and two fields
E_{0},B_{0}:\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{3}
which satisfy the constraint equations

\nabla_{x}\cdot E_{0}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f_{0}\,\mathrm{d}v,\qquad\qquad\nabla_{x}\cdot B_{0}=0.

(5)

The local existence and uniqueness problem for the solutions to the Vlasov-Maxwell system has been adressed by Wollman and Glassey-Strauss
[
74
,
32
]
. While global weak solutions were constructed by DiPerna-Lions
[
21
]
(see also
[
60
]
), the existence of global in time classical solutions to the Vlasov-Maxwell system is only known in the pertubative regime
[
33
,
31
,
59
,
64
,
5
,
71
,
73
,
8
]
or under certain symmetry assumptions
[
26
,
27
,
30
,
48
,
69
]
. Although the problem remains open besides these specific cases, various continuation criteria have been obtained during the past decades
[
32
,
34
,
35
,
41
,
13
,
50
,
67
,
47
,
42
,
51
,
54
]
.

1.2 Asymptotic stability of vacuum for the Vlasov-Maxwell system

The solutions arising from sufficiently small and regular initial data are known, in addition to be global in time, to decay with a rate corresponding to the solutions of the linearised equations around
0
,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}f^{\mathrm{lin}}+\widehat{v}\cdot\nabla_{x}f^{\mathrm{lin}}=0,

(6)

\displaystyle\nabla_{x}\cdot E^{\mathrm{lin}}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f^{\mathrm{lin}}\mathrm{d}v,\qquad

\displaystyle\qquad\partial_{t}E^{\mathrm{lin}}=\nabla_{x}\times B^{\mathrm{lin}}-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\widehat{v}f^{\mathrm{lin}}\mathrm{d}v,

(7)

\displaystyle\nabla_{x}\cdot B^{\mathrm{lin}}=0,\qquad

\displaystyle\qquad\partial_{t}B^{\mathrm{lin}}=-\nabla_{x}\times E^{\mathrm{lin}}.

(8)

Thus, for any such solution
(f,E,B)
to (
1
)–(
3
),

\hskip-28.45274pt\forall\,(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3},\qquad\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}v\,\lesssim t^{-3},\qquad|E|(t,x)+|B|(t,x)\lesssim\langle t+|x|\rangle^{-1}\langle t-|x|\rangle^{-1}.

(9)

This result was first obtained for compactly supported initial data by Glassey-Strauss
[
33
]
, where estimates on the first order derivatives of the electromagnetic field were derived as well. Soon after, in the multi-species case, the smallness assumptions on the individual density distribution functions has been relaxed
[
31
]
. Later, Schaeffer
[
64
]
removed the support restriction on the velocity variable. However, his method lead to a loss on the estimate of
\int_{v}f\mathrm{d}v
.

In recent years, all the compact support assumptions on the initial data were removed independently by
[
5
,
71
]
using robust approaches. More precisely, they rely on vector field methods as well as, for the latter, Fourier analysis. It allowed them to obtain (almost) optimal pointwise decay estimates, similar to (
9
), on the solutions and their higher order derivatives. Furthermore, in
[
5
]
, the initial decay assumption in
v
is optimal and improved estimates on certain
null
components of the electromagnetic field are derived. Thereafter, Wei-Yang proved a global existence result allowing for large initial Maxwell fields
[
73
]
. Thus, their result implies the asymptotic stability of electromagnetic fields in vacuum, of regularity
C^{2}
and decaying fast enough, for the Vlasov-Maxwell system. Their work, based on the framework of Glassey-Strauss, does not require any compact support restriction on the data and provides the optimal decay rates (
9
).

In
[
8
]
, we provided a shorter proof of the main results of
[
5
,
71
]
and we allowed, as
[
73
]
, the electromagnetic field to be large. Moreover, we investigate further the asymptotic dynamics of the solutions. Once the optimal decay estimates (
9
) are proved, the next question one may ask is whether or not
f
and
(E,B)
can be approached by a linear solution.

1.3 Modified scattering for the small data solutions to the Vlasov-Maxwell system

The purpose of scattering theory is to compare the dynamics of a perturbed system, here the Vlasov-Maxwell equations, with a free dynamics, which is meant to be much simpler. At first glance, we can consider the free dynamics of the linear Vlasov equation and the homogeneous Maxwell equations. However, we will see that in order to achieve the three requirements of a satisfactory scattering theory, a more involved free dynamics such as the one of the linearised system (
6
)–(
8
) must be considered. We refer to
[
58
]
for a general introduction on this subject.

Ideally, one would like the asymptotic behavior of the solutions to be captured by past and future
scattering states
. For the free relativistic transport equation (
6
), the distribution function
f^{\mathrm{lin}}
is constant along the characteristics, which are
timelike straight lines
, the trajectories of isolated massive bodies. More precisely,

\exists\,f_{\infty}:\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\to\mathbb{R},\;\;\forall\,(t,x,v)\in\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v},\qquad\qquad f^{\mathrm{lin}}(t,x+t\widehat{v},v)=f_{\infty}(x,v),

so that the asymptotic dynamics of
f^{\mathrm{lin}}
can be fully described by the knowledge of
f_{\infty}
. In that case, the (future) scattering state
f_{\infty}
turns out to also correspond to the initial data
f^{\mathrm{lin}}(0,\cdot,\cdot)
. Consider an electromagnetic field
(E^{\mathrm{hom}},B^{\mathrm{hom}})
in vacuum, that is a solution to the homogeneous Maxwell equations

\nabla_{x}\cdot E^{\mathrm{hom}}=0,\qquad\nabla_{x}\cdot B^{\mathrm{hom}}=0,\qquad\partial_{t}E^{\mathrm{hom}}=\nabla_{x}\times B^{\mathrm{hom}},\qquad\partial_{t}B^{\mathrm{hom}}=-\nabla_{x}\times E^{\mathrm{hom}}.

Then, under suitable assumptions on the data,
(rE^{\mathrm{hom}},rB^{\mathrm{hom}})
converges along
null rays
, the trajectories of photons. There exists
E_{\infty},\,B_{\infty}:\mathbb{R}_{u}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}\to\mathbb{R}^{3}
, the radiation fields of
E^{\mathrm{hom}}
and
B^{\mathrm{hom}}
along
future null infinity

\mathcal{I}^{+}\!\simeq\mathbb{R}_{u}\!\times\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}
, such that

\hskip-14.22636pt\forall\,(u,\omega)\in\mathbb{R}_{u}\!\times\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega},\;\;\qquad\lim_{r\to+\infty}rE^{\mathrm{hom}}(r+u,r\omega)=E_{\infty}(u,\omega),\quad\;\lim_{r\to+\infty}rB^{\mathrm{hom}}(r+u,r\omega)=B_{\infty}(u,\omega).

(10)

Moreover, the future scattering state
(E_{\infty},B_{\infty})
satisfies constraint equations on
\mathcal{I}^{+}
inherited from the Maxwell equations. By denoting abusively by
\omega
the function
\omega\mapsto\omega\in\mathbb{S}^{2}
, they can be written as

\omega\cdot E_{\infty}=0,\qquad\omega\cdot B_{\infty}=0,\qquad E_{\infty}+\omega\times B_{\infty}=0,\qquad B_{\infty}-\omega\times E_{\infty}=0.

(11)

Conversally, we proved in
[
8
, Theorem
7.6
]
that for any
L^{2}_{u,\omega}
scattering state
(E_{\infty},B_{\infty})
satisfying the constraint equations (
11
), there exists a unique
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{+},L^{2}_{x})
solution
(E^{\mathrm{hom}},B^{\mathrm{hom}})
to the vacuum Maxwell equations such that (
10
) holds, at least in a weak sense.

Let us now review the scattering results that we derived in
[
8
]
for the solutions
(f,E,B)
to the Vlasov-Maxwell system arising from sufficiently regular data and a small initial distribution function.

•

For the Vlasov field, a
linear scattering
statement would be

\exists\,f_{\infty}\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}),\qquad\lim_{t\to+\infty}\|f(t,x+t\widehat{v},v)-f_{\infty}(x,v)\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}=0.

(12)

In other words,
f
would converge along the linear characteristics and would approach, for large times, the solution to the free relativistic transport equation (
6
) with scattering data
f_{\infty}
.
However, because of the long-range effects of the Lorentz force, (
12
) holds if and only if
2
2

2

In the multispecies case or if we allow the distribution function
f
to take negative values, linear scattering occurs for solutions arising from nontrivial initial data.

f(0,\cdot,\cdot)=0
. Instead, we could expect a weaker quantity, which contains less informations, to have a linear behavior for large times. It turns out that the spatial average of
f
, which is conserved in time for the solutions to (
6
), converges,

\exists\,Q_{\infty}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}),\quad\forall\,v\in\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v},\qquad\qquad\lim_{t\to+\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}f(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}v=Q_{\infty}(v).

It allowed us to determine, as in Corollary
2.17
below, the leading order contribution of the source terms in the Maxwell equations (
2
)–(
3
), which are the charge and the current density. In particular, once multiplied by their decay rate
t^{3}
, these two quantities have a self-similar asymptotic profile, as the corresponding linear ones. This enabled us to capture the large time behavior of the electromagnetic field along linear approximations of the trajectories of the massive particles,

\exists\,\mathrm{Lor}:\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\to\mathbb{R}^{3},\quad\forall\,(x,v)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v},\qquad\lim_{t\to+\infty}t^{2}\big(E(t,x+t\widehat{v})+\widehat{v}\times B(t,x+t\widehat{v})\big)=\mathrm{Lor}(v).

It is worth noting that, as we prove in Section
5
, the long range Lorentz force
t^{-2}\,\mathrm{Lor}
arises from a solution to the (asymptotic) Maxwell equations. Then, we obtained from this property that the density function
f
verifies a
modified scattering
dynamics. Heuristically, the characteristics of the Vlasov equation satisfy, for
t\gg 1
,

\dot{X}=\widehat{V},\qquad\qquad\dot{V}\approx t^{-2}\,\mathrm{Lor}(V),

so that we expect
V(t)\to v
as
t\to+\infty
. It yields to the approximations

V(t)\approx v-\frac{1}{t}\mathrm{Lor}(v),\qquad\dot{X}(t)\approx\widehat{v}+\partial_{t}\mathscr{C}_{t,v}+O\big(t^{-2}\big),\qquad\mathscr{C}^{k}_{t,v}:=\frac{\log(t)}{v^{0}}\left(\widehat{v}\cdot\mathrm{Lor}(v)\widehat{v}^{k}-\mathrm{Lor}^{k}(v)\right),

and then
X(t)\approx x+t\widehat{v}+\mathscr{C}_{t,v}
. Consistently with these heuristic computations, we proved that the distribution function converges along these logarithmic corrections of the linear characteristics. There exists
f_{\infty}\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})
such that

\hskip-28.45274pt\lim_{t\to+\infty}\big\|f\big(t,x+t\widehat{v}+\mathscr{C}_{t,v},v\big)-f_{\infty}(x,v)\big\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}=0.

(13)

A crucial property for the purpose of this paper is that the asymptotic Lorentz force is a functional of (the spatial average
Q_{\infty}
of)
f_{\infty}
. More precisely, it arises from an asymptotic electromagnetic field
v\mapsto(\mathbb{E}[f_{\infty}],\mathbb{B}[f_{\infty}])(v)
, which is given, for
1\leq k\leq 3
, by

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{k}[f_{\infty}](v)

\displaystyle:=-\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}|z|\leq 1\\
|z+\widehat{v}|<1-|z|\end{subarray}}\mathbf{Q}^{k}\big[f_{\infty}\big]\left(\frac{\widecheck{z+\widehat{v}}}{1-|z|}\right)\frac{\mathrm{d}z}{(1-|z|)^{4}|z|},

(14)

\displaystyle\mathbb{B}^{k}[f_{\infty}](v)

\displaystyle:=-\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}|z|\leq 1\\
|z+\widehat{v}|<1-|z|\end{subarray}}\mathbf{Q}^{ij}\big[f_{\infty}\big]\left(\frac{\widecheck{z+\widehat{v}}}{1-|z|}\right)\frac{\mathrm{d}z}{(1-|z|)^{4}|z|},

(15)

where
1\leq i,\,j\leq 3
are such that the signature of the permutation
(1,2,3)\mapsto(k,i,j)
is equal to
1
,
w\mapsto\widecheck{w}
is the inverse function of the relativistic speed
v\mapsto\widehat{v}
and

\mathbf{Q}^{k}\big[f_{\infty}\big](v)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\langle v\rangle^{5}\partial_{v^{k}}\big(\langle v\rangle f_{\infty}\big)(x,v)\mathrm{d}x,\qquad\quad\mathbf{Q}^{ij}\big[f_{\infty}\big](v)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\langle v\rangle^{5}\big(v^{i}\partial_{v^{j}}f_{\infty}-v^{j}\partial_{v^{i}}f_{\infty}\big)(x,v)\mathrm{d}x.

In fact, one can express
(\mathbb{E}[f_{\infty}],\mathbb{B}[f_{\infty}])
in terms of
f_{\infty}
, instead of its first order
v
-derivatives, and then gain regularity.

•

In contrast, the electromagnetic field exhibits a
linear scattering
dynamics. More precisely, it has a radiation field
(E_{\infty},B_{\infty})
along future null infinity
\mathcal{I}^{+}
, that is (
10
) holds for
(E,B)
. Furthermore, since Vlasov fields enjoy strong decay properties along null straight lines,
(E_{\infty},B_{\infty})
verifies the constraint equations (
11
) of the scattering states for the source free Maxwell equations. Thus,
(E,B)
approaches for large time the homogeneous solution
(E^{\mathrm{hom}},B^{\mathrm{hom}})
corresponding to this scattering state.

It turns out that
(f_{\infty},E_{\infty},B_{\infty})
satisfy two other constraint equations, given by (
22
)–(
23
) below, that we identify in this paper and which are crucial for the purpose of constructing the modified wave operator. They imply that, contrary to a smooth solution to the homogeneous Maxwell equations, the field
(E,B)
may have a nontrivial
electromagnetic memory effect

[
4
]
. Thus, in some sense,
(E,B)
has a linear behavior merely at first order. For this reason,
(f^{\mathrm{lin}},E^{\mathrm{lin}},B^{\mathrm{lin}})
, the solution to the linearised Vlasov-Maxwell system (
6
)–(
8
) with asymptotic data
(f_{\infty},E_{\infty},B_{\infty})
, is a better approximation than
(f^{\mathrm{lin}},E^{\mathrm{hom}},B^{\mathrm{hom}})
. Note that we study
f^{\mathrm{lin}}
throughout Section
2.3
and
(E^{\mathrm{lin}},B^{\mathrm{lin}})
in Proposition
8.4
.

•

We also derived similar statements for the derivatives of the solutions. In particular, although there is a loss of derivatives in the process, the smoother the initial data set
(f_{0},E_{0},B_{0})
is, the more regular the scattering state
(f_{\infty},E_{\infty},B_{\infty})
is.

•

The total energy of the system,

\mathbb{E}_{\mathrm{tot}}[f_{0},E_{0},B_{0}]:=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\langle v\rangle\,f_{0}(x,v)\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}|E_{0}(x)|^{2}+|B_{0}(x)|^{2}\mathrm{d}x,

(16)

which is a conserved quantity
3
3

3

More precisely, for any sufficiently regular solution to (
1
)–(
3
),
t\mapsto\mathbb{E}_{\mathrm{tot}}[f(t,\cdot,\cdot),E(t,\cdot),B(t,\cdot)]
is constant.
, is equal to the corresponding norm of the scattering state
(f_{\infty},E_{\infty},B_{\infty})
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\infty}[f_{\infty},E_{\infty},B_{\infty}]:=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\langle v\rangle\,f_{\infty}(x,v)\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{u}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}|E_{\infty}-\omega\times B_{\infty}|^{2}(u,\omega)+|B_{\infty}+\omega\times E_{\infty}|^{2}(u,\omega)\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\mathrm{d}u.

As formulated in
[
58
]
in a general setting, a satisfactory scattering theory for the Vlasov-Maxwell system must verify the following three properties.

1.

Existence of scattering states
: for any sufficiently regular scattering state
(f_{\infty},E_{\infty},B_{\infty})
, there exists a finite-energy solution
(f,E,B)
to the Vlasov-Maxwell system evolving to this given scattering state.

2.

Uniqueness of scattering states
: two finite-energy solutions to (
1
)–(
3
) corresponding to the same scattering state must coincide.

3.

Asymptotic completeness
: the global finite-energy solutions to the Vlasov-Maxwell system are either bound states or evolve to such a scattering state.

The first two properties would constitute a global well-posedness statement for the Vlasov-Maxwell system with asymptotic data. They allow to construct the (modified) wave operator
\mathscr{W}
, mapping any scattering state to the corresponding solution to (
1
)–(
3
). Note that we do not know if bound states of finite energy exist for the Vlasov-Maxwell system. Though, if the distribution function is small, (
9
) implies that such solutions does not exist.

According to the results proved in
[
8
]
,
3.
holds true for the solutions arising from a small initial Vlasov field. The main goal of this article is to complete the construction of the scattering theory for the Vlasov-Maxwell system with a small distribution function. In other words, we will prove
1.
and
2.
for this class of solutions. It will allow us to relate the past asymptotic dynamics, of the solutions
(f,E,B)
to (
1
)–(
3
) for which
f
is small, to the future one.

Remark 1.1
.

A global well-posedness result for the solutions to the Vlasov-Maxwell system would imply, together with Theorem
1.8
below, that existence and uniqueness of scattering states hold for the large data solutions as well.

Indeed, given a smooth and large scattering state
(f_{\infty},E_{\infty},B_{\infty})
, one can uniquely associate to it a solution
(f,E,B)
to (
1
)–(
3
), defined on
[T,+\infty[
and evolving to
(f_{\infty},E_{\infty},B_{\infty})
. However, Theorem
1.8
provides a lower bound on
T
which, without a smallness assumption on
\epsilon
, can be large. Then, we
could
extend
(f,E,B)
on
\mathbb{R}_{+}
and, by a density argument, construct a bijective isometric wave operator

\mathscr{W}:(f_{\infty},E_{\infty},B_{\infty})\mapsto\big(f(0,\cdot,\cdot),E(0,\cdot),B(0,\cdot)\big),\qquad\mathbb{E}_{\infty}[f_{\infty},E_{\infty},B_{\infty}]=\mathbb{E}_{\mathrm{tot}}[f(0,\cdot,\cdot),E(0,\cdot),B(0,\cdot)]<+\infty.

Finally, let us mention the work of Ben Artzi-Pankavich
[
53
]
. Given a small data solution to the Vlasov-Maxwell system
(f,E,B)
constructed by Glassey-Strauss in
[
33
]
, they proved modified scattering for the distribution function
f
.

1.4 Analogous results for the Vlasov-Poisson system

In contrast with the case of the Vlasov-Maxwell system, global existence holds for the classical solutions to the Vlasov-Poisson system
[
57
,
46
,
63
]
. Sharp decay rates for the small data solutions to (
4
) were first obtained in
[
3
]
and then, with several improvements, by
[
36
,
66
,
22
,
65
]
(see also
[
29
,
72
,
6
]
for the massive and massless relativistic Vlasov-Poisson systems). The study of the modified scattering dynamics of these solutions started with
[
18
]
and, more recently,
[
38
,
52
]
clearly identified the asymptotic electrostatic force field responsible for this phenomenon. It is related, through the Poisson equation, to the spatial average of the limit distribution function
f_{\infty}
. The understanding of these asymptotic dynamics allowed Flynn-Ouyang-Pausader-Widmayer to construct a scattering map for the small data solutions to the Vlasov-Poisson system
[
25
]
.

Let us mention finally that the asymptotic stability of other steady states has been recently adressed. The perturbations of a point charge exhibits a modified scattering dynamics
[
55
,
56
]
and the ones of the Poisson equilibrium scatter to linear solutions
[
37
]
. For the study of the solutions to the linearised
Vlasov-Poisson system near more general spatially homogenous equilibria, we refer to
[
49
,
39
]
.

1.5 General notations

In this subsection as well as in the next one, we introduce certain quantities, used throughout this article, in order to properly state the main results. We work on the
1+3
dimensional Minkowski spacetime
(\mathbb{R}^{1+3},\eta)
and we will use two sets of coordinates, the Cartesian
(x^{0}=t,x^{1},x^{2},x^{3})
, in which
\eta=\mathrm{diag}(-1,1,1,1)
, and null coordinates
(u,\underline{u},\theta,\varphi)
, where

u=t-r,\qquad\underline{u}=t+r,\qquad r:=|x|=\sqrt{|x^{1}|^{2}+|x^{2}|^{2}+|x^{3}|^{2}},

and
(\theta,\varphi)\in]0,\pi[\times]0,2\pi[
are spherical coordinates on the spheres of constant
(t,r)
. These coordinates are defined globally on
\mathbb{R}^{1+3}
apart from the usual degeneration of spherical coordinates and at
r=0
. Sometimes, for a tensor field
\mathrm{T}
defined on a subset of
\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}
, it will be convenient to write

\mathrm{T}(u,\underline{u},\omega):=\mathrm{T}\left(\frac{\underline{u}+u}{2},\frac{\underline{u}-u}{2}\omega\right),\qquad\qquad(u,\underline{u},\omega)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}.

We denote by
\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}=\sin(\theta)\mathrm{d}\theta\mathrm{d}\varphi
the volume form on the sphere
\mathbb{S}^{2}
. We will work with the null frame
(L,\underline{L},e_{\theta},e_{\varphi})
, where
L=2\partial_{u}
,
\underline{L}=2\partial_{\underline{u}}
are null derivatives and
(e_{\theta},e_{\varphi})
is the standard orthonormal basis on the spheres. More precisely,

L=\partial_{t}+\partial_{r},\qquad\underline{L}=\partial_{t}-\partial_{r},\qquad e_{\theta}=\frac{1}{r}\partial_{\theta},\qquad e_{\varphi}=\frac{1}{r\sin\theta}\partial_{\varphi}.

The Einstein summation convention will often be used, for instance
v^{\mu}\partial_{x^{\mu}}f=\sum_{\mu=0}^{3}v^{\mu}\partial_{x^{\mu}}f
. The lower case Latin indices goes from
1
to
3
and the Greek indices from
0
to
3
. We will raise and lower indices with respect to the Minkowski metric
\eta
, so that
v_{i}=\eta_{i\mu}v^{\mu}=v^{i}
and
v_{0}=-v^{0}
. Capital Roman letters, in particular
A
and
B
, will correspond to spherical variables.

The four-momentum vector
\mathbf{v}=(v^{\mu})_{0\leq\mu\leq 3}
is parameterized by
v=(v^{i})_{1\leq i\leq 3}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
and
v^{0}=\sqrt{1+|v|^{2}}
since the mass of the particles is equal to
1
. The relativistic speed
\widehat{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
is given by
\widehat{v}^{i}=\frac{v^{i}}{v^{0}}
and, for convenience, we define
\widehat{v}^{0}:=1
. Since we will perform the change of variables
y=x-\widehat{v}t
in integrals over the domain
\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
, we will have to consider the inverse function of
v\mapsto\widehat{v}
. Note that we already used it as well in order to define the asymptotic electromagnetic field (
14
)–(
15
).

Definition 1.2
.

Let
\widecheck{\;\color[rgb]{1,1,1}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\;}
be the operator defined, on the domain
\{y\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\;\;|y|<1\}
, as

y\mapsto\widecheck{y}=\frac{y}{\sqrt{1-|y|^{2}}},\qquad\text{so that}\qquad\forall\,|y|<1,\;\forall\,v\in\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v},\qquad\widehat{\widecheck{y}}=y,\quad\widecheck{\widehat{v}}=v.

Sometimes, for convenience, we will write
(|v^{0}|^{p}g)(w)
to denote
|w^{0}|^{p}g(w)
, where
w\in\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
and
g:\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
. For
a\in\mathbb{R}
and
x\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
, we will use the Japanese brackets
\langle a\rangle:=(1+|a|^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}
and
\langle x\rangle:=\langle|x|\rangle
. Finally, the notation
D_{1}\lesssim D_{2}
will stand for the statement that
\exists\,C>0
a positive constant independent of the solutions such as
D_{1}\leq CD_{2}
. Sometimes, we will write
\lesssim_{t_{0}}
if
C
depends on the initial time
t_{0}
.

1.6 The Vlasov-Maxwell system in geometric form

In order to capture the good behavior of certain geometric quantities associated to the solutions in the good null directions
(L,e_{\theta},e_{\varphi})
, which will in particular be useful for proving scattering results for the electromagnetic field, we represent
(E,B)
as a
2
-form. Let
F_{\mu\nu}
be the Faraday tensor and
J(f)_{\mu}
be the four-current density, defined in cartesian coordinates as

F=\begin{pmatrix}0&E^{1}&E^{2}&E^{3}\\
-E^{1}&0&-B^{3}&B^{2}\\
-E^{2}&B^{3}&0&-B^{1}\\
-E^{3}&-B^{2}&B^{1}&0\end{pmatrix},\qquad\qquad J(f):=\begin{pmatrix}-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f\mathrm{d}v\\
\vskip 2.0pt\cr\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\widehat{v}_{1}f\mathrm{d}v\\
\vskip 2.0pt\cr\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\widehat{v}_{2}f\mathrm{d}v\\
\vskip 2.0pt\cr\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\widehat{v}_{3}f\mathrm{d}v\end{pmatrix}.

(17)

The null decomposition
(\underline{\alpha}(F),\alpha(F),\rho(F),\sigma(F))
of
F
is defined, for
A\in\{\theta,\varphi\}
, as

\underline{\alpha}(F)_{e_{A}}:=F_{e_{A}\underline{L}},\qquad\alpha(F)_{e_{A}}:=F_{e_{A}L},\qquad\rho(F):=\frac{1}{2}F_{\underline{L}L},\qquad\sigma(F):=F_{e_{\theta}e_{\varphi}},

(18)

where
\underline{\alpha}(F)
and
\alpha(F)
are
1
-forms tangential to the
2
-spheres.

Remark 1.3
.

Note that
-J(f)_{0}
is the charge density and
(J(f)_{i})_{1\leq i\leq 3}
is the current density. The nonvanishing cartesian components of
F
are equal to a cartersian component of
\pm E
or
\pm B
.

Concerning the elements of the null decomposition of
F
, the
1
-forms
\alpha(F)
and
\underline{\alpha}(F)
verify

\alpha(F)_{e_{\theta}}\!=B\cdot e_{\varphi}-E\cdot e_{\theta},\quad\alpha(F)_{e_{\varphi}}\!=-B\cdot e_{\theta}-E\cdot e_{\varphi}\quad\underline{\alpha}(F)_{e_{\theta}}\!=-B\cdot e_{\varphi}-E\cdot e_{\theta},\quad\underline{\alpha}(F)_{e_{\varphi}}\!=B\cdot e_{\theta}-E\cdot e_{\varphi}.

Finally,
\rho(F)=E\cdot\omega
and
-\sigma(F)=B\cdot\omega
are the radial components of the electric and the magnetic field.

Then, the Vlasov equation (
1
) can be rewritten as

\mathbf{T}_{F}(f)=0,\qquad\qquad\mathbf{T}_{F}:f\mapsto\partial_{t}f+\widehat{v}\cdot\nabla_{x}f+\widehat{v}^{\mu}{F_{\mu}}^{j}\partial_{v^{j}}f,

(19)

and the Maxwell equations (
2
)–(
3
) are equivalent to the Gauss-Ampère law and the Gauss-Farady law

\nabla^{\mu}F_{\mu\nu}=J(f)_{\nu},\qquad\qquad\nabla^{\mu}{}^{*}\!F_{\mu\nu}=0,

(20)

where
{}^{*}\!F_{\mu\nu}=\frac{1}{2}F^{\lambda\sigma}\varepsilon_{\lambda\sigma\mu\nu}
is the Hodge dual of
F
and
\varepsilon
the Levi-Civita symbol. The Gauss-Faraday law can also be written as
\nabla_{[\lambda}F_{\mu\nu]}:=\nabla_{\lambda}F_{\mu\nu}+\nabla_{\mu}F_{\nu\lambda}+\nabla_{\nu}F_{\lambda\mu}=0
.

The intrinsic covariant differentiation on the spheres will be denoted by
\not{\nabla}
. The spherical divergence and curl of a
1
-form
\beta
tangential to the
2
-spheres, such as
\underline{\alpha}(F)
and
\alpha(F)
, are then

\not{\nabla}\cdot\beta:=\not{\nabla}_{e_{\theta}}\beta_{e_{\theta}}+\not{\nabla}_{e_{\varphi}}\beta_{e_{\varphi}},\qquad\not{\nabla}\times\beta:=\not{\nabla}_{e_{\theta}}\beta_{e_{\varphi}}-\not{\nabla}_{e_{\varphi}}\beta_{e_{\theta}},\qquad\qquad\not{\nabla}_{e_{A}}e_{B}:=\nabla_{e_{A}}e_{B}-\Gamma_{AB}^{r}\partial_{r},

where
\Gamma^{\lambda}_{\mu\nu}
are the Christofel symbols in the basis
(\partial_{t},\partial_{r},e_{\theta},e_{\varphi})
, so that
\Gamma^{0}_{AB}=0
.

We will further use the pointwise norms

\displaystyle|\alpha(F)|^{2}

\displaystyle:=|\alpha(F)_{e_{\theta}}|^{2}+|\alpha(F)_{e_{\varphi}}|^{2},\qquad\qquad|\underline{\alpha}(F)|^{2}:=|\underline{\alpha}(F)_{e_{\theta}}|^{2}+|\underline{\alpha}(F)_{e_{\varphi}}|^{2},

\displaystyle|F|^{2}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{0\leq\mu<\nu\leq 3}|F_{\mu\nu}|^{2}=\frac{1}{2}|\alpha(F)|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}|\underline{\alpha}(F)|^{2}+|\rho(F)|^{2}+|\sigma(F)|^{2}.

1.7 Charged electromagnetic field

If the plasma is not neutral, one cannot expect the electromagnetic field that we will construct from the scattering data to decay faster than
r^{-2}
. Indeed, if
(f,F)
is a sufficiently regular solution to (
19
)–(
20
) on
[T,+\infty[
, we obtain from Gauss’s law that the total charge

\forall\,t\geq T,\qquad\quad Q_{F}(t):=\lim_{r\rightarrow+\infty}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\rho(F)(t,r\omega)r^{2}\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f(t,x,v)dvdx=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f_{\infty}(x,v)dvdx,

is a conserved quantity and that
|F|=o(r^{-2})
implies
Q_{F}=0
. In order to avoid such a restrictive assumption, we introduce the pure charge part
\overline{F}
of
F
,

\hskip-12.80373pt\overline{F}(t,x):=\overline{\chi}(t-|x|)\frac{Q_{F}}{4\pi|x|^{2}}\frac{x_{i}}{|x|}\mathrm{d}t\wedge\mathrm{d}x^{i},\quad\;\rho\big(\overline{F}\big)(t,x)=\overline{\chi}(t-|x|)\frac{Q_{F}}{4\pi|x|^{2}},\;\;\,\alpha(\overline{F})=\underline{\alpha}(\overline{F})=\sigma(\overline{F})=0,

(21)

where
\overline{\chi}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R},[0,1])
is a cutoff function such that
\overline{\chi}(s)=1
for
s\leq-2
and
\chi(s)=0
for
s\geq-1
. It corresponds to the electromagnetic field generated by a point charge
Q_{F}
at
x=0
. Since
Q_{\overline{F}}=Q_{F}
, so that
F-\overline{F}
is chargeless, one can then expect
F
to have an asymptotic expansion of the form
F=\overline{F}+O(r^{-2-\delta})
, with
\delta>0
. In fact,
E=E^{\mathrm{df}}+E^{\mathrm{cf}}
and
B=B^{\mathrm{df}}+B^{\mathrm{cf}}
can be decomposed into their divergence-free and curl-free components. Then,
B^{\mathrm{cf}}=0
and
E^{\mathrm{cf},i}=\overline{F}_{0i}+O(r^{-3})
if
J(f)_{0}
is sufficiently regular.

1.8 Constraint equations satisfied by the scattering states

For a solution to the Maxwell equations with a source term decaying fast enough and arising from regular initial data, the null components
\alpha
,
\rho
and
\sigma
enjoy stronger decay properties than
\underline{\alpha}
. In particular, this holds true for any solution
(f,F)
to the Vlasov-Maxwell system (
19
)–(
20
) arising from sufficiently regular data and a small distribution function. The constraints (
11
) are the consequence of

\forall\,(u,\omega)\in\mathbb{R}_{u}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega},\qquad\;\;\lim_{r\to+\infty}r\alpha(F)(r+u,r\omega)=\lim_{r\to+\infty}r\rho(F)(r+u,r\omega)=\lim_{r\to+\infty}r\sigma(F)(r+u,r\omega)=0.

The radiation field along future null infinity of the electromagnetic field
F
can then be represented by a
1
-form
\underline{\alpha}^{\infty}
, defined on
\mathbb{R}_{u}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}
and tangential to the
2
-spheres, such that

\forall\,(u,\omega)\in\mathbb{R}_{u}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega},\qquad\;\;\lim_{r\to+\infty}r\underline{\alpha}(F)_{e_{A}}(r+u,r\omega)=\underline{\alpha}^{\infty}_{e_{A}},\qquad\qquad A\in\{\theta,\varphi\}.

We will prove in this article that there are in addition two constraint equations relating the limit distribution
f_{\infty}
with the radiation field
\underline{\alpha}^{\infty}
. In order to write them, we consider the Faraday tensor
\mathbb{F}[f_{\infty}]
associated through (
17
) to the asymptotic electromagnetic field
(\mathbb{E}[f_{\infty}],\mathbb{B}[f_{\infty}])
introduced in (
14
)–(
15
). It is a constant in time
2
-form defined on
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
. Then, the following relations hold,

\displaystyle\hskip-15.649pt\int_{\mathbb{R}_{u}}\!\not{\nabla}\cdot\underline{\alpha}^{\infty}(u,\omega)\mathrm{d}u=

\displaystyle\,\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f_{\infty}(x,v)-f_{\infty}(x,|v|\omega)\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x-\int_{\tau=0}^{+\infty}\frac{2\tau^{2}}{\langle\tau\rangle+\tau}\,\not{\nabla}\cdot\underline{\alpha}\big(\mathbb{F}[f_{\infty}]\big)(\tau\omega)\frac{\mathrm{d}\tau}{\langle\tau\rangle^{3}},

(22)

\displaystyle\hskip-15.649pt\int_{\mathbb{R}_{u}}\!\not{\nabla}\times\underline{\alpha}^{\infty}(u,\omega)\mathrm{d}u=

\displaystyle-\int_{\tau=0}^{+\infty}\frac{2\tau^{2}}{\langle\tau\rangle+\tau}\,\not{\nabla}\times\underline{\alpha}\big(\mathbb{F}[f_{\infty}]\big)(\tau\omega)\frac{\mathrm{d}\tau}{\langle\tau\rangle^{3}}.

(23)

These equations imply that the field
F
may have a nonvanishing electromagnetic memory effect
[
4
]
. It is a change of velocity, usually referred as a kick, experienced by a test charged particle which remained far from
r=0
during its evolution.

Remark 1.4
.

It is the field
F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]
, studied in Section
5
and capturing the asymptotic behavior of
F
, which gives rise to (
22
)–(
23
). In particular, we have
t^{2}F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}](t,t\widehat{v})=\mathbb{F}[f_{\infty}](v)
for
t\geq 4\,\langle v\rangle^{2}
and

\forall\,(t,x)\in[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3},\qquad\big|F-F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]\big|(t,x)\lesssim\Lambda\langle t+|x|\rangle^{-1}\,\langle t-|x|\rangle^{-\frac{3}{2}}.

It is a solution to the Maxwell equations with a well-chosen source term, governing the large time behavior of the current density
J(f)
.

Remark 1.5
.

If
v=\tau\omega
, then
2\tau^{2}(\langle\tau\rangle+\tau)^{-1}\langle\tau\rangle^{-3}=2|\widehat{v}|^{2}(1-|\widehat{v}|)
.

1.9 Future and past scattering states

We are now able to define the set of the scattering states for the Vlasov-Maxwell system.

Definition 1.6
.

A future scattering state for the Vlasov-Maxwell system is an ordered pair
(f_{\infty},\underline{\alpha}^{\infty})
where

•

f_{\infty}:\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\to\mathbb{R}
is a distribution function,

•

\underline{\alpha}^{\infty}
is a
1
-form defined on
\mathcal{I}^{+}\simeq\mathbb{R}_{u}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}
which is tangential to the
2
-spheres,

•

f_{\infty}
and
\underline{\alpha}^{\infty}
are sufficiently regular so that any quantity in (
22
)–(
23
) is well-defined and these equations are verified.

The distribution function is usually assumed to be nonnegative for physical reasons. Since this restriction is not required for the results of this paper to hold, we allow it to take negative values.

In order to relate the small data past asymptotic dynamics to the future one, we need to introduce the past scattering states as well. For this, given
(f,E,B)
, we define
(\widetilde{f},\widetilde{E},\widetilde{B})
as

\widetilde{f}(t,x,v):=f(-t,x,-v),\qquad(\widetilde{E},\widetilde{B})(t,x):=(E,-B)(-t,x).

This operation is an involution leaving invariant the set of the solutions to the Vlasov-Maxwell system. The null components of the Faraday tensor
\widetilde{F}
are related to the ones of
F
as follows,

\displaystyle\rho\big(\widetilde{F}\big)(t,x)

\displaystyle=\rho\big(F\big)(-t,x),\qquad\qquad\;\sigma\big(\widetilde{F}\big)(t,x)

\displaystyle=-\sigma\big(F\big)(-t,x),

\displaystyle\alpha\big(\widetilde{F}\big)(t,x)

\displaystyle=\underline{\alpha}\big(F\big)(-t,x),\qquad\qquad\underline{\alpha}\big(\widetilde{F}\big)(t,x)

\displaystyle=\alpha\big(F\big)(-t,x).

Definition 1.7
.

A past scattering state for the Vlasov-Maxwell system is an ordered pair
(f_{-\infty},\alpha^{-\infty})
such that
(\widetilde{f}_{\infty},\widetilde{\underline{\alpha}}^{\infty})
is a future scattering state, where

\widetilde{f}_{\infty}(x,v):=f_{-\infty}(x,-v),\qquad\qquad\widetilde{\underline{\alpha}}^{\infty}(u,\omega):=\alpha^{-\infty}(-u,\omega).

If
(f,F)
is a solution to the Vlasov-Maxwell system admitting
(f_{-\infty},\alpha^{-\infty})
as past scattering state,
\alpha^{-\infty}
is the radiation field of
F
along past null infinity
\mathcal{I}^{-}\simeq\mathbb{R}_{\underline{u}}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}
, that is

\forall\,(\underline{u},\omega)\in\mathbb{R}_{\underline{u}}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega},\qquad\qquad\lim_{r\to+\infty}\alpha(F)(-r+\underline{u},r\omega)=\alpha^{-\infty}(\underline{u},\omega).

1.10 Statement of the main results

We now state our result concerning the construction of the modified wave operator. For this, recall the correction
\mathscr{C}_{t,v}
to the linear spatial characteristics introduced in (
13
) and which is a functional of
f_{\infty}
. Certain estimates verified by the electromagnetic field will use notations not yet defined. We refer to Section
3.3
for the weighted
L^{2}_{x}
norms
\mathcal{E}^{K}_{7}[\cdot]
and
\mathcal{E}^{K,1}[\cdot]
as well as Section
5
for
F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]
.

Theorem 1.8
.

Let
N\geq 8
, and
(f_{\infty},\underline{\alpha}^{\infty})
be a future scattering state for the Vlasov-Maxwell system. Assume that there exists
\epsilon>0
and
\Lambda\geq 1
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{|\kappa_{z}|+|\kappa_{v}|\leq N+1}\,\sup_{(z,v)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\langle z\rangle^{N+12}\,\langle v\rangle^{25+|\kappa_{v}|}\,\big|\partial_{z}^{\kappa_{z}}\partial_{v}^{\kappa_{v}}f_{\infty}(z,v)\big|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\epsilon},

(24)

\displaystyle\sup_{\gamma_{u}+|\gamma_{\omega}|\leq N+1}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{u}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\langle u\rangle^{2}\log^{2}\!\big(1+\langle u\rangle\big)\big|\nabla_{\partial_{u}}^{\gamma_{u}}\not{\nabla}^{\gamma_{\omega}}\underline{\alpha}^{\infty}(u,\omega)\big|^{2}\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda.

(25)

Let further
T\geq 2
be an initial time. There exists an absolute constant
\varepsilon_{0}>0
and
C_{\Lambda}:=\exp\big(\exp\big(C\sqrt{\Lambda}\big)\big)
, where
C>0
depends only on
N
, such that the next statement hold. If
C_{\Lambda}\epsilon\log^{-1}(T)\leq\varepsilon_{0}
, then there exists a unique solution
(f,F)
to the Vlasov-Maxwell system, defined on
[T,+\infty[
, such that

•

the following asymptotic conditions hold,

\displaystyle\forall\,(x,v)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v},\qquad\qquad

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to+\infty}f\big(t,x+t\widehat{v}+\mathscr{C}_{t,v},v\big)=f_{\infty}(x,v),

\displaystyle\forall\,(u,\omega)\in\mathbb{R}_{u}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega},\qquad\qquad

\displaystyle\quad\;\,\lim_{r\to+\infty}r\underline{\alpha}(F)(r+u,r\omega)=\underline{\alpha}^{\infty}(u,\omega).

•

The function
g(t,z,v):=f(t,z+t\widehat{v}+\mathscr{C}_{t,v},v)
verifies the uniform
L^{2}_{z,v}
bound

\sup_{\kappa_{t}+|\kappa_{z}|+|\kappa_{v}|\leq N}\,\sup_{t\geq T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\langle z\rangle^{16+2N-2|\kappa_{v}|}\,\langle v\rangle^{30+2|\kappa_{v}|}\big|\partial_{t}^{\kappa_{t}}\partial_{z}^{\kappa_{z}}\partial_{v}^{\kappa_{v}}g(t,z,v)\big|^{2}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}z\leq C_{\Lambda}\epsilon.

•

The electromagnetic field
F
and its derivatives up to order
N
are uniformly bounded in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})
and

\sup_{t\geq T}\,\mathcal{E}^{K}_{7}\big[F-F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]\big](t)+\sup_{|\gamma|=7}\,\sup_{t\geq T}\,\mathcal{E}^{K,1}\big[\nabla_{t,x}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}\big(F-F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]\big)\big](t)\lesssim\Lambda.

Moreover, if
T=2
and
C_{\Lambda}\epsilon\leq\varepsilon_{0}
, then

\forall\,|\gamma|\leq N-1,\qquad\qquad\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\langle x\rangle^{2+2|\gamma|}\big|\nabla_{t,x}^{\gamma}\big(F-\overline{F}\,\big)\big|^{2}(2,x)\mathrm{d}x\lesssim\Lambda.

Remark 1.9
.

The finiteness of higher moments of
f_{\infty}
is sufficient in order to propagate stronger norms for
f
, as it is stated by Proposition
7.15
. If
N\geq 9
, the higher order derivatives of
F
satisfy similar weighted
L^{2}_{x}
estimates. However, without stronger velocity decay assumptions for
f_{\infty}
, the norms are slightly less convenient to write.

Remark 1.10
.

In fact, we use a slightly weaker decay assumption on
\underline{\alpha}^{\infty}
than (
25
). Similarly, as suggested by Remark
7.25
,
2-\delta
powers of
|z|
and
6-\delta
powers of
|v|
could be saved in (
24
) but the
L^{2}_{z,v}
estimates for
g
would be weaker and would not allow us to construct the scattering map.

Remark 1.11
.

If
\epsilon
is large, the solution
(f,F)
constructed here is a future-global-in-time large solution to the Vlasov-Maxwell system in the following sense. It is defined for all time
t\geq T
,
F
is of size
\Lambda
in
L^{2}_{x}
and
f
is of size
\epsilon
in
L^{p}_{x,v}
, so that
(f,F)
is large in
L^{p}_{x,v}\times L^{2}_{x}
for any
1\leq p\leq\infty
. The solution is however initially dispersed since
T\gg 1
and, for instance,
|J(f)|(T,x,v)\lesssim T^{-3}
.

Using the involution
\sim
introduced in the previous Section
1.9
, Theorem
1.8
ensures as well that for any sufficiently regular past scattering state, there exists a unique solution to the Vlasov-Maxwell system converging to it. Thus, Theorem
1.8
and
[
8
, Theorem
2.11
]
provide us a small data scattering map for the Vlasov-Maxwell equations, for which the electromagnetic field is allowed to be large.

Theorem 1.12
.

Let
N\geq 8
,
\epsilon>0
,
\Lambda\geq 1
and
(f_{-\infty},\alpha^{-\infty})
be a past scattering state for the Vlasov-Maxwell system verifying the bounds (
24
)–(
25
). There exists an absolute constant
\varepsilon_{1}>0
and
D_{\Lambda}:=\exp\big(\exp\big(D\sqrt{\Lambda}\big)\big)
, where
D>C
depends only on
N
, such that the following statement hold. If
\epsilon D_{\Lambda}\leq\varepsilon_{1}
, then

•

there exists a unique classical solution
(f,F)
to the Vlasov-Maxwell system (
1
)–(
3
), defined for all time
t\in\mathbb{R}
, and a future scattering state
(f_{+\infty},\underline{\alpha}^{+\infty})
, such that

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to\pm\infty}f\big(t,x+t\widehat{v}\pm\mathscr{C}_{t,v},v\big)=f_{\pm\infty}(x,v),\qquad\qquad

\displaystyle\forall\,t>0,\quad\mathscr{C}_{\pm t,v}^{k}:=-\log(|t|)\frac{\delta_{k}^{i}-\widehat{v}_{k}\widehat{v}^{i}}{v^{0}}\widehat{v}^{\mu}\mathbb{F}_{\mu i}[f_{\pm\infty}](v),

for all
(x,v)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
, as well as

\displaystyle\lim_{r\to+\infty}r\alpha(F)(-r+\underline{u},r\omega)=\alpha^{-\infty}(\underline{u},\omega),\qquad\qquad

\displaystyle\lim_{r\to+\infty}r\underline{\alpha}(F)(r+u,r\omega)=\underline{\alpha}^{+\infty}(u,\omega),

for all
\omega\in\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}
,
u\in\mathbb{R}_{u}
and
\underline{u}\in\mathbb{R}_{\underline{u}}
. Moreover, the initial data satisfy

\displaystyle\sup_{|\kappa_{z}|+|\kappa_{v}|\leq N-4}\,\langle z\rangle^{8+|\kappa_{z}|}\,\langle v\rangle^{15+|\kappa_{v}|}\big|\partial_{t}^{\kappa_{t}}\partial_{z}^{\kappa_{z}}\partial_{v}^{\kappa_{v}}f(0,z,v)\big|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\Lambda}\epsilon,

\displaystyle\sup_{|\gamma|\leq N-3}\langle x\rangle^{\frac{5}{2}+|\gamma|}\big|\nabla_{t,x}^{\gamma}\big(F-\overline{F}\,\big)\big|(0,x)

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda

•

The future scattering state verifies the pointwise bounds

\displaystyle\sup_{(z,v)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\,\langle z\rangle^{4-|\kappa_{v}|}\,\langle v\rangle^{5+|\kappa_{v}|}\big|\partial_{z}^{\kappa_{z}}\partial_{v}^{\kappa_{v}}f_{+\infty}(z,v)\big|

\displaystyle\leq D_{\Lambda}\sqrt{\epsilon},\qquad\qquad

\displaystyle|\kappa_{z}|+|\kappa_{v}|\leq N-6,

\displaystyle\sup_{(u,\omega)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}\langle u\rangle^{\frac{3}{2}}\big|\nabla_{\partial_{u}}^{\gamma_{u}}\,\not{\nabla}^{\gamma_{\omega}}\underline{\alpha}^{+\infty}\big|(u,\omega)

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda,\qquad\qquad

\displaystyle\gamma_{u}+|\gamma_{\omega}|\leq N-7.

•

The total energy of the system is conserved. For all
t\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}v^{0}f(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}v+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}|F(t,x)|^{2}\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f_{-\infty}(x,v)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}v+\frac{1}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{\underline{u}}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\big|\alpha^{-\infty}(\underline{u},\omega)\big|^{2}\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\mathrm{d}\underline{u}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f_{+\infty}(x,v)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}v+\frac{1}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{u}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\big|\underline{\alpha}^{+\infty}(u,\omega)\big|^{2}\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\mathrm{d}u.

Remark 1.13
.

Part of the loss of regularity is purely technical and could be avoided, Theorem
1.8
provides
L^{2}
bounds on the solutions whereas we propagated
L^{\infty}
estimates in
[
8
, Theorem
2.11
]
.

Consistently with Remark
1.9
, under stronger decay assumptions on
f_{-\infty}
, Proposition
7.15
and the analysis carried out in
[
8
]
imply that the future scattering state
f_{+\infty}
possibly belongs to a stronger weighted Sobolev space.

Corollary 1.14
.

Let
(f,F)
be one of the solutions to the Vlasov-Maxwell system considered in Theorem
1.12
. If, for
N_{z}\geq 12
and
N_{v}\geq 25
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{B}(N,N_{z},N_{v}):=\sup_{|\kappa_{z}|+|\kappa_{v}|\leq N}\big\|\,\langle z\rangle^{N_{z}+N}\,\langle v\rangle^{N_{v}+|\kappa_{v}|}\,\partial_{z}^{\kappa_{z}}\partial_{v}^{\kappa_{v}}f_{-\infty}\big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}<+\infty,

then, there exists
C>0
, depending only on
(\Lambda,N,N_{z},N_{v})
such that, for any
|\kappa_{z}|+|\kappa_{v}|\leq N-6
,

\sup_{(z,v)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\,\langle z\rangle^{N_{z}-7-|\kappa_{v}|}\,\langle v\rangle^{N_{v}-16+|\kappa_{v}|}\big|\partial_{z}^{\kappa_{z}}\partial_{v}^{\kappa_{v}}f_{+\infty}(z,v)\big|\leq C\mathbf{B}(N,N_{z},N_{v}).

The main ingredients of the proof of Theorem
1.8
are presented in Section
2.5
.

1.11 Related open problems

We list here some questions connected to the results proved in this article.

1.

In the multispecies case or if we allow
f
to take negative values, can a solution to the Vlasov-Maxwell system satisfy linear scattering as
t\to-\infty
and modified scattering as
t\to+\infty
? This problem was raised in the fifth part of
[
25
, Remark
1.2
]
.

2.

Given a past and a future radiation field, does the knowledge of the scattering map allows us to determine the properties of the plasma? In other words, given
(E_{-\infty},B_{-\infty})
and
(E_{+\infty},B_{+\infty})
, is there a unique solution
(f,E,B)
to the Vlasov-Maxwell system such that the past (respectively the future) radiation field of
(E,B)
corresponds to
(E_{-\infty},B_{-\infty})
(respectively
(E_{+\infty},B_{+\infty})
)?

This question, which is part of inverse scattering problems, has been raised to me by Claude Bardos.
(E_{-\infty},B_{-\infty})
would correspond to a signal emitted by a far away observer in the direction of the plasma and
(E_{+\infty},B_{+\infty})
would be the signal measured by this observer after the interaction between the electromagnetic field and the particles.

3.

In the linear case,
t^{3}J(f)(t,t\widehat{v})
has an asymptotic expansion in powers of
t^{-1}
. In the nonlinear case, although the four-current density has a linear behavior at zeroth order, that is
t^{3}J(f)(t,t\widehat{v})\to\int_{z}\langle v\rangle^{5}f_{\infty}(z,v)\mathrm{d}z
, we expect it to have a polyhomogeneous expansion, as the charge density in the context of the Vlasov-Poisson system
[
12
]
. More generally, it would be interesting to determine the asymptotic expansion of
J(f)
as well as
(E,B)
.

1.12 Structure of the paper

We introduce in Section
2
the tools used throughout this article. We further analyse the solutions to the linear Vlasov equation in order to motivate the introduction of the asymptotic Maxwell equations. Then, we present the key ideas of the proof. In Section
3
, we prove energy estimates for both the distribution functions and the electromagnetic field. Section
4
is devoted to scattering results for the Maxwell equations. In particular, we construct different wave operators for the Maxwell equations with a vanishing or a strongly decaying source term.

The asympotic Maxwell equations are introduced in Section
5
. We perform a thorough analysis of its solution
F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]
, which captures the large time dynamics of
F
. In Section
6
, we set up the Picard iteration which will allow us to construct the class of solutions
(f,F)
to the Vlasov-Maxwell system presented in Theorem
1.8
. If
N\geq 9
, for the purpose of working in a more natural functional space and simplifying the presentation of the proof, we will first make a stronger assumption on
f_{\infty}
than (
24
). The modifications to bring to the proof in order to deal with the general case are presented in Section
10
. We control the sequence of approximate solutions to (
1
)–(
2
) in a suitable functional space throughout Sections
7
–
8
. In particular, we study the Vlasov equation (respectively the Maxwell equations), with a prescribed electromagnetic field (respectively a prescribed current density) and asymptotic data. We conclude the proof of Theorem
1.8
by proving in Section
9
that the sequence of approximate solutions is Cauchy for a weaker energy norm. Finally, Section
A
contains elliptic estimates.

2
Preliminaries

2.1 Commutation vector fields

Our analysis of the electromagnetic field will rely on vector field methods, initially introduced in
[
40
]
in order to study solutions to wave equations and then adapted to the Maxwell equations by
[
19
]
. It will in particular allow us to obtain improved decay estimates for the good null components
\alpha
,
\rho
and
\sigma
. These kind of approaches are usually based on

•

a set of weighted derivatives (vector fields)
commuting
with the linear operator of the equations studied.

•

Energy estimates, used to control
L^{p}
norms of the solutions and their derivatives, such as Proposition
3.6
below. Vector fields are here used as
multipliers
.

•

Weighted Sobolev inequalities, such as Proposition
3.13
stated in the next Section
3
, providing decay estimates on the fields.

The set of the commutators for the Maxwell equations is composed of Killing vector fields and the scaling vector field
S
, which is merely conformal Killing. They then generate (conformal) isometries of the Minkowski space.

Definition 2.1
.

Let
\mathbb{K}
be the set composed by the vector fields

\partial_{t},\qquad\partial_{x^{k}},\qquad\Omega_{ij}:=x^{i}\partial_{x^{j}}-x^{j}\partial_{x^{i}},\qquad\Omega_{0k}:=t\partial_{x^{k}}+x^{k}\partial_{t},\qquad S:=t\partial_{t}+x^{\ell}\partial_{x^{\ell}}=t\partial_{t}+r\partial_{r},

where
1\leq k\leq 3
and
1\leq i<j\leq 3
.

Remark 2.2
.

The vector fields
\partial_{x^{\mu}}
(respectively
\Omega_{ij}
and
\Omega_{0k}
) are the infinitesimal generators of translations (respectively rotations and Lorentz boosts). Usually, the set
\mathbb{K}\setminus\{S\}
is denoted by
\mathbb{P}
.

To commute the Maxwell equations, it is important to differentiate the electromagnetic field geometrically. For a
2
-form
F
and a vector field
Z=Z^{\mu}\partial_{x^{\mu}}
, the Lie derivative
\mathcal{L}_{Z}(F)
of
F
with respect to
Z
is defined, in coordinates, as

\mathcal{L}_{Z}(F)_{\mu\nu}=Z(F_{\mu\nu})+\partial_{\mu}(Z^{\lambda})F_{\lambda\nu}+\partial_{\nu}(Z^{\lambda})F_{\mu\lambda}.

(26)

Similarly, if
J
is
1
-form, its Lie derivative with respect to
Z
is

\mathcal{L}_{Z}(J)_{\nu}=Z(J_{\nu})+\partial_{x^{\nu}}(Z^{\lambda})J_{\lambda}.

Lemma 2.3
.

Let
F
be a solution to the Maxwell equations with source term
J
,

\nabla^{\mu}F_{\mu\nu}=J_{\nu},\qquad\qquad\nabla^{\mu}{}^{*}\!F_{\mu\nu}=0.

Then, for all Killing vector field
Z\in\mathbb{K}\setminus\{S\}
, we have

\nabla^{\mu}\mathcal{L}_{Z}(F)_{\mu\nu}=\mathcal{L}_{Z}(J)_{\nu},\qquad\nabla^{\mu}{}^{*}\!\mathcal{L}_{Z}(F)_{\mu\nu}=0\,;\qquad\quad\;\nabla^{\mu}\mathcal{L}_{S}(F)_{\mu\nu}=\mathcal{L}_{S}(J)_{\nu}+2J_{\nu},\qquad\nabla^{\mu}{}^{*}\!\mathcal{L}_{S}(F)_{\mu\nu}=0.

Thus, if
F
is solution to the vacuum Maxwell equations
\nabla^{\mu}F_{\mu\nu}=\nabla^{\mu}{}^{*}\!F_{\mu\nu}=0
, then same holds true for
\mathcal{L}_{Z}F
for all conformal Killing vector field
Z\in\mathbb{K}
.

In the context of the Vlasov-Maxwell system, controlling the derivatives of
F
requires to bound
ZJ(f)
. Motivated by this problem, Fajman-Joudioux-Smulevici developed a vector field method for Vlasov equations compatible with the one for wave equations
[
23
]
. Their approach relies on the fact that for any Killing vector field
Z
, its complete lift
4
4

4

We refer to
[
23
, Section
2G
]
for more details about the relations between the Vlasov operator on a Lorentzian manifold and the complete lift of its Killing vector fields.

\widehat{Z}
commute with
v^{0}\mathbf{T}_{0}
, where
\mathbf{T}_{0}:=\partial_{t}+\widehat{v}\cdot\nabla_{x}
is the linear transport operator. The scaling, which verifies
[\mathbf{T}_{0},S]=\mathbf{T}_{0}
, will also be useful.

Definition 2.4
.

Let
\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{S}
be the set composed by

\widehat{\partial_{t}}=\partial_{t},\quad\widehat{\partial_{x^{k}}}=\partial_{x^{k}},\quad\widehat{\Omega}_{ij}:=x^{i}\partial_{x^{j}}-x^{j}\partial_{x^{i}}+v^{i}\partial_{v^{j}}-v^{j}\partial_{v^{i}},\quad\widehat{\Omega}_{0k}:=t\partial_{x^{k}}+x^{k}\partial_{t}+v^{0}\partial_{v^{k}},\quad\widehat{S}=t\partial_{t}+r\partial_{r}+3,

where
1\leq k\leq 3
and
1\leq i<j\leq 3
.

Remark 2.5
.

Although the differential operator
S+3
is not the complete lift of
S
, we will denote it by
\widehat{S}
for simplicity. We chose to work with it in order to lighten the presentation and simplify the study of the commuted Maxwell equations (see Proposition
2.8
below).

Remark 2.6
.

The good commutation properties of
S
with the free transport operator
\mathbf{T}_{0}
are explained by the relations
S=t\mathbf{T}_{0}+(x^{i}-t\widehat{v}^{i})\partial_{x^{i}}
and
\mathbf{T}_{0}(x-t\widehat{v})=0
, so that
x-t\widehat{v}
is conserved along the linear flow.

For the purpose of considering higher order derivatives, we introduce an ordering on
\mathbb{K}
and
\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{S}
,

\mathbb{K}=\{Z^{i}\,|\,1\leq i\leq 11\},\qquad\qquad\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{S}=\{\widehat{Z}^{i}\,|\,1\leq i\leq 11\}.

It will be convenient to assume that
Z^{11}=\widehat{Z}^{11}=S
and
\widehat{Z^{i}}=\widehat{Z}^{i}
for any
1\leq i\leq 10
. Moreover, for a multi-index
\kappa\in\llbracket 1,11\rrbracket^{p}
of length
|\kappa|=p
, we denote by
\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\kappa}}
the Lie derivative
\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\kappa_{1}}}\dots\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\kappa_{p}}}
of order
|\kappa|
. Similarly, we define
\widehat{Z}^{\kappa}
as
\widehat{Z}^{\kappa_{1}}\dots\widehat{Z}^{\kappa_{p}}
. Note the equivalence between the pointwise norms

\;\sup_{|\gamma|\leq N}\left|\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}(F)\right|\lesssim\sup_{|\beta|\leq N}\,\sup_{0\leq\mu,\nu\leq 3}\left|Z^{\beta}(F_{\mu\nu})\right|\lesssim\sup_{|\gamma|\leq N}\left|\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}(F)\right|.

(27)

In order to exploit certain hierarchies in the commuted equations, we will denote, for a multi-index
\beta
, by
\beta_{H}
(respectively
\beta_{T}
) the number of homogeneous vector fields (respectively translations) composing
\widehat{Z}^{\beta}
as well as
Z^{\beta}
. For instance,

Z^{\beta}=S\partial_{t}\Omega_{23}\partial_{x^{1}}\Omega_{02},\qquad\qquad|\beta|=5,\quad\beta_{H}=3,\quad\beta_{T}=2.

The following geometric formula, proved in
[
7
, Lemma
2.8
]
, will be fundamental for us.

Lemma 2.7
.

Let
H
be a
2
-form and
h:[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be a function, both sufficiently regular. Let further
Z\in\mathbb{K}\setminus\{S\}
be a Killing vector field and
\widehat{Z}\in\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{S}\setminus\{S\}
be its complete lift. Then, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{Z}\big(J(h)\big)=J(\widehat{Z}h),\qquad\qquad\qquad\mathcal{L}_{S}\big(J(h)\big)=J(Sh)+J(h)

as well as

\displaystyle\widehat{Z}\left(v^{\mu}{H_{\mu}}^{j}\partial_{v^{j}}h\right)=v^{\mu}{\mathcal{L}_{Z}(H)_{\mu}}^{j}\partial_{v^{j}}h+v^{\mu}{H_{\mu}}^{j}\partial_{v^{j}}\widehat{Z}h,

\displaystyle S\left(v^{\mu}{H_{\mu}}^{j}\partial_{v^{j}}h\right)=v^{\mu}{\mathcal{L}_{S}(H)_{\mu}}^{j}\partial_{v^{j}}h-2v^{\mu}{H_{\mu}}^{j}\partial_{v^{j}}h+v^{\mu}{H_{\mu}}^{j}\partial_{v^{j}}Sh.

Iterating Lemmata
2.3
and
2.7
, we obtain that the structure of the Vlasov-Maxwell equations (
19
)–(
20
) are preserved by commutation.

Proposition 2.8
.

Let
(f,F)
be a sufficiently regular solution to the Vlasov-Maxwell system. For any multi-index
\beta
, there exists
C^{\beta}_{\gamma,\kappa}\in\mathbb{Z}
such that

\displaystyle\mathbf{T}_{F}\big(\widehat{Z}^{\beta}f\big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{|\kappa|\leq|\beta|-1}\,\sum_{|\gamma|+|\kappa|\leq|\beta|}C^{\beta}_{\gamma,\kappa}\;\widehat{v}^{\mu}{\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}(F)_{\mu}}^{j}\partial_{v^{j}}\widehat{Z}^{\kappa}f,

\displaystyle\nabla^{\mu}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\beta}}(F)_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=J(\widehat{Z}^{\beta}f),\qquad\qquad\nabla^{\mu}{}^{*}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\beta}}(F)_{\mu\nu}=0.

The vector field method developed by Fajman-Joudioux-Smulevici
[
23
]
for the study of relativistic transport equations lead to a proof of the stability of Minkowski spacetime for both the massive and massless Einstein-Vlasov system
[
24
,
10
]
(these results were also obtained by
[
45
,
70
,
68
]
using different approaches). Our analysis carried out in
[
5
,
8
]
of the small data solutions to the Vlasov-Maxwell system rely on such techniques as well.

Vector field methods have also been used in order to derive boundedness and decay estimates for the solutions to the massless Vlasov equation on a fixed Schwarzschild or slowly rotating Kerr black hole
[
1
,
9
]
. However, due to the difficulties related to the trapped null geodesics, the control of certain derivatives of the solutions still remain to be understood. In this perspective, Velozo Ruiz-Velozo Ruiz studied in
[
61
]
the small data solutions to the Vlasov-Poisson system with an external trapping potential by developing a commuting vector field approach adapted to such problems. It turns out that these solutions exhibit, in dimension
2
, a modified scattering dynamic
[
11
]
.

Finally, in the non-relativistic setting, such approaches have been applied to the Vlasov-Poisson and Vlasov-Yakawa systems
[
66
,
22
]
as well as in the collisional regime
[
16
,
15
,
17
]
.

2.2 Weights capturing the good behavior of distribution functions for
t\sim|x|
and
|x|\geq t

Since the (massive) particles follow timelike trajectories, most of the matter should be located in the interior of the light cone when
t\gg 1
. We then expect Vlasov fields to enjoy stronger decay estimates near and in the exterior of the light cone
t=|x|
. Moreover, as for electromagnetic fields, we expect the four-current density
J(f)
to have a better behavior in the good null directions
(L,e_{\theta},e_{\varphi})
. For this, we introduce
(v^{L},v^{\underline{L}},v^{e_{1}},v^{e_{2}})
, the null components of the four-momentum vector
\mathbf{v}
, and
\not{v}=(v^{e_{\theta}},v^{e_{\varphi}})
, its angular part, so that

\mathbf{v}=v^{L}L+v^{\underline{L}}\underline{L}+v^{e_{\theta}}e_{\theta}+v^{e_{\varphi}}e_{\varphi},\qquad v^{L}=\frac{v^{0}+\frac{x_{i}}{r}v^{i}}{2},\qquad v^{\underline{L}}=\frac{v^{0}-\frac{x_{i}}{r}v^{i}}{2},\qquad|\not{v}|^{2}=|v^{e_{\theta}}|^{2}+|v^{e_{\varphi}}|^{2}.

Sometimes, we will write
v^{\underline{L}}(x)
or
v^{\underline{L}}(\omega)
in order to emphasise the dependence of this quantity in the spherical variables
\omega=x/|x|
. For convenience, we define as well

\widehat{v}^{0}:=\frac{v^{0}}{v^{0}}=1,\qquad\widehat{v}^{L}:=\frac{v^{L}}{v^{0}},\qquad\widehat{v}^{\underline{L}}:=\frac{v^{\underline{L}}}{v^{0}},\qquad\not{\widehat{v}}:=\frac{\not{v}}{v^{0}},\qquad\widehat{v}^{e_{A}}:=\frac{v^{e_{A}}}{v^{0}},\quad A\in\{\theta,\varphi\}.

In the linear setting, the improved decay properties can be obtained by exploiting the weight

\langle x-t\widehat{v}\rangle,\qquad\qquad\mathbf{T}_{0}(\langle x-t\widehat{v}\rangle)=\widehat{v}^{\mu}\partial_{x^{\mu}}(\langle x-t\widehat{v}\rangle)=0,

which is then constant, equal to
\langle x\rangle
, along the linear flow
t\mapsto(t,x+t\widehat{v},v)
. Consequently, if
\mathbf{T}_{0}(f)=0
, one can propagate boundedness for
\langle x-t\widehat{v}\rangle^{a}f
and obtain extra decay for
f
in the favourable regions using the next inequalities.

Lemma 2.9
.

For all
(t,x,v)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
, there holds

1+|\not{v}|^{2}\leq 4\langle v\rangle^{2}\widehat{v}^{\underline{L}},\qquad\qquad\widehat{v}^{\underline{L}}(x)\lesssim\frac{\max(t-|x|,1)}{\langle t+|x|\rangle}\langle x-t\widehat{v}\rangle,\qquad\qquad\big|\not{\widehat{v}}\big|(x)\lesssim\frac{\langle x-t\widehat{v}\rangle}{\langle t+|x|\rangle}.

It implies in particular

\forall\;(t,x,v)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v},\qquad 1\lesssim\frac{\max(t-|x|,1)}{\langle t+|x|\rangle}\langle v\rangle^{2}\langle x-t\widehat{v}\rangle.

Proof.

The first inequality is implied by the mass-shell relation
\eta(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v})=-1
, which implies
-4v^{L}v^{\underline{L}}+|\not{v}|^{2}=-1
, and
v^{L}\leq v^{0}
. For the second one, use first

2tv^{\underline{L}}(x)=(t-|x|)v^{0}-\frac{x_{i}}{|x|}v^{0}\big(t\widehat{v}^{i}-x^{i}\big),\qquad|x|v^{\underline{L}}(x)=(|x|-t|)v^{\underline{L}}(x)+tv^{\underline{L}}(x)

in order to deal with the interior of the light cone
t\geq|x|
. If
|x|\geq t
, remark further that
|x|-t\leq|x-t\widehat{v}|
. Finally, for the last one we have

|\not{v}(x)|^{2}=\frac{1}{|x|^{2}}\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq 3}|x^{i}v^{j}-x^{j}v^{i}|^{2},\quad\;x^{i}v^{j}-x^{j}v^{i}=v^{j}(x^{i}-t\widehat{v}^{i})-v^{i}(x^{j}-t\widehat{v}^{j})=\frac{x^{i}}{t}(t\widehat{v}^{j}-x^{j})-\frac{x^{j}}{t}(t\widehat{v}^{i}-x^{i}).

2.3 Linear Vlasov fields

The purpose of this section, where we will recall results concerning the asymptotic behavior of solutions to the free massive relativistic transport equation
\mathbf{T}_{0}(f)=\partial_{t}f+\widehat{v}\cdot\nabla_{x}f=0
, is twofold.

1.

We will initialise the Picard iteration for the proof of Theorem
1.8
by considering
f_{1}(t,x,v):=f_{\infty}(x-t\widehat{v},v)
, the unique solution to the free Vlasov equation equal to
f_{\infty}
at
t=0
. Moreover, certain of the estimates proved in this section will in fact be useful later in the paper as well.

2.

The results of this section will give insights concerning distribution functions satisfying modified scattering
f(t,x+t\widehat{v}+\mathscr{C}_{t,v},v)\to f_{\infty}(x,v)
. In particular, the velocity averages of such a distribution function, which decays as
t^{-3}
, behave as the ones of
f_{1}
. More precisely
|J(\widehat{Z}^{\beta}f)-J(\widehat{Z}^{\beta}f_{1})|(t,x)=O(t^{-4}\log(t))
. It will motivate us to introduce the asymptotic Maxwell equations.

Let
N\in\mathbb{N}
and assume that
f_{\infty}
satisfy the assumptions of Theorem
1.8
. Recall that
[\mathbf{T}_{0},\widehat{Z}]=0
for any
\widehat{Z}\in\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{S}\setminus\{S\}
,
[\mathbf{T}_{0},\widehat{S}]=\mathbf{T}_{0}
and
\mathbf{T}_{0}(\langle v\rangle)=\mathbf{T}_{0}(x-t\widehat{v})=0
. Thus, for any
(N_{z},N_{v})\in\mathbb{N}^{2}
,

\forall\,(t,x,v)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v},\quad\langle x-t\widehat{v}\rangle^{N_{z}}\langle v\rangle^{N_{v}}\widehat{Z}^{\beta}f_{1}(t,x,v)=\langle x\rangle^{N_{z}}\langle v\rangle^{N_{v}}\widehat{Z}^{\beta}f_{1}(0,x,v),\qquad|\beta|\leq N+1.

Recall that
\beta_{H}
denotes the number of homogeneous vector fields composing
\widehat{Z}^{\beta}
. We then deduce, in view of the decay assumption (
24
) on
f_{\infty}
, that

\hskip-28.45274pt\forall\,(t,x,v)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v},\qquad\langle x-t\widehat{v}\rangle^{12+N-\beta_{H}}\langle v\rangle^{25}\big|\widehat{Z}^{\beta}f_{1}\big|(t,x,v)\lesssim\sqrt{\epsilon},\qquad\qquad|\beta|\leq N+1.

(28)

Moreover, using the equation
\mathbf{T}_{0}(\widehat{Z}^{\beta}f)=0
and performing integration by parts in
x
, we have further the conservation of the spatial averages,

\hskip-17.07164pt\forall\,(t,v)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v},\quad\langle v\rangle^{N_{v}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\widehat{Z}^{\beta}f_{1}(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}x=\langle v\rangle^{N_{v}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\widehat{Z}^{\beta}f_{1}(0,x,v)\mathrm{d}x,\qquad\qquad|\beta|\leq N+1.

(29)

2.3.1 Asymptotic behavior of the current density

The goal now is to derive the large time behavior of
J(\widehat{Z}^{\beta}f_{1})
, which is closely related to the spatial average of
\widehat{Z}^{\beta}f_{1}
. For this, we recall a standard identity, proved for instance in
[
8
, Lemma
2.9
]
.

Lemma 2.10
.

For all
(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
, the Jacobian determinant of
v\mapsto x-t\widehat{v}
is equal to
-t^{3}|v^{0}|^{-5}
.

In order to be able to apply the next results later in this paper, we perform an analysis in a slightly more general setting. Let

f:[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\rightarrow\mathbb{R},\qquad\qquad h(t,z,v):=f(t,z+t\widehat{v},v),\qquad\qquad T\geq 0,

and remark that
h
is contant if
f=f_{1}
. Here, we will assume that
f
behaves well along the linear characteristics
t\mapsto(t,x+t\widehat{v},v)
but we will not require
h
to be constant. Though, we stress that in the next statements, all the norms appearing in the right hand sides of the inequalities will be conserved in time if
f=f_{1}
.

The optimal
L^{\infty}_{x}
and
L^{2}_{x}
decay can be obtained through the next estimate.

Proposition 2.11
.

There holds, for all
(t,x)\in[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
,

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}|f|(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}v\lesssim\frac{1}{\langle t+|x|\rangle^{3}}\sup_{(z,v)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\langle x-t\widehat{v}\rangle^{4}\,\langle v\rangle^{5}\big|f(t,x,v)\big|.

Similarly, we have for all
t\geq T
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\langle t+|x|\rangle^{3}\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}|f|(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}v\bigg|^{2}\mathrm{d}x\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\langle x-t\widehat{v}\rangle^{4}\,\langle v\rangle^{5}\big|f(t,x,v)\big|^{2}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x.

.

Proof.

By applying Hölder’s inequality in the variable
v
, we reduce the problem to the proof of

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\frac{\mathrm{d}v}{\langle x-t\widehat{v}\rangle^{4}\,\langle v\rangle^{5}}\lesssim\frac{1}{\langle t+|x|\rangle^{3}}.

(30)

If
t\leq 4\langle x\rangle
, we have
2\langle x-t\widehat{v}\rangle\geq\langle t+|x|\rangle
and the inequality holds. Otherwise, we use Lemma
2.10
in order to perform the change of variables
y=x-t\widehat{v}
. Since
t\gtrsim\langle t+|x|\rangle
in that case, this concludes the proof.

Applying this result to
\langle x-t\widehat{v}\rangle^{a}\,\langle v\rangle^{2a}f(t,x,v)
and using Lemma
2.9
, one can derive improved decay estimates for Vlasov fields near and in the exterior of the light cone
t=|x|
.

Corollary 2.12
.

For any
a\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
, we have

\forall\,(t,x)\in[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3},\qquad\quad\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}|f|(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}v\lesssim\frac{|\max(t-|x|,1)|^{a}}{\langle t+|x|\rangle^{3+a}}\sup_{(z,v)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\langle x-t\widehat{v}\rangle^{4+a}\,\langle v\rangle^{5+2a}\big|f(t,x,v)\big|.

By requiring more regularity on
f
, one can refine the previous result. In this paper, we will mainly use
L^{2}_{x}
estimates but analogous ones hold in
L^{p}_{x}
,
1\leq p\leq\infty
.

Proposition 2.13
.

For all
t\geq T
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\langle t+|x|\rangle^{5}\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}v-\frac{\mathds{1}_{|x|<t}}{t^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{y}}\Big[|v^{0}|^{5}f\Big]\bigg(t,y,\frac{\widecheck{\;x\;}}{t}\bigg)

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}y\bigg|^{2}\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sup_{|\beta|\leq 1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\langle x-t\widehat{v}\rangle^{8}\,\langle v\rangle^{14}\big|\widehat{Z}^{\beta}f(t,x,v)\big|^{2}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x.

Proof.

Let
t\geq T
. Recall from Lemma
2.9
that
\langle x\rangle\lesssim\langle x-t\widehat{v}\rangle\,\langle v\rangle^{2}
for all
|x|\geq t
. Consequently, using also the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality as well as
v\mapsto\langle v\rangle^{-4}\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})
,

\displaystyle\int_{|x|\geq t}\langle x\rangle^{5}\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}v\bigg|^{2}\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{|x|\geq t}\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\langle x-t\widehat{v}\rangle^{\frac{5}{2}}\,\langle v\rangle^{5}\,f(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}v\bigg|^{2}\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq\int_{|x|\geq t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\langle x-t\widehat{v}\rangle^{5}\,\langle v\rangle^{14}\,|f|^{2}(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x.

Consider now the case
|x|<t
and
t\geq 1
. We perform the change of variables
y=x-t\widehat{v}
in order to get

t^{3}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}v=\int_{|y-x|<t}\Big[|v^{0}|^{5}h\Big]\bigg(t,y,\frac{\widecheck{x-y}}{t}\bigg)\mathrm{d}y,

where we recall that
\widecheck{\;\color[rgb]{1,1,1}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\;}
, the inverse of the relativistic speed operator, is introduced in Definition
1.2
. Let
h_{0}(t,y,w):=[|v^{0}|^{5}h](t,y,\widecheck{w})=(1-|w|^{2})^{-5/2}h(t,y,\widecheck{w})
, so that

t^{3}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}v=\int_{|y-x|<t}\Big[|v^{0}|^{5}h\Big]\bigg(t,y,\frac{\widecheck{\;x\;}}{t}\bigg)\mathrm{d}y+\int_{|y-x|<t}\int_{\tau=0}^{1}\frac{y}{t}\cdot\nabla_{w}h_{0}\Big(t,y,\frac{x-\tau y}{t}\Big)\mathrm{d}\tau\mathrm{d}y.

We point out that
|x|<t
and
|y-x|<t
implies
|x-\tau y|<t
. We then get from the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality in
y
that

\int_{|x|<t}t^{5}\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}v-\frac{1}{t^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{y}}\Big[|v^{0}|^{5}h\Big]\bigg(t,y,\frac{\widecheck{\;x\;}}{t}\bigg)\mathrm{d}y\bigg|^{2}\mathrm{d}x\leq\mathcal{I}_{1}+\mathcal{I}_{2},

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{1}

\displaystyle:=\int_{|x|<t}\frac{1}{t}\int_{|y-x|\geq t}\langle y\rangle^{4}\Big||v^{0}|^{5}h\Big|^{2}\bigg(t,y,\frac{\widecheck{\;x\;}}{t}\bigg)\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}x,

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{2}

\displaystyle:=\int_{|x|<t}\frac{1}{t}\int_{|y-x|<t}\int_{\tau=0}^{1}\frac{|y|^{2}}{t^{2}}\langle y\rangle^{4}\big|\nabla_{w}h_{0}\big|^{2}\Big(t,y,\frac{x-\tau y}{t}\Big)\mathrm{d}\tau\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}x.

In order to bound
\mathcal{I}_{1}
recall that
|x|<t
and remark that, for
v=\widecheck{x/t}
and any
y\in\mathbb{R}
such that
|y-x|\geq t
,

1=|v^{0}|^{2}\left(1-\frac{|x|^{2}}{t^{2}}\right)\leq|v^{0}|^{2}\frac{|y|(t+|x|)}{t^{2}}\leq 2\frac{|y||v^{0}|^{2}}{t}.

It allows us to get

\mathcal{I}_{1}\leq 4\int_{|x|<t}\frac{1}{t^{3}}\int_{|y-x|\geq t}\langle y\rangle^{6}\Big||v^{0}|^{7}h\Big|^{2}\bigg(t,y,\frac{\widecheck{\;x\;}}{t}\bigg)\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}x\leq 4\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{y}}\int_{|x|<t}\langle y\rangle^{6}\Big||v^{0}|^{7}h\Big|^{2}\bigg(t,y,\frac{\widecheck{\;x\;}}{t}\bigg)\frac{\mathrm{d}x}{t^{3}}\mathrm{d}y.

Thus, performing the reverse change of variables
x=t\widehat{v}
, one obtains

\mathcal{I}_{1}\leq 4\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{y}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\langle y\rangle^{6}|v^{0}|^{9}|h|^{2}(t,y,v)\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}y.

For
\mathcal{I}_{2}
, since
|\nabla_{w}\widecheck{w}|\lesssim|1-|w|^{2}|^{-3/2}=\langle\widecheck{w}\rangle^{3}=|v^{0}(\widecheck{w})|^{3}
, we have

\left|\nabla_{w}h_{0}\right|(t,y,w)\lesssim\Big||v^{0}|^{7}h\Big|(t,y,\widehat{w})+\Big||v^{0}|^{8}\nabla_{v}h\Big|(t,y,\widehat{w}).

We then deduce from Fubini’s theorem that

\mathcal{I}_{2}=\int_{\tau=0}^{1}\int_{|y|<2t}\int_{\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\,|\,|x|<t,\,|x-y|<t\}}\langle y\rangle^{6}\Big||v^{0}|^{7}h\Big|^{2}\bigg(t,y,\frac{\widecheck{x-\tau y}}{t}\bigg)+\langle y\rangle^{6}\Big||v^{0}|^{8}\nabla_{v}h\Big|^{2}\bigg(t,y,\frac{\widecheck{x-\tau y}}{t}\bigg)\frac{\mathrm{d}x}{t^{3}}\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}\tau.

Hence, the change of variables
x/t=\tau y/t+\widehat{v}
yields

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\tau=0}^{1}\int_{|y|<2t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\langle y\rangle^{6}|v^{0}|^{9}|h|^{2}(t,y,v)+\langle y\rangle^{6}|v^{0}|^{9}\big|v^{0}\nabla_{v}h\big|^{2}(t,y,v)\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{y}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\langle y\rangle^{6}|v^{0}|^{9}\Big(|h|^{2}+\big|v^{0}\nabla_{v}h\big|^{2}\Big)(t,y,v)\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}y.

To conclude it suffices to use
|v^{0}\nabla_{v}h|(t,y,v)\lesssim\langle y\rangle\,\sup_{|\beta|=1}\big|\widehat{Z}^{\beta}f\big|(t,y+t\widehat{v},v)
, which will be proved thereafter in Lemma
2.14
, and to make, for fixed
v
, the linear change of variables
y=x-t\widehat{v}
.

We now would like to relate
\widehat{Z}^{\beta}f
to weighted derivatives of
h
for two reasons. First, in order to conclude the proof of the previous proposition. Secondly, if
f
and its derivatives satisfy
linear scattering
,
\widehat{Z}^{\beta}f(t,x+t\widehat{v},v)
converges as
t\to+\infty
to
h_{\infty}^{\beta}(x,v)
, we would like to be able to express
h^{\beta}
in terms of derivatives of
h_{\infty}
, the limit of
h
. If
f=f_{1}
, it will give us the relation between
\widehat{Z}^{\beta}f_{1}
and the derivatives of
f_{\infty}
.

Lemma 2.14
.

The following relations hold, for all
(t,z,v)\in[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
,

\big(\partial_{x^{k}}f\big)(t,z+t\widehat{v},v)=\partial_{z^{k}}h(t,z,v),\qquad\big(Sf\big)(t,z+t\widehat{v},v)=Sh(t,z,v),\qquad\big(\widehat{\Omega}_{ij}f\big)(t,z+t\widehat{v},v)=\widehat{\Omega}_{ij}h(t,z,v),

for any
1\leq i<j\leq 3
and
1\leq k\leq 3
. For the time derivative and the Lorentz boosts, we have

\displaystyle\big(\partial_{t}f\big)(t,z+t\widehat{v},v)

\displaystyle=\partial_{t}h(t,z,v)-\widehat{v}\cdot\nabla_{z}h(t,x,v),

\displaystyle\big(\widehat{\Omega}_{0k}f\big)(t,z+t\widehat{v},v)

\displaystyle=v^{0}\partial_{v^{k}}h(t,z,v)-z^{k}\widehat{v}\cdot\nabla_{z}h(t,z,v)+z^{k}\partial_{t}h(t,z,v)+\widehat{v}^{k}t\partial_{t}h(t,z,v).

Proof.

Since
f(t,x,v)=h(t,x-t\widehat{v},v)
and
h(t,z,v)=f(t,z+t\widehat{v},v)
, we have, for any
1\leq k\leq 3
,

\displaystyle\partial_{x^{k}}f(t,x,v)

\displaystyle=\partial_{z^{k}}h(t,x-t\widehat{v},v),\qquad\qquad\partial_{t}f(t,x,v)=\big(\partial_{t}h-\widehat{v}\cdot\nabla_{z}h\big)(t,z-t\widehat{v},v),

\displaystyle v^{0}\partial_{v^{k}}f(t,x,v)

\displaystyle=v^{0}\partial_{v^{k}}h(t,x-t\widehat{v},v)-t\partial_{z^{k}}h(t,x-t\widehat{v},v)+t\widehat{v}_{k}\big[\widehat{v}\cdot\nabla_{z}h\big](t,x-t\widehat{v},v).

It implies the stated relations. For instance,

\displaystyle\big(\widehat{\Omega}_{ij}f\big)(t,z+t\widehat{v},v)

\displaystyle=\!\big(z^{i}+t\widehat{v}^{i}\big)\partial_{x^{j}}f(t,z+t\widehat{v},v)-\big(z^{j}+t\widehat{v}^{j}\big)\partial_{x^{i}}f(t,z+t\widehat{v},v)+\!\big[v^{i}\partial_{v^{j}}f-v^{j}\partial_{v^{i}}f\big](t,z+t\widehat{v},v)

\displaystyle=z^{i}\partial_{z^{j}}h(t,z,v)-z^{j}\partial_{z^{i}}h(t,z,v)+v^{i}\partial_{v^{j}}h(t,z,v)-v^{j}\partial_{v^{i}}h(t,z,v)=\widehat{\Omega}_{ij}h(t,z,v).

Motivated by these identities, we introduce the following set of weighted derivatives, which will turn out to be very convenient to use in the context of modified scattering as well.

Definition 2.15
.

Let
\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{S}^{\infty}
be the set composed of the following vector fields, defined on
\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
,

\partial_{z^{k}},\quad\widehat{\Omega}_{ij}\!=\!z^{i}\partial_{z^{j}}-z^{j}\partial_{z^{i}}+v^{i}\partial_{v^{j}}-v^{j}\partial_{v^{i}},\quad\widehat{S}\!=\!t\partial_{t}+z^{i}\partial_{z^{i}}+3,\quad\partial_{t}^{\infty}\!:=\partial_{t}-\widehat{v}\cdot\nabla_{z},\quad\widehat{\Omega}_{0k}^{\infty}\!:=\!z^{k}\partial_{t}^{\infty}+\widehat{v}^{k}t\partial_{t}+v^{0}\partial_{v^{k}},

where
1\leq k\leq 3
and
1\leq i<j\leq 3
. We will denote by
\widehat{Z}_{\infty}
a generic vector field of
\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{S}^{\infty}
and we consider an ordering on this set compatible with the one on
\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{S}
, in the sense that the label of
\partial_{t}^{\infty}
(respectively
\partial_{z^{k}}
,
\widehat{\Omega}_{ij}
,
\widehat{\Omega}_{0k}^{\infty}
and
\widehat{S}
) corresponds to the one of
\partial_{t}
(respectively
\partial_{x^{k}}
,
\widehat{\Omega}_{ij}
,
\widehat{\Omega}_{0k}
and
\widehat{S}
). Note then that, given a multi-index
\kappa
,
\kappa_{H}
still corresponds to the number of homogeneous vector fields composing
\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\kappa}
.

Remark 2.16
.

When applied to a function independent of
t
, such as
f_{\infty}
, the operators
\partial_{t}^{\infty}
,
\widehat{\Omega}_{0k}^{\infty}
and
\widehat{S}
correspond to
-\widehat{v}\cdot\nabla_{z}
,
z^{i}\partial_{z^{i}}+3
and
-z^{k}\widehat{v}\cdot\nabla_{z}+v^{0}\partial_{v^{k}}
.

One can derive the next estimate of
J(\widehat{Z}^{\beta}f_{1})
by applying Proposition
2.13
to
f=\widehat{v}_{\nu}\widehat{Z}^{\beta}f_{1}
as well as the uniform bounds (
28
) and the conservation of the spatial averages (
29
). Note further that
\widehat{v}_{\nu}=\frac{x_{\nu}}{t}
for
v=\frac{\widecheck{\,x\,}}{t}
.

Corollary 2.17
.

Recall that
\widehat{v}_{0}=-1
and
x_{0}=-t
. For any
|\beta|\leq N
and all
t\geq T
,

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\langle t+|x|\rangle^{5}\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\widehat{v}_{\nu}\widehat{Z}^{\beta}f_{1}(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}v-\frac{\mathds{1}_{|x|<t}}{t^{3}}\frac{x_{\nu}}{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}\Big[|v^{0}|^{5}\widehat{Z}^{\beta}_{\infty}f_{\infty}\Big]\bigg(z,\frac{\widecheck{\;x\;}}{t}\bigg)\mathrm{d}y\bigg|^{2}\mathrm{d}x\lesssim\epsilon.

In the context of the Vlasov-Maxwell system,
f
does not verify linear scattering but converges along correction of the linear characteristics
t\mapsto(t,x+t\widehat{v}+\mathscr{C}_{t,v},v)
to a limit distribution, say
h_{\infty}
. However, if
\widehat{Z}\in\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{S}
is a homogeneous vector field,
\widehat{Z}f
does not satisfy modified scattering. Instead, we proved in
[
8
, Theorem
2.11
]
that

•

there exists
\widehat{Z}^{\mathrm{mod}}
, a logarithmical correction of
\widehat{Z}
, such that modified scattering holds for
\widehat{Z}^{\mathrm{mod}}f
. Moreover, its limit distribution is
\widehat{Z}_{\infty}h_{\infty}
.

•

The spatial average of
\widehat{Z}f
verifies
