\big|\int_{x}\widehat{Z}f(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}x-\int_{z}\widehat{Z}_{\infty}h_{\infty}(z,v)\mathrm{d}z\big|\lesssim t^{-1}\log^{2}(t)
. In some sense, although
\widehat{Z}f
does not behave as a linear solution for large time, its spatial average does.

For this reason, the vector fields
\widehat{Z}_{\infty}
remain relevant in the nonlinear problem. However, the coordinate system
(t,y,v)=(t,x+t\widehat{v},v)
is not well adapted anymore in order to study the solutions for large times.

2.3.2 Improved estimates for the derivatives

It turns out that
J(\partial_{x^{\mu}}f_{1})
enjoys stronger decay properties than
J(f_{1})
. This is captured by the identities

\hskip-17.07164pt(t^{2}-r^{2})\partial_{t}=tS-x^{i}\Omega_{0i},\qquad\qquad(t^{2}-r^{2})\partial_{x^{k}}=t\Omega_{0k}-x^{k}S-x^{i}\Omega_{ki},\quad 1\leq k\leq 3.

(31)

As in the previous subsection, we first derive properties holding for a general distribution function
f
. By performing an induction, based on the relations (
31
) as well as integration by parts in
v
, one can derive the next result.

Lemma 2.18
.

Let
f:[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\to\mathbb{R}
be a sufficiently regular function. For any muti-index
\kappa
,

\forall\,(t,x)\in[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3},\qquad\langle t-|x|\rangle^{|\kappa|}\bigg|\partial_{t,x}^{\kappa}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}v\bigg|\lesssim\sup_{|\beta|\leq|\kappa|}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\big|\widehat{Z}^{\beta}f\big|(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}v.

Then, by applying Lemma
2.9
, one can in fact strengthen this last estimate near the light cone.

Corollary 2.19
.

For any muti-index
\kappa
,

\forall\,(t,x)\in[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3},\qquad\langle t+|x|\rangle^{|\kappa|}\bigg|\partial_{t,x}^{\kappa}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}v\bigg|\lesssim\sup_{|\beta|\leq|\kappa|}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\langle x-t\widehat{v}\rangle^{|\kappa|}\,\langle v\rangle^{2|\kappa|}\big|\widehat{Z}^{\beta}f\big|(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}v.

These two results will be sufficient for the purpose of this paper. However, for completeness and in order to ensure that the source term of the commuted asymptotic Maxwell equations, introduced below in Section
5
, have the correct form, we identify now the leading order term in the asymptotic expansion of
J(\partial_{x^{\mu}}f_{1})
. Note that one cannot directly obtain it through Proposition
2.13
since the spatial average of
\partial_{x^{\mu}}f_{1}
vanishes. It turns out that it is easier to derive first the asymptotic behavior of the following derivatives of
f_{1}
,

Y_{\mu\nu}:=\widehat{v}_{\mu}\partial_{x^{\nu}}-\widehat{v}_{\nu}\partial_{x^{\mu}},\qquad 0\leq\mu,\,\nu\leq 3.

The average in
v
of
Y_{\mu\nu}f
is the source term of the wave equation satisfied by the cartesian component
F_{\mu\nu}
of the electromagnetic field if
(f,F)
is a solution to the Vlasov-Maxwell system. These derivatives enjoy a kind of
null condition
and behave better than
\partial_{x^{\mu}}f_{1}
. The large time behavior of these latter quantities will then be obtained by exploiting the relations

\partial_{t}=|v^{0}|^{2}\big(\mathbf{T}_{0}+\widehat{v}^{i}Y_{0i}\big),\qquad\quad\qquad\partial_{x^{k}}=|v^{0}|^{2}\big(-Y_{0k}-\widehat{v}^{k}\mathbf{T}_{0}+\widehat{v}^{i}Y_{ki}\big),\quad 1\leq k\leq 3.

(32)

Lemma 2.20
.

We have, for any
0\leq\mu<\nu\leq 3
and all
(t,x)\in[T+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
,

\bigg|(-1)^{\delta_{\mu}^{0}}\,t\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}Y_{\mu\nu}f(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}v-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\widehat{\Omega}_{\mu\nu}f(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}v\bigg|\lesssim\frac{1}{\langle t+|x|\rangle}\sup_{|\beta|\leq 1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\langle v\rangle^{2}\,\langle x-t\widehat{v}\rangle^{2}\,\big|\widehat{Z}^{\beta}f\big|(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}v.

Proof.

Note first the relations

\displaystyle-tY_{0k}

\displaystyle=t\left(\partial_{x^{k}}+\widehat{v}_{k}\partial_{t}\right)=\Omega_{0k}-(x^{k}-t\widehat{v}^{k})\partial_{t}=-v^{0}\partial_{v^{k}}+\widehat{\Omega}_{0k}-\widehat{v}^{k}-\partial_{t}\big[(x^{k}-t\widehat{v}^{k})\cdot\big],

\displaystyle tY_{ij}

\displaystyle=t\left(\widehat{v}_{i}\partial_{x^{j}}-\widehat{v}_{j}\partial_{x^{i}}\right)=-\big(v^{i}\partial_{v^{j}}-v^{j}\partial_{v^{i}}\big)+\widehat{\Omega}_{ij}-(x^{i}-t\widehat{v}^{i})\partial_{x^{j}}+(x^{j}-t\widehat{v}^{j})\partial_{x^{i}}.

Then, perform integration by parts in
v
and apply Corollary
2.19
to
(x^{\ell}-t\widehat{v}^{\ell})f
,
1\leq\ell\leq 3
. It remains to remark that, for all
\widehat{Z}\in\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{S}
, we have
|\widehat{Z}(x-t\widehat{v})|\lesssim\langle x-t\widehat{v}\rangle
.

We are finally able to describe the asymptotic behavior of the derivatives of the current density
J(f_{1})
. We focus on the time decay as Vlasov fields enjoy strong spatial decay in the exterior of the light cone
\{|x|\geq t\}
.

Corollary 2.21
.

Let
1\leq k\leq 3
and define the operators

D_{t}:h\mapsto-\widehat{\Omega}_{0i}^{\infty}\big(v^{i}v^{0}h\big)-h,\qquad\qquad D_{x^{k}}:h\mapsto\widehat{\Omega}_{0k}^{\infty}\big(|v^{0}|^{2}h\big)+\widehat{\Omega}_{ki}\big(v^{i}v^{0}h\big).

(33)

There holds, for all
t\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}t^{5}\bigg|J\big(\partial_{t}f_{1}\big)_{\nu}(t,x)-\frac{\mathds{1}_{|x|<t}}{t^{4}}\frac{x_{\nu}}{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{y}}\Big[|v^{0}|^{5}D_{t}f_{\infty}\Big]\bigg(y,\frac{\widecheck{\;x\;}}{t}\bigg)\mathrm{d}y+\delta_{\nu}^{0}\frac{\mathds{1}_{|x|<t}}{t^{4}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{y}}\Big[|v^{0}|^{5}f_{\infty}\Big]\bigg(y,\frac{\widecheck{\;x\;}}{t}\bigg)\mathrm{d}y\bigg|^{2}\!\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}t^{5}\bigg|J\big(\partial_{x^{k}}f_{1}\big)_{\nu}(t,x)-\frac{\mathds{1}_{|x|<t}}{t^{4}}\frac{x_{\nu}}{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{y}}\Big[|v^{0}|^{5}D_{x^{k}}f_{\infty}\Big]\bigg(y,\frac{\widecheck{\;x\;}}{t}\bigg)\mathrm{d}y-\delta_{\nu}^{k}\frac{\mathds{1}_{|x|<t}}{t^{4}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{y}}\Big[|v^{0}|^{5}f_{\infty}\Big]\bigg(y,\frac{\widecheck{\;x\;}}{t}\bigg)\mathrm{d}y\bigg|^{2}\!\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon.

Proof.

Fix
0\leq\nu\leq 3
and
1\leq k\leq 3
. Since
\partial_{t}=-\widehat{v}\cdot\nabla_{x}+\mathbf{T}_{0}
and
\mathbf{T}_{0}(\widehat{v}^{k}f_{1})=0
, it suffices to deal with the case of the spatial derivatives. Next, using the relation (
32
), we get

\widehat{v}_{\nu}\partial_{x^{k}}f_{1}=|v^{0}|^{2}\big[-Y_{0k}-\widehat{v}^{k}\mathbf{T}_{0}+\widehat{v}^{i}Y_{ki}\big](\widehat{v}_{\nu}f_{1})=-Y_{0k}\big(\widehat{v}_{\nu}|v^{0}|^{2}f_{1}\big)+Y_{ki}\big(\widehat{v}_{\nu}v^{i}v^{0}f_{1}\big).

Furthermore,

\widehat{\Omega}_{0k}\big(\widehat{v}_{\nu}|v^{0}|^{2}f_{1}\big)+\widehat{\Omega}_{ki}\big(\widehat{v}_{\nu}v^{i}v^{0}f_{1}\big)=\widehat{v}_{\nu}\widehat{\Omega}_{0k}\big(|v^{0}|^{2}f_{1}\big)+\widehat{v}_{\nu}\widehat{\Omega}_{ki}\big(v^{i}v^{0}f_{1}\big)+\delta_{\nu}^{k}(1-|\widehat{v}|^{2})|v^{0}|^{2}f_{1}

and
(1-|\widehat{v}|^{2})|v^{0}|^{2}=1
. Then, apply Lemma
2.20
to
f=\widehat{v}_{\nu}|v^{0}|^{2}f_{1}
as well as
f=\widehat{v}_{\nu}v^{i}v^{0}f_{1}
and then Proposition
2.13
to
\widehat{v}_{\nu}\widehat{\Omega}_{0k}\big(|v^{0}|^{2}f_{1}\big)
,
\widehat{v}_{\nu}\widehat{\Omega}_{ik}\big(v^{i}v^{0}f_{1}\big)
as well as
f_{1}
, which are all solutions to the linear Vlasov equation. It remains to use the conservation laws (
28
)–(
29
).

2.4 The Vlasov equation in variables adapted to modified scattering

Let
F
be a sufficiently regular electromagnetic field. One of the main step of the proof of Theorem
1.8
consists in proving that under suitable assumptions on
F
, in particular
t^{2}F(t,x+t\widehat{v})\sim\mathbb{F}[f_{\infty}](v)
, there exists a unique function
f
satisfying
\mathbf{T}_{F}(f)=0
and
f(t,x+t\widehat{v}+\mathscr{C}_{t,v},v)\to f_{\infty}(x,v)
as
t\to+\infty
. For this, it is convenient to work in a more appropriate coordinate system. We then introduce

\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}:(t,x,v)\mapsto x+t\widehat{v}+\mathscr{C}_{t,v},\qquad\qquad\mathscr{C}_{t,v}^{k}:=-\log(t)\frac{\delta_{k}^{j}-\widehat{v}_{k}\widehat{v}^{j}}{v^{0}}\widehat{v}^{\mu}\mathbb{F}_{\mu j}[f_{\infty}](v),

(34)

and we remark that
(t,x,v)\mapsto(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}(t,x,v),v)
is a diffeomorphism from
\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
to
\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
. To a distribution function
f:[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\to\mathbb{R}
, we associate
g:[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\to\mathbb{R}
defined as

\forall\,(t,z,v)\in[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v},\qquad g(t,z,v)=f(t,z+t\widehat{v}+\mathscr{C}_{t,v},v)=f\big(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}(t,z,v),v\big).

(35)

Then, the modified scattering statement for
f
is equivalent to
g(t,z,v)\to f_{\infty}(z,v)
. Furthermore, we have

\displaystyle\partial_{t}g(t,z,v)

\displaystyle=\left(\partial_{t}+\widehat{v}\cdot\nabla_{x}-\frac{\delta^{i}_{j}-\widehat{v}^{i}\widehat{v}_{j}}{tv^{0}}\widehat{v}^{\mu}{\mathbb{F}_{\mu}}^{j}[f_{\infty}](v)\partial_{x^{i}}\right)f\big(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}(t,z,v),v\big),

(36)

\displaystyle\partial_{z^{j}}g(t,z,v)

\displaystyle=\partial_{x^{j}}f\big(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}(t,z,v),v\big),

(37)

\displaystyle v^{0}\partial_{v^{j}}g(t,z,v)

\displaystyle=\left(v^{0}\partial_{v^{j}}+t(\delta_{j}^{i}-\widehat{v}^{j}\widehat{v}^{i})\partial_{x^{i}}+v^{0}\partial_{v^{j}}\mathscr{C}^{i}_{t,v}\partial_{x^{i}}\right)f\big(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}(t,z,v),v\big).

(38)

Consequently,
f(t,x,v)
is solution to the Vlasov equation
\mathbf{T}_{F}(f)=0
on
[T,+\infty[
if and only if
g
satisfies

\forall\,(t,z,v)\in[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v},\qquad\mathbf{T}_{F}^{\infty}(g)(t,z,v)=0,

where
\mathbf{T}_{F}^{\infty}
is the Vlasov operator
\mathbf{T}_{F}
in the coordinate system
(t,z,v)=(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}(t,x,v),v)
,

\mathbf{T}_{F}^{\infty}:=\partial_{t}-\frac{\delta_{j}^{i}-\widehat{v}_{j}\widehat{v}^{i}}{tv^{0}}\widehat{v}^{\mu}\Big(t^{2}{F_{\mu}}^{j}(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})-{\mathbb{F}_{\mu}}^{j}[f_{\infty}](v)\Big)\partial_{z^{i}}+\frac{\widehat{v}^{\mu}}{v^{0}}{F_{\mu}}^{j}(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})\left(v^{0}\partial_{v^{j}}-v^{0}\partial_{v^{j}}\mathscr{C}^{i}_{t,v}\partial_{z^{i}}\right).

(39)

We will then study the equation
\mathbf{T}_{F}^{\infty}(g)=0
with the asymptotic data
g(t,z,v)\to f_{\infty}(z,v)
. We will further control the derivatives
\widehat{Z}^{\beta}_{\infty}g
up to order
N
. Note that they provide as much informations as the standard derivatives, in the sense that

\sup_{|\beta|\leq p}\langle z\rangle^{\,p-\beta_{H}}\big|\widehat{Z}^{\beta}_{\infty}g\big|\lesssim\sup_{\kappa_{t}+|\kappa_{z}|+|\kappa_{t}|\leq p}\big(t+\langle z\rangle\big)^{\kappa_{t}}\langle z\rangle^{|\kappa_{z}|}\,\langle v\rangle^{|\kappa_{v}|}\big|\partial_{t}^{\kappa_{t}}\partial_{z}^{\kappa_{z}}\partial_{v}^{\kappa_{v}}g\big|\lesssim\sup_{|\beta|\leq p}\langle z\rangle^{\,p-\beta_{H}}\big|\widehat{Z}^{\beta}_{\infty}g\big|,

on
[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
and for any
p\leq N
. We chose to use them for the reasons presented in Section
2.3.1
and since it is much easier to relate these derivatives to
\widehat{Z}^{\beta}f
, which have to be controlled in order to estimate the source terms in the commuted Maxwell equations.

Lemma 2.22
.

For
g(t,z,v)=f(t,z+t\widehat{v}+\mathscr{C}_{t,v},v)
, we have for any
1\leq k\leq 3
and
1\leq i<j\leq 3
,

\displaystyle\big(\partial_{x^{k}}f\big)(t,z+t\widehat{v}+\mathscr{C}_{t,v},v)

\displaystyle=\partial_{z^{k}}g(t,z,v),

\displaystyle\big(\partial_{t}f\big)(t,z+t\widehat{v}+\mathscr{C}_{t,v},v)

\displaystyle=\partial_{t}^{\infty}g(t,z,v)-\partial_{t}\mathscr{C}_{t,v}^{\ell}\partial_{z^{\ell}}g(t,z,v),

\displaystyle\big(Sf\big)(t,z+t\widehat{v}+\mathscr{C}_{t,v},v)

\displaystyle=Sg(t,z,v)+\big(\mathscr{C}^{\ell}_{t,v}-t\partial_{t}\mathscr{C}_{t,v}^{\ell}\big)\partial_{z^{\ell}}g(t,z,v),

\displaystyle\big(\widehat{\Omega}_{ij}f\big)(t,z+t\widehat{v}+\mathscr{C}_{t,v},v)

\displaystyle=\widehat{\Omega}_{ij}g(t,z,v)+\big[\mathscr{C}^{i}_{t,v}\partial_{z^{j}}-\mathscr{C}^{j}_{t,v}\partial_{z^{i}}\big]g(t,z,v)-\big(v^{i}\partial_{v^{j}}-v^{j}\partial_{v^{i}}\big)\big(\mathscr{C}_{t,v}^{\ell}\big)\partial_{z^{\ell}}g(t,z,v),

\displaystyle\big(\widehat{\Omega}_{0k}f\big)(t,z+t\widehat{v}+\mathscr{C}_{t,v},v)

\displaystyle=\widehat{\Omega}_{0k}^{\infty}g(t,z,v)+\mathscr{C}^{k}_{t,v}\partial_{t}^{\infty}g(t,z,v)-v^{0}\partial_{v^{k}}\big(\mathscr{C}_{t,v}^{\ell}\big)\partial_{z^{\ell}}g(t,z,v)

\displaystyle\quad-\big(t\widehat{v}^{k}+z^{k}+\mathscr{C}^{k}_{t,v}\big)\partial_{t}\mathscr{C}_{t,v}^{\ell}\partial_{z^{\ell}}g(t,z,v).

Proof.

Recall from Lemma
2.14
that if
h(t,z,v):=g(t,z-\mathscr{C}_{t,v},v)
, we have
\big(\widehat{Z}f\big)(t,x+t\widehat{v},v)=\widehat{Z}_{\infty}h(t,x,v)
for any
\widehat{Z}\in\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{S}
. It then remains to relate the derivatives of
h
to the one of
g
.

2.5 Key elements of the proof

We will construct the solution
(f,F)
by a Picard iteration. For this, we have to study the Cauchy problem for both the Maxwell and the Vlasov equations with asymptotic data.

2.5.1 The Maxwell equations with scattering data

Given a sufficiently regular distribution function
f:[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}\to\mathbb{R}
satisfying modified scattering to
f_{\infty}
, we would like to prove a well-posedness result for the asymptotic Cauchy problem

\nabla^{\mu}G_{\mu\nu}=J(f)_{\nu},\qquad\nabla^{\mu}{}^{*}\!G_{\mu\nu}=0,\qquad\qquad\qquad\lim_{r\to+\infty}r\underline{\alpha}(G)(r+u,r\omega)=\underline{\alpha}^{\infty}(u,\omega).

(40)

The regularity of
J(f)
and the assumptions on
\underline{\alpha}^{\infty}
will allow us to prove that the asymptotic Cauchy problem (
40
) is well-posed in
L^{\infty}([T,+\infty[,H^{N}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))
. In view of the assumptions on
f
, our linear analysis suggests

1.

that
J(f)\sim t^{3}
, so that we should not expect
G
to decay faster than
t^{-2}
in, say, the region
t\geq 2r
. This is related with the long range effect of the electromagnetic field, preventing the distribution function to scatter linearly. The problem then is that a naive energy estimate will not provide us enough informations on
G
in order to close our Picard iteration.

2.

Since the modified scattering dynamics of
f
is captured by
f_{\infty}
, we expect
J(f)
to have the same leading order term than the current density
J(f_{1})
of the linear solution, which is given by Corollary
2.17
.

3.

The same should hold for
J(\widehat{Z}^{\beta}f)
and
J(\widehat{Z}^{\beta}f_{1})
, up to order
N-1
. However, since a loss of one derivative is required in the estimates of Corollary
2.17
, we will not be able to derive an asymptotic expansion for the top order derivatives
J(\widehat{Z}^{\kappa}f)
,
|\kappa|=N
.

Remark 2.23
.

Since
f
merely enjoys a modified scattering dynamics, the next terms in the asymptotic expansion of
J(f)
should be polyhomogeneous. In contrast, the expansion of
J(f_{1})
is polynomial.

Remark 2.24
.

For the forward problem, in order to prove global well-posedness for the small data solutions to the Vlasov-Maxwell system, one also has to deal with a difficulty specific to the top order derivatives. However, the loss of regularity was not a problem when we proved in
[
8
]
that asymptotic completeness hold.

Motivated by
1.
and
2.
, we consider the solution
F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]
to the asymptotic Maxwell equations,

\nabla^{\mu}F^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\mu\nu}[f_{\infty}]=J^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\nu}[f_{\infty}],\qquad\qquad\nabla^{\mu}{}^{*}\!F^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\mu\nu}[f_{\infty}]=0,

where
J^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\nu}[f_{\infty}]
is the leading order term of
J(f_{1})_{\nu}
, as given by Corollary
2.17
. In order to uniquely define it, we further assume that at time
t=1
, the divergence free parts of both the electric and magnetic field of
F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]
vanish. It implies that
F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}](1,\cdot)
is completely determined by

J^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{0}[f_{\infty}](1,\cdot)
through a Poisson equation. It turns out that
F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]
fully captures the asymptotic behavior of
G
.

•

Along the trajectories of free massive particles, we expect

|t^{2}G(t,x+t\widehat{v})-\mathbb{F}[f_{\infty}](v)|\lesssim\langle x\rangle^{\frac{3}{2}}\,\langle v\rangle^{3}t^{-1/2},

(41)

which is indeed a convergence estimate satisfied by
F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]
. In fact, by allowing the initial time, here set equal to
1
, to go to zero, we have in a certain sense
t^{2}F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}](t,t\widehat{v})=\mathbb{F}[f_{\infty}](v)
. We refer to Proposition
5.3
for more details.

•

The decay of
G
in the region where
|x|\geq 2t
, near spatial infinity, is governed by its pure charge part
\overline{F}
introduced in (
21
), which is the tail of its electric field. It turns out that the same property holds for
F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]
and its pure charge part is equal to the one of
G
.

•

The radiation field
\underline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]
of
F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]
strongly decays and verifies the constraint equations (
22
)–(
23
). The derivation of these two equations requires a thorough analysis of the null properties of
F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]
since certains quantities are at the threshold of integrability. It implies in particular that
\underline{\alpha}^{\infty}-\underline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]
is the radiation field of a strongly decaying solution to the vacuum Maxwell equations.

These results, proved in Section
5
, reduces the problem of improving the estimate for
G
to the analysis of

\nabla^{\mu}H_{\mu\nu}=J(f)_{\nu}-J^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\nu}[f_{\infty}],\quad\;\nabla^{\mu}{}^{*}\!H_{\mu\nu}=0,\qquad\quad\lim_{r\to+\infty}r\underline{\alpha}(H)(r+u,r\omega)=\underline{\alpha}^{\infty}(u,\omega)-\underline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}](u,\omega).

To deal with this new Cauchy problem with scattering data, we construct in Section
4
a wave operator for the Maxwell equations with a prescribed strongly decaying source terms, mapping strongly decaying radiation fields to strongly decaying solutions. The improvement of the estimates for
\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}G
, with
|\gamma|\leq N-1
, is similar and rely on the next property. The structure of the asymptotic Maxwell equations is preserved by commutation,

\nabla^{\mu}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}(F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}])_{\mu\nu}=J^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\nu}\big[\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}_{\infty}f_{\infty}\big],\qquad\qquad\nabla^{\mu}{}^{*}\!\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}(F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}])_{\mu\nu}=0,

for any
Z^{\gamma}
composed by homogeneous vector fields
\Omega_{0k}
,
\Omega_{ij}
and
S
. In particular, we have

t^{2}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}(F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}])(t,x+t\widehat{v})=\mathbb{F}\big[\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}_{\infty}f_{\infty}\big]+O\big(t^{-1}\big).

Remark 2.25
.

Although
\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]
and
F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}_{\infty}f_{\infty}]
share part of their asymptotic properties, they are not equal (see Proposition
10.1
). Let us also mention that when
Z^{\gamma}
contains a translation
\partial_{x^{\lambda}}
, a similar commutation formula holds but we will not require it since
J(\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}f)
already strongly decays.

Finally, we deal with
3.
and improve the estimate of the top order derivatives in Section
8.3
by exploiting the Glassey-Strauss decomposition of the derivatives of the field. It allows for a gain of regularity reminiscent of the elliptic regularity in the context of the Vlasov-Poisson system. More precisely, we will be able to derive the asymptotic behavior of
\nabla_{t,x}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}G
, where
|\gamma|=N-1
, by taking advantage of the strong decay properties of
J(\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}f)-J^{\mathrm{asymp}}[\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}_{\infty}f_{\infty}]
. Fix for instance
1\leq k\leq 3
and
0\leq\mu<\nu\leq 3
. The idea is to exploit that

\Box\,\partial_{x^{k}}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}(G)_{\mu\nu}=\partial_{x^{\nu}}\partial_{x^{k}}J(\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}f)_{\mu}-\partial_{x^{\mu}}\partial_{x^{k}}J(\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}f)_{\nu}

in order to use the representation formula for the wave equation. Then, Glassey-Strauss decomposed
\partial_{x^{\lambda}}
as a combination of derivatives tangential to backward light cones and the linear Vlasov operator
\mathbf{T}_{0}
, which is transverse to light cones. In such a way, one can perform integration by parts twice and express
\partial_{x^{k}}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}(G)_{\mu\nu}
as a functional of
J(\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}f)
instead of its derivatives. In order to exploit this decomposition, we would like
\nabla_{\partial_{x^{k}}}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]
to verify a similar property. For this, we identify a singular solution to the linear Vlasov equation giving rise to the current density
J^{\mathrm{asymp}}[\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}_{\infty}f_{\infty}]
. An approximation argument then provides us the corresponding decomposition.

2.5.2 The Vlasov equation with asymptotic data

We consider this time, for an electromagnetic field
F
behaving as the solution of (
40
), the Cauchy problem

\mathbf{T}_{F}^{\infty}(g)=0,\qquad\qquad\lim_{t\to+\infty}g(t,x,v)=f_{\infty}(x,v).

(42)

In order to prove well-posedness and to propagate regularity backward in time for
g
, we will be lead to commute the Vlasov equation. As it is suggested by Proposition
2.8
, the structure of the nonlinear terms are conserved by commutation with
\widehat{Z}_{\infty}\in\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{S}^{\infty}
. If
\widehat{Z}_{\infty}\neq\widehat{S}
, we will prove

\big|\mathbf{T}_{F}^{\infty}\big(\widehat{Z}_{\infty}g\big)\big|\lesssim\Big|\frac{\widehat{v}^{\mu}}{tv^{0}}\Big(t^{2}\mathcal{L}_{Z}(F)_{\mu j}(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})-\mathbb{F}_{\mu j}\big[\widehat{Z}_{\infty}f_{\infty}\big](v)\Big)\Big|\big|\nabla_{z}g\big|+\Big|\frac{\widehat{v}^{\mu}}{v^{0}}\mathcal{L}_{Z}(F)_{\mu j}(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})\Big|\sup_{\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\prime}}\big|\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\prime}g\big|+\text{better terms,}

where we recall
\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}=z+t\widehat{v}+\mathscr{C}_{t,v}
. Now, in view of decay estimates such as (
41
) satisfied by the electromagnetic field for the forward problem, we expect

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{\widehat{v}^{\mu}}{tv^{0}}\Big(t^{2}\mathcal{L}_{Z}(F)_{\mu j}(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})-\mathbb{F}_{\mu j}\big[\widehat{Z}_{\infty}f_{\infty}\big](v)\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda\,\langle z\rangle^{\frac{3}{2}}\,\langle v\rangle^{2}t^{-\frac{3}{2}}\log^{\frac{3}{2}}(t),

(43)

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{\widehat{v}^{\mu}}{v^{0}}\mathcal{L}_{Z}(F)_{\mu j}(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda\,\langle t+|z|\rangle^{-1}\,\langle t-|\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}|\rangle^{-1}.

(44)

We then identify two problems, which are in fact related.

1.

Although
z
and
v
are almost conserved by the flow of
\mathbf{T}_{F}^{\infty}
, allowing for the propagation of moments for the solution and its derivatives, the polynomial loss
\langle z\rangle^{\frac{3}{2}}\,\langle v\rangle^{2}
in the right hand side of (
43
) seems too strong in order to close the estimates through a Grönwall type inequality.

2.

The decay rate of the electromagnetic field degenerates near the light cone, that is for
t\sim|\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}|
in (
44
).

In order to deal with
2.
, one could expect to exploit the strong decay properties of massive fields near and in the exterior of the light cone, the regions where
t\sim r
and
r\geq t
. In the most favourable case, where
f_{\infty}
and then
g
are compactly supported, we have
\langle t-|\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}|\rangle\geq\delta t
on the support of
g
, for a certain
0<\delta<1
depending on the support of
f_{\infty}
and
\Lambda
. Equivalently,
f(t,x,v)=0
for, say,
|v|\geq R
or
|x|\geq R+tR/\langle R\rangle
and
\langle t-r\rangle\gtrsim t
on the support of
f(t,\cdot,\cdot)
. Otherwise, these strong decay properties are captured by Lemma
2.9
, at the cost of moments in
z
and
v
. Then, as for the problem
1.
, we would not be able to close the energy estimates in such a way. Indeed, bounding a given norm of the solution would require to control a stronger one, carrying more powers of
|z|
and
|v|
. The idea then consists in exploiting the
null structure
of the Lorentz force. Expanding
\widehat{v}^{\mu}{\mathcal{L}_{Z}F_{\mu}}^{j}
according to the null frame
(\underline{L},L,e_{\theta},e_{\varphi})
, we obtain terms having as a factor

•

either a good component
\alpha
,
\rho
or
\sigma
of
\mathcal{L}_{Z}F
, which turn out to decay as
\langle t+r\rangle^{-2}
,

•

or a good null component of the four-momentum vector,
\widehat{v}^{\underline{L}}
,
\widehat{v}^{e_{\theta}}
or
\widehat{v}^{e_{\varphi}}
. They will allow us to take advantage of the
t-r
decay in the energy estimates.

These observations yield an improvement of (
44
) where the
t-r
decay has been either transformed into
t+r
decay or made usable in order to prove boundedness for the solutions. Similar considerations allow to obtain an alternative version of (
41
),

\hskip-14.22636pt\Big|\frac{\widehat{v}^{\mu}}{tv^{0}}\Big(t^{2}\mathcal{L}_{Z}(F)_{\mu j}(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})-\mathbb{F}_{\mu j}\big[\widehat{Z}_{\infty}f_{\infty}\big](v)\Big)\Big|\big|\nabla_{z}g\big|\lesssim\Lambda\bigg(\frac{|\widehat{v}^{\underline{L}}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\log(t)}{t\,\langle t-|\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}|\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}}+\frac{\log(t)}{t^{\frac{3}{2}}}\bigg)\langle z\rangle\,\big|\nabla_{z}g\big|.

(45)

Note first that the right hand side does not carry any
v
weight anymore. Although the time decay is weaker than for (
41
), exploiting the
t-|\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}|
decay through the factor
|\widehat{v}^{\underline{L}}|^{1/2}
will allow us to compensate for this. However, the weight
\langle z\rangle
, even though it is crucially weaker than in (
41
), still has to be handled. For this, we take advantage of hierarchies in the commuted equations.

•

If
\widehat{Z}_{\infty}
is a translation
\partial_{t}^{\infty}
or
\partial_{z^{k}}
, then
\mathbb{F}[\widehat{Z}_{\infty}f_{\infty}]=0
and
Z=\partial_{t}
or
Z=\partial_{x^{k}}
. Consequently, the derivative of the electromagnetic field has a better behavior,
|\mathcal{L}_{Z}F|\lesssim\Lambda\,\langle t+r\rangle^{-1}\,\langle t-r\rangle^{-2}
, implying that
|\mathbf{T}_{F}^{\infty}(\widehat{Z}_{\infty}g)|
strongly decays.

•

If
\widehat{Z}_{\infty}
is an homogeneous vector field, we remark that the worst error term (
45
) carries the factor
\langle z\rangle\,\big|\nabla_{z}g\big|
. Since the derivatives
\partial_{z^{k}}g
behaves better, it makes the system of the commuted equations
triangular
. More concretely, we should be able to control the quantities
\langle z\rangle\,\big|\nabla_{z}g\big|
and
|\widehat{Z}_{\infty}g|
.

More generally, we will prove boundedness for the
L^{2}
norms of quantities of the form
\langle z\rangle^{N_{z}-\beta_{H}}\langle v\rangle^{N_{v}}\,\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\beta}g
, where we recall that
\beta_{H}
is the number of homogeneous vector fields composing
\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\beta}
.

Remark 2.26
.

Considering hierarchies of the form
\langle z\rangle^{N_{z}-a\beta_{H}}\langle v\rangle^{N_{v}}\,\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\beta}g
, with
a>1
, would make good error terms problematic. This explains why we could not lose more powers of
\langle z\rangle
in (
45
).

Finally, let us mention that working with large electromagnetic fields forces us to close the energy estimates through Grönwall type inequalities. For this reason, we carefully chose the foliation used to define the energy norms of Vlasov field in (
46
) below. In particular, it allows for an application of a two variables Gronwall inequality, in
(t,u)
, where
u=t-r
.

3
Energy and decay estimates

We fix, for all this section,
T\geq 0
as well as
h:[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\to\mathbb{R}
and a
2
-form
F
, defined on
[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
, both sufficiently regular. We will have to control the flux of the solutions through two different families of hypersurfaces, in addition to the
\{t=t_{\mathrm{cst}}\}
ones, that we start by introducing.

3.1 Ingoing and outgoing light cones

The future scattering state of a smooth electromagnetic field is a tensor field depending on the variables
(u,\omega)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}
, given by the limits of
rE(u,\underline{u},\omega)
and
rB(u,\underline{u},\omega)
as
\underline{u}\to+\infty
. This motivates the introduction, for any
\underline{u}\geq 0
, of the ingoing light cones

\underline{C}_{\underline{u}}:=\big\{(t,x)\in[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\,\big|\;t+|x|=\underline{u}\big\},\qquad\qquad\mathrm{d}\mu_{\underline{C}_{\underline{u}}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}r^{2}\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\mathrm{d}u,

where
\mathrm{d}\mu_{\underline{C}_{\underline{u}}}
is the induced volume form of
\underline{C}_{\underline{u}}
, in accordance with the choice of the null vector field
\sqrt{2}^{\,-1}\underline{L}
as its Lorentzian normal vector.

r=0
t=T
\underline{u}
\underline{u}
t
r
\underline{C}_{\underline{u}}
\{t+|x|\geq\underline{u},\;t\geq T\}
The sets
\underline{C}_{\underline{u}}
.

Controlling the flux of the solutions through truncated outgoing light cones will allow us to take advantage of the null structure of the system. Let, for all
T\leq t_{0}\leq s\leq+\infty
and
u\in\mathbb{R}
,

C_{u}^{t_{0},s}:=\big\{(t,x)\in[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\,\big|\;t-|x|=u,\;t_{0}\leq t\leq s\big\},\qquad C_{u}^{t_{0}}:=C_{u}^{t_{0},+\infty},\qquad\qquad\mathrm{d}\mu_{C_{u}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}r^{2}\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\mathrm{d}\underline{u},

with
\mathrm{d}\mu_{C_{u}}
the induced volume form of
C_{u}:=C^{T}_{u}
, chosen in accordance with the choice of the null vector field
\sqrt{2}^{\,-1}L
as its normal vector. For a sufficiently regular function
\psi:[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\to\mathbb{R}
,

\int_{\underline{C}_{\underline{u}}}\psi\,\mathrm{d}\mu_{\underline{C}_{\underline{u}}}\!=\int_{u=2T-\underline{u}}^{\underline{u}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\psi(u,\underline{u},\omega)\frac{r^{2}}{\sqrt{2}}\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\mathrm{d}\underline{u},\quad\;\;\int_{C_{u}^{t_{0},s}}\psi\,\mathrm{d}\mu_{C_{u}}\!=\int_{\underline{u}=\max(2t_{0}-u,u)}^{2s-u}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\psi(u,\underline{u},\omega)\frac{r^{2}}{\sqrt{2}}\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\mathrm{d}\underline{u}.

Remark 3.1
.

The future scattering states are in fact defined on a part of the conformal boundary of the Minkowski space, future null infinity
\mathcal{I}^{+}
, which corresponds to the future end points of the null rays
t-|x|=u
. It can be viewed as
\underline{C}_{+\infty}
. More precisely,

(t,r,\omega)\mapsto\big(T(t,r)=\tan^{-1}(t+r)+\tan^{-1}(t-r),R(t,r)=\tan^{-1}(t+r)-\tan^{-1}(t-r),\omega\big)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{S}^{3}

is a conformal diffeomorphism between Minkowski spacetime and the interior of the triangle
0\leq R\leq\pi
,
|T|=\pi-R
of the space
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{S}^{3}
, equipped with the metric
-\mathrm{d}T^{2}+\mathrm{d}R^{2}+\sin^{2}(R)\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}
. Then

\mathcal{I}^{+}:=\{(T,R,\omega)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{S}^{3}\;/\;0<R<\pi,\quad T=\pi-R\}.

The past scattering states, obtained as the limit of
rE(u,\underline{u},\omega)
and
rB(u,\underline{u},\omega)
as
u\to-\infty
, are defined on past null infinity
\mathcal{I}^{-}=\{0<R<\pi,\;T=R-\pi\}
, which can be viewed as
t-|x|=-\infty
.

All the timelike geodesics, that is the lines
t\mapsto(t,x+\widehat{v}t)
with
v\in\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
, terminate at future timelike infinity
i^{+}=(0,\pi)
as
t\to+\infty
and past timelike infinity
i^{-}=(0,-\pi)
as
t\to-\infty
.

r=0
t=T
t=t_{0}
t_{0}-u
t
t=u^{\prime}
r
C_{u^{\prime}}^{T}
C_{u}^{t_{0}}
r=0
\mathcal{I}^{+}
,

\underline{u}=+\infty
\mathcal{I}^{-}
,
u=-\infty
i^{-}
i^{0}
i^{+}
\underline{u}=\mathrm{cst}
u=\mathrm{cst}
The sets
C_{u}^{t_{0}}
and the Penrose diagram of the Minkowski space.

3.2 Energy inequality for Vlasov fields

In order to ultimately prove boundedness in
L^{2}_{z,v}
for quantities such as
\langle z\rangle^{p}\,\langle v\rangle^{q}\,\widehat{Z}^{\beta}_{\infty}g
, we will first estimate a refined norm of the solutions. In view of the hypersurfaces on which we will control them and even if we will estimate the error terms in the coordinate system
(t,z,v)
, it is more sensible to perform the energy inequalities using the variables
(t,x,v)
. Let, for
t_{0}\geq T
and
u\in\mathbb{R}
,

\overline{\mathbb{E}}\big[h\big](t_{0},u):=\int_{|x|\leq t_{0}-u}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\big|h\big|^{2}(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x+\sqrt{2}\int_{C_{u}^{t_{0}}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\widehat{v}^{\underline{L}}\big|h\big|^{2}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}\mu_{C_{u}},

(46)

where, more explicitly,

\sqrt{2}\int_{C_{u}^{t_{0}}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}v^{\underline{L}}\big|h\big|^{2}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}\mu_{C_{u}}=\int_{\underline{u}=\max(u,2t_{0}-u)}^{+\infty}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}v^{\underline{L}}(\omega)\big|h\big|^{2}\Big(\frac{\underline{u}+u}{2},\frac{\underline{u}-u}{2}\omega\Big)\mathrm{d}vr^{2}\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\mathrm{d}\underline{u},\qquad r=\frac{\underline{u}-u}{2}.

We now make a few remarks concerning the choice of the foliation of
[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
.

•

The domain of the first integral is the part of a constant
t
hypersurface located in
\{t-|x|\geq u\}
. The one of the second integral is the truncated outgoing null cone
C_{u}^{t_{0}}
, the part of
\{t-|x|=u\}
located in
\{t\geq t_{0}\}
.

•

Controlling the
L^{2}
norm of the distribution function on null cones
C_{u}^{t_{0}}
will allow us to exploit the decay in
t-r
in the energy estimates.

•

We consider such truncations of the constant
t
hypersurfaces in order to be able to apply Gronwall’s inequality when we will perform energy estimates (see Lemma
7.16
). The error terms will decay either as
t^{-\frac{4}{3}}
or as
\langle u\rangle^{-\frac{4}{3}}
.

The next energy inequality is adapted to the study of solutions to the Vlasov equation with asymptotic data.

Proposition 3.2
.

We have, for all
t_{0}\geq T
and
u\in\mathbb{R}
,

\overline{\mathbb{E}}\big[h\big](t_{0},u)\leq 2\int_{t=t_{0}}^{+\infty}\int_{|x|\leq t-u}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\big|\mathbf{T}_{F}\big(h\big)h\big|(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t+\lim_{t\to+\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\big|h\big|^{2}(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x.

Proof.

The proof is based on the divergence theorem, applied to the current density of
h^{2}
, which verifies

\nabla_{\mu}J\big(h^{2}\big)^{\mu}=\partial_{x^{\mu}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\widehat{v}^{\mu}h^{2}\mathrm{d}v=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\mathbf{T}_{F}\big(h^{2}\big)\mathrm{d}v-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\partial_{v^{j}}\big(\,\widehat{v}^{\mu}{F_{\mu}}^{j}h^{2}\,\big)\mathrm{d}v=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\mathbf{T}_{F}\big(h^{2}\big)\mathrm{d}v.

For any
T\leq t_{0}\leq s
and
u\in\mathbb{R}
, we introduce the domain

D^{t_{0},s}_{u}:=\big\{(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}^{1+3}\,\big|\;t_{0}\leq t\leq s,\;t-|x|\geq u\big\}.

Its boundary is composed by

•

\{(t_{0},x)\,|\,x\in\mathbb{R}^{3},\;|x|\leq t_{0}-u\}
and
\{(s,x)\,|\,x\in\mathbb{R}^{3},\;|x|\leq s-u\}
. Their unit outward normal vector, for the euclidian metric, are respectively
-\partial_{t}
and
\partial_{t}
. Note that they can be empty if
u
is large.

•

The truncated null cone
C_{u}^{t_{0},s}
. Its euclidian unit outward normal vector is
-\sqrt{2}^{\,-1}(\partial_{t}-\partial_{r})=-\sqrt{2}^{\,-1}\underline{L}
.

The euclidian divergence theorem, applied to
J(h^{2})
, in
D_{u}^{t_{0},s}
, yields

\int_{|x|\leq u-t_{0}}J\big(h^{2}\big)^{0}(\tau,x)\mathrm{d}x+\int_{C_{u}^{t_{0},s}}\sqrt{2}J\big(h^{2}\big)^{\underline{L}}\mathrm{d}\mu_{C_{u}}=\int_{|x|\leq u-s}J\big(h^{2}\big)^{0}(s,x)\mathrm{d}x+2\int_{D_{u}^{t_{0},s}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\mathbf{T}_{F}(h)h\,\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t.

Since
J(h^{2})^{0}=\int_{v}h^{2}\mathrm{d}v
and
J(h^{2})^{\underline{L}}=\int_{v}\widehat{v}^{\underline{L}}\,h^{2}\mathrm{d}v
, we get the result by letting
s\to+\infty
.

3.3 Energy inequalities for electromagnetic fields

The results of this section will be proved by applying the divergence theorem to the energy-momentum tensor of
F
, contracted with well-chosen vector fields (multipliers).

Definition 3.3
.

The energy-momentum tensor
\mathbb{T}[F]_{\mu\nu}
of the
2
-form
F
is defined as

\mathbb{T}[F]_{\mu\nu}:=F_{\mu\beta}{F_{\nu}}^{\beta}-\frac{1}{4}\eta_{\mu\nu}F_{\xi\lambda}F^{\xi\lambda}.

The null components of
\mathbb{T}[F]
are given by

\mathbb{T}[F]_{LL}=|\alpha(F)|^{2},\qquad\mathbb{T}[F]_{\underline{L}\underline{L}}=|\underline{\alpha}(F)|^{2},\qquad\mathbb{T}[F]_{L\underline{L}}=|\rho(F)|^{2}+|\sigma(F)|^{2}.

Moreover, if F a is solution to the Maxwell equations with a magnetic current,

\nabla^{\mu}F_{\mu\nu}=J_{\nu},\qquad\nabla^{\mu}{}^{*}\!F_{\mu\nu}=\widetilde{J}_{\nu},

(Mmc)

then

\nabla^{\mu}T[F]_{\mu\nu}=F_{\nu\lambda}J^{\lambda}+{}^{*}\!F_{\nu\lambda}\widetilde{J}^{\lambda}.

Remark 3.4
.

If
F
is a solution to the Maxwell equations, the magnetic current source term
\widetilde{J}
vanishes in (
Mmc
). However, in order to solve the vacuum Maxwell equations with data prescribed at future null infinity
\mathcal{I}^{+}
, we will consider a sequence of approximate solutions
(F^{n})_{n\geq 1}
such that
\nabla^{\mu}{}^{*}\!F^{n}_{\mu\nu}\neq 0
.

3.3.1 Estimates for the forward problem

We assume, for the next two Propositions, that
F
is a solution to the Maxwell equations with sufficiently regular source terms
J
,

\nabla^{\mu}F_{\mu\nu}=J_{\nu},\qquad\qquad\nabla^{\mu}{}^{*}\!F_{\mu\nu}=0.

(47)

We start by proving energy estimates by using
\partial_{t}
as a multiplier.

Proposition 3.5
.

If
F
verifies (
47
) on
[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
, then

\sup_{t\geq T}\|F(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}+\sup_{\underline{u}\geq T}\int_{\underline{C}_{\underline{u}}}|\underline{\alpha}(F)|^{2}\mathrm{d}\mu_{\underline{C}_{\underline{u}}}+\sup_{u\in\mathbb{R}}\int_{C_{u}^{T}}|\rho(F)|^{2}+|\sigma(F)|^{2}\mathrm{d}\mu_{C_{u}}\lesssim\|F(T,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}+\bigg|\int_{\tau=T}^{+\infty}\|J(\tau,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\mathrm{d}s\bigg|^{2}\!.

Proof.

Apply first the divergence theorem to
\mathbb{T}[F]_{\mu 0}
in the region
\{(\tau,x)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\,|\,T\leq\tau\leq t\}
. As
2\mathbb{T}[F]_{00}=|F|^{2}
and
\nabla^{\mu}\mathbb{T}[F]_{\mu 0}=F_{0\nu}J^{\nu}
, we get, for all
t\geq T
,

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}|F|^{2}(t,x)\mathrm{d}x-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}|F|^{2}(T,x)\mathrm{d}x=-2\int_{\tau=0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}F_{0\nu}J^{\nu}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}\tau\leq 2\sup_{T\leq\tau\leq t}\|F(\tau,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\int_{\tau=T}^{t}\|J(\tau,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\mathrm{d}\tau.

Fix now
u\in\mathbb{R}
and apply the divergence theorem in the region
\{(\tau,x)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\,|\,\tau-|x|\leq u,\;\tau\geq T\}
. As its boundary is
\{(T,x)\,|\,|x|\geq T-u\}\cup C_{u}^{T}
, we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{2}\int_{C_{u}^{T}}\mathbb{T}[F]_{L0}\mathrm{d}\mu_{C_{u}}-\int_{|x|\geq T-u}|F|^{2}(T,x)\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=-2\int_{\tau=T}^{\underline{u}}\int_{|x|\geq\tau-u}F_{0\nu}J^{\nu}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\leq 2\sup_{\tau\geq T}\|F(\tau,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\int_{\tau=T}^{+\infty}\|J(\tau,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\mathrm{d}\tau.

Then we use that
\mathbb{T}[F]_{\underline{L}L}=|\rho(F)|^{2}+|\sigma(F)|^{2}
and
\mathbb{T}[F]_{LL}=|\alpha(F)|^{2}\geq 0
. Finally, consider
\underline{u}\geq 0
and apply the divergence theorem in the region
\{(\tau,x)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\,|\,\tau+|x|\leq\underline{u},\;\tau\geq T\}
. As its boundary is composed by
\{(T,x)\,|\,|x|\leq\underline{u}-T\}
and
\underline{C}_{\underline{u}}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\sqrt{2}\int_{\underline{C}_{\underline{u}}}\mathbb{T}[F]_{\underline{L}0}\mathrm{d}\mu_{\underline{C}_{\underline{u}}}-\int_{|x|\leq\underline{u}-T}|F|^{2}(T,x)\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=-2\int_{\tau=T}^{\underline{u}}\int_{|x|\leq\underline{u}-\tau}F_{0\nu}J^{\nu}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\leq 2\sup_{T\leq\tau\leq\underline{u}}\|F(\tau,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\int_{\tau=T}^{t}\|J(\tau,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\mathrm{d}\tau.

It remains to use
\mathbb{T}[F]_{\underline{L}\underline{L}}=|\underline{\alpha}(F)|^{2}
and
\mathbb{T}[F]_{\underline{L}L}=|\rho(F)|^{2}+|\sigma(F)|^{2}\geq 0
.

Let
K
be the sum of
\partial_{t}
and the Morawetz vector field,

K:=\frac{1}{2}\langle t+r\rangle^{2}L+\frac{1}{2}\langle t-r\rangle^{2}\underline{L},

(48)

which is a conformal Killing vector field of Minkowski spacetime with conformal factor
4t
,

\nabla_{\mu}K_{\nu}+\nabla_{\nu}K_{\mu}=4t\eta_{\mu\nu}.

As
\mathbb{T}[F]
is symmetric and trace-free, that is
{\mathbb{T}[F]_{\mu}}^{\mu}=0
, we have

2\mathbb{T}[F]_{\mu\nu}\nabla^{\mu}K^{\nu}=\mathbb{T}[F]_{\mu\nu}\left(\nabla^{\mu}K^{\nu}+\nabla^{\nu}K^{\mu}\right)=4t\mathbb{T}[F]_{\mu\nu}\eta^{\mu\nu}=4t{\mathbb{T}[F]_{\mu}}^{\mu}=0,

so that

\nabla^{\mu}\left(\mathbb{T}[F]_{\mu\nu}K^{\nu}\right)=\nabla^{\mu}\left(\mathbb{T}[F]_{\mu\nu}\right)K^{\nu}.

(49)

The vector field
K
, used a multiplier, then provides a conservation law for solutions to the vacuum Maxwell equations, that is (
47
) for
J=0
. More generally,
K
can be used in order to propagate the weighted norms

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{K}[F](t)

\displaystyle:=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\!\left[\langle t+r\rangle^{2}|\alpha(F)|^{2}\!+\langle t-r\rangle^{2}|\underline{\alpha}(F)|^{2}\!+\big(\langle t+r\rangle^{2}+\langle t-r\rangle^{2}\big)(|\rho(F)|^{2}+|\sigma(F)|^{2})\right](t,x)\mathrm{d}x,

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{K}[F]_{t}^{s}(u)

\displaystyle:=\sqrt{2}\int_{C_{u}^{t,s}}\langle\underline{u}\rangle^{2}|\alpha(F)|^{2}+\langle u\rangle^{2}\big(|\rho(F)|^{2}+|\sigma(F)|^{2}\big)\mathrm{d}\mu_{C_{u}}.

(50)

In this paper, the forward problems will not require to control the fluxes through the outgoing null cones.

Proposition 3.6
.

Assume that
F
is solution to (
47
) on
[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
. Then,

\sup_{t\geq T}\mathcal{E}^{K}[F](t)\leq 2\mathcal{E}^{K}[F](T)+8\bigg|\int_{\tau=T}^{+\infty}\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\langle\tau+|x|\rangle^{2}\big|J(\tau,x)\big|^{2}\mathrm{d}x\bigg|^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathrm{d}\tau\bigg|^{2}.

Proof.

Observe that, in view of the null components of
K
and
\mathbb{T}[F]
, we have with
u=t-r
and
\underline{u}=t+r
,

\displaystyle 2\mathbb{T}[F]_{\underline{L}\nu}K^{\nu}

\displaystyle=\langle u\rangle^{2}|\underline{\alpha}(F)|^{2}+\langle\underline{u}\rangle^{2}\big(|\rho(F)|^{2}+|\sigma(F)|^{2}\big),

(51)

\displaystyle 2\mathbb{T}[F]_{L\nu}K^{\nu}

\displaystyle=\langle\underline{u}\rangle^{2}|\alpha(F)|^{2}+\langle u\rangle^{2}\big(|\rho(F)|^{2}+|\sigma(F)|^{2}\big).

(52)

Note further that
4\mathbb{T}[F]_{0\nu}K^{\nu}=2\mathbb{T}[F]_{L\nu}K^{\nu}+2\mathbb{T}[F]_{L\nu}K^{\nu}
and fix
t\geq T
. By applying the divergence theorem to
4\mathbb{T}[F]_{\mu\nu}K^{\nu}
, in the time slab
T\leq\tau\leq t
, we get

\mathcal{E}^{K}[F](t)=\mathcal{E}^{K}[F](T)-4\int_{\tau=T}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\big[K^{\mu}F_{\mu\nu}J^{\nu}\big](\tau,x)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}\tau.

Expanding
K^{\mu}F_{\mu\nu}
according to the null frame
(\underline{L},L,e_{\theta},e_{\varphi})
, we obtain, as
2K=\langle t+r\rangle^{2}L+\langle t-r\rangle^{2}\underline{L}
,

\displaystyle\big|K^{\mu}F_{\mu\nu}\big|\leq\,\langle t+r\rangle^{2}\big|F_{L\nu}\big|+\langle t-r\rangle^{2}\big|F_{\underline{L}\nu}\big|\leq\langle t+r\rangle^{2}\big(\big|\alpha(F)\big|+\big|\rho(F)\big|\big)+\langle t-r\rangle^{2}\big(\big|\underline{\alpha}(F)\big|+\big|\rho(F)\big|\big).

Consequently, the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality in
x
yields

\mathcal{E}^{K}[F](t)\leq\mathcal{E}^{K}[F](T)+4\int_{\tau=T}^{t}\big|\mathcal{E}^{K}[F](\tau)\big|^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\langle\tau+|x|\rangle^{2}\big|J(\tau,x)\big|^{2}\mathrm{d}x\bigg|^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathrm{d}\tau

and it remains to use a Grönwall type inequality.

3.3.2 Estimates for the backward problem

For forward problems, the norm
\mathcal{E}^{K}[\cdot]
can be strengthened in the exterior of the light cone
\{|x|\geq t\}
by a factor
\langle t-r\rangle^{a}
,
a\geq 0
. For the backward problem, as pointed out by
[
43
]
, it is in the interior region
\{|x|\leq t\}
that one can use these weights in order to derive improved estimates. It is explained by

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\nabla^{\mu}\big(\mathbb{T}[F]_{\mu\nu}K^{\nu}|\max(t-r,1)|^{a}\big)

\displaystyle=\nabla^{\mu}\big(\mathbb{T}[F]_{\mu\nu}K^{\nu}\big)|\max(t-r,1)|^{a}+\mathbb{T}[G]_{L\nu}K^{\nu}\,\nabla^{L}\big(|\max(t-r,1)|^{a}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\nabla^{\mu}\big(\mathbb{T}[F]_{\mu\nu}K^{\nu}\big)|\max(t-r,1)|^{a}=\nabla^{\mu}\big(\mathbb{T}[F]_{\mu\nu}\big)K^{\nu}|\max(t-r,1)|^{a},

(53)

which ensues from

L(t-r)=e_{\theta}(t-r)=e_{\varphi}(t-r)=0,\qquad\qquad 2\nabla^{L}=-\nabla_{\underline{L}}=-2\nabla_{\partial_{u}},\qquad\qquad\mathbb{T}[G]_{L\nu}K^{\nu}\geq 0.

Hence, when will apply the divergence theorem, the error term arising from the factor
|\max(t-r,1)|^{a}
will have a good sign. For the construction of the modified wave operator, we will crucially use this property in order to deal with the top order derivatives. Consider then, for all
T\leq t\leq s
,
u\in\mathbb{R}
and any
a\geq 0
, the energy norms and energy fluxes

\mathcal{E}^{K,a}[F](t):=\mathcal{E}^{K}\big[|\max(t-r,1)|^{a}\,F\big](t),\qquad\qquad\mathcal{F}^{K,a}[F]_{t}^{s}(u):=\big|\max(u,1)\big|^{a}\,\mathcal{F}^{K}[F]_{t}^{s}(u).

Let further the higher order energy norms, for
N\in\mathbb{N}
,

\mathcal{E}^{K,a}_{N}[F](t):=\sup_{|\gamma|\leq N}\mathcal{E}^{K,a}[\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F](t),\qquad\qquad\mathcal{F}^{K,a}_{N}[F]_{t}^{s}(u):=\sup_{|\gamma|\leq N}\mathcal{F}^{K,a}[\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F]_{t}^{s}(u).

(54)

Proposition 3.7
.

Let
F
be a solution to the Maxwell equations with a magnetic current (
Mmc
). Then, for all
T\leq t\leq s
and any
a\geq 0
,

\mathcal{E}^{K,a}[F](t)+\sup_{u\in\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{F}^{K,a}[F]_{t}^{s}(u)\leq 2\mathcal{E}^{K,a}[F](s)+8\int_{\tau=t}^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\big|\max(\tau-|x|,1)\big|^{2a}\Big|F_{\lambda\nu}J^{\nu}K^{\lambda}+{}^{*}\!F_{\lambda\nu}\widetilde{J}^{\nu}K^{\lambda}\Big|(\tau,x)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}\tau.

Proof.

Let
X
be the multiplier
|\max(t-|x|,1)|^{2a}K
. Fix further
T\leq t\leq s
. By applying the Lorentzian divergence theorem to
\mathbb{T}[F]_{\mu\nu}X^{\nu}
in the domain
\{(\tau,x)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\,|\,t\leq\tau\leq s\}
, we get,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\Big[\mathbb{T}[F]_{0\nu}X^{\nu}\Big](t,x)\mathrm{d}x-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\Big[\mathbb{T}[F]_{0\nu}X^{\nu}\Big](s,x)\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\int_{\tau=t}^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\Big[\nabla^{\mu}\big(\mathbb{T}[F]_{\mu\nu}X^{\nu}\big)\Big](\tau,x)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\tau=t}^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\Big[\nabla^{\mu}\big(\mathbb{T}[F]_{\mu\nu}\big)X^{\nu}\Big](\tau,x)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}\tau,

since
\nabla^{\mu}\big(\mathbb{T}[F]_{\mu\nu}X^{\nu}\big)\leq\nabla^{\mu}\big(\mathbb{T}[F]_{\mu\nu}\big)X^{\nu}
according to (
53
). Simarly, fix
u\in\mathbb{R}
and apply the divergence theorem in the region
\{(\tau,x)\,|\,\tau-|x|\geq u,\;t\leq\tau\leq s\}
. It yields

\displaystyle\int_{|x|\leq t+u}\Big[\mathbb{T}[F]_{0\nu}X^{\nu}\Big](t,x)\mathrm{d}x+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\int_{C_{u}^{t,s}}\mathbb{T}[F]_{L\nu}X^{\nu}\mathrm{d}\mu_{C_{u}}-

\displaystyle\int_{|x|\leq s+u}\Big[\mathbb{T}[F]_{0\nu}X^{\nu}\Big](s,x)\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\int_{\tau=t}^{s}\int_{|x|\leq\tau+u}\Big[\nabla^{\mu}\big(\mathbb{T}[F]_{\mu\nu}\big)X^{\nu}\Big](\tau,x)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}\tau.

We then deduce that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\Big[\mathbb{T}[F]_{0\nu}X^{\nu}\Big](t,x)\mathrm{d}x+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\sup_{u\in\mathbb{R}}\int_{C_{u}^{t,s}}\mathbb{T}[F]_{L\nu}X^{\nu}\mathrm{d}\mu_{C_{u}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\Big|\mathbb{T}[F]_{0\nu}X^{\nu}\Big|(s,x)\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\quad\,+2\int_{\tau=t}^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\Big|\nabla^{\mu}\big(\mathbb{T}[F]_{\mu\nu}\big)X^{\nu}\Big|(\tau,x)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}\tau.

Recall that
\nabla^{\mu}\mathbb{T}[F]_{\mu\nu}=F_{\nu\lambda}J^{\lambda}+{}^{*}\!F_{\nu\lambda}\widetilde{J}^{\nu}
as well as (
51
)–(
52
) in order to compute the other integrands.

3.4 Pointwise decay estimates for electromagnetic fields

In the linear case, the cartesian components of
F
decay as
t^{-1}
near the light cone since they are solution to the homogeneous wave equation. We explain here how to derive improved estimates in the region
t\sim r
for the good null components
\alpha(F)
,
\rho(F)
and
\sigma(F)
when the energy norm
\mathcal{E}^{K}_{2}[\cdot]
of
F
is uniformly bounded. For this, we will need results which rely on the relations

\displaystyle(t-r)\underline{L}=S-\frac{x^{i}}{r}\Omega_{0i},\quad(t+r)L=S+\frac{x^{i}}{r}\Omega_{0i},\quad re_{\theta}=-\cos(\varphi)\Omega_{13}-\sin(\varphi)\Omega_{23},\quad re_{\varphi}=\Omega_{12}.

(55)

Since
t\Omega_{ij}=x^{i}\Omega_{0j}-x^{j}\Omega_{0i}
, we can gain time decay through
e_{\theta}
and
e_{\varphi}
as well. These identities reflect that the solutions enjoy better decay properties in the directions tangential to the light cone,
L
,
e_{\theta}
and
e_{\varphi}
.

Lemma 3.8
.

The following properties hold.

•

For any rotation
\Omega\in\{\Omega_{12},\,\Omega_{13},\,\Omega_{23}\}
, the operator
\mathcal{L}_{\Omega}
commutes with the null decomposition,

\mathcal{L}_{\Omega}\big(\underline{\alpha}(G)\big)=\underline{\alpha}\left(\mathcal{L}_{\Omega}G\right),\quad\;\mathcal{L}_{\Omega}\big(\alpha(G)\big)=\alpha\left(\mathcal{L}_{\Omega}G\right),\quad\;\mathcal{L}_{\Omega}\big(\rho(G)\big)=\rho\left(\mathcal{L}_{\Omega}G\right),\quad\;\mathcal{L}_{\Omega}\big(\sigma(G)\big)=\sigma\left(\mathcal{L}_{\Omega}G\right).

The operators
\nabla_{L}
and
\nabla_{\underline{L}}
commute as well with the null decomposition, e.g.
\nabla_{L}\alpha(G)=\alpha(\nabla_{L}G)
.

•

For any null component
\zeta\in\{\underline{\alpha},\alpha,\rho,\sigma\}
, we have, for all
(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
,

\langle t-|x|\rangle\left|\nabla_{\underline{L}}\zeta(G)\right|\!(t,x)+\langle t+|x|\rangle\left|\nabla_{L}\zeta(G)\right|\!(t,x)+\langle x\rangle\left|\not{\nabla}\zeta(G)\right|\!(t,x)\lesssim\sup_{|\gamma|\leq 1}\left|\zeta(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}G)\right|\!(t,x).

Proof.

These are well-known properties, which are proved for instance in
[
7
, Appendix
D
]
.

In the same spirit, we have the following property.

Lemma 3.9
.

Let
Z^{\gamma}\in\mathbb{K}^{|\gamma|}
composed by at least one translation
\partial_{x^{\lambda}}
, that is
\gamma_{T}\geq 1
. If
F
is sufficiently regular, we have for any null component
\zeta\in\{\underline{\alpha},\alpha,\rho,\sigma\}
,

\big|\zeta\big(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F\big)\big|(t,x)\lesssim\sup_{|\kappa|\leq|\gamma|}\frac{\big|\zeta\big(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\kappa}}F\big)\big|(t,x)}{\langle t-|x|\rangle}+\frac{\big|\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\kappa}}F\big|(t,x)}{\langle t+|x|\rangle}.

Proof.

Let
0\leq\lambda\leq 3
. As, for any
Z\in\mathbb{K}
,
[Z,\partial_{x^{\lambda}}]=0
or there exists
0\leq\nu\leq 3
such that
[Z,\partial_{x^{\lambda}}]=\pm\partial_{x^{\nu}}
, it suffices to consider the case where
Z^{\gamma}=\partial_{x^{\lambda}}Z^{\beta}
,
|\beta|=|\gamma|-1
. Introduce then
G=\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\beta}}F
.

•

If
\lambda=0
, we use
\mathcal{L}_{\partial_{t}}=\nabla_{\partial_{t}}
,
2\partial_{t}=L+\underline{L}
and Lemma
3.8
.

•

Otherwise
1\leq\lambda\leq 3
and we consider
(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}
. In view of (
31
) and since
\partial_{x^{\mu}}\in\mathbb{K}
, we have

\langle t-|x|\rangle\,\big|\mathcal{L}_{\partial_{x^{\lambda}}}G\big|(t,x)=\langle t-|x|\rangle\,\big|\nabla_{\partial_{x^{\lambda}}}G\big|(t,x)\lesssim\sup_{Z\in\mathbb{K}}\big|\nabla_{Z}G\big|(t,x)\lesssim\sup_{|\kappa|\leq 1}\big|\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\kappa}}G\big|(t,x).

It implies the result if
t\leq 1
or
|x|\geq 2t
. Otherwise
|x|\leq 2t
and we write
t\partial_{x^{k}}=\Omega_{0k}-x^{k}\partial_{t}
, so that

t\big|\zeta\big(\mathcal{L}_{\partial_{x^{\lambda}}}G\big)\big|(t,x)\leq\big|\zeta\big(\nabla_{\Omega_{0k}}G\big)\big|(t,x)+|x|\big|\zeta\big(\nabla_{\partial_{t}}G\big)\big|(t,x)\leq\big|\nabla_{\Omega_{0k}}G\big|(t,x)+|x|\big|\zeta\big(\mathcal{L}_{\partial_{t}}G\big)\big|(t,x).

Since the case
\lambda=0
holds and
t\gtrsim\langle t+|x|\rangle
, this concludes the proof.

In order to derive improved estimates for certain null components of
F
, we will sometimes take advantage of the null structure of the Maxwell equations. For this, we have to express them in the null frame instead of the Cartersian one as it is done in (
2
)–(
3
).

Lemma 3.10
.

Assume that
F
is solution to the Maxwell equations with a magnetic current (
Mmc
), that is

\nabla^{\mu}F_{\mu\nu}=J_{\nu},\qquad\qquad\nabla^{\mu}{}^{*}\!F_{\mu\nu}=\widetilde{J}_{\nu}.

Then, these equations are equivalent to

\displaystyle\hskip-22.76219ptr^{-1}\nabla_{\underline{L}}(r\alpha(F))_{e_{A}}-\not{\nabla}_{e_{A}}\rho(F)-\varepsilon^{AB}\not{\nabla}_{e_{B}}\sigma(F)

\displaystyle=J_{e_{A}}+\varepsilon^{AB}\widetilde{J}_{e_{B}},\qquad\qquad A\in\{\theta,\varphi\},

(56)

\displaystyle\hskip-22.76219ptr^{-1}\nabla_{L}(r\underline{\alpha}(F))_{e_{A}}+\not{\nabla}_{e_{A}}\rho(F)-\varepsilon^{AB}\not{\nabla}_{e_{B}}\sigma(F)

\displaystyle=J_{e_{A}}-\varepsilon^{AB}\widetilde{J}_{e_{B}},\qquad\qquad A\in\{\theta,\varphi\},

(57)

\displaystyle\hskip-22.76219ptr^{-2}\nabla_{\underline{L}}(r^{2}\rho(F))+\not{\nabla}\cdot\underline{\alpha}(F)

\displaystyle=J_{\underline{L}},

(58)

\displaystyle\hskip-22.76219ptr^{-2}\nabla_{\underline{L}}(r^{2}\sigma(F))+\not{\nabla}\times\underline{\alpha}(F)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{J}_{\underline{L}},

(59)

\displaystyle\hskip-22.76219pt-r^{-2}\nabla_{L}(r^{2}\rho(F))+\not{\nabla}\cdot\alpha(F)

\displaystyle=J_{L},

(60)

\displaystyle\hskip-22.76219pt-r^{-2}\nabla_{L}(r^{2}\sigma(F))-\not{\nabla}\times\alpha(F)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{J}_{L}.

(61)

Remark 3.11
.

These equations, which are
[
19
,
(M_{1}^{\prime\prime})
–
(M^{\prime\prime}_{6})
]
in the vacuum case case
J=\widetilde{J}=0
, suggests that
\alpha(F)
,
\rho(F)
and
\sigma(F)
have a better behavior than
F
. Indeed, using (
56
) and (
58
)–(
59
), one can prove that the bad derivative
\underline{L}
of these composents decays at least as
r^{-2}
near the light cone
\{t=r\}
, where
\nabla_{\underline{L}}F
merely decay as
r^{-1}
.

Using part of (
56
)–(
61
), we are able to deduce improved estimates for the derivative of
r\underline{\alpha}(F)
,
r^{2}\rho(F)
and
r^{2}\sigma(F)
in the
\underline{u}
direction, which will turn out to be crucial in order to prove that these quantities converge along null rays in the framework of this paper.

Corollary 3.12
.

Assume that
F
is solution to (
Mmc
). Then, we have on
[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
,

\displaystyle\left|\nabla_{L}\big(r\underline{\alpha}(F)\big)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim r|J|+r\big|\widetilde{J}\,\big|+\sup_{|\kappa|\leq 1}\left|\rho(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\kappa}}F)\right|+\left|\sigma(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\kappa}}F)\right|,

\displaystyle\left|\nabla_{L}\big(r^{2}\rho(F)\big)\right|+\left|\nabla_{L}\big(r^{2}\sigma(F)\big)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim r^{2}|J_{L}|+r^{2}\big|\widetilde{J}_{L}\,\big|+\sup_{|\kappa|\leq 1}r\left|\alpha(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\kappa}}F)\right|.

Proof.

It suffices to combine (
57
), (
60
) and (
61
) with Lemma
3.8
.

We now prove decay estimates for
F
, which rely on Sobolev embeddings as well as (
56
).

Proposition 3.13
.

We have, for all
(t,x)\in[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
,

\displaystyle|\underline{\alpha}(F)|(t,x)

\displaystyle\lesssim\langle t+|x|\rangle^{-1}\,\langle t-|x|\rangle^{-\frac{3}{2}}\left|\mathcal{E}^{K}_{2}[F](t)\right|^{\frac{1}{2}},

\displaystyle|\alpha(F)|(t,x)+|\rho(F)|(t,x)+|\sigma(F)|(t,x)

\displaystyle\lesssim\langle t+|x|\rangle^{-2}\,\langle t-|x|\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left|\mathcal{E}^{K}_{2}[F](t)\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}.

If there exists
h:[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\to\mathbb{R}
such that
F
is solution to

\nabla^{\mu}F_{\mu\nu}=J(h)_{\nu},\qquad\qquad\nabla^{\mu}{}^{*}\!F_{\mu\nu}=0,

then the best null component
\alpha(F)
verifies

\displaystyle|\alpha(F)|(t,x)

\displaystyle\lesssim\langle t+|x|\rangle^{-\frac{5}{2}}\bigg(\,\left|\mathcal{E}^{K}_{2}[F](t)\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}+\sup_{|\kappa|\leq 1}\langle t\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Big\|\langle v\rangle^{\frac{5}{2}}\,\langle x-t\widehat{v}\rangle^{3}\,\widehat{Z}^{\kappa}h(t,x,v)\Big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}\,\bigg).

Proof.

According to the weighted Klainerman-Sobolev of
[
43
, Lemma
3.3
]
, for any sufficiently regular function
\psi:[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\to\mathbb{R}
, we have

\forall\,(t,x)\in[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3},\qquad\langle t-|x|\rangle^{\frac{3}{2}}\,\langle t+|x|\rangle\,|\psi|(t,x)\lesssim\sup_{|\gamma|\leq 2}\big\|\big\langle t-|\cdot|\big\rangle\,Z^{\gamma}\psi(t,\cdot)\big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})}\,.

Applying it to any cartesian component
F_{\mu\nu}
,
0\leq\mu,\,\nu\leq 3
, of the electromagnetic field, we get

\forall\,(t,x)\in[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3},\qquad\langle t-|x|\rangle^{\frac{3}{2}}\,\langle t+|x|\rangle\,|F|(t,x)\lesssim\left|\mathcal{E}^{K}_{2}[F](t)\right|^{\frac{1}{2}},

which implies the estimate for
\underline{\alpha}(F)
. In order to derive the better behavior of the good null components of
F
, we apply
[
7
, Proposition
3.13
]
(the vacuum case was originally treated in
[
19
, Theorem
3.1
-
3.2
]
). It provides the stated estimates for
\rho(F)
as well as
\sigma(F)
and the first estimate for
\alpha(F)
is derived in its proof. It provides as well

|\alpha(F)|(t,x)\lesssim\langle t+|x|\rangle^{-\frac{5}{2}}\,\bigg(\,\left|\mathcal{E}^{K}_{2}[F](t)\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}+\sup_{|\kappa|\leq 1}\big\||\cdot|^{2}\,\cancel{\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\kappa}}J}(h)\,(t,\cdot)\big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\bigg),

(62)

where
\cancel{\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\kappa}}J}(h):=\big(|\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\kappa}}J(h)_{e_{\theta}}|^{2}+|\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\kappa}}J(h)_{e_{\varphi}}|^{2}\big)^{1/2}
is the angular part of
\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\kappa}}J(h)
.
Apply Lemmata
2.7
and
2.9
in order to get

\sup_{|\kappa|\leq 1}\big|\cancel{\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\kappa}}J}(h)\big|(t,x)\lesssim\sup_{|\beta|\leq 1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\Big|\not{\widehat{v}}\,\widehat{Z}^{\beta}h\Big|(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}v\lesssim\sup_{|\beta|\leq 1}\frac{1}{\langle x\rangle}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\langle x-t\widehat{v}\rangle\big|\widehat{Z}^{\beta}h\big|(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}v.

By applying the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality in
v
, one obtains

\displaystyle\sup_{|\kappa|\leq 1}\big\||\cdot|^{2}\,\cancel{\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\kappa}}J}(h)\,(t,\cdot)\big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{|\kappa|\leq 1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}|x|^{2}\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\langle x-t\widehat{v}\rangle\big|\widehat{Z}^{\kappa}h\big|(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}v\bigg|^{2}\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{|\kappa|\leq 1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\langle v\rangle^{5}\,\langle x-t\widehat{v}\rangle^{6}\big|\widehat{Z}^{\kappa}h\big|^{2}(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}v\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\frac{|x|^{2}\mathrm{d}v}{\langle x-t\widehat{v}\rangle^{4}\,\langle v\rangle^{5}}\mathrm{d}x.

It remains to bound the second integral in
v
by
\langle t\rangle^{-1}
using (
30
).

3.5 Control of the initial data for the Maxwell equations

Let
t_{0}\geq 0
be an initial time,
J
be a sufficiently regular current defined on
[t_{0},+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
and
F
be the unique solution to

\nabla^{\mu}F_{\mu\nu}=J_{\nu},\qquad\nabla^{\mu}{}^{*}\!F_{\mu\nu}=0,\qquad\qquad\nabla\times E(t_{0},\cdot)=\nabla\times B(t_{0},\cdot)=0,

(63)

where
E^{i}:=F_{0i}
and
B^{i}:=-\varepsilon^{ijk}F_{jk}
are the electric and magnetic fields. We assume here that their initial divergence free part, according to Helmholtz decomposition, vanish. Thus, there exists
\phi:\mathbb{R}^{3}\to\mathbb{R}
such that

B(t_{0},\cdot)=0,\qquad\qquad E(t_{0},\cdot)=\nabla\phi,\qquad\Delta\phi=-J_{0}(t_{0},\cdot).

In particular
\nabla\times E(t_{0},\cdot)=\nabla\times B(t_{0},\cdot)=0
is equivalent to

\nabla_{\partial_{x^{i}}}F_{0j}(t_{0},\cdot)=\nabla_{\partial_{x^{j}}}F_{0i}(t_{0},\cdot),\qquad\nabla_{\partial_{x^{i}}}{}^{*}\!F_{0j}(t_{0},\cdot)=\nabla_{\partial_{x^{j}}}{}^{*}\!F_{0i}(t_{0},\cdot),\qquad\qquad 1\leq i,\,j\leq 3.

In this subsection, we control
F(t_{0},\cdot)
and its derivatives in two cases. For this, note that the Maxwell equations and the initial assumptions imply

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(\partial_{t}^{n-1}B)(t_{0},\cdot)

\displaystyle=-\partial_{t}^{n-1}(\nabla_{x}\times E)(t_{0},\cdot)=0,

for any
n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
. Thus,
B
and all its spacetime derivatives initially vanish. In order to control the initial time derivatives of the electric field, we will use the following relation, obtained similarly. For any component
1\leq i\leq 3
and multi-index
\beta
,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(\partial_{t}^{n-1}\partial_{x}^{\beta}E^{i})(t_{0},\cdot)=-\partial_{t}^{n-1}\partial_{x}^{\beta}J_{i}(t_{0},\cdot).

(64)

First, we assume that
J
is generated by a distribution function.

Proposition 3.14
.

Let
N\geq 0
,
f:[t_{0},+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\to\mathbb{R}
be a sufficiently regular function and assume that
J=J(f)
. Then, for any
|\gamma|\leq N
,

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\big|\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F\big|^{2}(t_{0},x)\mathrm{d}x\lesssim_{t_{0}}\sup_{|\beta|\leq N}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\langle x\rangle^{3}\,\langle v\rangle^{4}\big|\widehat{Z}^{\beta}f\big|^{2}(t_{0},x,v)\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x.

Proof.

In view of the definition of the Lie derivative and the fact that the elements of
\mathbb{K}
are translations
\partial_{x^{\mu}}
or homogeneous vector fields, we have, for
|\gamma|\leq N
,

\big|\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F\big|(t_{0},x)\lesssim_{t_{0}}\sup_{|\xi|\leq|\gamma|}\,\langle x\rangle^{|\xi|}\big|\nabla_{t,x}^{\xi}F\big|(t_{0},x)=\sup_{|\xi|\leq|\gamma|}\,\langle x\rangle^{|\xi|}\big|\nabla_{x}^{\xi}E\big|(t_{0},x)+\sup_{n+|\beta|\leq|\gamma|,\,n\geq 1}\,\langle x\rangle^{n+|\beta|}\big|\nabla_{t}^{n}\nabla_{x}^{\beta}E\big|(t_{0},x),

since
B
and all its derivatives initially vanish. As
E(t_{0},\cdot)=\nabla\phi
, we estimate the spatial derivatives of the initial electric field by applying Lemma
A.3
to
\phi
. The time derivatives can be controlled using (
64
) as well as Lemma
2.18
, which provides

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\langle x\rangle^{2n+2|\beta|}\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\widehat{v}\partial_{t}^{n-1}\partial_{x}^{\beta}f(t_{0},x,v)\mathrm{d}v\bigg|^{2}\mathrm{d}x\lesssim_{t_{0}}\sup_{|\zeta|\leq n-1+|\beta|}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\langle x\rangle^{2}\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\big|\widehat{Z}^{\zeta}f\big|(t_{0},x,v)\mathrm{d}v\bigg|^{2}\mathrm{d}x.

We finally handle the integrals on the right hand side by applying the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality in
v
.

In the perspective of studying the asymptotic Maxwell equations, we prove stronger estimates for a compactly supported source term.

Proposition 3.15
.

Let
N\geq 1
and assume that
t_{0}\leq 3
and that
J(t_{0},\cdot)
is supported in
\{|x|\leq 3\}
. Recall

\overline{F}(t,x)=\overline{\chi}(t-|x|)\frac{Q}{4\pi|x|^{2}}\frac{x_{i}}{|x|}\mathrm{d}t\wedge\mathrm{d}x^{i},\qquad\qquad Q=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}J_{0}(t_{0},x)\mathrm{d}x,

the pure charge part of
F
. Then, for any
|\gamma|\leq N-1
and
|\kappa|\leq 1
,

\forall\,x\in\mathbb{R}^{3},\qquad\langle x\rangle^{3+|\kappa|}\left|\nabla_{t,x}^{\kappa}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}(F-\overline{F})\right|\!(t_{0},x)\lesssim\sup_{|\xi|\leq N}\big\|\nabla_{t,x}^{\xi}J(t_{0},\cdot)\big\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})}.

Moreover, the following
L^{2}
estimate holds for any
|\gamma|\leq N
and
|\kappa|\leq 1
,

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\langle x\rangle^{\frac{5}{2}+2|\kappa|}\left|\nabla_{t,x}^{\kappa}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}(F-\overline{F})\right|^{2}\!(t_{0},x)\mathrm{d}x\lesssim\sup_{|\xi|\leq N}\big\|\nabla_{t,x}^{\xi}J(t_{0},\cdot)\big\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})}.

Proof.

Note that
|\partial_{t,x}^{\kappa}\partial_{t}\overline{F}_{0i}|(t_{0},x)\lesssim|Q|\mathds{1}_{|x|\leq 5}
. Hence, by similar computations as the one performed in the previous proof, we have for any
|\kappa|\leq 1
and
|\gamma|\leq N
,

\displaystyle\langle x\rangle^{|\kappa|}\left|\nabla_{t,x}^{\kappa}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}(F-\overline{F})\right|(t_{0},x)\lesssim

\displaystyle\sup_{1\leq i\leq 3}\sup_{|\xi|\leq|\gamma|+1}\,\langle x\rangle^{|\xi|}\big|\partial_{x}^{\xi}(F_{0i}-\overline{F}_{0i})\big|(t_{0},x)

\displaystyle+\sup_{n+|\beta|\leq|\gamma|+1,\,n\geq 1}\,\langle x\rangle^{n+|\beta|}\big|\nabla_{t}^{n}\nabla_{x}^{\beta}E\big|(t_{0},x)+|Q|\mathds{1}_{|x|\leq 5}.

Using (
64
), we can directly control the time derivatives of
E(t_{0},\cdot)
, which turn out to be compactly supported. Next,

\sup_{1\leq i\leq 3}\sup_{|\xi|\leq|\gamma|+1}\,\langle x\rangle^{|\xi|}\big|\partial_{x}^{\xi}(F_{0i}-\overline{F}_{0i})\big|(t_{0},x)\leq\sup_{|\xi|\leq|\gamma|+1}\,\langle x\rangle^{|\xi|}\Big|\nabla_{x}^{\xi}\nabla\Big(\phi(x)+\frac{Q}{4\pi|x|}\overline{\chi}(t_{0}-|x|)\Big)\Big|+|Q|\mathds{1}_{|x|\leq 5}.

If
Q\neq 0
, one cannot apply Lemma
A.4
directly. Instead, remark that
\widetilde{\phi}=\phi+\frac{1}{4\pi r}Q\overline{\chi}(t_{0}-r)
verifies

\Delta\widetilde{\phi}=\widetilde{\psi},\qquad\widetilde{\psi}:=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f(t_{0},\cdot,v)\mathrm{d}v+\frac{Q}{4\pi r}\chi^{\prime\prime}(t_{0}-r),\qquad\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\widetilde{\psi}=0.

As
\chi=1
on
]-\infty,-2]
and
\chi=0
on
[-1,+\infty[
, the result ensues from Lemma
A.4
.

3.6 Study of the pure charge part of the electromagnetic field

The previous Proposition
3.15
suggests that
\overline{F}
plays a crucial role in the analysis of the asymptotic behavior of electromagnetic field of charge
Q\neq 0
. In fact, as noticed by
[
44
]
, the long range effect of the charge is only relevant in the exterior of the light cone
|x|\geq t
. In particular, in the context of the Vlasov-Maxwell system, the asymptotic behavior of
r^{2}\rho(F)
along null rays
r\mapsto(r+u,r\omega)
will be governed, as
u\to-\infty
, by
r^{2}\rho(\overline{F})
. We collect here classical properties of
\overline{F}
.

Proposition 3.16
.

Let
Q\in\mathbb{R}
. The following properties hold.

1.

The null components of
\overline{F}
verify, for all
(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
,

\rho\big(\overline{F}\,\big)(t,x)=\frac{Q}{4\pi|x|^{2}}\overline{\chi}(t-|x|),\qquad\qquad\underline{\alpha}\big(\overline{F}\,\big)(t,x)=\alpha\big(\overline{F}\,\big)(t,x)=\sigma\big(\overline{F}\,\big)(t,x)=0.

2.

\overline{F}
is a solution to the Maxwell equations
\nabla^{\mu}\overline{F}_{\mu\nu}=\overline{J}_{\mu\nu}
and
\nabla^{\mu}{}^{*}\!\overline{F}_{\mu\nu}=0
, where the source term
\overline{J}
is compactly supported and given by

\overline{J}_{0}(t,x):=\frac{Q}{4\pi|x|^{2}}\overline{\chi}^{\prime}(t-|x|),\qquad\qquad\overline{J}_{i}(t,x):=-\frac{Q}{4\pi|x|^{2}}\frac{x_{i}}{|x|}\overline{\chi}^{\prime}(t-|x|),\quad 1\leq i\leq 3.

Its null components are

\overline{J}_{\underline{L}}(t,x)=\frac{Q}{2\pi|x|^{2}}\overline{\chi}^{\prime}(t-|x|),\qquad\qquad\overline{J}_{L}(t,x)=\overline{J}_{e_{\theta}}(t,x)=\overline{J}_{e_{\varphi}}(t,x)=0.

3.

The Lie derivatives with respect to the scaling vector field and the rotational derivatives are chargeless,

\mathcal{L}_{S}\overline{F}(t,x)=(t-|x|)\overline{\chi}^{\prime}(t-|x|)\frac{Q}{4\pi|x|^{2}}\frac{x_{i}}{|x|}\mathrm{d}t\wedge\mathrm{d}x^{i},\qquad\qquad\qquad\mathcal{L}_{\Omega_{ij}}\overline{F}=0,\quad 1\leq i<j\leq 3.

4.

Let
Z^{\gamma}\!\in\!\mathbb{K}^{|\gamma|}
. There exists
C_{\gamma}>0
such that the null components of
\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}\overline{F}
verify,
\forall\,(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\!\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
,

\displaystyle\big|\underline{\alpha}\big(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}\overline{F}\,\big)\big|(t,x)+\big|\rho\big(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}\overline{F}\,\big)\big|(t,x)+\big|\sigma\big(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}\overline{F}\,\big)\big|(t,x)

\displaystyle\leq C_{\gamma}\frac{Q}{\langle t+|x|\rangle^{2}}\mathds{1}_{|x|\geq t+1},

\displaystyle\big|\alpha\big(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}\overline{F}\,\big)\big|(t,x)

\displaystyle\leq C_{\gamma}\frac{Q\,\langle t-|x|\rangle}{\langle t+|x|\rangle^{3}}\mathds{1}_{|x|\geq t+1}.

The ones of
\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}\overline{J}
satisfy, for any
A\in\{\theta,\varphi\}
and for all
(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
,

\langle t+|x|\rangle^{2}\big|\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}\big(\overline{J}\,\big)_{\underline{L}}\big|(t,x)+\langle t+|x|\rangle^{3}\big|\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}\big(\overline{J}\,\big)_{e_{A}}\big|(t,x)+\langle t+|x|\rangle^{4}\big|\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}\big(\overline{J}\,\big)_{L}\big|(t,x)\leq C_{\gamma}Q\mathds{1}_{1\leq|x|-t\leq 2}.

Proof.

The first part of the statement is given by direct computations. The equations
\nabla^{\mu}{}^{*}\!\overline{F}_{\mu\nu}=0
, equivalent to
\nabla_{[\lambda}\overline{F}_{\mu\nu]}=0
, follow from
\overline{F}_{ij}=0
and from the fact that the electric field associated to
\overline{F}
is radial, so that
\nabla_{i}\overline{F}_{0j}=\nabla_{j}\overline{F}_{0i}
. For the other ones, we have

\displaystyle\nabla^{i}\overline{F}_{i0}

\displaystyle=-\frac{Q}{4\pi}\partial_{x^{i}}\!\bigg(\frac{x^{i}}{|x|^{3}}\overline{\chi}(t-|x|)\!\bigg)\!=-\frac{Q}{4\pi}\!\left(\left(\frac{3}{|x|^{3}}-3\frac{x_{i}x^{i}}{|x|^{5}}\right)\overline{\chi}(t-|x|)-\frac{x^{i}x_{i}}{|x|^{4}}\overline{\chi}^{\prime}(t-|x|)\!\right)\!=\frac{Q}{4\pi|x|^{2}}\overline{\chi}^{\prime}(t-|x|),

\displaystyle\nabla^{\mu}\overline{F}_{\mu i}

\displaystyle=-\partial_{t}\overline{F}_{0i}=-\frac{Q}{4\pi}\frac{x^{i}}{|x|^{3}}\overline{\chi}^{\prime}(t-|x|).

For the third part, recall from (
26
) the expression of the Lie derivative and use
S(x_{i}/|x|^{3})=-2x_{i}/|x|^{3}
,
S(t-|x|)=t-|x|
as well as
\Omega_{ij}(|x|)=\Omega_{ij}(t)=0
. Finally, the estimates for the null components of the derivatives of
\overline{F}
and
\overline{J}
were obtained in
[
44
, Proposition
5.3
]
and
[
44
, Equations
(3.52a)
–
(3.52c)
]
. They can be derived, through an induction, by exploiting the following properties.

•

For any
Z\in\mathbb{K}
and any
X,\,Y\in\{\underline{L},\,L,\,e_{\theta},\,e_{\varphi}\}
, we have

\mathcal{L}_{ZZ^{\gamma}}\big(\,\overline{J}\,\big)_{X}=Z\big(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}\big(\,\overline{J}\,\big)_{X}\big)-\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}\big(\,\overline{J}\,\big)_{[Z,X]},

as well as

\mathcal{L}_{ZZ^{\gamma}}\big(\,\overline{F}\,\big)_{XY}=Z\big(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}\big(\,\overline{F}\,\big)_{XY}\big)-\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}\big(\,\overline{F}\,\big)_{[Z,X]Y}-\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}\big(\,\overline{F}\,\big)_{X[Z,Y]}.

•

The components of the commutation vector fields with respect to the null frame are given by

\displaystyle 2\partial_{t}=L+\underline{L},\quad 2S=\underline{u}L+u\underline{L},\quad\partial_{x^{k}}=-\frac{\omega_{k}}{2}\underline{L}+\frac{\omega_{k}}{2}L+\omega_{k}^{e_{A}}e_{A},\quad\Omega_{0k}=\frac{\omega_{k}}{2}(\underline{u}L-u\underline{L})+t\omega_{k}^{e_{A}}e_{A},

\displaystyle\Omega_{ij}=(x^{i}\omega_{j}^{e_{A}}-x^{j}\omega_{i}^{e_{A}})e_{A},\quad\;\;\omega_{i}:=\langle\partial_{x^{i}},\partial_{r}\rangle=\frac{x^{i}}{|x|},\quad\omega^{e_{A}}_{i}:=\langle\partial_{x^{i}},e_{A}\rangle,\quad\;\;1\leq k\leq 3,\quad 1\leq i<j\leq 3,

where
\omega_{i}^{e_{A}}
is a smooth function of the spherical variables
(\theta,\varphi)\in]0,\pi[\times]0,2\pi[
(see (
66
) below).

•

The commutator of elements of
\mathbb{K}
and the null frame are, for
1\leq i<j\leq 3
,
1\leq k\leq 3
and
A\in\{\theta,\varphi\}
,

\displaystyle[\partial_{t},L]=[\partial_{t},\underline{L}]=0,\quad[\partial_{t},e_{A}]=0,\quad[S,L]=-L,\quad[S,\underline{L}]=-\underline{L},\quad[S,e_{A}]=-e_{A},

\displaystyle[\Omega_{ij},L]=[\Omega_{ij},\underline{L}]=0,\quad[\Omega_{ij},e_{A}]=-e_{A}(\Omega^{B}_{ij})e_{B}-\Omega_{ij}^{B}[e_{A},e_{B}]^{D}e_{D},\quad\Omega_{ij}^{B}=r(\omega_{i}\omega_{j}^{e_{B}}-\omega_{j}\omega_{i}^{e_{B}}),

\displaystyle[\Omega_{0k},L]=\frac{t-r}{r}\omega_{k}^{e_{B}}e_{B}-\omega_{k}L,\quad[\Omega_{0k},\underline{L}]=-\frac{t+r}{r}\omega^{e_{B}}_{k}e_{B}+\omega_{k}\underline{L},

\displaystyle[\Omega_{0k},e_{A}]=\frac{\omega^{e_{A}}_{k}}{2r}((t-r)\underline{L}-(t+r)L)+t\omega_{k}^{e_{B}}\not{\Gamma}^{D}_{BA}e_{D},

\displaystyle[\partial_{x^{k}},L]=-[\partial_{x^{k}},\underline{L}]=\frac{1}{r}(\partial_{x^{k}}-\frac{x^{k}}{r}\partial_{r})=\frac{1}{r^{2}}\left(\frac{x^{j}}{r}\Omega_{kj}\right),\quad[\partial_{x^{k}},e_{A}]=\partial_{x^{k}}(\omega_{\ell}^{e_{A}})\partial_{x^{\ell}},

where
\not{\Gamma}_{AB}^{D}
are the Christofell symbols in the nonholonomic basis
(e_{\theta},e_{\varphi})
of
\mathbb{S}^{2}
,
\not{\nabla}_{e_{A}}e_{B}=\not{\Gamma}_{AB}^{D}e_{D}
. In particular, for any
X\in\mathcal{N}:=\{\underline{L},L,e_{\theta},e_{\varphi}\}
,
[\partial_{x^{k}},X]=\sum_{Y\in\mathcal{N}}r^{-1}a_{Y}(\omega)Y
, where
a_{Y}
is a smooth function of
(\theta,\varphi)
.

•

For any
1\leq k\leq 3
and
1\leq i<j\leq 3
, we have, with
u=t-|x|
,

\partial_{t}(u)=1,\qquad\partial_{x^{k}}(u)=-\frac{x^{k}}{|x|},\qquad S(u)=u,\qquad\Omega_{ij}(u)=0,\qquad\Omega_{0k}(u)=-\frac{x^{k}}{|x|}u.

(65)

We now give a schematic form for
\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}\overline{F}
, which will be useful in order to prove scattering results.

Proposition 3.17
.

Let
Z^{\gamma}\in\mathbb{K}^{|\gamma|}
containing
\gamma_{T}\geq 0
translations
\partial_{x^{\lambda}}
. Then, for any cartesian components
0\leq\mu,\,\nu\leq 3
, we can write
\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}(\overline{F}\,)_{\mu\nu}(t,x)
as a linear combination of terms of the form

\frac{Q}{|x|^{2+\gamma_{T}}}P\Big(\frac{x}{|x|},\frac{t}{|x|}\Big)(t-|x|)^{p}\chi^{(q)}(t-|x|),

where
0\leq p\leq q
and
P
is a polynomial. Thus,
r^{2}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}(\overline{F}\,)_{\mu\nu}(r+u,r\omega)
converges as
r\to+\infty
, for all
(u,\omega)\in\mathbb{R}_{u}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}
. If
u\leq-2
, the limit is independent of
u
.

Proof.

The result follows from an induction, that the elements of
\mathbb{K}
are either translations
\partial_{x^{\lambda}}
or homogeneous vector fields as well as (
65
).

In Section
5
, for simplicity, we will work with a similar but slightly different quantity to
\overline{F}
. The motivation is that its cartesian componenents will be solution to the homogeneous wave equation. The singular electromagnetic field
\frac{Qx_{i}}{4\pi|x|^{3}}\mathrm{d}t\wedge\mathrm{d}x^{i}
, generated by a point charge
Q
located at
x=0
, derives from the potential
A=Q(4\pi r)^{-1}\mathrm{d}t
. It turns out that
A
satisfies the Lorenz gauge
\partial_{x^{\mu}}A^{\mu}=0
, so that
\Box A_{\lambda}=0
on
\mathbb{R}\times(\mathbb{R}^{3}\setminus\{0\})
. To deal with our evolution problem and the singularity of the Newton potential, we introduce

\overline{A}(t,x):=\overline{\chi}(t-|x|)A(t,x)=\frac{Q}{4\pi|x|}\overline{\chi}(t-|x|)\mathrm{d}t,

which is smooth and verifies
\Box\overline{A}_{\mu}=0
as well. It motivates the introduction of

\widetilde{F}(t,x):=\mathrm{d}\overline{A}(t,x)=\overline{F}(t,x)+\frac{Qx_{i}}{4\pi|x|^{2}}\overline{\chi}^{\prime}(t-|x|)\mathrm{d}t\wedge\mathrm{d}x^{i},

which, in view of
[\Box,\partial_{x^{\lambda}}]=0
and
\Box\overline{A}_{\lambda}=0
, verifies
\Box\widetilde{F}_{\mu\nu}=0
.

Proposition 3.18
.

For any multi-index
\gamma
and
0\leq\mu,\,\nu\leq 3
, we have
\Box\,\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}(\widetilde{F})_{\mu\nu}=0
. Moreover,

\forall\,(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3},\qquad\big|\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}\big(\widetilde{F}-\overline{F}\big)\big|(t,x)\leq C_{\gamma}Q\,\langle t+|x|\rangle^{-1}\,\mathds{1}_{1\leq|x|-t\leq 2}.

Proof.

These results essentially follows from the next properties.

•

For any
Z\in\mathbb{K}\setminus\{S\}
,
|\Box,Z]=0
and
[\Box,S]=2\,\Box
.

•

For any
Z=Z^{\lambda}\partial_{x^{\lambda}}\in\mathbb{K}
and any
2
-form
H
, we have
\mathcal{L}_{Z}(H)_{\mu\nu}=Z(H_{\mu\nu})+\partial_{x^{\mu}}(Z^{\lambda})H_{\lambda\nu}+\partial_{x^{\nu}}(Z^{\lambda})H_{\mu\lambda}
.

•

Z^{\lambda}
is either a monomial,
\pm x^{\mu}
, or a constant,
0
or
1
.

•

We have
|\chi^{(n)}(t-|x|)|\leq\mathds{1}_{1\leq|x|-t\leq 2}
for any
n\geq 1
. Hence,
|x|\gtrsim\langle t+|x|\rangle
on the support of
\chi^{(n)}
.

Using
1.
,
2.
and
3.
, together with an induction, one gets
\Box\,\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}(\widetilde{F})_{\mu\nu}=0
for any
Z^{\gamma}\in\mathbb{K}^{\gamma|}
. The pointwise decay estimates ensue from
3.
,
4.
and (
65
).

4
Scattering results for the Maxwell equations

We fix, for all this section, an initial time
T\geq 0
.

4.1 Forward evolution of electromagnetic fields

This section is devoted to general scattering results for the Maxwell equations in Minkowski spacetime. We start by giving a definition of the radiation field of an electromagnetic field which holds in a very general setting.

Definition 4.1
.

Let
F
be a
2
-form defined on
[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
. If there exists a
1
-form
\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}
, defined on
\mathbb{R}_{u}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}
and tangential to the
2
-spheres, such that

r\underline{\alpha}(F)(r+u,r\omega)\xrightharpoonup[r\to+\infty]{}\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}(u,\omega)\qquad\qquad\text{in $\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\big(\mathbb{R}_{u}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}\big)$,}

we will say that
\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}
is the radiation field of
F
along future null infinity
\mathcal{I}^{+}
.

Remark 4.2
.

The convergence could also be written
r\underline{\alpha}(F)(u,\underline{u},\omega)\xrightharpoonup{}\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}(u,\omega)
, as
\underline{u}\to+\infty
, where it is implied that
r\underline{\alpha}(F)(\cdot,\underline{u},\cdot)
is extended by
0
for
u<2T-\underline{u}
and
u>\underline{u}
.

We prove now that any solution
F
to the Maxwell equations scatters provided that the initial data as well as the source term are sufficiently regular and enjoy enough decay. Then, we relate the derivatives of its radiation field to the ones of
\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F
. For this, we will use the following quantities depending only on the spherical variables,

\displaystyle\omega_{i}:=\langle\partial_{x^{i}},\partial_{r}\rangle=\frac{x^{i}}{|x|},\qquad\omega^{e_{A}}_{i}:=\langle\partial_{x^{i}},e_{A}\rangle,\qquad 1\leq i\leq 3,\quad A\in\{\theta,\varphi\},

(66)

\displaystyle\omega^{e_{\theta}}_{1}=\cos(\varphi)\cos(\theta),\quad\omega^{e_{\theta}}_{2}=\sin(\varphi)\cos(\theta),\quad\omega^{e_{\theta}}_{3}=-\sin(\theta),\quad\omega^{e_{\varphi}}_{1}=-\sin(\varphi),\quad\omega^{e_{\varphi}}_{2}=\cos(\varphi),\quad\omega^{e_{\varphi}}_{3}=0,

By an abuse of notation, we will sometimes denote the functions
(u,\omega)\mapsto u
,
(u,\omega)\mapsto\omega_{i}
and
(u,\omega)\mapsto\omega_{i}^{e_{A}}
by
u
,
\omega_{i}
and
\omega_{i}^{e_{A}}
. The assumptions of the next result will be satisfied by the electromagnetic field and its derivatives in the context of the study of the solutions to the Vlasov-Maxwell system with a small distribution function.

Proposition 4.3
.

Let
F
be a sufficiently regular solution to the Maxwell equations (
47
), with source term
J
. Let
J^{\gamma}
be the current such that

\forall\,|\gamma|\leq 1,\qquad\nabla^{\mu}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}(F)_{\mu\nu}=J_{\nu}^{\gamma},\qquad\nabla^{\mu}{}^{*}\!\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}(F)_{\mu\nu}=0
