and assume that there exists
D\geq 0
such that

\sup_{|\gamma|\leq 1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\left|\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F\right|^{2}(T,x)\mathrm{d}x+\sup_{|\gamma|\leq 1}\,\sup_{t\geq T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\langle t+|x|\rangle^{3}|J^{\gamma}|^{2}(t,x)\mathrm{d}x\leq D.

Then,
\sup_{t\geq T}\|\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}\lesssim D
for any
|\gamma|\leq 1
and
F
has a radiation field
\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}
along
\mathcal{I}^{+}
satisfying

\forall\,U\geq 1,\;\underline{u}\geq 2U,\quad\int_{|u|\leq U}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\left|\big[r\underline{\alpha}(F)\big](u,\underline{u},\omega)-\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\!(u,\omega)\right|^{2}\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\mathrm{d}u\lesssim\frac{UD}{\langle\underline{u}\rangle^{\frac{3}{4}}},\quad\qquad\left\|\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{u}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega})}\lesssim D^{\frac{1}{2}}.

For any
Z\in\mathbb{K}
, there exists a
1
-form
\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}_{\,Z}
, defined on
\mathbb{R}_{u}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}
and tangential to the
2
-spheres, such that

\big[r\underline{\alpha}(\mathcal{L}_{Z}F)\big](\cdot,\underline{u},\cdot)\xrightharpoonup[\underline{u}\to+\infty]{}\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}_{\,Z}\qquad\text{ in $L^{2}\big(\mathbb{R}_{u}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}\big)$}.

Moreover, we have for any
1\leq k\leq 3
and
1\leq i<j\leq 3
,

\displaystyle\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}_{\,\partial_{t}}

\displaystyle=\nabla_{u}\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}},\qquad

\displaystyle\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}_{\,\partial_{x^{k}}}=-\omega_{k}\nabla_{u}\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}},\qquad\qquad\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}_{\,S}=u\nabla_{u}\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}+\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}},

\displaystyle\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}_{\,\Omega_{ij}}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}_{\Omega_{ij}}\big(\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\big),\qquad

\displaystyle\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}_{\,\Omega_{0k}}=-\omega_{k}u\nabla_{u}\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}-2\omega_{k}\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}+\omega^{e_{A}}_{k}\not{\nabla}_{e_{A}}\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}.

Proof.

Working with the null coordinates
(u,\underline{u},\omega)
, where
\omega\in\mathbb{S}^{2}
,
u=t-r
and
\underline{u}=t+r
, will be convenient here. Note first that the assumption on the source term and Proposition
3.5
provide, for all
|\gamma|\leq 1
,

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}_{+}}\|\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}+\sup_{\underline{u}\in\mathbb{R}_{+}}\int_{u=2T-\underline{u}}^{\underline{u}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\big|r\underline{\alpha}(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F)\big|^{2}(u,\underline{u},\omega)\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle+\sup_{u\in\mathbb{R}}\int_{\underline{u}=2T-u}^{+\infty}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\big[|r\rho(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F)|^{2}+|r\sigma(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F)|^{2}\big](u,\underline{u},\omega)\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\mathrm{d}\underline{u}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F(0,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}+\bigg|\int_{\tau=0}^{+\infty}\frac{D^{\frac{1}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}\tau}{\langle\tau\rangle^{\frac{3}{2}}}\bigg|^{2}\lesssim D.

In the following, in order to avoid technicalities related to the domain of integrations, we extend
F
and its derivatives by
0
for
u>\underline{u}
and
u<2T-\underline{u}
.

Let us prove now that
F
admits a radiation field. For this, recall that
L=2\partial_{\underline{u}}
. Then, remark that for a smooth function
\psi:\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\to\mathbb{R}
and for all
0\leq\underline{u}_{1}\leq\underline{u}_{2}
,
\omega\in\mathbb{S}_{\omega}
,

\forall\,|u|\leq\underline{u}_{1},\qquad|\psi(u,\underline{u}_{2},\omega)-\psi(u,\underline{u}_{1},\omega)|\leq 2\int_{\underline{u}=\underline{u}_{1}}^{\underline{u}_{2}}|L\psi|(u,\underline{u},\omega)\mathrm{d}\underline{u}.

We then obtain, applying Minkowski’s integral inequality, that for
U\geq 1
and for all
2U\leq\underline{u}_{1}\leq\underline{u}_{2}
,

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{|u|\leq U}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}

\displaystyle\left|\big[r\underline{\alpha}(F)\big](u,\underline{u}_{1},\omega)-\big[r\underline{\alpha}(F)\big](u,\underline{u}_{2},\omega)\right|^{2}\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\mathrm{d}u\bigg|^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\lesssim\int_{\underline{u}=\underline{u}_{1}}^{\underline{u}_{2}}\bigg|\int_{|u|\leq U}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\left|\nabla_{L}\big[r\underline{\alpha}(F)\big](u,\underline{u},\omega)\right|^{2}\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\mathrm{d}u\bigg|^{\frac{1}{2}}\!\mathrm{d}\underline{u}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\lesssim\frac{1}{\langle\underline{u}_{1}\rangle^{\frac{1}{4}}}\bigg|\int_{\underline{u}=\underline{u}_{1}}^{+\infty}\int_{|u|\leq U}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\frac{\langle\underline{u}\rangle^{\frac{3}{2}}}{r^{2}}\left|r\nabla_{L}\big[r\underline{\alpha}(F)\big](u,\underline{u},\omega)\right|^{2}\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}\underline{u}\bigg|^{\frac{1}{2}}.

The goal now consists in bounding the right hand side by
\langle\underline{u}_{1}\rangle^{-\frac{3}{8}}
. For this, recall from Corollary
3.12
that

\left|\nabla_{L}\big(r\underline{\alpha}(F)\big)\right|\lesssim r|J|+\sup_{|\kappa|\leq 1}\left|\rho(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\kappa}}F)\right|+\left|\sigma(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\kappa}}F)\right|.

(67)

Consider
|\kappa|\leq 1
and remark that we have
2r=\underline{u}-u\gtrsim\langle\underline{u}\rangle
on the domain of integration since
\underline{u}\geq 2U
and
|u|\leq U
. We then have, using the uniform boundedness of the flux of
\rho(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\kappa}}F)
and
\sigma(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\kappa}}F)
on outgoing null cones,

\int_{\underline{u}=\underline{u}_{1}}^{+\infty}\int_{|u|\leq U}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\frac{\langle\underline{u}\rangle^{\frac{3}{2}}}{r^{2}}\left|\big(\left|r\rho(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\kappa}}F)\right|+\left|r\sigma(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\kappa}}F)\right|\big)\right|^{2}\!(u,\underline{u},\omega)\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\mathrm{d}u\,\mathrm{d}\underline{u}\lesssim\int_{|u|\leq U}\frac{D\,\mathrm{d}u}{\langle\underline{u}_{1}\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim\frac{UD}{\langle\underline{u}_{1}\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

As the domaine of integration is included in
\{t+|x|\geq\underline{u}_{1}\}
and
r^{2}\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\mathrm{d}u\,\mathrm{d}\underline{u}=2\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t
, we have, in view of the assumptions on the source term,

\displaystyle\int_{\underline{u}=\underline{u}_{1}}^{+\infty}\int_{|u|\leq U}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\frac{\langle\underline{u}\rangle^{\frac{3}{2}}}{r^{2}}\left|r^{2}J\right|^{2}\!(u,\underline{u},\omega)\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\mathrm{d}u\,\mathrm{d}\underline{u}\leq\frac{2}{\langle\underline{u}_{1}\rangle^{\frac{1}{4}}}\int_{t=\underline{u}_{1}}^{+\infty}\frac{1}{\langle t\rangle^{\frac{5}{4}}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\langle t+|x|\rangle^{3}|J|^{2}(t,x)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\lesssim\frac{D}{\langle\underline{u}_{1}\rangle^{\frac{1}{4}}}.

The previous estimates imply that
\underline{u}\mapsto\big(r\underline{\alpha}(F)\big)(\cdot,\underline{u},\cdot)
is Cauchy, and then converges, in
L^{2}([-U,U]\times\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega})
. Furthermore, the rate of convergence is bounded below by
UD\,\langle\underline{u}\rangle^{-3/8}
.

We proved that for any
Z\in\mathbb{K}
,
\underline{u}\mapsto[r\underline{\alpha}(\mathcal{L}_{Z}F)](\cdot,\underline{u},\cdot)
is uniformly bounded in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{u}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega})
. It implies the existence of a strictly increasing and unbounded sequence
(\underline{u}_{n})_{n\geq 0}
as well as
1
-forms tangential to the
2
-spheres
\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}_{\,Z}
, such that

\forall\,Z\in\mathbb{K},\qquad\qquad\big(r\underline{\alpha}(\mathcal{L}_{Z}F)\big)(\cdot,\underline{u}_{n},\cdot)\rightharpoonup\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}_{\,Z}\qquad\text{as $n\to+\infty$, in $L^{2}\big(\mathbb{R}_{u}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}\big)$}.

(68)

We now prove that the quantities
\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}_{\,Z}
are uniquely defined, that is that they do depend on the sequence
(\underline{u}_{n})_{n\geq 0}
, by relating them to
\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}
. It will imply the last part of the statement of the Proposition. For this, we fix
U\geq 1
and we will write
\psi\rightharpoonup\psi^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}
if we have the convergence

\psi(\cdot,\underline{u},\cdot)\rightharpoonup\psi^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\qquad\text{as $\underline{u}\to+\infty$, in $\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\big(\,]-U,U\,[\times\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}\big)$}.

If the convergence only holds for a striclty increasing and divergent sequence of advanced times
\mathbf{s}=(\underline{u}_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
, we will write
\psi\rightharpoonup_{\mathbf{s}}\psi^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}
. Recall the quantities
\omega_{j}^{A}:=\langle e_{A},\partial_{x^{j}}\rangle
, which only depend on the variable
\omega\in\mathbb{S}^{2}
. Let us prove, for any
1\leq j\leq 3
and
B\in\{\theta,\varphi\}
,

r\underline{\alpha}(F)\rightharpoonup\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}},\quad\frac{r}{2}\underline{L}\big(\underline{\alpha}(F)_{e_{B}}\big)\rightharpoonup\partial_{u}\!\left(\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}_{e_{B}}\right),\quad r^{2}\omega_{j}^{A}e_{A}\big(\underline{\alpha}(F)_{e_{B}}\big)\rightharpoonup\omega_{j}^{A}e_{A}\!\left(\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}_{e_{B}}\right)

(69)

and that there exists
\mathbf{s}=(\underline{u}_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
, depending possibly on
U
, such that, for any
|\gamma|\leq 1
and
Z\in\mathbb{K}
,

r\underline{\alpha}(\mathcal{L}_{Z}F)\rightharpoonup_{\mathbf{s}}\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}_{\,Z},\quad r^{2}L\big(\underline{\alpha}(F)_{e_{B}}\big)\rightharpoonup_{\mathbf{s}}-\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}_{e_{B}},\quad r\rho(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F)\rightharpoonup_{\mathbf{s}}0,\quad r\sigma(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F)\rightharpoonup_{\mathbf{s}}0,\quad|F|\rightharpoonup_{\mathbf{s}}0.

(70)

Then, the result would ensue from
[
8
, Proposition
B.2
]
. In order to prove these weak convergences and conclude the proof, we will need the following three estimates. As
r\gtrsim\underline{u}\gtrsim\langle t\rangle
for
\underline{u}\geq 2U
and
|u|\leq U
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\underline{u}=2U}^{+\infty}\int_{|u|\leq U}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\left|F\right|^{2}(u,\underline{u},\omega)\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}\underline{u}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\underline{u}=2U}^{+\infty}\int_{|u|\leq U}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\frac{1}{|\underline{u}|^{2}}|F|^{2}(u,\underline{u},\omega)r^{2}\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}\underline{u},

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{t=2U}^{+\infty}\frac{1}{\langle t\rangle^{2}}\|F(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}\mathrm{d}t\lesssim\int_{t=0}^{+\infty}\frac{D\,\mathrm{d}t}{\langle t\rangle^{2}}\lesssim D.

(71)

Using the uniform boundedness of
\rho(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F)
and
\sigma(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F)
on outgoing null cones
t-r=u
, we have

\forall\,U\geq 1,\qquad\int_{\underline{u}=U}^{+\infty}\int_{|u|\leq U}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\left[\big|r\rho(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F)\big|^{2}+\big|r\sigma(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F)\big|^{2}\right](u,\underline{u},\omega)\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\,\mathrm{d}u\,\mathrm{d}\underline{u}\lesssim UD.

(72)

Finally, for all
t\in\mathbb{R}_{+},\,r>0
,

\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}|J|^{2}(t,r\omega)\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\leq 2\int_{s=r}^{+\infty}\!\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}|J||\nabla_{\partial_{r}}J|(t,r\omega)\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\mathrm{d}s\lesssim\!\frac{1}{r^{2}\langle t+r\rangle^{3}}\sup_{|\gamma|\leq 1}\int_{|x|\geq r}\langle t+|x|\rangle^{3}|J^{\gamma}|^{2}(t,x)\mathrm{d}x,

since
\partial_{x^{i}}J=J^{\kappa_{i}}
, if
\kappa_{i}
is such that
Z^{\kappa_{i}}=\partial_{x^{i}}
. Then, using the assumption on the source terms
J^{\gamma}
, we get for all
\underline{u}\geq 0
,

\int_{|u|\leq U}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}r^{4}|J|^{2}(u,\underline{u},\omega)\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\mathrm{d}u\lesssim\frac{UD}{\langle\underline{u}\rangle},\qquad r^{2}|J|(\cdot,\underline{u},\cdot)\xrightharpoonup[\underline{u}\to+\infty]{}0\quad\text{in $\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}_{u}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega})$}.

(73)

We now prove the weak convergences (
69
)–(
70
) as follows.

•

We already proved
r\underline{\alpha}(F)\rightharpoonup\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}
. It is in fact a strong convergence in
L^{2}([-U,U]\times\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega})
.

•

Note that
r\underline{L}=\underline{L}r+1
and
\underline{L}=2\partial_{u}
. As
r\underline{\alpha}(F)\rightharpoonup\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}
, we have
\underline{\alpha}(F)\rightharpoonup 0
since
2r=\underline{u}-u\sim\underline{u}
on compact subsets of
\mathbb{R}_{u}
, so that
r\underline{L}\big(\underline{\alpha}(F)_{e_{B}}\big)\rightharpoonup 2\partial_{u}(\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}_{e_{B}})
.

•

Consider
(t,r)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}
,
\psi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}_{u}\times\mathbb{S}^{2})
and denote by
\vec{v}_{i}
the vector field
\omega_{i}^{e_{A}}e_{A}
, which is the projection on the
2
-spheres of
\partial_{x^{i}}
. Since
(re_{\theta},re_{\varphi})=(\partial_{\theta},\partial_{\varphi}/\sin(\theta))
, we have

\displaystyle\omega_{i}^{A}r^{2}\big(e_{A}(\underline{\alpha}(F)_{e_{B}})\big)(t,r\omega)\psi(u,\omega)

\displaystyle=r\psi(u,\omega)\vec{v}_{i}\cdot\not{\nabla}\big(\underline{\alpha}(F)_{e_{B}}(t,r\omega)\big),

\displaystyle\omega_{i}^{A}e_{A}(\underline{\alpha}(F)_{e_{B}}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\big)(u,\omega)\psi(u,\omega)

\displaystyle=\psi(u,\omega)\vec{v}_{i}\cdot\not{\nabla}\big(\underline{\alpha}(F)_{e_{B}}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\big)(u,\omega).

As
r\underline{\alpha}(F)\rightharpoonup\underline{\alpha}(F)^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}
,
r^{2}\omega_{j}^{A}e_{A}\big(\underline{\alpha}(F)_{e_{B}}\big)\rightharpoonup\omega_{j}^{A}e_{A}\big(\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}_{e_{B}}\big)
ensues from the divergence theorem on
\mathbb{S}^{2}
.

•

From (
71
)–(
72
), we get that there exists
\mathbf{s}^{\prime}=(\underline{u}^{\prime}_{n})_{n\geq 0}
such that
\underline{u}^{\prime}_{n}\to+\infty
as
n\to+\infty
and

\big\|F(\cdot,\underline{u}^{\prime}_{n},\cdot)\big\|^{2}_{L^{2}([-U,U]\times\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega})}+\big\|r\rho(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F)(\cdot,\underline{u}^{\prime}_{n},\cdot)\big\|^{2}_{L^{2}([-U,U]\times\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega})}+\big\|r\sigma(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F)(\cdot,\underline{u}^{\prime}_{n},\cdot)\big\|^{2}_{L^{2}([-U,U]\times\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega})}\lesssim\frac{D}{\langle\underline{u}^{\prime}_{n}\rangle},

for any
|\gamma|\leq 1
. We then deduce
F\rightharpoonup_{\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}0
,
r\rho(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F)\rightharpoonup_{\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}0
and
r\rho(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F)\rightharpoonup_{\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}0
.

•

Remark first that
r^{2}L(\underline{\alpha}(F)_{e_{B}})=rL(r\underline{\alpha}(F)_{e_{B}})-r\underline{\alpha}(F)_{e_{B}}
and
r\underline{\alpha}(F)_{e_{B}}\rightharpoonup\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}
. Then, using (
67
) and
\nabla_{L}e_{B}=0
, we get

\left|rL\big(r\underline{\alpha}(F)_{e_{B}}\big)\right|\lesssim r^{2}|J|+\sum_{|\gamma|\leq 1}r|\rho(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F)|+r|\sigma(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F)|\rightharpoonup_{\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}0,

where, in the last step, we used (
73
) and the weak convergences that we just proved.

•

Finally, for any
\mathbb{Z}\in\mathbb{K}
,
n\mapsto\big(r\underline{\alpha}(\mathcal{L}_{Z}F)\big)(\cdot,\underline{u}_{n}^{\prime},\cdot)
is bounded in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{u}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega})
. Consequently, there exists a subsequence
\mathbf{s}
of
\mathbf{s}^{\prime}=(\underline{u}^{\prime}_{n})_{n\geq 0}
and
\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}_{\,Z}
such that
r\underline{\alpha}(\mathcal{L}_{Z}F)\rightharpoonup_{\mathbf{s}}\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}_{\,Z}
.

Motivated by the previous result and since our approach is based on vector field methods, we introduce particular weighted derivatives for radiation fields.

Definition 4.4
.

Let
\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}
be a
1
-form, defined on
\mathbb{R}_{u}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}
and tangential to the
2
-spheres. For any multi-index
\gamma
, we define recursively, at least in a weak sense, the
2
-forms
\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}_{\,\gamma}
as follows.

•

\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}_{\,\gamma}:=\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}
if
|\gamma|=0
.

•

If
Z^{\gamma}=\partial_{t}Z^{\kappa}
, then
\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}_{\,\gamma}:=\nabla_{u}\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}_{\,\kappa}
.

•

If
Z^{\gamma}=\partial_{x^{i}}Z^{\kappa}
with
1\leq i\leq 3
, then
\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}_{\,\gamma}:=-\omega_{i}\nabla_{u}\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}_{\,\kappa}
.

•

If
Z^{\gamma}=SZ^{\kappa}
, then
\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}_{\,\gamma}:=u\nabla_{u}\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}_{\,\kappa}+\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}_{\,\kappa}
.

•

If
Z^{\gamma}=\Omega_{0i}Z^{\kappa}
with
1\leq i\leq 3
, then
\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}_{\,\gamma}:=-\omega_{i}u\nabla_{u}\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}_{\,\kappa}-2\omega_{i}\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}_{\,\kappa}+\omega^{e_{A}}_{i}\not{\nabla}_{e_{A}}\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}_{\,\kappa}
.

•

If
Z^{\gamma}=\Omega_{jk}Z^{\kappa}
with
1\leq j<k\leq 3
, then
\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}_{\,\gamma}:=\mathcal{L}_{\Omega_{jk}}\big(\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}_{\,\kappa}\big)
.

We now establish an improved scattering result which requires stronger assumptions on the data and the source term. It shows that the conditions we will impose on the radiation fields for the backward problem are consistent. The hypotheses of the next result will be satisfied by
F-F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]
.

Proposition 4.5
.

Let
F
be a solution to the Maxwell equations (
47
), with source term
J
. Let
J^{\gamma}
be the current such that

\forall\,|\gamma|\leq 3,\qquad\nabla^{\mu}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}(F)_{\mu\nu}=J_{\nu}^{\gamma},\qquad\nabla^{\mu}{}^{*}\!\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}(F)_{\mu\nu}=0

and assume that there exist
\delta>0
and
D\geq 0
such that

\mathcal{E}^{K}_{3}[F](T)+\sup_{|\gamma|\leq 3}\,\sup_{(t,x)\in[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\,\langle t+|x|\rangle^{7+2\delta}|J^{\gamma}|^{2}(t,x)\leq D.

Then,
F
has a radiation field along future null infinity
\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}
and

\rho^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\!:(u,\omega)\mapsto-\frac{1}{2}\not{\nabla}\cdot\int_{s=-\infty}^{u}\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}(s,\omega)\mathrm{d}s,\qquad\sigma^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\!:(u,\omega)\mapsto-\frac{1}{2}\not{\nabla}\times\int_{s=-\infty}^{u}\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}(s,\omega)\mathrm{d}s

(74)

are well-defined and go to
0
as
|u|\to+\infty
. Moreover, for all
(t,x)\in[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
, we have

\displaystyle|\alpha(F)|(t,x)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\delta^{-1}\sqrt{D}}{\langle t+|x|\rangle^{\frac{5}{2}}},

(75)

\displaystyle\left|r^{2}\rho(F)(t,x)-\rho^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\!\left(t-|x|,\frac{x}{|x|}\right)\right|+\left|r^{2}\sigma(F)(t,x)-\sigma^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\!\left(t-|x|,\frac{x}{|x|}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\delta^{-1}\sqrt{D}}{\langle t+|x|\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}},

(76)

\displaystyle\left|r\underline{\alpha}(F)(t,x)-\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\!\left(t-|x|,\frac{x}{|x|}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\delta^{-1}\sqrt{D}}{\langle t+|x|\rangle\,\langle t-|x|\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}},

(77)

Proof.

The energy inequality of Proposition
3.6
, applied to
\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F
for any
|\gamma|\leq 3
, and the assumptions on the data as well as
J
give

\sup_{t\geq T}\mathcal{E}^{K}_{3}\big[F\big](t)\leq 2\mathcal{E}^{K}_{3}\big[F\big](T)+8\sup_{|\gamma|\leq 3}\bigg|\int_{\tau=T}^{+\infty}\frac{1}{\langle\tau\rangle^{1+\delta}}\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\langle\tau+|x|\rangle^{4+2\delta}\big|J^{\gamma}(\tau,x)\big|^{2}\mathrm{d}x\bigg|^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathrm{d}\tau\bigg|^{2}\leq 20\delta^{-2}D.

Now, applying Proposition
3.13
as well as (
62
), we get, for any
|\gamma|\leq 1
and for all
(t,x)\in[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
,

\displaystyle|\underline{\alpha}(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F)|(t,x)\lesssim\delta^{-1}D^{\frac{1}{2}}\,\langle t+|x|\rangle^{-1}\langle t-|x|\rangle^{-\frac{3}{2}},\qquad

\displaystyle|\alpha(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F)|(t,x)\lesssim\delta^{-1}D^{\frac{1}{2}}\,\langle t+|x|\rangle^{-\frac{5}{2}},

(78)

\displaystyle|\rho(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F)|(t,x)\lesssim\delta^{-1}D^{\frac{1}{2}}\,\langle t+|x|\rangle^{-2}\langle t-|x|\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}},\qquad

\displaystyle|\sigma(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F)|(t,x)\lesssim\delta^{-1}D^{\frac{1}{2}}\,\langle t+|x|\rangle^{-2}\langle t-|x|\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

(79)

Applying Corollary
3.12
, we then obtain, for all
(t,x)\in[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
,

\displaystyle\left|\nabla_{L}\left(r^{2}\rho(F)\right)\right|(t,x)+\left|\nabla_{L}\left(r^{2}\sigma(F)\right)\right|(t,x)\lesssim\frac{\delta^{-1}D^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\langle t+|x|\rangle^{\frac{3}{2}}},\qquad\qquad\left|\nabla_{L}\big(r\underline{\alpha}(F)\big)\right|(t,x)\lesssim\frac{\delta^{-1}D^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\langle t+|x|\rangle^{2}\,\langle t-|x|\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

We use now the null coordinates
u=t-|x|
,
\underline{u}=t+|x|
,
\omega=x/|x|
and we recall that
L=2\partial_{\underline{u}}
. Fix
(u,\omega)\in\mathbb{R}_{u}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}
and integrate the previous estimates between
|u|\leq\underline{u}\leq\underline{z}
. We get

\displaystyle\big|r^{2}\rho(F)(u,\underline{u},\omega)-r^{2}\rho(F)(u,\underline{z},\omega)\big|+\big|r^{2}\sigma(F)(u,\underline{u},\omega)-r^{2}\sigma(F)(u,\underline{z},\omega)\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta^{-1}D^{\frac{1}{2}}\,\langle\underline{u}\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}},

(80)

\displaystyle\big|r\underline{\alpha}(F)(u,\underline{u},\omega)-r\underline{\alpha}(F)(u,\underline{z},\omega)\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta^{-1}D^{\frac{1}{2}}\,\langle\underline{u}\rangle^{-1}\langle u\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

(81)

This implies the existence of
\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}
,
\rho^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}
and
\sigma^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}
as well the stated rate of convergences. Moreover, for fixed
(\underline{u},\omega)\in[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{S}^{2}
, we obtain by integrating the null Maxwell equations (
58
)–(
59
) between
u\geq-\underline{u}+2T
and
\underline{u}
. As
r=0
when
t+r=t-r
, we get

\bigg|r^{2}\rho(F)(u,\underline{u},\omega)-\int_{s=u}^{\underline{u}}r^{2}\not{\nabla}\cdot\underline{\alpha}(F)(s,\underline{u},\omega)\mathrm{d}s\bigg|\lesssim\frac{D^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\langle\underline{u}\rangle^{\frac{3}{2}+\delta}},\quad\;r^{2}\sigma(F)(u,\underline{u},\omega)+\int_{s=u}^{\underline{u}}r^{2}\not{\nabla}\times\underline{\alpha}(F)(s,\underline{u},\omega)\mathrm{d}s=0.

Using
re_{\theta}=\partial_{\theta}
,
re_{\varphi}=\sin^{-1}(\theta)\partial_{\varphi}
together with (
80
)–(
81
), we then deduce that the relations (
74
) between
\rho^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}
,
\sigma^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}
and
\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}
hold in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}_{u}\times\mathbb{S}^{2})
and then, as
\rho^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}
and
\sigma^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}
are continous, in
C^{0}(\mathbb{R}_{u}\times\mathbb{S}^{2})
. Furthermore, (
79
)–(
80
) imply
|\rho^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}|(u,\omega)+|\sigma^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}|(u,\omega)\lesssim\langle u\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}}
, so that these two functions go to
0
as
|u|\to+\infty
.

4.2 Backward evolution from scattering data at future null infinity for the Maxwell equations

This section is devoted to an important step of the proof of Theorem
1.8
. More precisely, we will study the inverse problems of the one addresed by Proposition
4.5
. For this, we start by introducing the functional space of the radiation fields that we will consider here.

Definition 4.6
.

Let
\mathcal{S}^{K}_{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\!
be the set of the
1
-forms
\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\!
on
\mathbb{R}_{u}\!\times\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}
which are tangential to the
2
-spheres and such that the functions

\rho^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\!:(u,\omega)\mapsto-\frac{1}{2}\not{\nabla}\cdot\int_{s=-\infty}^{u}\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}(s,\omega)\mathrm{d}s,\qquad\sigma^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\!:(u,\omega)\mapsto-\frac{1}{2}\not{\nabla}\times\int_{s=-\infty}^{u}\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}(s,\omega)\mathrm{d}s

(82)

are well defined and, for almost every
\omega\in\mathbb{S}^{2}
,

\not{\nabla}\cdot\int_{\mathbb{R}_{u}}\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}(u,\omega)\mathrm{d}u=\not{\nabla}\times\int_{\mathbb{R}_{u}}\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}(u,\omega)\mathrm{d}u=0.

(83)

If
\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{I}^{+}}^{K}
is sufficiently regular, let for any
N\in\mathbb{N}
and all
(u,\omega)\in\mathbb{R}_{u}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}
the pointwise norm

\big\|\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\!\big\|_{N}(u,\omega):=\sup_{\kappa_{u}+|\kappa_{a}|\leq N}\langle u\rangle^{\kappa_{u}+1}\left|\nabla_{\partial_{u}}^{\kappa_{u}}\!\not{\nabla}^{\kappa_{a}}\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\right|\!(u,\omega)+\langle u\rangle^{\kappa_{u}}\left|\nabla_{\partial_{u}}^{\kappa_{u}}\not{\nabla}^{\kappa_{a}}\rho^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\right|\!(u,\omega)+\langle u\rangle^{\kappa_{u}}\!\left|\nabla_{\partial_{u}}^{\kappa_{u}}\not{\nabla}^{\kappa_{a}}\sigma^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\right|\!(u,\omega)

and, for
a\geq 0
, the higher order energy norm

\overline{\mathcal{E}}^{\,a}_{N}\big[\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\big]:=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{u}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\langle u\rangle^{2a}\big\|\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\big\|^{2}_{N}(u,\omega)\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\mathrm{d}u.

In order to prove a scattering result with assumptions allowing for the gain of regularity on the source term discussed in Section
2.5.1
, we need to associate to any locally integrable current
J
a sequence of electromagnetic fields. This sequence will be used in order to construct the solution to the Maxwell equations with a prescribed source term and asymptotic data in Proposition
4.9
.

Definition 4.7
.

Let
J
be a four-current density defined on
[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
. Let, for any integer
n\geq T
,
S(J,n)
be the unique solution to

\nabla^{\mu}S(J,n)_{\mu\nu}=\chi\big(t/n\big)J_{\nu},\qquad\nabla^{\mu}{}^{*}\!S(J,n)_{\mu\nu}=0,\qquad\qquad S(J,n)(n,\cdot)=0,

where
\chi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R},[0,1])
is a cutoff function such that
\chi(s)=1
for all
s\leq 1/4
and
\chi(s)=0
for all
s\geq 1/2
.

Remark 4.8
.

We introduced
\chi
to ensure that the derivatives of
S(J,n)
vanish as well for
t=n
.

We now state the main result of this section. A higher order version holds but we do not prove it in order to avoid technicalities and since it is not required for the proof of Theorem
1.8
.

Proposition 4.9
.

Let
a\geq 0
,
\delta>0
,
\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{I}^{+}}^{K}
and
J
be a four-current density defined. Assume

\overline{\mathcal{E}}^{\,a+\delta}_{1}\big[\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\big]<+\infty

and that there exists a constant
B_{\mathrm{source}}>0
such that, for any
n\geq T
, the field
S(J,n)
generated by the source term
J
verifies

\forall\,t\in[T,+\infty[,\qquad\mathcal{E}^{K,a}\big[S(J,n)\big](t)\lesssim\langle t\rangle^{-\delta}B_{\mathrm{source}}.

(84)

Then, there exists a unique solution
F
to the Maxwell equations

\nabla^{\mu}F_{\mu\nu}=J,\qquad\qquad\nabla^{\mu}{}^{*}\!F_{\mu\nu}=0,

admitting
\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}
as radiation field along future null infinity
\mathcal{I}^{+}
. Moreover,

\exists\,C_{\delta}>0,\quad\forall\,t\geq T,\qquad\mathcal{E}^{K,a}[F](t)\leq C_{\delta}\,\overline{\mathcal{E}}^{\,a+\delta}_{1}\big[\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\big]+B_{\mathrm{source}}.

Remark 4.10
.

In the context of Theorem
1.8
, if
N
is the order of regularity of the solution, the construction of
\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}(F-F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}])
, with
|\gamma|\leq N-1
, will rely on this result, applied for
a=0
. We will suitably estimate the top order derivatives
\nabla_{t,x}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}(F-F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}])
,
|\gamma|=N-1
, by appealing to Proposition
4.9
for
a=1
.

4.2.1 Preparatories

Before starting the proof of Proposition
4.9
, we give a sufficient condition on
J
for (
84
) to hold. In the course of Theorem
1.8
, we will use it for all but the top order derivatives of the source term
J(f)-J^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]
. In view of Remark
4.10
, we focus here on the case
a=0
.

Proposition 4.11
.

Let
\delta>0
,
B>0
and
J
be a four-current defined on
[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
satisfying

\forall\,t\in[T,+\infty[,\qquad\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\langle t+|x|\rangle^{4+\delta}\big|J\big|^{2}(t,x)\mathrm{d}x\leq B.

Then, there exists an absolute constant
D>0
such that
S(J,n)
verifies (
84
) for all
n\geq T
and for
a=0
as well as
B_{\mathrm{source}}=D\delta^{-1}B
.

Proof.

Let
n\geq T
and recall that
S(J,n)=0
on
[n,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
. Furthermore, the energy inequality of Proposition
3.7
provides

\forall\,t\in[T,n],\qquad\mathcal{E}^{K}\big[S(J,n)\big](t)\leq 8\int_{\tau=t}^{n}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\big|K^{\mu}S(J,n)_{\mu\nu}J^{\nu}\big|(\tau,x)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}\tau.

Expanding
K^{\mu}S(J,n)_{\mu\nu}
according to the null frame
(\underline{L},L,e_{\theta},e_{\varphi})
, we get, as
2K=\langle t+r\rangle^{2}L+\langle t-r\rangle^{2}\underline{L}
,

\displaystyle\big|K^{\mu}S(J,n)_{\mu\nu}\big|\leq\,\langle t+r\rangle^{2}\big|S(J,n)_{L\nu}\big|+\langle t-r\rangle^{2}\big|S(J,n)_{\underline{L}\nu}\big|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad\lesssim\langle t+r\rangle^{2}\big(\big|\alpha\big(S(J,n)\big)\big|+\big|\rho\big(S(J,n)\big)\big|\big)+\langle t-r\rangle^{2}\big(\big|\underline{\alpha}\big(S(J,n)\big)\big|+\big|\rho\big(S(J,n)\big)\big|\big).

The Cauchy-Schwarz inequality in
x
then provides, for all
T\leq t\leq n
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{K}\big[S(J,n)\big](t)

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\tau=t}^{n}\big|\mathcal{E}^{K}\big[S(J,n)\big](\tau)\big|^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\langle t+|x|\rangle^{2}\big|J\big|^{2}(\tau,x)\mathrm{d}x\bigg|^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\lesssim B^{\frac{1}{2}}\sup_{t\leq\tau\leq n}\big|\mathcal{E}^{K}\big[S(J,n)\big](\tau)\big|^{\frac{1}{2}}\int_{\tau=t}^{n}\frac{\mathrm{d}\tau}{\langle\tau\rangle^{1+\frac{\delta}{2}}}\lesssim\delta^{-1}B^{\frac{1}{2}}\langle t\rangle^{-\frac{\delta}{2}}\sup_{t\leq\tau\leq n}\big|\mathcal{E}^{K}\big[S(J,n)\big](\tau)\big|^{\frac{1}{2}},

which implies the result.

4.2.2 Set up of the proof of Proposition
4.9

We fix
a\geq 0
,
\delta>0
,
\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}
as well as
J
satisfying the assumptions of Proposition
4.9
. We consider further
\rho^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}
and
\sigma^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}
, given by (
83
). We will construct
F
as the limit of a sequence
(F^{n})
of approximate solutions to the Maxwell equations with source term
J
. All these
2
-forms will have the same leading order term
F^{\mathrm{lead}}
, defined as

\displaystyle\alpha\big(F^{\mathrm{lead}}\big)(t,r\omega)

\displaystyle=0,\qquad\qquad

\displaystyle\underline{\alpha}\big(F^{\mathrm{lead}}\big)(t,r\omega)=\frac{1}{r}\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}(t-r,\omega)\chi\big(\langle t-r\rangle/r\big),

\displaystyle\rho\big(F^{\mathrm{lead}}\big)(t,r\omega)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{r^{2}}\rho^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}(t-r,\omega)\chi\big(\langle t-r\rangle/r),\qquad\qquad

\displaystyle\sigma\big(F^{\mathrm{lead}}\big)(t,r\omega)=\frac{1}{r^{2}}\sigma^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}(t-r,\omega)\chi\big(\langle t-r\rangle/r\big),

where we recall that
\chi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R},[0,1])
verifies
\chi(s)=1
for
s\leq 1/4
and
\chi(s)=0
for
s\geq 1/2
.

4.2.3 Study of the approximate solution

We start by estimating how close
F^{\mathrm{lead}}
is to be a solution to the vacuum Maxwell equations.

Lemma 4.12
.

F^{\mathrm{lead}}
verifies the Maxwell equations with a magnetic current (
Mmc
), with source terms
J^{\mathrm{err}}
and
\widetilde{J}^{\mathrm{err}}
satisfying, for all
(t,r\omega)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
,

\displaystyle\big|J^{\mathrm{err}}_{\underline{L}}\big|(t,r\omega)+\big|\widetilde{J}^{\mathrm{err}}_{\underline{L}}\big|(t,r\omega)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\langle t+r\rangle^{3}}\big\|\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\big\|_{1}(t-r,\omega)\,\mathds{1}_{\langle t-r\rangle\leq\frac{r}{2}},

\displaystyle\sup_{A\in\{\theta,\varphi\}}\big|J^{\mathrm{err}}_{e_{A}}\big|(t,r\omega)+\big|\widetilde{J}^{\mathrm{err}}_{e_{A}}\big|(t,r\omega)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\langle t+r\rangle^{3}}\big\|\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\big\|_{1}(t-r,\omega)\,\mathds{1}_{\langle t-r\rangle\leq\frac{r}{2}},

\displaystyle\big|J^{\mathrm{err}}_{L}\big|(t,r\omega)+\big|\widetilde{J}^{\mathrm{err}}_{L}\big|(t,r\omega)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\langle t-r\rangle}{\langle t+r\rangle^{4}}\big\|\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\big\|_{1}(t-r,\omega)\,\mathds{1}_{\langle t-r\rangle\leq\frac{r}{2}}.

Furthermore, the null components of
F^{\mathrm{lead}}
verify
\alpha(F^{\mathrm{lead}})=0
and

\displaystyle\big|\underline{\alpha}\big(F^{\mathrm{lead}}\big)\big|(t,r\omega)

\displaystyle\lesssim\langle t+r\rangle^{-1}\langle t-r\rangle^{-1}\big\|\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\big\|_{0}(t-r,\omega)\,\mathds{1}_{\langle t-r\rangle\leq\frac{r}{2}},

\displaystyle\big|\rho\big(F^{\mathrm{lead}}\big)\big|(t,r\omega)+\big|\sigma\big(F^{\mathrm{lead}}\big)\big|(t,r\omega)

\displaystyle\lesssim\langle t+r\rangle^{-2}\big\|\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\big\|_{0}(t-r,\omega)\,\mathds{1}_{\langle t-r\rangle\leq\frac{r}{2}}.

Proof.

We first determine
J^{\mathrm{err}}
and
\widetilde{J}^{\mathrm{err}}
using the definition of
F^{\mathrm{lead}}
and the equations (
Mmc
) expressed in the null frame. First, since
e_{\theta}=r^{-1}\partial_{\theta}
and
e_{\varphi}=r^{-1}\sin^{-1}(\theta)\partial_{\varphi}
, we get from (
56
)–(
57
) that, for any
A\in\{\theta,\varphi\}
,

\displaystyle J_{e_{A}}^{\mathrm{err}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{r^{3}}\varepsilon^{AB}\not{\nabla}_{e_{B}}\sigma^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\!(t-r,\omega)\chi\big(\langle t-r\rangle/r\big)-\frac{\langle t-r\rangle}{2r^{3}}\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}_{e_{A}}(t-r,\omega)\chi^{\prime}\big(\langle t-r\rangle/r\big),

\displaystyle\widetilde{J}_{e_{A}}^{\mathrm{err}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{r^{3}}\varepsilon^{AB}\not{\nabla}_{e_{B}}\rho^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\!(t-r,\omega)\chi\big(\langle t-r\rangle/r\big)-\frac{\langle t-r\rangle}{2r^{3}}\varepsilon^{AB}\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}_{e_{B}}(t-r,\omega)\chi^{\prime}\big(\langle t-r\rangle/r\big).

Next, as
\nabla_{\underline{L}}\rho^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}+\not{\nabla}\cdot\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}=\nabla_{\underline{L}}\sigma^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}+\not{\nabla}\times\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}=0
holds, we have from (
58
)–(
59
)

\displaystyle J_{\underline{L}}^{\mathrm{err}}=\frac{(t-r)r+\langle t-r\rangle^{2}}{\langle t-r\rangle r^{4}}\rho^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\!(t-r,\omega)\chi^{\prime}\big(\langle t-r\rangle/r\big),\qquad\widetilde{J}_{\underline{L}}^{\mathrm{err}}=\frac{(t-r)r+\langle t-r\rangle^{2}}{\langle t-r\rangle r^{4}}\sigma^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\!(t-r,\omega)\chi^{\prime}\big(\langle t-r\rangle/r\big).

Finally, we obtain from (
60
)–(
61
) that

J_{L}^{\mathrm{err}}=\frac{\langle t-r\rangle}{r^{4}}\rho^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\!(t-r,\omega)\chi^{\prime}\big(\langle t-r\rangle/r\big),\qquad\widetilde{J}_{L}^{\mathrm{err}}=\frac{\langle t-r\rangle}{r^{4}}\sigma^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\!(t-r,\omega)\chi^{\prime}\big(\langle t-r\rangle/r\big).

Recall further the definition of
F^{\mathrm{lead}}
. We then deduce, as
\langle t-r\rangle\leq r/2
and then
\langle t+r\rangle\lesssim r
on the support of
\chi
, that all the stated estimates hold.

We now bound the energy norm of
F^{\mathrm{lead}}
.

Corollary 4.13
.

For all
t\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
, we have
\mathcal{E}^{K,a}\big[F^{\mathrm{lead}}\big](t)\lesssim\overline{\mathcal{E}}^{\,a}_{0}\big[\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\big]
.

Proof.

In view of the support of the cutoff function
\chi
,
F^{\mathrm{lead}}(t,r\omega)
vanishes for
0\leq t\leq 1
or
r\leq 2
. According to Lemma
4.12
, we have

\forall\,t\in\mathbb{R}_{+},\qquad\mathcal{E}^{K,a}\big[F^{\mathrm{lead}}\big](t)\lesssim\int_{r=0}^{+\infty}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\langle t-r\rangle^{2a}\,\big\|\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\big\|^{2}_{0}(t-r,\omega)\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\frac{r^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r}{\langle t+r\rangle^{2}}\leq\overline{\mathcal{E}}^{\,a}_{0}\big[\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\big].

4.2.4 Sequence of approximate solutions

Recall from Definition
4.7
the field
S(J,n)
and consider, for any time
n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
such that
n\geq T
, the
2
-forms
F^{n}
and
R^{n}
defined as

F^{n}=F^{\mathrm{lead}}+R^{n}+S(J,n),\qquad\qquad\nabla^{\mu}R^{n}_{\mu\nu}=-\chi(t/n)J_{\nu}^{\mathrm{err}},\quad\nabla^{\mu}{}^{*}\!R^{n}=-\chi(t/n)\widetilde{J}_{\nu}^{\mathrm{err}},\quad R^{n}(n,\cdot)=0.

Since
\chi(s)=0
for all
s\geq 1/2
, the derivatives of
R^{n}
vanish as well at
t=n
. Furthermore, as
\chi(s)=1
for all
s\leq 1/4
,
F^{n}
is a solution to the Maxwell equations with source term
J
on
[0,n/4]
. Our goal now is to prove that
F^{n}
converges to
F
, as
n\to+\infty
, with
F
satisfying the conclusion of Proposition
4.9
. We start by a result which will allow us to estimate the energy of
F^{n}
and to prove that it is a Cauchy sequence.

Lemma 4.14
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
,
G
be a sufficiently regular
2
-form and
D
be either
J^{\mathrm{err}}
or
\widetilde{J}^{\mathrm{err}}
. Then, for all
0\leq t\leq n
,

\int_{\tau=t}^{n}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\!|\max(\tau-|x|,1)|^{2a}\!\left|K^{\mu}G_{\mu\nu}D^{\nu}\right|\!(\tau,x)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}\tau\!\lesssim\!\frac{\delta^{-1}}{\langle t\rangle^{\frac{\delta}{2}}}\left|\mathcal{E}^{a+\delta}_{1}\big[\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\big]\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}\!\left|\sup_{t\leq\tau\leq n}\!\mathcal{E}^{K,a}[G](\tau)+\sup_{u\in\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{F}^{K,a}[G]_{t}^{n}(u)\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}\!.

Proof.

As
2K=\langle t+r\rangle^{2}L+\langle t-r\rangle^{2}\underline{L}
, there holds

\displaystyle\hskip-31.29802pt\left|K^{\mu}G_{\mu\nu}D^{\nu}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|2\langle t-r\rangle^{2}\rho(G)D^{L}-2\langle t+r\rangle^{2}\rho(G)D^{\underline{L}}-\langle t-r\rangle^{2}\underline{\alpha}(G)_{e_{A}}D^{e_{A}}-\langle t+r\rangle^{2}\alpha(G)_{e_{A}}D^{e_{A}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\langle t-r\rangle^{2}|\rho(G)||D_{\underline{L}}|+\langle t+r\rangle^{2}|\rho(G)||D_{L}|+\langle t-r\rangle^{2}|\underline{\alpha}(G)||\not{D}|+\langle t+r\rangle^{2}|\alpha(G)||\not{D}|,

(85)

where
|\not{D}|^{2}:=|D_{e_{\theta}}|^{2}+|D_{e_{\varphi}}|^{2}
. According to Lemma
4.12
, we have

\displaystyle\left|K^{\mu}G_{\mu\nu}D^{\nu}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\!\left(\frac{\langle t-r\rangle^{2}}{\langle t+r\rangle^{3}}|\rho(G)|+\frac{\langle t-r\rangle}{\langle t+r\rangle^{2}}|\rho(G)|+\frac{\langle t-r\rangle^{2}}{\langle t+r\rangle^{3}}|\underline{\alpha}(G)|+\frac{1}{\langle t+r\rangle}|\alpha(G)|\right)\big\|\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\!\big\|_{1}.

We then deduce, as
|t-r|\leq t+r
,
r\leq t+r
and
t\leq t+r
, that

\displaystyle\left|K^{\mu}G_{\mu\nu}D^{\nu}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\bigg(\frac{\langle t+r\rangle}{\langle t\rangle^{1+\delta}}|\rho(G)|+\frac{\langle t-r\rangle}{\langle t\rangle^{1+\delta}}|\underline{\alpha}(G)|+\frac{\langle t+r\rangle}{\langle\tau-r\rangle^{\frac{1+\delta}{2}}\,\langle\tau+r\rangle^{\frac{1+\delta}{2}}}|\alpha(G)|\bigg)\frac{\langle t-r\rangle^{\delta}}{r}\big\|\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\big\|_{1}.

Consequently, we have

\int_{\tau=t}^{n}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}|\max(\tau-|x|,1)|^{2a}\left|K^{\mu}G_{\mu\nu}D^{\nu}\right|(\tau,x)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}\tau\lesssim\mathbf{I}_{\rho,\underline{\alpha}}(t)+\mathbf{I}_{\alpha}(t),

where, using the shorthand
w_{\tau-|x|}:=|\max(\tau-|x|,1)|^{a}
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{I}_{\rho,\underline{\alpha}}(t)

\displaystyle:=\int_{\tau=t}^{n}\frac{1}{\langle\tau\rangle^{1+\delta}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}w_{\tau-|x|}\Big(\langle\tau+r\rangle\,|\rho(G)|+\langle\tau-r\rangle\,|\underline{\alpha}(G)|\Big)\frac{\langle\tau-r\rangle^{a+\delta}}{r}\big\|\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\big\|_{1}\bigg(\tau-r,\frac{x}{|x|}\bigg)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}\tau,

\displaystyle\mathbf{I}_{\alpha}(t)

\displaystyle:=\int_{\tau=t}^{n}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}w_{\tau-|x|}\frac{\langle\tau+r\rangle}{\langle\tau-r\rangle^{\frac{1+\delta}{2}}\,\langle\tau+r\rangle^{\frac{1+\delta}{2}}}|\alpha(G)|\frac{\langle\tau-r\rangle^{a+\delta}}{r}\big\|\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\big\|_{1}\bigg(\tau-r,\frac{x}{|x|}\bigg)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}\tau.

Apply the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality in the variable
x
in order to get

\displaystyle\mathbf{I}_{\rho,\underline{\alpha}}(t)

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\tau=t}^{n}\frac{1}{\langle\tau\rangle^{1+\delta}}\left|\mathcal{E}^{K,a}[G](\tau)\int_{r=0}^{+\infty}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\langle\tau-r\rangle^{2a+2\delta}\big\|\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\big\|_{1}^{2}\!(\tau-r,\omega)\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\mathrm{d}r\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}\!\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|\sup_{t\leq\tau\leq n}\mathcal{E}^{K,a}[G](\tau)\;\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{1}^{\,a+\delta}\big[\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\big]\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}\int_{\tau=t}^{n}\frac{\mathrm{d}\tau}{\langle\tau\rangle^{1+\delta}}\leq\frac{\sqrt{2}}{\delta\,\langle t\rangle^{\delta}}\left|\,\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{1}^{\,a+\delta}\big[\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\big]\sup_{t\leq\tau\leq n}\mathcal{E}^{K,a}[G](\tau)\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Next, for
\mathbf{I}_{\alpha}(t)
, we perform the change of variables
(u,\underline{u},\omega)=(\tau-|x|,\tau+|x|,x/|x|)
and we apply the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality. We obtain

\displaystyle\mathbf{I}_{\alpha}(t)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{u=-\infty}^{n}\int_{\underline{u}=\max(2t-u,u)}^{2n-u}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\frac{w_{u}\,\langle\underline{u}\rangle}{\langle u\rangle^{\frac{1+\delta}{2}}\,\langle\underline{u}\rangle^{\frac{1+\delta}{2}}}|\alpha(G)|(u,\underline{u},\omega)\frac{\langle u\rangle^{a+\delta}}{r}\big\|\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\big\|_{1}(u,\omega)\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\mathrm{d}\underline{u}\mathrm{d}u.

Thus, by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality in
u
, we get
\mathbf{I}_{\alpha}(t)\leq|\mathbf{I}^{1}_{t}\cdot\mathbf{I}^{2}_{t}|^{1/2}
, where,

\displaystyle\mathbf{I}^{1}_{t}

\displaystyle:=\int_{u=-\infty}^{n}\int_{\underline{u}=\max(2t-u,u)}^{2n-u}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\frac{1}{\langle u\rangle^{1+\delta}}|w_{u}|^{2}\langle\underline{u}\rangle^{2}|\alpha(G)|^{2}(u,\underline{u},\omega)\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\mathrm{d}\underline{u}\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle=\sqrt{2}\int_{u=-\infty}^{n}\frac{1}{\langle u\rangle^{1+\delta}}\int_{C_{u}^{t,n}}|w_{u}|^{2}\langle\underline{u}\rangle^{2}|\alpha|^{2}\mathrm{d}\mu_{C_{u}}\mathrm{d}u\leq\,\sup_{u\in\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{F}^{K,a}[G]_{t}^{n}(u)\int_{u=-\infty}^{+\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}u}{\langle u\rangle^{1+\delta}}\leq\frac{4}{\delta}\,\sup_{u\in\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{F}^{K,a}[G]_{t}^{n}(u)

and

\displaystyle\mathbf{I}^{2}_{t}

\displaystyle:=\int_{u=-\infty}^{n}\int_{\underline{u}=\max(2t-u,u)}^{2n-u}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\frac{1}{\langle\underline{u}\rangle^{1+\delta}}\langle u\rangle^{2a+2\delta}\big\|\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\big\|_{1}^{2}(u,\omega)\,\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\mathrm{d}\underline{u}\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle=\int_{\underline{u}=t}^{+\infty}\frac{1}{\langle\underline{u}\rangle^{1+\delta}}\int_{u=2t-\underline{u}}^{\underline{u}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\langle u\rangle^{2a+2\delta}\big\|\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\big\|_{1}^{2}(u,\omega)\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}\underline{u}\leq\overline{\mathcal{E}}^{\,a+\delta}_{1}\big[\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\big]\int_{\underline{u}=t}^{+\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}\underline{u}}{\langle\underline{u}\rangle^{1+\delta}}\lesssim\frac{\overline{\mathcal{E}}^{\,a+\delta}_{1}\big[\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\big]}{\delta\,\langle t\rangle^{\delta}}.

We are now able to bound the remainder part
(R^{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
and to prove that it is a Cauchy sequence.

Proposition 4.15
.

There exists a constant
C
depending only on
\delta
such that, for every
n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
,

\forall\,t\in\mathbb{R}_{+},\qquad\;\mathcal{E}^{K,a}[R^{n}](t)+\sup_{u\in\mathbb{R}}\,\mathcal{F}^{K,a}[R^{n}]_{t}^{n}(u)\leq C\,\langle t\rangle^{-\delta}\,\overline{\mathcal{E}}^{\,a+\delta}_{1}\big[\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\big].

Moreover, for all
1\leq n_{1}\leq n_{2}
,

\sup_{0\leq t\leq n_{1}}\mathcal{E}^{K,a}[R^{n_{1}}-R^{n_{2}}](t)\leq C\,n_{1}^{-\delta}\,\overline{\mathcal{E}}^{\,a+\delta}_{1}\big[\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\big].

Proof.

Fix
n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
and apply the energy estimate of Proposition
3.7
to
R^{n}
, which vanishes as well as its derivatives at
t=n
. We get, for all
t\in[0,n]
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{K,a}[R^{n}](t)+\sup_{u\in\mathbb{R}}\,\mathcal{F}^{K,a}[R^{n}]_{t}^{n}(u)\leq 8\int_{\tau=t}^{n}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}|\max(\tau-|x|,1)|^{2a}\big|K^{\mu}R^{n}_{\mu\nu}J^{\mathrm{err},\nu}+K^{\mu}{}^{*}\!R^{n}_{\mu\nu}\widetilde{J}^{\mathrm{err},\nu}\big|(\tau,x)\mathrm{d}\tau\mathrm{d}x.

Note now that

\mathcal{E}^{K,a}\big[{}^{*}\!R^{n}\big]=\mathcal{E}^{K,a}\big[R^{n}\big],\qquad\qquad\mathcal{F}^{K,a}\big[{}^{*}\!R^{n}\big]=\mathcal{F}^{K,a}\big[R^{n}\big].

Indeed, according to
[
19
, Equation
(3.9)
]
or by direct computations, for any
2
-form
G
,

\rho({}^{*}\!G)=\sigma(G),\qquad\sigma({}^{*}\!G)=-\rho(G),\qquad\underline{\alpha}({}^{*}\!G)_{e_{A}}=\varepsilon^{AB}\underline{\alpha}(G)_{e_{B}},\qquad\alpha({}^{*}\!G)_{e_{A}}=\varepsilon^{BA}\alpha(G)_{e_{B}},\qquad A\in\{\theta,\varphi\}.

Apply Lemma
4.14
for
G=R^{n}
as well as
G={}^{*}R^{n}
. It yields

\displaystyle\forall\,t\in[T,n],\qquad\mathcal{E}^{K,a}[R^{n}](t)+\sup_{u\in\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{F}^{K,a}[R^{n}]_{t}^{n}(u)\lesssim\frac{\delta^{-1}}{\langle t\rangle^{\frac{\delta}{2}}}\Big|\overline{\mathcal{E}}^{\,a+\delta}_{1}\big[\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\big]\Big|^{\frac{1}{2}}\left|\mathcal{E}^{K,a}[R^{n}](t)+\sup_{u\in\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{F}^{K,a}[R^{n}]_{t}^{n}(u)\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}\!,

from which we obtain the stated estimate for
R^{n}
on
[0,n]
, and then on
\mathbb{R}_{+}
since
R^{n}=0
for
t\geq n
.

Consider now
1\leq n_{1}<n_{2}
and introduce
G:=R^{n_{2}}-R^{n_{1}}
, which satisfies

\nabla^{\mu}G_{\mu\nu}=\left(\chi(t/n_{1})-\chi(t/n_{2})\right)J_{\nu}^{\mathrm{err}},\qquad\nabla^{\mu}{}^{*}\!G_{\mu\nu}=\left(\chi(t/n_{1})-\chi(t/n_{2})\right)\widetilde{J}^{\mathrm{err}}_{\nu}.

As
\chi(s)=1
for all
s\leq 1/4
, the source term vanishes for
t\leq n_{1}/4
, so that, by Proposition
3.7
,

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq n_{1}}\mathcal{E}^{K,a}[G](t)+\sup_{u\in\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{F}^{K,a}[G]_{0}^{n_{1}}

\displaystyle(u)\leq\mathcal{E}^{K,a}[G](n_{1})

\displaystyle+\int_{\tau=\frac{n_{1}}{4}}^{n_{1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}|\max(\tau-|x|,1)|^{2a}\big|\,\overline{K}_{0}^{\mu}G_{\mu\nu}J^{\mathrm{err},\nu}+\overline{K}_{0}^{\mu}{}^{*}\!G_{\mu\nu}\widetilde{J}^{\mathrm{err},\nu}\big|(\tau,x)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}\tau,

As
R^{n_{1}}(n_{1},\cdot)=0
, we have
\mathcal{E}^{K,a}[G](n_{1})=\mathcal{E}^{K,a}[R^{n_{2}}](n_{1})\lesssim n_{1}^{-\delta}\,\overline{\mathcal{E}}^{\,a+\delta}_{1}[\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}]
. Moreover, by Lemma
4.14
, applied to
G
and
{}^{*}\!G
, the second term on the right hand side of the previous inequality is bounded above by

\left\langle\frac{n_{1}}{4}\right\rangle^{-\frac{\delta}{2}}\big|\overline{\mathcal{E}}^{\,a+\delta}_{1}\big[\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\big]\big|^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big|\sup_{n_{1}/4\leq\tau\leq n_{1}}\mathcal{E}^{K,a}[G](\tau)+\sup_{u\in\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{F}^{K,a}[G]_{n_{1}/4}^{n_{1}}(u)\Big|^{\frac{1}{2}}.

It implies the energy estimate for
G=R^{n_{1}}-R^{n_{2}}
.

We finally study the sequence of approximate solutions
(F^{n})_{n\geq T}
.

Proposition 4.16
.

There exists a constant
C
, depending only on
\delta
, such that the following estimates hold. For any
n\geq T
and
T\leq n_{1}<n_{2}
, we have

\sup_{T\leq t\leq n}\mathcal{E}^{K,a}[F^{n}](t)\leq C\,\overline{\mathcal{E}}^{\,a+\delta}_{1}\big[\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\big]+B_{\mathrm{source}},\qquad\quad\sup_{T\leq t\leq n_{1}}\mathcal{E}^{K,a}[F^{n_{2}}-F^{n_{1}}](t)\leq\frac{C}{n_{1}^{\delta}}\,\overline{\mathcal{E}}^{\,a+\delta}_{1}\big[\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\big]+\frac{2}{n_{1}^{\delta}}B_{\mathrm{source}}.

Proof.

As
F^{n}=F^{\mathrm{lead}}+R^{n}+S^{n}
, the two parts of the result ensue from Corollary
4.13
, the previous Proposition
4.15
and the assumptions on
S(J,n)
.

We are now able to conclude this section.

Proof
(Proof of Proposition
4.9
.).

Consider
T_{0}\geq T
and let
(\mathbb{B},\sup_{[T,T_{0}]}\mathcal{E}^{K,a}[\cdot])
be the subset of the locally integrable
2
-form
G
defined on
[T,T_{0}]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}
and such that
\sup_{T\leq\tau\leq T_{0}}\mathcal{E}^{K,a}[G](\tau)<+\infty
. Then,

•

F^{n}
is a solution to the Maxwell equations with source term
J
on
[T,T_{0}]
, for all
n\geq 4T_{0}
.

•

By Proposition
4.16
,
(F^{n})_{n\geq 4T_{0}}
is bounded by
C\,\overline{\mathcal{E}}^{\,a+\delta}_{1}\big[\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\big]+B_{\mathrm{source}}
in
\mathbb{B}
.

•

Still by Proposition
4.16
,
(F^{n})_{n\geq 4T_{0}}
is a Cauchy sequence in
\mathbb{B}
.

Consequently,
(F^{n})_{n\geq 4T_{0}}
converges in
\mathbb{B}
to a
2
-form
F^{\infty}_{T_{0}}
, defined on
[T,T_{0}]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}
and such that

\nabla^{\mu}F^{\infty}_{T_{0},\,\mu\nu}=J_{\nu},\qquad\nabla^{\mu}{}^{*}\!F^{\infty}_{T_{0},\,\mu\nu}=0.

Since for any
p\in\mathbb{N}
and any
T_{0}\geq T
,
F^{\infty}_{T_{0}+p}=F^{\infty}_{T_{0}}
on
[T,T_{0}]
, we can define a
2
-form
F
on
[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}
by
F(t,x):=F^{\infty}_{\lceil t\rceil}(t,x)
. The field
F
is then a weak solution to the Maxwell equations, with source term
J
, which satisfies, according to Proposition
4.16
,

\sup_{t\geq T}\mathcal{E}^{K,a}[F](t)\leq C\,\overline{\mathcal{E}}^{\,a+\delta}_{1}\big[\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\big]+B_{\mathrm{source}},\qquad\;\forall\,n\in\mathbb{N}^{*},\quad\sup_{t\geq T}\mathcal{E}^{K,a}[F-F^{n}](t)\leq\frac{C}{n^{\delta}}\,\overline{\mathcal{E}}^{\,a+\delta}_{1}\big[\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\big]+\frac{2}{n^{\delta}}B_{\mathrm{source}}.

Finally, as
F^{n}=F^{\mathrm{lead}}
on
[n,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
, we get that
\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}
is the radiation field of
F
in the sense of Definition
4.1
. Finally, the uniqueness of the solution
F
is given by Theorem
4.20
, stated thereafter.

4.3 Wave operators for the vacuum Maxwell equations

We apply the results of this section, for a vanishing source term and an initial time
T=0
, in order to construct wave operators for

\nabla^{\mu}F_{\mu\nu}=0,\qquad\qquad\nabla^{\mu}{}^{*}\!F_{\mu\nu}=0,

(86)

between weighted
L^{2}
Hilbert spaces. For this, we refine the results of Proposition
4.5
when the source term
J
vanishes. Recall the space of radiation fields
\mathcal{S}^{K}_{\mathcal{I}^{+}}
, introduced in Definition
4.6
.

Proposition 4.17
.

Let
F
be a solution to the vacuum Maxwell equations such that
\mathcal{E}^{K}_{3}[F](0)<+\infty
. Then,
F
has a radiation field along future null infinity
\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}
which belongs to
\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{I}^{+}}^{K}
and such that

\big\|\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\big\|^{2}_{\mathcal{S}^{K}_{\mathcal{I}^{+}}}:=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{u}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\langle u\rangle^{2}\big|\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\big|^{2}(u,\omega)+4\left(\big|\rho^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\big|^{2}+\big|\sigma^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\big|^{2}\right)(u,\omega)\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\mathrm{d}u=\mathcal{E}^{K}[F](0).

Proof.

Remark first that the conclusions of Proposition
4.5
hold. In particular,
\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}
,
\rho^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}
as well as
\sigma^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}
exist and
\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\!\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{I}^{+}}^{K}
. By a density argument, it suffices to prove the conservation law for smooth and compactly supported initial data. Assume then that
F(0,\cdot)
is supported in
\{|x|\leq R\}
for a certain
R>0
. Applying the divergence theorem to
\mathbb{T}[F]_{\mu\nu}K^{\nu}
in the region
\{(\tau,x)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\,|\,\tau+|x|\leq\underline{u}\}
, for all
\underline{u}\geq R
, we get

\displaystyle\mathbf{F}(\underline{u}):=

\displaystyle\int_{|u|\leq\underline{u}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\langle u\rangle^{2}\,|r\underline{\alpha}(F)|^{2}(u,\underline{u},\omega)+\langle\underline{u}\rangle^{2}\left[|r\rho(F)|^{2}+|r\sigma(F)|^{2}\right](u,\underline{u},\omega)\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{|x|\leq\underline{u}}\langle x\rangle^{2}\,\left[|\alpha(F)|^{2}+|\underline{\alpha}(F)|^{2}+2(|\rho(F)|^{2}+|\sigma(F)|^{2})\right]\!(0,x)\,\mathrm{d}x=\mathcal{E}^{K}[F](0).

As
\Box F_{\mu\nu}=0
, we then deduce that
F
, as well as
\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}
,
\rho^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}
and
\sigma^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}
, are supported in
\{|u|=|t-r|\leq R\}
. Hence, using that the domain of integration is compact,
\underline{u}=2r+u
and the estimates (
79
), we obtain

\mathbf{F}(\underline{u})=\int_{|u|\leq R}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}4\big[|r^{2}\rho(F)|^{2}+|r^{2}\sigma(F)|^{2}\big](u,\underline{u},\omega)+\langle u\rangle^{2}|r\underline{\alpha}(F)|^{2}(u,\underline{u},\omega)\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\mathrm{d}u+O(\underline{u}^{-1}).

It remains to use (
80
)–(
81
).

Note then that
\big(\mathcal{S}^{K}_{\mathcal{I}^{+}},\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{S}^{K}_{\mathcal{I}^{+}}}\big)
is a Hilbert space. We define now the space of the initial data.

Definition 4.18
.

Let
\mathcal{S}^{K}_{\{t=0\}}
be the set of the
2
-forms
F_{0}
, defined on
\mathbb{R}^{1+3}
, independent of the time variable
t
and such that

\|F_{0}\|_{\mathcal{S}^{K}_{\{t=0\}}}:=\mathcal{E}^{K}[F_{0}]<+\infty,

where the norm
\mathcal{E}^{K}[\cdot]
is defined in (
50
). Then,
\big(\mathcal{S}^{K}_{\{t=0\}},\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{S}^{K}_{\{t=0\}}}\big)
is a Hilbert space.

We now construct an isometry between the initial data and the scattering states of (
86
). In fact, we already constructed a similar result in
[
8
]
, for different Hilbert spaces. For completeness and since we will require it once in this paper, we recall it here. For this, we introduce energy spaces associated to the multiplier
\partial_{t}
.

Definition 4.19
.

Let
\mathcal{S}^{\partial_{t}}_{\{t=0\}}
be the set of the
2
-forms
F_{0}
, defined on
\mathbb{R}^{1+3}
and independent of
t
, such that

\|F_{0}\|_{\mathcal{S}^{\partial_{t}}_{\{t=0\}}}^{2}:=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\big|\alpha(F_{0})\big|^{2}(x)+\big|\underline{\alpha}(F_{0})\big|^{2}(x)+2\big|\rho(F_{0})\big|^{2}(x)+2\big|\sigma(F_{0})\big|^{2}(x)\mathrm{d}x<+\infty.

Let further, for
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
\mathcal{S}^{\partial_{t},N}_{\{t=0\}}\subset\mathcal{S}^{\partial_{t}}_{\{t=0\}}
be the set of the
2
-forms
F_{0}
on
\mathbb{R}^{1+3}
independent of
t
verifying

\|F_{0}\|^{2}_{\mathcal{S}^{\partial_{t},N}_{\{t=0\}}}:=\sum_{|\gamma|\leq N}\|\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}(F_{0})(0,\cdot)\|^{2}_{\mathcal{S}^{\partial_{t}}_{\{t=0\}}}<+\infty.

Consider
\mathcal{S}^{\partial_{t}}_{\mathcal{I}^{+}}
, the set of the
1
-forms
\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}
on
\mathbb{R}_{u}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}
which are tangential to the
2
-spheres and such that

\big\|\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\big\|^{2}_{\mathcal{S}^{\partial_{t}}_{\mathcal{I}^{+}}}:=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{u}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\big|\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\big|^{2}(u,\omega)\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\mathrm{d}u<+\infty.

Finally, for
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
\mathcal{S}^{\partial_{t},N}_{\mathcal{I}^{+}}
denotes the sets of the
1
-forms
\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\in\mathcal{S}^{K}_{\mathcal{I}^{+}}
satisfying

\big\|\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\big\|^{2}_{\mathcal{S}^{\partial_{t},N}_{\mathcal{I}^{+}}}:=\sum_{|\gamma|\leq N}\big\|\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}_{\,\gamma}\big\|^{2}_{\mathcal{S}^{\partial_{t}}_{\mathcal{I}^{+}}}<+\infty.

Then, for any
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
\big(\mathcal{S}^{\partial_{t},N}_{\{t=0\}},\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{S}^{\partial_{t},N}_{\{t=0\}}}\big)
and
\big(\mathcal{S}^{\partial_{t},N}_{\mathcal{I}^{+}},\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{S}^{\partial_{t},N}_{\mathcal{I}^{+}}}\big)
are Hilbert spaces.

Theorem 4.20
.

The forward map

F
^+ :

S
^
∂
_t_
{
t=0
}

∩
C_c^
∞

⟶
S
^
∂
_t_
I
^+

F_0

⟼lim
_
u

→
+
∞
r
α
(F)(u,
u
,ω),

where
F
is the unique solution to the vacuum Maxwell equations (
86
) such that
F(0,\cdot)=F_{0}
, is well-defined and preserves the norm
\|F_{0}\|_{\mathcal{S}^{K}_{\{t=0\}}}=\|\mathscr{F}^{+}(F)\|_{\mathcal{S}^{K}_{\mathcal{I}^{+}}}
. Moreover,

•

this map can be uniquely extended in a bijective isometry
\mathscr{F}^{+}:\mathcal{S}^{\partial_{t}}_{\{t=0\}}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}^{\partial_{t}}_{\mathcal{I}^{+}}
.

•

For
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
\mathscr{F}^{+}|_{\mathcal{S}^{\partial_{t},N}_{\{t=0\}}}
is a bijective isometry between the spaces of higher regularity
\mathcal{S}^{\partial_{t},N}_{\{t=0\}}
and
\mathcal{S}^{\partial_{t},N}_{\mathcal{I}^{+}}
.

•

\mathscr{F}^{+}|_{\mathcal{S}^{K}_{\{t=0\}}}\!:\mathcal{S}^{K}_{\{t=0\}}\!\rightarrow\mathcal{S}^{K}_{\mathcal{I}^{+}}
is a bijective isometry between Hilbert spaces of more strongly decaying data.

Remark 4.21
.

Similar considerations as those presented in Section
1.9
allow one to construct various scattering maps for the vacuum Maxwell equations (
86
).

Proof.

All but the last part of the statement are given by
[
8
, Theorem
7.6
and Corollary
7.15
]
. According to Proposition
4.17
,
\mathscr{F}^{+}|_{\mathcal{S}^{K}_{\{t=0\}}\cap C_{c}^{\infty}}
extends to an injective isometry from
\mathcal{S}^{K}_{\{t=0\}}
to
\mathcal{S}^{K}_{\mathcal{I}^{+}}
. Then, by Proposition
4.9
, applied for
J=0
and
T=0
, we get that any smooth and compactly supported radiation field is in the image of
\mathscr{F}^{+}
, implying that the map is onto.

5
The asymptotic Maxwell equations

We fix, for all this section,
f_{\infty}:\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\to\mathbb{R}
a sufficiently regular function. Motivated by Corollary
2.17
, we introduce the asymptotic
4
-current density generated by a constant in time distribution function.

Definition 5.1
.

For any
h\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v},\mathbb{R})
, we define its asymptotic current
J^{\mathrm{asymp}}[h]
as

\forall\,|x|<t,\quad J^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\nu}[h](t,x)=\frac{1}{t^{3}}\frac{x_{\nu}}{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}\big[|v^{0}|^{5}h\big]\bigg(z,\frac{\widecheck{\;x\;}}{t}\bigg)\mathrm{d}z,\qquad\qquad\forall\,|x|\geq t>0,\quad J^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\nu}[h](t,x)=0.

Remark 5.2
.

If
h\in C^{0}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v},\mathbb{R})
and
\int_{z}\langle v\rangle^{5}h(z,v)\mathrm{d}z\to 0
as
|v|\to+\infty
, then
J^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]
vanishes and is continous at any point of the light cone
t=|x|
.

As we shall see, such a current is in fact generated by a singular solution to the linear Vlasov equation. This section is devoted to the study of
F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]
, the unique solution to the asymptotic Maxwell equations associated to
f_{\infty}
, with initial time
t_{0}>0
,

\begin{cases}\nabla^{\mu}F^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\mu\nu}[f_{\infty}]=J^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\nu}[f_{\infty}],\\[3.0pt]
\nabla^{\mu}{}^{*}\!F^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\mu\nu}[f_{\infty}]=0,\\[3.0pt]
\nabla\times E(t_{0},\cdot)=\nabla\times B(t_{0},\cdot)=0,\end{cases}

(87)

where
E(t_{0},\cdot)
and
B(t_{0},\cdot)
are the initial electric and magnetic field,

E^{i}(t_{0},\cdot):=F^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{0i}[f_{\infty}](t_{0},\cdot),\qquad B^{i}(t_{0},\cdot):=-\varepsilon^{ijk}F^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{jk}[f_{\infty}](t_{0},\cdot).

Recall from Section
3.5
that the initial assumptions imply that
B(t_{0},\cdot)=0
and
E(t_{0},\cdot)=\nabla\phi
, where
\phi
is solution to the Poisson equation
\Delta\phi=-J_{0}^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}](t_{0},\cdot)
.

On the top of the estimates derived in this section, we will control the asymptotic electromagnetic field
\mathbb{F}[f_{\infty}]
and the correction coefficients. In the next sections, we will always take
t_{0}=1
. The wider range
0<t_{0}\leq 2
will however be allowed in this section in order to prove the following statement, which is reminiscent of the case of the Vlasov-Poisson system
[
38
]
.

Proposition 5.3
.

Let
(t,v)\!\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*}\!\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
. Then,
F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}](t,t\widehat{v})\to t^{-2}\mathbb{F}[f_{\infty}](v)
as the initial time
t_{0}\to 0
.

5.1 Singular solution to the linear Vlasov equation

Motivated by Corollary
2.17
, we would like to interpret the leading order term
J^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{0}[f_{\infty}]
in the asymptotic expansion of
J(f_{1})_{0}=-\int_{v}f_{1}\mathrm{d}v
as the velocity average of a (singular) solution to the linear Vlasov equation. This formalism will lead to considerable simplifications, for the commutation of (
87
) and in particular for the derivation of the Glassey-Strauss decomposition of
\nabla F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]
. We assume here that
f_{\infty}\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})
goes fast enough to
0
as
|z|+|v|\to\infty
.

Definition 5.4
.

Let
f^{\mathrm{sing}}[f_{\infty}]\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\big(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\big)
be the distribution

f^{\mathrm{sing}}[f_{\infty}](t,x,v):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}f_{\infty}(z,v)\mathrm{d}z\,\delta(x-t\widehat{v}),

where
\delta
is the Dirac delta distribution in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
. We define its velocity averages as

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}w(v)f^{\mathrm{sing}}[f_{\infty}]\mathrm{d}v(t,x):=\frac{\mathds{1}_{t>|x|}}{t^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}\Big[w(v)|v^{0}|^{5}f_{\infty}\Big]\bigg(z,\frac{\widecheck{\;x\;}}{t}\bigg)\mathrm{d}z,

for any sufficiently regular function
w:\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\to\mathbb{R}
.

Remark 5.5
.

The velocity average with the weight function
w(v)=\widehat{v}_{\nu}
is equal to
J^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\nu}[f_{\infty}]
.

We now investigate certain basic properties of this distribution, which explain in particular that we abusively use the term velocity average of
f^{\mathrm{sing}}[f_{\infty}]
. Note first that if
(\psi_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
is a mollifier in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
and

h_{n}(t,x,v):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}f_{\infty}(z,v)\mathrm{d}z\,\psi_{n}(x-t\widehat{v}),

then
(h_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
converges to
f^{\mathrm{sing}}[f_{\infty}]
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\big(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\big)
.

Lemma 5.6
.

The distribution
f^{\mathrm{sing}}[f_{\infty}]
verifies

f^{\mathrm{sing}}[f_{\infty}](t,x,v)=\frac{\mathds{1}_{t>|x|}}{t^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}|v^{0}|^{5}f_{\infty}(z,v)\mathrm{d}z\,\delta\bigg(v=\frac{\widecheck{\;x\;}}{t}\bigg)

and is a singular solution to the linear Vlasov equation,
\mathbf{T}_{0}(f^{\mathrm{sing}}[f_{\infty}])=0
. Furthermore,

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}wh_{n}\mathrm{d}v\xrightarrow[n\to+\infty]{}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}wf^{\mathrm{sing}}[f_{\infty}]\mathrm{d}v\qquad\text{in $L^{\infty}_{\mathrm{loc}}\big(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*},L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})\big)$,}

for any
w\in C^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v},\mathbb{R})
such that
w|v^{0}|^{5}\int_{z}f_{\infty}(z,\cdot)\mathrm{d}z\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})
.

Proof.

The alternative expression for
f^{\mathrm{sing}}[f_{\infty}]
follows from the composition rule for the Dirac delta function as well as Lemma
2.10
. As
\mathbf{T}_{0}(v)=\mathbf{T}_{0}(x-t\widehat{v})=0
, we have
\mathbf{T}_{0}(h_{n})=0
, which implies that
f^{\mathrm{sing}}[f_{\infty}]
is solution to the linear Vlasov equation in the space of the distributions. Finally, the convergence of the velocity averages holds since
(\psi_{n})_{n\geq 1}
is an approximation to the identity.

We are now interested in computing
\widehat{Z}f^{\mathrm{sing}}[f_{\infty}]
.

Lemma 5.7
.

For any homogeneous vector field
\widehat{Z}\in\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{S}\setminus\{\partial_{t},\partial_{x^{1}},\partial_{x^{2}},\partial_{x^{3}}\}
, we have

\widehat{Z}f^{\mathrm{sing}}[f_{\infty}]=f^{\mathrm{sing}}\big[\widehat{Z}_{\infty}f_{\infty}\big].

Proof.

Let
\psi\in C^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3},\mathbb{R})
,
1\leq i<j\leq 3
and
1\leq k\leq 3
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\widehat{\Omega}_{0k}\big(\psi(x-t\widehat{v})\big)

\displaystyle=-(x^{k}-t\widehat{v}^{k})\widehat{v}\cdot\nabla\psi(x-t\widehat{v}),\qquad\qquad\widehat{\Omega}_{ij}\big(\psi(x-t\widehat{v})\big)=\big[\Omega_{ij}\psi\big](x-t\widehat{v}),

\displaystyle S\big(\psi(x-t\widehat{v})\big)

\displaystyle=(x-t\widehat{v})\cdot\nabla\psi(x-t\widehat{v}).

Consider now
\phi\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})
a compactly supported function and recall that the Dirac delta function belongs to
H^{-1}
. We denote by
\langle\cdot|\cdot\rangle_{L^{2}_{t,x,v}}
the inner product of
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})
and by
\langle\cdot|\cdot\rangle
the duality bracket for
H^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})
. Performing integration by parts in
x
, we have

\Big\langle\widehat{\Omega}_{0k}\big(\psi_{n}(x-t\widehat{v})\big)\,\Big|\,\phi\Big\rangle_{L^{2}_{t,x,v}}=\Big\langle\psi_{n}(x-t\widehat{v})\,\Big|\,(x^{k}-t\widehat{v}^{k})\widehat{v}\cdot\nabla_{x}\phi+\widehat{v}^{k}\phi\Big\rangle_{L^{2}_{t,x,v}}\xrightarrow[n\to+\infty]{}\Big\langle\delta(x-t\widehat{v})\,\Big|\,\widehat{v}^{k}\phi\Big\rangle,

since
x^{k}-t\widehat{v}^{k}
vanishes on the support of
\delta(x-t\widehat{v})
. Next, by integration by parts in
z
, we have

\widehat{\Omega}_{0k}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}f_{\infty}(z,v)\mathrm{d}z=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}v^{0}\partial_{v^{k}}f_{\infty}(z,v)\mathrm{d}z=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}\widehat{\Omega}^{\infty}_{0k}f_{\infty}(z,v)\mathrm{d}z-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}\widehat{v}^{k}f_{\infty}(z,v)\mathrm{d}z,

(88)

for almost all
(t,v)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
. Consequently, by continuity of the distributional partial derivatives, we obtain

\widehat{\Omega}_{0k}f^{\mathrm{sing}}[f_{\infty}](t,x,v)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}\widehat{\Omega}^{\infty}_{0k}f_{\infty}(z,v)\mathrm{d}z\,\delta(x-t\widehat{v})=f^{\mathrm{sing}}\big[\widehat{\Omega}^{\infty}_{0k}f_{\infty}\big](t,x,v).

Similarly, there holds

\displaystyle\Big\langle\widehat{\Omega}_{ij}\big(\psi_{n}(x-t\widehat{v})\big)\,\Big|\,\phi\Big\rangle_{L^{2}_{t,x,v}}

\displaystyle=\Big\langle\psi_{n}(x-t\widehat{v})\,\Big|\,(x^{i}-t\widehat{v}^{i})\partial_{x^{j}}\phi-(x^{j}-t\widehat{v}^{j})\partial_{x^{i}}\phi\Big\rangle_{L^{2}_{t,x,v}}\xrightarrow[n\to+\infty]{}0,

\displaystyle\Big\langle S\big(\psi_{n}(x-t\widehat{v})\big)\,\Big|\,\phi\Big\rangle_{L^{2}_{t,x,v}}

\displaystyle=\Big\langle\psi_{n}(x-t\widehat{v})\,\Big|\,-(x-t\widehat{v})\cdot\nabla_{x}\phi-3\phi\Big\rangle_{L^{2}_{t,x,v}}\xrightarrow[n\to+\infty]{}\Big\langle\delta(x-t\widehat{v})\,\Big|\,-3\phi\Big\rangle.

Then, the result ensues from
\widehat{S}=S+3
as well as, by integration by parts in
z
,

\widehat{\Omega}_{ij}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}f_{\infty}(z,v)\mathrm{d}z=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}\widehat{\Omega}_{ij}f_{\infty}(z,v)\mathrm{d}z,\qquad S\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}f_{\infty}(z,v)\mathrm{d}z=0=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}\widehat{S}f_{\infty}(z,v)\mathrm{d}z.

(89)

The cases of the translations are slightly more complicated. It turns out that that, as in Section
2.3.2
, it is in fact easier to deal first with
Y_{\mu\nu}=\widehat{v}_{\mu}\partial_{x^{\nu}}-\widehat{v}_{\nu}\partial_{x^{\mu}}
.

Lemma 5.8
.

For any
1\leq k\leq 3
and
1\leq i<j\leq 3
, there holds

Y_{0k}f^{\mathrm{sing}}[f_{\infty}]\!=\frac{1}{t}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}\!f_{\infty}(z,v)\mathrm{d}z\,v^{0}\partial_{v^{k}}\!\Big[\delta(x-t\widehat{v})\Big],\quad Y_{ij}f^{\mathrm{sing}}[f_{\infty}]\!=\!\frac{-1}{t}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}\!f_{\infty}(z,v)\mathrm{d}z\,\big(v^{i}\partial_{v^{j}}-v^{j}\partial_{v^{i}}\big)\Big[\delta(x-t\widehat{v})\Big].

Proof.

Recall the relations

\displaystyle-tY_{0k}

\displaystyle=t\left(\partial_{x^{k}}+\widehat{v}_{k}\partial_{t}\right)=\Omega_{0k}-(x^{k}-t\widehat{v}^{k})\partial_{t}=-v^{0}\partial_{v^{k}}+\widehat{\Omega}_{0k}-\widehat{v}^{k}-\partial_{t}\big[(x^{k}-t\widehat{v}^{k})\cdot\big],

\displaystyle tY_{ij}

\displaystyle=t\left(\widehat{v}_{i}\partial_{x^{j}}-\widehat{v}_{j}\partial_{x^{i}}\right)=-\big(v^{i}\partial_{v^{j}}-v^{j}\partial_{v^{i}}\big)+\widehat{\Omega}_{ij}-(x^{i}-t\widehat{v}^{i})\partial_{x^{j}}+(x^{j}-t\widehat{v}^{j})\partial_{x^{i}}.

Next, we claim that

Y_{\mu\nu}f^{\mathrm{sing}}[f_{\infty}]=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}f_{\infty}(z,v)\mathrm{d}zY_{\mu\nu}(\delta(x-t\widehat{v})),\qquad\qquad\widehat{\Omega}_{0k}\big[\delta(x-t\widehat{v})\big]-\widehat{v}^{k}\delta(x-t\widehat{v})=\widehat{\Omega}_{ij}\big[\delta(x-t\widehat{v})\big]=0.

The first equality is straightforward since the spatial average of
f_{\infty}
is independent of
(t,x)
. The other ones were derived during the proof of Lemma
5.7
. The result then ensues from

\Big\langle\partial_{x^{\nu}}\big[(x^{\ell}-t\widehat{v}^{\ell})\psi_{n}(x-t\widehat{v})\big]\,\Big|\,\phi\Big\rangle_{L^{2}_{t,x,v}}=\Big\langle\psi_{n}(x-t\widehat{v})\,\Big|\,(x^{\ell}-t\widehat{v}^{\ell})\partial_{x^{\nu}}\phi\Big\rangle_{L^{2}_{t,x,v}}\xrightarrow[n\to+\infty]{}0,

which holds for any
0\leq\nu\leq 3
,
\ell\neq\nu
and all compactly supported function
\phi\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})
.

Using the relations

\partial_{t}=|v^{0}|^{2}\big(\mathbf{T}_{0}+\widehat{v}^{i}Y_{0i}\big),\qquad\qquad\partial_{x^{k}}=|v^{0}|^{2}\big(-Y_{0k}-\widehat{v}^{k}\mathbf{T}_{0}+\widehat{v}^{i}Y_{ki}\big)

as well as
\mathbf{T}_{0}f^{\mathrm{sing}}[f_{\infty}]=0
, we finally obtain the following result.

Lemma 5.9
.

For any
1\leq k\leq 3
, there holds

\displaystyle\partial_{t}f^{\mathrm{sing}}[f_{\infty}](t,x,v)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}|v^{0}|^{2}f_{\infty}(z,v)\mathrm{d}z\,v^{i}\partial_{v^{i}}\Big[\delta(x-t\widehat{v})\Big],

\displaystyle\partial_{x^{k}}f^{\mathrm{sing}}[f_{\infty}](t,x,v)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}|v^{0}|^{2}f_{\infty}(z,v)\mathrm{d}z\,\big(v^{0}\partial_{v^{k}}-\widehat{v}^{i}v_{i}\partial_{v^{k}}+\widehat{v}^{i}v_{k}\partial_{v^{i}}\big)\Big[\delta(x-t\widehat{v})\Big].

In order to consistently define averages in
v
for these quantities, we compute, by performing integration by parts in
z
as in (
88
)–(
89
),

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}\partial_{v^{i}}\big(v^{i}|v^{0}|^{2}f_{\infty}\big)\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}\widehat{\Omega}^{\infty}_{0i}\big(v^{i}v^{0}f_{\infty}\big)\mathrm{d}z,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}\partial_{v^{k}}\big(|v^{0}|^{3}f_{\infty}\big)-\partial_{v^{k}}\big(\widehat{v}^{i}v_{i}|v^{0}|^{2}f_{\infty}\big)+\partial_{v^{i}}\big(\widehat{v}^{i}v_{k}|v^{0}|^{2}f_{\infty}\big)\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}\widehat{\Omega}^{\infty}_{0k}\big(|v^{0}|^{2}f_{\infty}\big)+\widehat{\Omega}_{ki}\big(v^{i}v^{0}f_{\infty}\big)\mathrm{d}z.

Since we will in particular be interested in their
4
-current density, we further remark that, for
1\leq\ell\leq 3
,

|v^{0}|^{2}v^{i}\partial_{v^{i}}\big(\widehat{v}^{\ell}\big)=\widehat{v}^{\ell},\qquad|v^{0}|^{2}\big(v^{0}\partial_{v^{k}}-\widehat{v}^{i}v_{i}\partial_{v^{k}}+\widehat{v}^{i}v_{k}\partial_{v^{i}}\big)(\widehat{v}^{\ell})=\delta_{k}^{\ell}

and we recall the operators
D_{x^{k}}:h\mapsto\widehat{\Omega}_{0k}^{\infty}\big(|v^{0}|^{2}h\big)+\widehat{\Omega}_{ki}\big(v^{i}v^{0}h\big)
as well as
D_{t}:h\mapsto-\widehat{\Omega}_{0i}^{\infty}\big(v^{i}v^{0}h\big)-h
.

Definition 5.10
.

We define the
4
-current densities

J\big(\partial_{t}f^{\mathrm{sing}}[f_{\infty}]\big)_{\nu}(t,x)=\frac{\mathds{1}_{t>|x|}}{t^{4}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}\Big[\widehat{v}_{\nu}|v^{0}|^{5}D_{t}f_{\infty}\Big]\bigg(z,\frac{\widecheck{\;x\;}}{t}\bigg)\mathrm{d}z-\delta_{\nu}^{0}\,\frac{\mathds{1}_{t>|x|}}{t^{4}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}\Big[|v^{0}|^{5}f_{\infty}\Big]\bigg(z,\frac{\widecheck{\;x\;}}{t}\bigg)\mathrm{d}z

as well as, for
1\leq k\leq 3
,

J\big(\partial_{x^{k}}f^{\mathrm{sing}}[f_{\infty}]\big)_{\nu}(t,x)=\frac{\mathds{1}_{t>|x|}}{t^{4}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}\Big[\widehat{v}_{\nu}|v^{0}|^{5}D_{x^{k}}f_{\infty}\Big]\bigg(z,\frac{\widecheck{\;x\;}}{t}\bigg)\mathrm{d}z+\delta_{\nu}^{k}\,\frac{\mathds{1}_{t>|x|}}{t^{4}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}\Big[|v^{0}|^{5}f_{\infty}\Big]\bigg(z,\frac{\widecheck{\;x\;}}{t}\bigg)\mathrm{d}z.

Remark 5.11
.

As
\widehat{v}_{\nu}=x_{\nu}/t
if
v=\widecheck{x/t}
, the first term on the right hand side of both equations can be rewritten using the functional
t^{-1}J^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\nu}[\cdot]
.

Remark 5.12
.

Since
(\psi_{n})_{n\geq 1}
is a mollifier, we have, for any
\widehat{Z}\in\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{S}
and all
t\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*}
,

J\big(\widehat{Z}h_{n}\big)_{\nu}\xrightarrow[n\to+\infty]{}J\big(\widehat{Z}f^{\mathrm{sing}}[f_{\infty}]\big)_{\nu}\qquad\qquad\text{in $L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})$}.

Thus, according to Corollaries
2.17
and
2.21
, for any
\widehat{Z}\in\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{S}
,
J(\widehat{Z}f^{\mathrm{sing}}[f_{\infty}])
is the leading order term in the asymptotic expansion of
J(f_{1})
.

5.2 Commutation of the system

The results of this section suggest that the derivatives of
F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]
are a good approximation, near timelike infinity, that is along timelike straight lines
t\mapsto(t,t\widehat{v})
, of the derivatives of the electromagnetic field
\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F
that we will construct during the proof of Theorem
1.8
.

We start by computing the derivatives of the current density
J^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]
.

Lemma 5.13
.

Let
h:\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\to\mathbb{R}
be a sufficiently regular function. Then, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\Omega_{0k}}\big(J^{\mathrm{asymp}}[h]\big)

\displaystyle=J^{\mathrm{asymp}}\big[\widehat{\Omega}_{0k}^{\infty}h\big],\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad

\displaystyle 1\leq k\leq 3,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\Omega_{ij}}\big(J^{\mathrm{asymp}}[h]\big)

\displaystyle=J^{\mathrm{asymp}}\big[\widehat{\Omega}_{ij}h\big],\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad

\displaystyle 1\leq i<j\leq 3,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{S}(J^{\mathrm{asymp}}[h])

\displaystyle=J^{\mathrm{asymp}}\big[\widehat{S}h\big]-2J^{\mathrm{asymp}}[h].

For the translations
\partial_{x^{\lambda}}
,
0\leq\lambda\leq 3
, there holds

\mathcal{L}_{\partial_{x^{\lambda}}}\big(J^{\mathrm{asymp}}[h]\big)_{\nu}=t^{-1}J^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\nu}\big[D_{x^{\lambda}}h\big]-(-1)^{\delta_{0}^{\lambda}}\,\delta_{\nu}^{\lambda}\,t^{-1}J^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{0}[h],

where the operators
D_{x^{\lambda}}
are defined in (
33
). Furthermore,
J^{\mathrm{asymp}}[h]
is divergence free.

Remark 5.14
.

Note that
J^{\mathrm{asymp}}[\partial^{\infty}_{t}h]=J^{\mathrm{asymp}}[\partial_{x^{k}}h]=0
reflects that
\int_{v}\partial_{x^{\lambda}}f_{1}\mathrm{d}v
decays faster than
t^{-3}
.

Proof.

We apply, for any
n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
, Lemma
2.7
in order to relate
\mathcal{L}_{Z}J(h_{n})
to
J(\widehat{Z}h_{n})
. Recall further that
J(h_{n})
is divergence free. The result is obtained, in view of Remark
5.12
, by letting
n\to+\infty
and by applying Lemmata
5.7
and
5.9
.

We are now able to state the higher order commutation formula for both the Maxwell and the asymptotic Maxwell equations. In order to simplify the presentation, we will only consider operators of the form
Z^{\gamma}=\partial_{t,x}^{\kappa}Z^{\beta}
, where
Z^{\beta}
is only composed by homogeneous vector fields,
|\beta|=\beta_{H}
. This reduction is possible in view of the next identity.

Lemma 5.15
.

Let
Z^{\gamma}\in\mathbb{K}^{|\gamma|}
containng
\gamma_{T}
translation
\partial_{x^{\nu}}
. There exists
C^{\gamma}_{\kappa,\beta}\in\mathbb{Z}
such that

Z^{\gamma}=\sum_{|\kappa|=\gamma_{T}}\sum_{|\beta|\leq|\gamma|-\gamma_{T}}C^{\gamma}_{\kappa,\beta}\,\partial_{t,x}^{\kappa}Z^{\beta}.

Proof.

It suffices to use that, for all
0\leq\mu\leq 3
and
Z\in\mathbb{K}
, we have
[\partial_{x^{\mu}},Z]=0
or there exists
0\leq\nu\leq 3
such that
[\partial_{x^{\mu}},Z]=\pm\partial_{x^{\nu}}
.

Proposition 5.16
.

Let
f:[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\to\mathbb{R}
and
F
be a solution to the Maxwell equations

\nabla^{\mu}F_{\mu\nu}=J(f)_{\nu},\qquad\qquad\nabla^{\mu}{}^{*}\!F_{\mu\nu}=0.

Assume that they are both sufficiently regular and consider
Z^{\gamma}\in\mathbb{K}^{|\gamma|}
.

•

If
Z^{\gamma}
is only composed by homogeneous vector fields
\Omega_{0k}
,
\Omega_{ij}
and
S
, then

\displaystyle\nabla^{\mu}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}(F)_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=J\big(\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}f\big)_{\nu},\qquad

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\,\nabla^{\mu}{}^{*}\!\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}(F)_{\mu\nu}=0,

\displaystyle\nabla^{\mu}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}\big(F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]\big)_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=J^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\nu}\big[\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}_{\infty}f_{\infty}\big],\qquad\qquad\qquad

\displaystyle\nabla^{\mu}{}^{*}\!\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}\big(F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]\big)_{\mu\nu}=0.

Moreover, if
Z^{\gamma}
contains the scaling vector field
S
, then
J^{\mathrm{asymp}}\big[\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}_{\infty}f_{\infty}\big]=0
.

•

If
Z^{\gamma}=\partial_{t,x}^{\kappa}Z^{\beta}
, with
|\kappa|=\gamma_{T}\geq 1
, then

\nabla^{\mu}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}(F)_{\mu\nu}=J\big(\partial_{t,x}^{\kappa}\widehat{Z}^{\beta}f\big)_{\nu},\qquad\qquad\nabla^{\mu}{}^{*}\!\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}(F)_{\mu\nu}=0,

and there exists
C^{\gamma,\lambda}_{\kappa,\beta,\nu}\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\displaystyle\nabla^{\mu}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}\big(F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]\big)_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{t^{|\kappa|}}J^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\nu}\big[D_{t,x}^{\kappa}\widehat{Z}^{\beta}_{\infty}f\big]+\sum_{0\leq\lambda\leq 3}\sum_{|\xi|<p}\sum_{|\beta|\leq|\gamma|-|\xi|}C^{\gamma,\lambda}_{\kappa,\beta,\nu}\,\frac{1}{t^{|\kappa|}}J^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\lambda}\big[D_{t,x}^{\xi}\widehat{Z}^{\beta}_{\infty}f\big],

\displaystyle\nabla^{\mu}{}^{*}\!\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}\big(F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]\big)_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=0.

Remark 5.17
.

In view of Corollary
2.17
, if
Z^{\gamma}
is only composed by homogeneous vector fields, it suggests that the asymptotic behavior of
\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F
is captured by
\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]
if
f
satisfies modified scattering to the density function
f_{\infty}
.

In fact, the same observation holds when
Z^{\gamma}
is composed by
p\geq 1
translations but we will not require it for the proof of Theorem
1.8
. One can easily check it for the case
p=1
by computing the coefficients
C^{\gamma,\lambda}_{\kappa,\beta,\nu}
using Lemma
5.13
and by comparing the commutation formulas with Corollary
2.21
.

Proof.

These relations are obtained by iterating Lemmata
2.3
,
2.7
and
5.13
. If
Z^{\gamma}_{\infty}
