is only composed by homogeneous vector field and contains at least once
S
, there exists
|\beta|=|\gamma|-1
such that
\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\gamma}=\widehat{S}\widehat{Z}^{\beta}_{\infty}
. Indeed,
[S,\widehat{\Omega}_{0k}^{\infty}]=[S,\widehat{\Omega}_{ij}]=0
. Then, by integration by parts in
z
,

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}\widehat{S}\widehat{Z}^{\beta}_{\infty}f_{\infty}(z,\cdot)\mathrm{d}z=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}z^{i}\partial_{z^{i}}\widehat{Z}^{\beta}_{\infty}f_{\infty}(z,\cdot)\mathrm{d}z+3\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}\widehat{Z}^{\beta}_{\infty}f_{\infty}(z,\cdot)\mathrm{d}z=0.

5.3 Estimates for the solution to the asymptotic Maxwell equations

This subsection is devoted to the study of the large time behavior of
F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]
and its derivatives. For this, it will be convenient to introduce, for any
N_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
,

\hskip-14.22636ptQ:=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f_{\infty}(z,v)\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}z,\qquad\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}}[f_{\infty}]:=\sup_{|\kappa_{z}|+|\kappa_{v}|\leq N_{0}}\,\sup_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\langle x\rangle^{4+|\kappa_{x}|}\,\langle v\rangle^{7+3|\kappa_{v}|}\big|\partial_{z}^{\kappa_{z}}\partial_{v}^{\kappa_{v}}f_{\infty}\big|(z,v).

One needs first to estimate the initial data
F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}](t_{0},\cdot)
.

Proposition 5.18
.

Recall that
\overline{F}(t,x)=\overline{\chi}(t-|x|)\frac{Qx_{i}}{4\pi|x|^{3}}\mathrm{d}t\wedge\mathrm{d}x^{i}
. Then, for any
|\gamma|\leq N_{0}
,

\forall\,|\kappa|\leq 1,\qquad\sup_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\,\langle x\rangle^{3+|\kappa|}\left|\nabla_{t,x}^{\kappa}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}\big(F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]-\overline{F})\right|\!(t_{0},x)\lesssim_{t_{0}}\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}].

For any
|\gamma|\leq N_{0}+1
, we have

\forall\,|\kappa|\leq 1,\qquad\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\langle x\rangle^{\frac{5}{2}+2|\kappa|}\left|\nabla_{t,x}^{\kappa}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}\big(F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]-\overline{F})\right|^{2}\!(t_{0},x)\mathrm{d}x\bigg|^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim_{t_{0}}\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}].

Proof.

Recall that
J^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}](t_{0},\cdot)
is supported in
\{|x|\leq t_{0}\}
. Remark that

-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}J^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{0}(t_{0},x)\mathrm{d}x=\int_{|x|<t_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}\bigg\langle\frac{\widecheck{\;x\;}}{t_{0}}\bigg\rangle^{5}f_{\infty}\bigg(z,\frac{\widecheck{\;x\;}}{t_{0}}\bigg)\frac{\mathrm{d}x}{t_{0}^{3}}=Q,

where we used Lemma
2.10
in order to perform the change of variables
x=t_{0}\widehat{v}
. Note also the bound

\langle x\rangle^{p}\left|J^{\mathrm{asymp}}[h](t_{0},x)\right|\leq\mathds{1}_{|x|<t_{0}}\frac{\langle t_{0}\rangle^{p}}{t_{0}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}\frac{\mathrm{d}z}{\langle z\rangle^{4}}\,\sup_{(z,v)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\langle z\rangle^{4}\,\langle v\rangle^{5}|h(z,v)|\lesssim_{t_{0}}\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{0}[h]\mathds{1}_{|x|<t_{0}},\qquad\quad p\in\mathbb{N},

which holds for any function
h\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})
decaying fast enough. The result is now a direct consequence of Propositions
3.15
and
5.16
.

We now state our main result concerning
F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]
. For this, we recall
\mathbb{F}[\cdot]
introduced in (
14
)–(
15
) (see also Definition
5.42
below).

Proposition 5.19
.

Let
N_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
,
Z^{\gamma}\in\mathbb{K}^{|\gamma|}
be a differential operator of order
|\gamma|\leq N_{0}
and assume
1\leq t_{0}\leq 2
.

•

Denoting by
0\leq\gamma_{T}\leq|\gamma|
the number of translations
\partial_{x^{\lambda}}
composing
Z^{\gamma}
, we have

\forall\,(t,x)\in[t_{0},+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3},\qquad\left|\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]\right|(t,x)\lesssim\langle t+|x|\rangle^{-1}\,\langle t-|x|\rangle^{-1-\gamma_{T}}\,\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}].

•

For all
(t,x)\in[t_{0},+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
such that
|x|<t
, there holds

\left|\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}\big(F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]\big)(t,x)-\frac{1}{t^{2}}\mathbb{F}\big[\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\gamma}f_{\infty}\big]\bigg(\frac{\widecheck{\;x\;}}{t}\bigg)\right|\lesssim\frac{\overline{E}_{N_{0}+1}\big[|v^{0}|^{2}f_{\infty}\big]}{\langle t+|x|\rangle\,\langle t-|x|\rangle^{2}}.

Finally, in the exterior of the light cone, there holds

\forall\,|x|\geq t\geq t_{0},\qquad\left|\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}\big(F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]\big)\right|(t,x)\lesssim\frac{Q}{\langle t+|x|\rangle^{2}}\,\mathds{1}_{|x|-t\geq 1}+\frac{\overline{E}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}]}{\langle t+|x|\rangle\,\langle t-|x|\rangle^{2}}.

Remark 5.20
.

If
Z^{\gamma}
is only composed by homogeneous vector fields,
S
,
\Omega_{0k}
and
\Omega_{ij}
, and contains at least once the scaling vector field
S
, then we have the improved estimate

\forall\,(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3},\qquad\left|\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]\right|(t,x)\lesssim\langle t+|x|\rangle^{-1}\,\langle t-|x|\rangle^{-2}\,\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}].

If
\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}_{\infty}
contains at least one translation (
\partial_{t}^{\infty}
or
\partial_{z^{k}}
) or
\widehat{S}
, then
\mathbb{F}\big[\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\gamma}f_{\infty}\big]=0
. If
\gamma_{T}\geq 1
, we could prove a more precise statement implying in particular that there exists
v\mapsto G^{\gamma}(v)
such that
\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}\big(F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]\big)(t,t\widehat{v})\sim t^{-2-\gamma_{T}}G^{\gamma}(v)
.

Remark 5.21
.

L^{2}
estimates could be proved for
\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]
up to order
N+1
.

Since we would like to derive very precise informations on the asymptotic behavior of
F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]
, in particular along timelike straight lines
t\mapsto(t,x+t\widehat{v})
, we will exploit the wave equation satisfied by each component
F^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\mu\nu}[f_{\infty}]
in order to work with representation formula.

Definition 5.22
.

For any
Z^{\gamma}\in\mathbb{K}^{|\gamma|}
, with
|\gamma|\leq N_{0}
, we consider the decomposition

\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]=F^{\gamma,\mathrm{hom}}+F^{\gamma,\mathrm{inh}},

where, for any
0\leq\mu<\nu\leq 3
,

\Box F_{\mu\nu}^{\gamma,\mathrm{hom}}=0,\qquad F^{\gamma,\mathrm{hom}}_{\mu\nu}(t_{0},\cdot)=\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}(F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}])_{\mu\nu}(t_{0},\cdot),\quad\;\partial_{t}F^{\gamma,\mathrm{hom}}_{\mu\nu}(t_{0},\cdot)=\partial_{t}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}(F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}])_{\mu\nu}(t_{0},\cdot)

and

\Box F_{\mu\nu}^{\gamma,\mathrm{inh}}=\partial_{x^{\nu}}J^{\gamma}_{\mu}-\partial_{x^{\mu}}J^{\gamma}_{\nu},\qquad\qquad F^{\gamma,\mathrm{inh}}_{\mu\nu}(t_{0},\cdot)=0,\qquad\partial_{t}F^{\gamma,\mathrm{hom}}_{\mu\nu}(t_{0},\cdot)=0,

where the source term
J^{\gamma}_{\nu}:=\nabla^{\lambda}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}(F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}])_{\lambda\nu}
is given by the commutation formula of Proposition
5.16
.

Remark 5.23
.

F^{\gamma,\mathrm{hom}}
is well-defined for
|\gamma|=N_{0}+1
and we will see that it is the case of
F^{\gamma,\mathrm{inh}}
as well.

5.3.1 Control of the source term

We will naturally be lead to estimate several times the asymptotic electromagnetic current. We start by controlling it pointwise.

Lemma 5.24
.

Let
h:\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\to\mathbb{R}
be a sufficiently regular function. Then, for all
(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
,

\big|J^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\underline{L}}\big|(t,x)\leq\frac{2}{t^{3}}\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{0}[h]\,\mathds{1}_{t>|x|},\qquad\big|J^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{L}\big|(t,x)\leq\frac{t-|x|}{t^{4}}\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{0}[h]\,\mathds{1}_{t>|x|},\qquad\big|J^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{e_{A}}\big|(t,x)=0,

where
A\in\{\theta,\,\varphi\}
. Moreover, we have improved estimates near the light cone,

\forall(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*}\times\mathbb{R}^{3},\qquad\big|J^{\mathrm{asymp}}\big|(t,x)\leq\frac{(t-|x|)^{p}}{t^{3+p}}\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{0}\big[|v^{0}|^{2p}h\big]\,\mathds{1}_{t>|x|},\qquad\qquad p\in\mathbb{R}_{+}.

Proof.

As
J^{\mathrm{asymp}}[h](t,x)=0
for
|x|\geq t
, we fix
t>|x|
. Then, remark that

J^{\mathrm{asymp}}[h](t,x)=\frac{1}{t^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}\Big[|v^{0}|^{5}h\Big]\bigg(z,\frac{\widecheck{\;x\;}}{t}\bigg)\mathrm{d}z\,\Gamma(t,x),\qquad\Gamma(t,x):=-\mathrm{d}t+\frac{x_{i}}{t}\mathrm{d}x^{i}.

The first three inequalities then follow from

\Gamma_{\underline{L}}(t,x)=-\frac{t+|x|}{t},\qquad\qquad\Gamma_{L}(t,x)=-\frac{t-|x|}{t},\qquad\qquad\Gamma_{e_{A}}(t,x)=0,\quad A\in\{\theta,\,\varphi\}.

For the last one, use that
2|v^{0}|^{2}=2t^{2}(t^{2}-|x|^{2})^{-1}\geq t(t-|x|)^{-1}
if
v=\frac{\widecheck{\;x\;}}{t}
.

We will also be interested in the conservation of the charge.

Lemma 5.25
.

Let
h:\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\to\mathbb{R}
be a sufficiently regular function. Then, for all
\tau>0
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}J_{0}^{\mathrm{asymp}}[h](\tau,x)\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\int_{u=0}^{2\tau}\int_{\mathbb{S}_{\omega}^{2}}J_{\underline{L}}^{\mathrm{asymp}}[h]\Big(\frac{\tau+u}{2},\frac{\tau-u}{2}\omega\Big)\frac{r^{2}}{2}\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\mathrm{d}u=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}h(x,v)\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x.

Proof.

Let
t>0
and recall that
J^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}](t,\cdot)
is supported in
\{|x|\leq t\}
. Remark that

-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}J^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{0}[h](t,x)\mathrm{d}x=\int_{|x|<t_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}\bigg\langle\frac{\widecheck{\;x\;}}{t}\bigg\rangle^{5}h\bigg(z,\frac{\widecheck{\;x\;}}{t}\bigg)\frac{\mathrm{d}x}{t^{3}}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}h(z,v)\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x,

where we used Lemma
2.10
in order to perform the change of variables
x=t\widehat{v}
. Fix now
\tau>0
and recall from Lemma
5.13
that
J^{\mathrm{asymp}}[h]
is divergence free. Consequently, the divergence theorem, applied in the region
\{t+|x|\leq 2\tau,\,t\geq\tau\}
, provides

\int_{|x|\leq\tau}J^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{0}[h](\tau,x)\mathrm{d}x-\int_{u=0}^{2\tau}\int_{\mathbb{S}_{\omega}^{2}}J_{\underline{L}}^{\mathrm{asymp}}[h]\Big(\frac{\tau+u}{2},\frac{\tau-u}{2}\omega\Big)\frac{r^{2}}{2}\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\mathrm{d}u=0.

It remains to use again that
J^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{0}[h](\tau,x)=0
for all
|x|\geq\tau
.

5.3.2 Estimates for integrals on spheres or cones

We are interested here in controlling quantities appearing in the representation formula for a solution to the wave equation with a source term and initial data decaying fast enough.

Lemma 5.26
.

For any function
h\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
and all
(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
,

\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}|h|(x+t\omega)\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\leq\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}12\pi\,\langle t+|x|\rangle^{-1}\,\langle t-|x|\rangle^{-2}\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{3}}\,\langle y\rangle^{3}|h(y)|,\\
8\pi\,t^{-1}\langle t+|x|\rangle^{-1}\,\langle t-|x|\rangle^{-2}\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{3}}\,\langle y\rangle^{4}|h(y)|.\end{array}\right.

Proof.

For all
(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
, we have

\forall\,\omega\in\mathbb{S}^{2},\qquad|x+t\omega|\geq|t-|x||,

(90)

so that

\forall\,(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*}\times\mathbb{R}^{3},\qquad\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}|h|(x+t\omega)\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\leq\frac{1}{\langle t-|x|\rangle}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}|\langle\cdot\rangle\,h|(x+t\omega)\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}.

Then, apply
[
73
, Lemma
4.1
]
to the function
y\mapsto\langle y\rangle\,h(y)
.

We are now able to estimate solutions to the free wave equation.

Lemma 5.27
.

Let
K_{0}\geq 0
and
\phi:\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\to\mathbb{R}
be a solution to
\Box\phi=0
such that
\langle x\rangle^{3}|\phi(0,x)|+\langle x\rangle^{4}|\nabla_{t,x}\phi|(0,x)\leq K_{0}
. Then,

\forall\,(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3},\qquad|\phi|(t,x)\leq 3K_{0}\,\langle t+|x|\rangle^{-1}\,\langle t-|x|\rangle^{-2}.

Proof.

The result follows from Lemma
5.26
and Kirchoff’s formula

4\pi\phi(t,x)=\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\phi(0,x+t\omega)\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}+t\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\omega\cdot\nabla_{x}\phi(0,x+t\omega)+\partial_{t}\phi(0,x+t\omega)\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}.

(91)

An analogous
L^{2}
estimate hold.

Lemma 5.28
.

Let
\phi:\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\to\mathbb{R}
be a sufficiently regular solution to
\Box\phi=0
. Then,

\forall\,t\in\mathbb{R}_{+},\qquad\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\langle t-|x|\rangle^{\frac{5}{2}}|\phi(t,x)|^{2}\mathrm{d}x\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\langle x\rangle^{\frac{5}{2}}|\phi(0,x)|^{2}+\langle x\rangle^{\frac{9}{2}}|\nabla_{t,x}\phi(0,x)|^{2}\mathrm{d}x.

Proof.

By Kirchoff’s formula (
91
), (
90
) and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,

\langle t-|x|\rangle^{\frac{5}{2}}|\phi(t,x)|^{2}\!\lesssim\!\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\langle x+t\omega\rangle^{\frac{5}{2}}|\phi(0,x+t\omega)|^{2}\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}+\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\langle x+t\omega\rangle^{\frac{9}{2}}|\nabla_{t,x}\phi(0,x+t\omega)|^{2}\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\!\frac{\langle t-|x|\rangle^{2}\,t^{2}\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}}{\langle x+t\omega\rangle^{4}}.

By Lemma
5.26
, the second factor of the last term in the right hand side is bounded by
8\pi
. It then remains to integrate over
\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}
, to apply Fubini’s theorem and to perform the change of variables
y(x)=x+t\omega
.

In order to deal with solutions to the inhomogeneous wave equation, we will use the next result.

Lemma 5.29
.

Consider, for any
(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
,
a\geq 4
and
b\geq 7/2
,

\mathcal{K}^{1}_{a}(t,x):=\int_{|y-x|\leq t}\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{\langle t-|y-x|+|y|\rangle^{a}\,|y-x|},\quad\qquad\mathcal{K}^{2}_{b}(t,x):=\int_{|y-x|\leq t}\mathds{1}_{t-|y-x|\geq|y|}\frac{\langle t-|y-x|-|y|\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}y}{\langle t-|y-x|\rangle^{b}\,|y-x|^{2}}.

Then, we have

\mathcal{K}^{1}_{a}(t,x)\lesssim\langle t+|x|\rangle^{-1}\,\langle t-|x|\rangle^{-a+3},\qquad\qquad\mathcal{K}^{2}_{b}(t,x)\lesssim\langle t\rangle^{-2}\langle t-|x|\rangle^{-b+\frac{7}{2}}\,\mathds{1}_{t\geq|x|}.

Proof.

Fix
(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
and remark that

t-|y-x|+|y|\geq t-|y|-|x|+|y|=t-|x|,\qquad t-|y-x|+|y|\geq|y|\geq|x|-|y-x|\geq|x|-t

on the domain of integration, so that
\mathcal{K}^{p}_{a}(t,x)\leq\langle t-|x|\rangle^{-a+4}\,\mathcal{K}^{p}_{4}(t,x)
. It then suffices to treat the case
a=4
and, by continuity,
x\neq 0
. According to
[
28
, Lemma
6.5.2
]
,

\mathcal{K}^{1}_{4}(t,x)=\frac{2\pi}{|x|}\int_{\tau=0}^{t}\int_{\lambda=||x|-t+\tau|}^{|x|+t-\tau}\frac{\lambda\,\mathrm{d}\lambda\mathrm{d}\tau}{\langle\tau+\lambda\rangle^{4}}\leq\frac{2\pi}{|x|}\int_{\tau=0}^{t}\int_{\lambda=||x|-t+\tau|}^{|x|+t-\tau}\frac{\mathrm{d}\lambda\mathrm{d}\tau}{\langle\tau+\lambda\rangle^{3}}.

We perform the change of variables
\underline{u}=\tau+\lambda
and
u=\tau-\lambda
. On the domain of integration, we have
||x|-t|\leq\underline{u}\leq t+|x|
and
u\leq||x|-t|
. Since
2\tau\geq 0
, we have further
u\geq-\underline{u}
, so that

\mathcal{K}^{1}_{4}(t,x)\leq\frac{\pi}{|x|}\int_{\underline{u}=||x|-t|}^{t+|x|}\int_{u=-\underline{u}}^{||x|-t|}\frac{\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}\underline{u}}{\langle\underline{u}\rangle^{3}}\leq\frac{2\pi}{|x|}\int_{\underline{u}=||x|-t|}^{t+|x|}\frac{\mathrm{d}\underline{u}}{\langle\underline{u}\rangle^{2}}\leq\frac{4\pi(1+t+|x|-1-|t-|x||)}{|x|(1+t+|x|)(1+|t-|x||)}.

Since
t+|x|-|t-|x||=2\min(t,|x|)
, this implies the result for
\mathcal{K}^{1}_{4}(t,x)
. Next, since
t-|y-x|-|y|\leq t-|x|
, we have
\mathcal{K}^{2}_{b}(t,x)=0
if
|x|\geq t
. Otherwise
t>|x|
and as previously, it suffices to treat the case
b=7/2
. We have

\mathcal{K}^{2}_{\frac{7}{2}}(t,x)\leq\langle t-|x|\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\,\mathbf{K}_{[0,\frac{t}{2}]}+\langle t-|x|\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\,\mathbf{K}_{[\frac{t}{2},t]},\qquad\qquad\mathbf{K}_{I}:=\int_{|y-x|\in I}\mathds{1}_{t-|y-x|\geq|y|}\frac{\,\mathrm{d}y}{\langle t-|y-x|\rangle^{\frac{7}{2}}\,|y-x|^{2}}.

On the domain of integration of
\mathbf{K}_{[0,\frac{t}{2}]}
, we have
t-|y-x|\geq t/2
, so that

\langle t-|x|\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\,\mathbf{K}_{[0,\frac{t}{2}]}\leq\frac{\langle t-|x|\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}}{2\langle t\rangle^{\frac{7}{2}}}\int_{r=0}^{\frac{t}{2}}\mathrm{d}r=\frac{t\,\langle t-|x|\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}}{4\langle t\rangle^{\frac{7}{2}}}\leq\frac{1}{4}\langle t\rangle^{-2}.

Applying again
[
28
, Lemma
6.5.2
]
, we have

\mathbf{K}_{[\frac{t}{2},t]}=\frac{2\pi}{|x|}\int_{\tau=0}^{\frac{t}{2}}\int_{\lambda=||x|-t+\tau|}^{|x|+t-\tau}\mathds{1}_{\tau\geq\lambda}\frac{\lambda\,\mathrm{d}\lambda\mathrm{d}\tau}{\langle\tau\rangle^{\frac{7}{2}}(t-\tau)}\leq\frac{4\pi}{|x|t}\int_{\tau=0}^{\frac{t}{2}}\frac{(\tau-\min(\tau,||x|-t+\tau|))\mathrm{d}\tau}{\langle\tau\rangle^{\frac{5}{2}}}.

Note that
0\leq 2\tau\leq t-|x|
implies
||x|-t+\tau|\geq\tau
. Since
t\geq|x|
, we then have, for all
0\leq\tau\leq t/2
,

\tau-\min(\tau,||x|-t+\tau|)\leq\min(t-|x|,2\tau-t+|x|)\leq\min(t-|x|,|x|).

We then deduce

\langle t-|x|\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\,\mathbf{K}_{[\frac{t}{2},t]}\lesssim\frac{\langle t-|x|\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\,\min(t-|x|,|x|)}{|x|t}\int_{\tau=\frac{t-|x|}{2}}^{\frac{t}{2}}\frac{\mathrm{d}\tau}{\langle\tau\rangle^{\frac{5}{2}}}\lesssim\frac{\langle t-|x|\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\,\min(t-|x|,|x|)}{|x|\langle t\rangle\,\langle t-|x|\rangle^{\frac{3}{2}}}\lesssim\frac{1}{\langle t\rangle^{2}}.

5.3.3 Study of the homogeneous term

In view of the initial decay of
F^{\gamma,\mathrm{hom}}
, an adaptation of Lemma
5.27
would give
\langle t+|x|\rangle|F^{\gamma,\mathrm{hom}}|(t,x)\lesssim\langle t-|x|\rangle^{-1}
(see for instance
[
8
, Proposition
2.21
]
). However,

•

this estimate merely provides the decay rate
\langle u\rangle^{-1}
for the radiation field of
F^{\gamma,\mathrm{hom}}
. In fact, we will prove that it decays at least as
\langle u\rangle^{-2}
.

•

Moreover, it does not allow us to prove that
F^{\gamma,\mathrm{hom}}
does not contribute to the asymptotic electromagnetic field
\mathbb{F}[f_{\infty}]
. This can only hold if
t^{2}F^{\gamma,\mathrm{hom}}
goes to
0
along timelike straight lines.

Our goal is to improve this naive estimate. The idea consists in isolating the leading order term in the asymptotic expansion of
F^{\gamma,\mathrm{hom}}(t_{0},\cdot)
, which is
\frac{Qx_{i}}{4\pi|x|^{3}}\mathrm{d}t\wedge\mathrm{d}x^{i}
when
|\gamma|=0
. We address these issues by exploiting the solution to the homogeneous wave equation
\widetilde{F}
, introduced in Section
3.6
and estimated in Proposition
3.18
. Indeed,

•

F^{\gamma,\mathrm{hom}}-\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}\widetilde{F}
enjoys stronger decay properties than
F^{\gamma,\mathrm{hom}}
.

•

r|\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}\widetilde{F}(r+u,r\omega)|\lesssim\epsilon\,\mathds{1}_{-1\leq u\leq-2}+O(r^{-1})
, so that the radiation field of
\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}\widetilde{F}
, which can in fact be explicitly computed, is then compactly supported.

•

\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}\widetilde{F}
vanishes in the interior of the light cone, so that
t^{2}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}\widetilde{F}(t,x+t\widehat{v})=0
for
t
large enough.

Let us then prove stronger decay estimates for
F^{\gamma,\mathrm{hom}}
.

Proposition 5.30
.

We have, for any
|\gamma|\leq N_{0}
and all
(t,x)\in[t_{0},+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
,

\left|F^{\gamma,\mathrm{hom}}\right|(t,x)\lesssim_{t_{0}}\langle t+|x|\rangle^{-2}\,\mathds{1}_{|x|-t\geq 1}\,Q+\langle t+|x|\rangle^{-1}\,\langle t-|x|\rangle^{-2}\,\overline{E}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}].

Remark 5.31
.

We will use once, in Section
5.5
that, if
|\gamma|\leq N_{0}-1
and
Z\in\mathbb{K}
,

\forall\,t\geq|x|+2,\qquad\left|\mathcal{L}_{Z}F^{\gamma,\mathrm{hom}}\right|(t,x)\lesssim_{t_{0}}\langle t+|x|\rangle^{-1}\,\langle t-|x|\rangle^{-2}\,\overline{E}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}].

Proof.

Note that
\Box(F^{\gamma,\mathrm{hom}}_{\mu\nu}-\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}(\widetilde{F})_{\mu\nu})=0
by Proposition
3.16
. Next, according to Proposition
5.18
,

\sup_{|\kappa|\leq N_{0}+1-|\gamma|}\,\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{3}}\langle x\rangle^{3+|\kappa|}\big|\nabla_{t,x}^{\kappa}\big(F^{\gamma,\mathrm{hom}}_{\mu\nu}-\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}(\widetilde{F})_{\mu\nu}\big)\big|(t_{0},x)\lesssim_{t_{0}}Q+\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}]\lesssim_{t_{0}}\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}],

where the higher order time derivatives are controlled by exploiting the homogeneous wave equation. Applying Lemma
5.27
, allowing us to estimate solutions to
\Box\phi=0
, we get

\forall\,(t,x)\in[t_{0},+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3},\qquad\big|F^{\gamma,\mathrm{hom}}-\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}(\widetilde{F})\big|(t,x)\lesssim_{t_{0}}\langle t+|x|\rangle^{-1}\,\langle t-|x|\rangle^{-2}\,\overline{E}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}].

(92)

It remains to apply Propositions
3.16
and
3.18
. For the statement of Remark
5.31
, use that for any
2
-form
H
and any
Z\in\mathbb{K}
,

|\mathcal{L}_{Z}(H)_{\mu\nu}|\lesssim\sup_{0\leq\mu<\nu\leq 3}|Z(H)_{\mu\nu}|+|H_{\mu\nu}|,\qquad[\Box,Z]=2\delta_{Z}^{S}\Box,\qquad|ZH_{\mu\nu}|\lesssim\langle x\rangle|\nabla_{t,x}H|

and apply Lemma
5.27
to
\mathcal{L}_{Z}(F^{\gamma,\mathrm{hom}}-\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}(\widetilde{F}))_{\mu\nu}
. The result ensues from Propositions
3.16
and
3.18
.

We need a refined estimate in order to prove Remark
5.20
.

Proposition 5.32
.

Let
Z^{\gamma}\in\mathbb{K}^{|\gamma|}
,
|\gamma|\leq N_{0}
, composed only by homogenous vector fields and containing at least once the scaling
S
. Then,

\forall\,(t,x)\in[t_{0},+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3},\qquad\left|F^{\gamma,\mathrm{hom}}\right|(t,x)\lesssim_{t_{0}}\langle t+|x|\rangle^{-1}\,\langle t-|x|\rangle^{-2}\,\overline{E}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}].

Proof.

Since
[S,\Omega_{\lambda k}]=0
for any
0\leq\lambda<k\leq 3
,
\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}\widetilde{F}
is in that case supported in
\{|t-|x||\leq 2\}
according to Propositions
3.16
and
3.18
. Thus,
|\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}\widetilde{F}|(t,x)\lesssim Q\langle t+|x|\rangle^{-1}\mathds{1}_{|t-|x||\leq 2}
and it remains to use (
92
).

Finally, we apply Proposition
5.18
together with Lemma
5.28
in order to derive an
L^{2}
estimate.

Proposition 5.33
.

For any
|\gamma|\leq N_{0}+1
,

\forall\,t\in\mathbb{R}_{+},\qquad\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\langle t-|x|\rangle^{\frac{5}{2}}\big|F^{\gamma,\mathrm{hom}}-\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}\widetilde{F}\big|^{2}(t,x)\mathrm{d}x\bigg|^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}].

5.3.4 Naive estimate of the inhomogeneous term

We start by a computation which will allow us to rewrite the source term of the wave equation satisfied by
F^{\gamma,\mathrm{inh}}
.

Lemma 5.34
.

Let
h:\mathbb{R}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\to\mathbb{R}
a sufficiently regular function and
1\leq i<j\leq 3
,
1\leq k\leq 3
. Then,

\displaystyle\partial_{x^{j}}J^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{i}\big[h\big]-\partial_{x^{i}}J^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{j}\big[h\big]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{t^{4}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\Big[|v^{0}|^{5}\widehat{\Omega}_{ij}h\Big]\bigg(z,\frac{\widecheck{\;x\;}}{t}\bigg)\mathrm{d}z,

\displaystyle\partial_{x^{k}}J^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{0}\big[h\big]-\partial_{t}J^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{k}\big[h\big]

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{t^{4}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\Big[|v^{0}|^{5}\widehat{\Omega}_{0k}^{\infty}h\Big]\bigg(z,\frac{\widecheck{\;x\;}}{t}\bigg)\mathrm{d}z.

Proof.

Recall that
J^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\ell}[h](t,x)=-\frac{x_{\ell}}{t}J^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{0}[h](t,x)
.

•

As
\partial_{x^{i}}(x_{j}/t)=\partial_{x^{j}}(x_{j}/t)=0
and
\Omega_{ij}=x^{i}\partial_{x^{j}}-x^{j}\partial_{x^{i}}
,

\partial_{x^{j}}J^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{i}\big[h\big]-\partial_{x^{i}}J^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{j}\big[h\big]=-t^{-1}\,\Omega_{ij}\big(J^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{0}[h]\big).

•

Since
\partial_{t}(x_{k}/t)=-x_{k}/t^{2}
and
\Omega_{0k}=t\partial_{x^{k}}+x^{k}\partial_{t}
, one gets

\partial_{x^{k}}J^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{0}\big[h\big]-\partial_{t}J^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{k}\big[h\big]=\frac{1}{t}\Omega_{0k}\big(J^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{0}\big[h\big]\big)-\frac{x_{k}}{t^{2}}J^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{0}\big[h\big]=\frac{1}{t}\Omega_{0k}\big(J^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{0}\big[h\big]\big)+\frac{1}{t}J^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{k}\big[h\big].

The result follows from Lemma
5.13
and
\mathcal{L}_{Z}(J)_{0}=Z(J_{0})+\partial_{t}Z^{\mu}J_{\mu}
, for any
1
-form
J
and vector field
Z
.

We are now able to derive a general estimate, which will allow us, together with Proposition
5.30
, to prove the first part of Proposition
5.19
.

Proposition 5.35
.

Let
Z^{\gamma}\in\mathbb{K}^{|\gamma|}
with
|\gamma|\leq N_{0}
. We have,

\forall\,(t,x)\in[t_{0},+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3},\qquad|F^{\gamma,\mathrm{inh}}|(t,x)\lesssim_{t_{0}}\langle t+|x|\rangle^{-1}\,\langle t-|x|\rangle^{-1-\gamma_{T}}\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}].

If
Z^{\gamma}
is only composed by homogeneous vector fields and contains at least once the scaling
S
, then
F^{\gamma,\mathrm{inh}}=0
.

Proof.

Assume first that
\gamma_{T}=0
. Combining the commutation formula of Proposition
5.16
with the previous Lemma
5.34
, one has

\left|\Box F^{\gamma,\mathrm{inh}}\right|(t,x)\lesssim_{t_{0}}\frac{1}{t^{4}}\sup_{|\beta|=|\gamma|+1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\Big[|v^{0}|^{5}\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\beta}h\Big]\bigg(z,\frac{\widecheck{\;x\;}}{t}\bigg)\mathrm{d}z\lesssim_{t_{0}}\frac{1}{\langle t+|x|\rangle^{4}}\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{|\gamma|+1}[f_{\infty}],

for all
t\in[t_{0},+\infty[
and
|x|<t
. Since
\Box F^{\gamma,\mathrm{inh}}
is supported in the interior of the light cone
|x|\leq t
, the estimate is then provided by Lemma
5.29
. The last part of the lemma is clear since
\Box F^{\gamma,\mathrm{inh}}=0
in that case according to Proposition
5.16
. If
\gamma_{T}\geq 1
, then we have similarly

\left|\Box F^{\gamma,\mathrm{inh}}\right|(t,x)\lesssim_{t_{0}}\frac{1}{t^{4+\gamma_{T}}}\sup_{|\kappa|=\gamma_{T}+1}\,\sup_{|\beta|\leq|\gamma|-\gamma_{T}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\Big[|v^{0}|^{5}D^{\kappa}_{t,x}\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\beta}h\Big]\bigg(z,\frac{\widecheck{\;x\;}}{t}\bigg)\mathrm{d}z\lesssim_{t_{0}}\frac{1}{\langle t+|x|\rangle^{4+\gamma_{T}}}\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{|\gamma|+1}[f_{\infty}]

and we apply again Lemma
5.29
.

5.3.5 Gain of regularity

We now prove a more precise estimate for
F^{\gamma,\mathrm{inh}}
in the case where either
|\gamma|=0
or
Z^{\gamma}
is only composed by Lorentz boosts
\Omega_{0k}
and rotational vector fields
\Omega_{ij}
. The representation formula for the solution to the wave equation provides an explicit expression for
F^{\gamma,\mathrm{inh}}
. According to the commutation formula of Proposition
5.16
and Lemma
5.34
, we have, for all
(t,x)\in[t_{0},+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
,

F^{\gamma,\mathrm{inh}}_{\mu\nu}(t,x)=\mathfrak{s}_{\mu\nu}\int_{|y-x|\leq t-t_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}\Big[|v^{0}|^{5}\Omega^{\infty}_{\mu\nu}\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\gamma}f_{\infty}\Big]\bigg(z,\frac{\widecheck{\;\;\;y\;\;\;}}{t-|y-x|}\bigg)\mathrm{d}z\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{(t-|y-x|)^{4}|y-x|},

for any
0\leq\mu<\nu\leq 3
, where
\mathfrak{s}_{\mu\nu}=1
if
\mu\neq 0
and
\mathfrak{s}_{0\nu}=-1
. This formula is not satisfying since we would like to express
F^{\gamma,\mathrm{inh}}
as a functional of
\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}_{\infty}f_{\infty}
instead of a functional of its derivatives. Our strategy relies on the Glassey-Strauss decomposition of the electromagnetic field,
[
32
, Theorem
3
]
. In order to fit into the framework of Glassey-Strauss, we introduce the following integral kernels.

Definition 5.36
.

Let, for all
(\omega,v)\in\mathbb{S}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
,
\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{T}}_{\mu\nu}(\omega,v)
be the antisymmetric valued matrix defined by

\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{T}}_{0k}(\omega,v)=-\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{T}}_{k0}(\omega,v):=\frac{\omega_{k}+\widehat{v}_{k}}{|v^{0}|^{2}(1+\omega\cdot\widehat{v})^{2}},\qquad\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{T}}_{ij}(\omega,v):=\frac{\omega_{i}\widehat{v}_{j}-\omega_{j}\widehat{v}_{i}}{|v^{0}|^{2}(1+\omega\cdot\widehat{v})^{2}},\qquad 1\leq i,\,j,\,k\leq 3,

where
\omega_{i}:=x_{i}/|x|
if
x\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\setminus\{0\}
satisfies
\omega=x/|x|
. Let further, for any
1\leq\ell\leq 3
,

\mathbf{a}^{\ell}_{0k}(\omega,z)=-\mathbf{a}^{\ell}_{k0}(\omega,z):=\delta^{\ell}_{k}-\frac{\omega_{k}+\widehat{v}_{k}}{1+\omega\cdot z}\widehat{v}^{\ell},\qquad\mathbf{a}^{\ell}_{ij}(\omega,v):=\widehat{v}_{j}\,\delta^{\ell}_{i}-\widehat{v}_{i}\,\delta^{\ell}_{j}+\frac{\widehat{v}_{i}\omega_{j}-\widehat{v}_{j}\omega_{i}}{1+\omega\cdot\widehat{v}}\widehat{v}^{\ell},\qquad\mathbf{a}^{\ell}_{00}(\omega,v):=0.

Remark 5.37
.

For any
\mu\in\llbracket 0,3\rrbracket
,
\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{T}}_{\mu\mu}=0
since
\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{T}}
is antisymmetric. Note also
1+\omega\cdot\widehat{v}=2v^{L}>0
.

The only informations that we will need on these functions are contained in the next result.

Lemma 5.38
.

For all
(\omega,v)\in\mathbb{S}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
and any
0\leq\mu,\,\nu\leq 3
,
|\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{T}}_{\mu\nu}(\omega,v)|\leq 4\langle v\rangle
and
|\mathbf{a}^{\ell}_{\mu\nu}(\omega,v)|\leq 4\langle v\rangle
.

Proof.

We refer to the first two inequalities of
[
8
, Corollary
5.5
]
.

In order to lighten the presentation of the next result, we will use the convention that
\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}_{\infty}f_{\infty}(z,\widecheck{u})=0
if
|u|\geq 1
.

Proposition 5.39
.

Let
Z^{\gamma}\in\mathbb{K}^{|\gamma|}
,
|\gamma|\leq N_{0}
, be only composed by homogeneous vector fields,
\gamma_{T}=0
. Then,

\forall\,(t,x)\in[t_{0},+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3},\qquad F^{\gamma,\mathrm{inh}}_{\mu\nu}(t,x;t_{0})=F^{\gamma,\mathrm{T}}_{\mu\nu}(t,x;t_{0})-F^{\gamma,\mathrm{sph}}_{\mu\nu}(t,x;t_{0}),

where, for any
0\leq\mu,\,\nu\leq 3
,

•

F^{\gamma,\mathrm{T}}=F^{\gamma,\mathrm{T},[0,t-t_{0}]}
and, for any
I\subset[0,t]
,

\displaystyle F^{\gamma,\mathrm{T},I}_{\mu\nu}(t,x;t_{0})

\displaystyle\qquad\;:=\int_{|y-x|\in I}\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{T}}_{\mu\nu}\bigg(\frac{y-x}{|y-x|},\frac{\widecheck{\qquad y\qquad}}{t-|y-x|}\bigg)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}\Big[|v^{0}|^{5}\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\gamma}f_{\infty}\Big]\bigg(z,\frac{\widecheck{\qquad y\qquad}}{t-|y-x|}\bigg)\mathrm{d}z\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{(t-|y-x|)^{3}|y-x|^{2}}.

•

F^{\gamma,\mathrm{sph}}_{\mu\nu}(t,x;t_{0})
is given as an integral over
\mathbb{S}^{2}
,

F^{\gamma,\mathrm{sph}}_{\mu\nu}(t,x;t_{0})=\frac{t-t_{0}}{t_{0}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\sigma}}\sigma_{k}\,\mathbf{a}^{k}_{\mu\nu}\left(\sigma,\frac{\widecheck{\;x+(t-t_{0})\sigma\;}}{t_{0}}\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}\Big[|v^{0}|^{5}\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}_{\infty}f_{\infty}\Big]\bigg(z,\frac{\widecheck{\;x+(t-t_{0})\sigma\;}}{t_{0}}\bigg)\mathrm{d}z\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\sigma}}.

Remark 5.40
.

As a consequence,
F^{\gamma,\mathrm{inh}}
is well-defined for
|\gamma|=N_{0}+1
as well.

Proof.

Let
|\gamma|\leq N_{0}
,
(\psi_{n})_{n\geq 1}
be a mollifier and
H^{n}
be a solution to

\nabla^{\mu}H^{n}_{\mu\nu}=J(h_{n})_{\nu},\quad\nabla^{\mu}{}^{*}\!H^{n}_{\mu\nu}=0,\qquad\qquad h_{n}(t,x,v):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}_{\infty}f_{\infty}(z,v)\mathrm{d}z\,\psi_{n}(x-t\widehat{v}).

Let further
H^{n,\mathrm{inh}}
be the unique solution to

\Box H^{n,\mathrm{inh}}_{\mu\nu}=\Box H^{n}_{\mu\nu}=\partial_{x^{\nu}}J(h_{n})_{\mu}-\partial_{x^{\mu}}J(h_{n})_{\nu},\qquad\qquad\qquad H^{n,\mathrm{inh}}_{\mu\nu}(t_{0},\cdot)=\partial_{t}H^{n,\mathrm{inh}}_{\mu\nu}(t_{0},\cdot)=0.

According to
[
32
, Theorem
3
]
(see also
[
8
, Proposition
5.3
]
), we have the decomposition

H^{n,\mathrm{inh}}_{\mu\nu}=-H^{\mathrm{sph}}_{\mu\nu}+H^{\mathrm{T}}_{\mu\nu}+H^{S}_{\mu\nu},

where

•

H^{\mathrm{sph}}_{\mu\nu}
is purely determined by the initial data since

\displaystyle H^{\mathrm{sph}}_{\mu\nu}(t,x)

\displaystyle:=(t-t_{0})\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\sigma}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\sigma_{k}\,\mathbf{a}^{k}_{\mu\nu}\left(\sigma,v\right)h_{n}(t_{0},x+(t-t_{0})\sigma,v)\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\sigma}}.

•

H^{\mathrm{T}}_{\mu\nu}
is given by

H^{\mathrm{T}}_{\mu\nu}(t,x):=\int_{|y-x|\leq t-t_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{T}}_{\mu\nu}\bigg(\frac{y-x}{|y-x|},v\bigg)h_{n}(t-|t-x|,y,v)\mathrm{d}v\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{|y-x|^{2}}.

•

H^{S}_{\mu\nu}
is a functional of
\mathbf{T}_{0}(h_{n})
. Since
h_{n}
is solution to the linear Vlasov equation, this term vanishes.

By Lemma
5.6
,
H^{\mathrm{sph}}_{\mu\nu}
and
H^{\mathrm{T}}_{\mu\nu}
converge respectively, as
n\to\infty
, to
F^{\gamma,\mathrm{sph}}_{\mu\nu}(\cdot,\cdot\,;t_{0})
and
F^{\gamma,\mathrm{T}}_{\mu\nu}(\cdot,\cdot\,;t_{0})
.

We are now able to derive the following estimates.

Proposition 5.41
.

Let
Z^{\gamma}\in\mathbb{K}^{|\gamma|}
, with
|\gamma|\leq N_{0}+1
, only composed by homogeneous vector fields. Then,

\displaystyle\forall\,(t,x)\in[t_{0},+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3},\qquad\qquad\left|F^{\gamma,\mathrm{T}}\right|(t,x;t_{0})

\displaystyle\lesssim t^{-2}\,\mathds{1}_{t>|x|}\,\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{0}\big[\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}_{\infty}f_{\infty}\big],

\displaystyle\left|F^{\gamma,\mathrm{sph}}\right|(t,x;t_{0})

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{t_{0}\,t}\,\mathds{1}_{0\leq t-|x|\leq 2t_{0}}\,\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{0}\big[\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}_{\infty}f_{\infty}\big].

Proof.

Recall from Proposition
5.39
the definition of
F^{\gamma,\mathrm{T}}
and
F^{\gamma,\mathrm{sph}}
and remark that they vanish in the exterior of the light cone. Indeed, if
|x|\geq t
, then the domain of integration is included in
\{(\tau,y)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\;|\;|y|\geq\tau\}
, where the integrands vanish. We then fix
t\geq t_{0}
and
|x|<t
. According to Lemma
5.38
and by support considerations,

\left|F^{\gamma,\mathrm{sph}}\right|(t,x;t_{0})\lesssim\frac{t-t_{0}}{t_{0}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\sigma}}\mathds{1}_{\big|\frac{x}{t_{0}}+\frac{t-t_{0}}{t_{0}}\sigma\big|<1}\,\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\sigma}}\sup_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\,\langle z\rangle^{4}\langle v\rangle^{6}\big|\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}_{\infty}f_{\infty}|(z,v).

Since the right hand side vanishes if
|t-t_{0}-|x||\geq t_{0}
, we get using the estimate of Lemma
5.26
for integrals over
\mathbb{S}^{2}
,

\frac{t-t_{0}}{t_{0}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\sigma}}\mathds{1}_{\big|\frac{x}{t_{0}}+\frac{t-t_{0}}{t_{0}}\sigma\big|<1}\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\lesssim\frac{t-t_{0}}{t_{0}^{3}}\frac{t_{0}}{t-t_{0}}\left\langle\frac{t-t_{0}+|x|}{t_{0}}\right\rangle^{-1}\mathds{1}_{t-|x|\leq 2t_{0}}\lesssim\frac{1}{t_{0}\,t}\mathds{1}_{t-|x|\leq 2t_{0}}.

Using again Lemma
5.38
as well as
\sqrt{2}v^{0}=\sqrt{2}t(t^{2}-|x|^{2})^{-1/2}\geq\sqrt{t}(t-|x|)^{-1/2}
if
\widehat{v}=x/t
, there holds

\left|F^{\gamma,\mathrm{T}}\right|(t,x;t_{0})\lesssim\mathcal{K}(t,x;t_{0})\sup_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\,\langle z\rangle^{4}\langle v\rangle^{7}\big|\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}_{\infty}f_{\infty}|(z,v),

with

\mathcal{K}(t,x;t_{0}):=\int_{|y-x|\leq t-t_{0}}\mathds{1}_{t-|y-x|>|y|}\frac{(t-|y-x|-|y|)^{\frac{1}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}y}{(t-|y-x|)^{\frac{7}{2}}|y-x|^{2}}.

Performing the change of variables
y^{\prime}=y/t_{0}
, we get

\mathcal{K}(t,x;t_{0})\lesssim\frac{1}{t_{0}^{2}}\int_{\big|y^{\prime}-\frac{x}{t_{0}}\big|\leq\frac{t-t_{0}}{t_{0}}}\mathds{1}_{\frac{t}{t_{0}}-|y^{\prime}-\frac{x}{t_{0}}|>|y^{\prime}|}\frac{\big(1+\frac{t-t_{0}}{t_{0}}-\big|y^{\prime}-\frac{x}{t_{0}}\big|-|y^{\prime}|\big)^{1/2}}{\big(1+\frac{t-t_{0}}{t_{0}}-\big|y^{\prime}-\frac{x}{t_{0}}\big|\big)^{7/2}}\frac{\mathrm{d}y^{\prime}}{\big|y^{\prime}-\frac{x}{t_{0}}\big|^{2}}\lesssim\frac{1}{t_{0}^{2}}\mathcal{K}^{2}_{7/2}\!\left(\frac{t-t_{0}}{t_{0}},\frac{x}{t_{0}}\right),

where
\mathcal{K}^{2}_{7/2}
is defined and estimated in Lemma
5.29
. To conclude the proof, note that for
t\geq t_{0}
, we have
t_{0}\langle(t-t_{0})/t_{0}\rangle\gtrsim t
.

5.3.6 Asymptotic electromagnetic field

We are now ready to determine the asymptotic expansion of
F^{\gamma,\mathrm{inh}}
. To write the leading order term in a synthetic way, we introduce the following quantity, which turns out to be an alternative expression for (
14
)–(
15
).

Definition 5.42
.

We define, for any sufficiently regular function
h:\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\to\mathbb{R}
,

\mathbb{F}_{\mu\nu}\big[h\big]:v\mapsto\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}|y|\leq 1\\
|y+\widehat{v}|<1-|y|\end{subarray}}\mathbf{w}^{T}_{\mu\nu}\!\bigg(\frac{y}{|y|},\frac{\widecheck{y+\widehat{v}}}{1-|y|}\bigg)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}\Big[|v^{0}|^{5}h\Big]\!\bigg(z,\frac{\widecheck{y+\widehat{v}}}{1-|y|}\bigg)\mathrm{d}z\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{(1-|y|)^{3}|y|^{2}}.

In fact, by assuming more regularity on
h
, we have

\mathbb{F}_{\mu\nu}\big[h\big](v)=\mathfrak{s}_{\mu\nu}\!\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}|y|\leq 1\\
|y+\widehat{v}|<1-|y|\end{subarray}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}\Big[|v^{0}|^{5}\widehat{\Omega}^{\infty}_{\mu\nu}h\Big]\!\bigg(z,\frac{\widecheck{y+\widehat{v}}}{1-|y|}\bigg)\mathrm{d}z\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{(1-|y|)^{4}|y|},\quad\mathfrak{s}_{\mu\nu}\!=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}-1&\mbox{if $\mu=0$, $1\leq\nu\leq 3$,}\\
1&\mbox{if $1\leq\mu<\nu\leq 3$}.\end{array}\right.

Remark 5.43
.

It is important to observe that the domain of integration is included in
\{0\leq|y|\leq\frac{1+|\widehat{v}|}{2}\}
, so that
1-|y|\geq\frac{1}{4\langle v\rangle^{2}}
. Indeed, if
|y|\geq\frac{1+|\widehat{v}|}{2}
, we have

|y+\widehat{v}|\geq|y|-1+1-|\widehat{v}|\geq\frac{1-|\widehat{v}|}{2}\geq 1-|y|.

Proposition 5.44
.

Let
Z^{\gamma}\in\mathbb{K}^{|\gamma|}
,
|\gamma|\leq N+1
, composed only by homogeneous vector fields. Then,

•

for all
(t,x)\in[t_{0},+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
such that
|x|\geq t
,
F^{\gamma,\mathrm{inh}}(t,x)=0
.

•

There exists a remainder
2
-form
F^{\gamma,\mathrm{rem}}
such that, for all
(t,x)\in[t_{0},+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
verifying
|x|<t
,

F^{\gamma,\mathrm{inh}}(t,x;t_{0})=\frac{1}{t^{2}}\mathbb{F}\big[\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}_{\infty}f_{\infty}\big]\!\left(\frac{\widecheck{\;x\;}}{t}\right)-F^{\gamma,\mathrm{rem}}_{\mu\nu}(t,x;t_{0}).

Furthermore, the remainder verifies

\left|F^{\gamma,\mathrm{rem}}_{\mu\nu}(t,x;t_{0})\right|\lesssim\frac{1}{t_{0}\,t}\mathds{1}_{t-|x|\leq 2t_{0}}\,\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{0}\big[\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}_{\infty}f_{\infty}\big].

•

For all
v\in\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
, we have
\big|\mathbb{F}\big[\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\gamma}f_{\infty}\big](v)\big|\lesssim\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{0}\big[\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}_{\infty}f_{\infty}\big]
.

Proof.

Let
0\leq\mu<\nu\leq 3
and
(t,x)\in[t_{0},+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
. The result for the case
|x|\geq t
follows from

F^{\gamma,\mathrm{inh}}(t_{0},\cdot\,;t_{0})=\partial_{t}F^{\gamma,\mathrm{inh}}(t_{0},\cdot\,;t_{0})=0,\qquad\qquad\forall\,|x^{\prime}|\geq t^{\prime}>0,\quad\Box F^{\gamma,\mathrm{inh}}(t^{\prime},x^{\prime};t_{0})=0.

Assume now that
|x|<t
and let, with the shorthand
t_{y}:=t-|t-x|
,

F^{\gamma,\mathrm{rem}}_{\mu\nu}(t,x;t_{0}):=\int_{t-t_{0}<|y-x|\leq t}\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{T}}_{\mu\nu}\bigg(\frac{y-x}{|y-x|},\frac{\widecheck{\;y\;}}{t_{y}}\bigg)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}\Big[|v^{0}|^{5}\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\gamma}f_{\infty}\Big]\bigg(z,\frac{\widecheck{\;y\;}}{t_{y}}\bigg)\mathrm{d}z\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{t_{y}^{3}|y-x|^{2}}+F^{\gamma,\mathrm{sph}}_{\mu\nu}(t,x;t_{0}).

Then, the stated decomposition of
F^{\gamma,\mathrm{inh}}
follows from Proposition
5.39
as well as the change of variables
y^{\prime}=t^{-1}(y-x)
. Next, remark that if
2t_{0}<t-|x|
, then
F^{\gamma,\mathrm{rem}}_{\mu\nu}(t,x;t_{0})=0
. Indeed, in that case,

|y|\geq|y-x|-|x|\geq t-t_{0}-|x|>t_{0}\geq t-|y-x|,\qquad\qquad F^{\gamma,\mathrm{sph}}_{\mu\nu}(t,x;t_{0})=0.

For the pointwise estimate, fix
v\in\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
and remark that
t-|t\widehat{v}|>2
for
t\geq 4\langle v\rangle^{2}
. Hence,

\forall\,t\geq 4\langle v\rangle^{2},\qquad\qquad F^{\gamma,\mathrm{rem}}(t,t\widehat{v};1)=0,\qquad t^{2}F^{\gamma,\mathrm{inh}}(t,t\widehat{v};1)=\mathbb{F}[\Omega_{v}^{\gamma}f_{\infty}](v).

It remains to apply Proposition
5.41
in order to get, for all
t\geq 4\langle v\rangle^{2}
,

|t^{2}F^{\gamma,\mathrm{inh}}(t,t\widehat{v};1)|=|t^{2}F^{\gamma,\mathrm{T}}(t,t\widehat{v};1)|\lesssim\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{0}\big[\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}_{\infty}f_{\infty}\big].

Finally, we derive the estimate for the remainder on its support using Proposition
5.41
,

t|F^{\gamma,\mathrm{rem}}|(t,x;t_{0})\lesssim t|F^{\gamma,\mathrm{inh}}|(t,x)+t^{-1}\big\|\mathbb{F}\big[\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}_{\infty}f_{\infty}\big]\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{v}}\lesssim t_{0}^{-1}\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{0}\big[\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}_{\infty}f_{\infty}\big].

Note that for all
(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}^{*}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
such that
|x|<t
, we have
F^{\gamma,\mathrm{rem}}_{\mu\nu}(t,x;t_{0})\to 0
, as
t_{0}\to 0
. Furthermore,
J^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}](t_{0},\cdot)
converges to the Dirac
\delta
function as
t_{0}\to 0
. Consequently, for all
|x|<t
,
F^{\gamma,\mathrm{hom}}(t,x)\to 0
as
t_{0}\to 0
, so that Proposition
5.3
holds.

We assume, for the rest of this section, that
1\leq t_{0}\leq 2
.

5.3.7 Proof of Proposition
5.19

Let
Z^{\gamma}\in\mathbb{K}^{|\gamma|}
with
|\gamma|\leq N_{0}
.

•

We have
|\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]|(t,x)\lesssim\langle t+|x|\rangle^{-1}\,\langle t-|x|\rangle^{-1}\,\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}]
according to Proposition
5.30
and
5.35
. The improved estimate, in the case
\gamma_{T}\geq 1
, then follows from Lemma
3.9
.

•

If
Z^{\gamma}
is only composed by homogeneous vector fields and contains the scaling
S
, the estimate stated in Remark
5.20
follows from Propositions
5.32
and
5.35
.

•

Finally, for the last part of the statement, remark first that by integration by parts in
z
,
\mathbb{F}\big[\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\gamma}f_{\infty}\big]=0
if
\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\gamma}
contains at least one translation or
\widehat{S}
. Thus, there is nothing more to prove in these cases. Otherwise
Z^{\gamma}
is only composed by boosts and rotations
\Omega_{\lambda k}
and one gets the result by combining Propositions
5.30
and
5.44
.

5.4 Estimates for the asymptotic electromagnetic field and the correction coefficients

In order to study algebraic relatons between
\mathbb{F}[\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}_{\infty}f_{\infty}]
,
|\gamma|\leq N_{0}+1
, we will make use of

\forall\,(t,v)\in[t_{0},+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v},\qquad\big|t^{2}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}\big(F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]\big)(t,t\widehat{v})-\mathbb{F}\big[\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}_{\infty}f_{\infty}\big](v)\big|\lesssim_{t_{0}}\langle t\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}}\,\langle v\rangle^{3}\,\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}],

(93)

for any
|\gamma|\leq N_{0}
. If
t-t|\widehat{v}|\geq 2t_{0}
, we use
2t-2t|\widehat{v}|\geq t\,\langle v\rangle^{-2}
as well as the precise estimate for
\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]
, for the interior of the light cone, given by Proposition
5.19
. Otherwise
t-t|\widehat{v}|\leq 4
, so that
t\,\langle v\rangle^{-2}\leq 8
and
we apply Propositions
5.19
and
5.44
in order to get

\big|t^{2}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}\big(F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]\big)(t,t\widehat{v})-\mathbb{F}^{\gamma}[f_{\infty}](v)\big|\leq t^{2}\big|\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]\big|(t,t\widehat{v})+\big|\mathbb{F}\big[\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}_{\infty}f_{\infty}\big]\big|(v)\lesssim\langle t\rangle\,\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}].

We are now able to relate
\mathbb{F}[\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}_{\infty}f_{\infty}]
to derivatives of
\mathbb{F}[f_{\infty}]
. In fact, in the context of the Vlasov-Maxwell system,
\mathbb{F}[f_{\infty}]
will only appear through the asymptotic Lorentz force
\widehat{v}^{\mu}{\mathbb{F}_{\mu}}^{j}[f_{\infty}]\partial_{x^{j}}
. For this reason, we also study this quantity.

Proposition 5.45
.

Let
h\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v},\mathbb{R})
such that
\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{1}[h]<+\infty
. The asymptotic electromagnetic field
\mathbb{F}[h]
verifies the following properties.

1.

Algebraic relations between the derivatives of
\mathbb{F}[h]
and
\mathbb{F}[\widehat{Z}_{\infty}f_{\infty}]
, for
\widehat{Z}_{\infty}\in\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{S}^{\infty}
. There holds

\displaystyle\mathbb{F}_{\mu\nu}\big[\widehat{\Omega}_{0k}^{\infty}h\big]

\displaystyle:=v^{0}\partial_{v^{k}}\mathbb{F}_{\mu\nu}[h]-2\widehat{v}^{k}\,\mathbb{F}_{\mu\nu}[h]+\delta_{\mu}^{0}\,\mathbb{F}_{k\nu}[h]+\delta_{\mu}^{k}\,\mathbb{F}_{0\nu}[h]+\delta_{\nu}^{0}\,\mathbb{F}_{\mu k}[h]+\delta_{\nu}^{k}\,\mathbb{F}_{\mu 0}[h],\quad

\displaystyle 1\leq k\leq 3,

\displaystyle\mathbb{F}_{\mu\nu}\big[\widehat{\Omega}_{jk}h\big]

\displaystyle:=\big(v^{j}\partial_{v^{k}}-v^{k}\partial_{v^{j}}\big)\mathbb{F}_{\mu\nu}[h]+\delta_{\mu}^{j}\,\mathbb{F}_{k\nu}[h]-\delta_{\mu}^{k}\,\mathbb{F}_{j\nu}[h]+\delta_{\nu}^{j}\,\mathbb{F}_{\mu k}[h]-\delta_{\nu}^{k}\,\mathbb{F}_{\mu j}[h],\quad\;1\leq

\displaystyle\,j<k\leq 3.

Otherwise
\widehat{Z}_{\infty}\in\{\partial_{t}^{\infty},\,\partial_{z^{1}},\,\partial_{z^{2}},\,\partial_{z^{3}},\,S\}
and
\mathbb{F}\big[\widehat{Z}_{\infty}h\big]=0
.

2.

Algebraic relations between the derivatives of
\widehat{v}^{\mu}\mathbb{F}_{\mu i}[h]
and
\widehat{v}^{\mu}\mathbb{F}_{\mu\ell}[\widehat{Z}_{\infty}f_{\infty}]
, for
\widehat{Z}_{\infty}\in\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{S}^{\infty}
and
1\leq i\leq 3
,

\displaystyle\frac{\widehat{v}^{\mu}}{v^{0}}\mathbb{F}_{\mu i}\big[\widehat{\Omega}^{\infty}_{0k}h\big](v)

\displaystyle:=v^{0}\partial_{v^{k}}\!\left(\frac{\widehat{v}^{\mu}}{v^{0}}\mathbb{F}_{\mu i}[h](v)\right)-\delta^{k}_{i}\,\widehat{v}^{\ell}\frac{\widehat{v}^{\mu}}{v^{0}}\mathbb{F}_{\mu\ell}[h](v),

\displaystyle\frac{\widehat{v}^{\mu}}{v^{0}}\mathbb{F}_{\mu i}\big[\widehat{\Omega}_{ik}h\big](v)

\displaystyle:=\big(v^{j}\partial_{v^{k}}-v^{k}\partial_{v^{j}}\big)\!\left(\frac{\widehat{v}^{\mu}}{v^{0}}\mathbb{F}_{\mu i}[h](v)\!\right)+\delta_{i}^{j}\,\frac{\widehat{v}^{\mu}}{v^{0}}\mathbb{F}_{\mu k}[h](v)-\delta_{i}^{k}\,\frac{\widehat{v}^{\mu}}{v^{0}}\mathbb{F}_{\mu j}[h](v).

3.

Uniform bound for
\mathbb{F}[h]
,

\forall\,v\in\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v},\qquad\big|\mathbb{F}[h]\big|(v)\lesssim\sup_{(z,p)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\langle z\rangle^{4}\,\langle p\rangle^{7}\big|h(z,p)\big|.

Proof.

By a density argument, we can reduce the proof to the case
\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{2}[h]<+\infty
. For any sufficiently regular
2
-form
G
, we have

\displaystyle v^{0}\partial_{v^{k}}\!\left(G_{\mu\nu}(t,t\widehat{v})\right)

\displaystyle=t\left(\delta_{k}^{i}-\widehat{v}^{k}\widehat{v}^{i}\right)\partial_{x^{i}}\!\left(G_{\mu\nu}\right)(t,t\widehat{v})=\left(\Omega_{0k}G_{\mu\nu}\right)\!(t,t\widehat{v})-t\widehat{v}^{k}\partial_{t}\!\left(G_{\mu\nu}\right)\!(t,t\widehat{v})-t\widehat{v}^{k}\widehat{v}^{i}\partial_{x^{i}}\!\left(G_{\mu\nu}\right)\!(t,t\widehat{v})

\displaystyle=\left(\Omega_{0k}G_{\mu\nu}\right)(t,t\widehat{v})-\widehat{v}^{k}\left(SG_{\mu\nu}\right)(t,t\widehat{v})

as well as

\mathcal{L}_{S}(G)_{\mu\nu}=S\left(G_{\mu\nu}\right)+2G_{\mu\nu},\qquad\mathcal{L}_{\Omega_{0k}}(G)_{\mu\nu}=\Omega_{0k}\left(G_{\mu\nu}\right)+\delta_{\mu}^{0}G_{k\nu}+\delta_{\mu}^{k}G_{0\nu}+\delta_{\nu}^{0}G_{\mu k}+\delta_{\nu}^{k}G_{\mu 0}.

Applying these relations for
G=F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[h]
and using the convergence estimate (
93
) for
f_{\infty}=h
, we get the first identity as
\mathbb{F}[\widehat{S}h]=0
. The case of the angular derivatives can be treated similarly since

(v^{j}\partial_{v^{k}}-v^{k}\partial_{v^{j}})\left(G_{\mu\nu}(t,t\widehat{v})\right)=\left(\Omega_{jk}G_{\mu\nu}\right)(t,t\widehat{v}),\qquad\mathcal{L}_{\Omega_{jk}}(G)_{\mu\nu}=\Omega_{jk}\left(G_{\mu\nu}\right)+\delta_{\mu}^{j}G_{k\nu}-\delta_{\mu}^{k}G_{j\nu}+\delta_{\nu}^{j}G_{\mu k}-\delta_{\nu}^{k}G_{\mu j}.

Next, since
\widehat{v}^{\mu}/v^{0}=v^{\mu}/|v^{0}|^{2}
, we have

\displaystyle v^{0}\partial_{v^{k}}\!\left(\frac{\widehat{v}^{\mu}}{v^{0}}\mathbb{F}_{\mu i}[h](v)\!\right)

\displaystyle=\widehat{v}^{\mu}\partial_{v^{k}}\mathbb{F}_{\mu i}[h](v)-2\widehat{v}^{k}\frac{\widehat{v}^{\mu}}{v^{0}}\mathbb{F}_{\mu i}[h](v)+\frac{\widehat{v}^{k}}{v^{0}}\mathbb{F}_{0i}[h](v)+\frac{1}{v^{0}}\mathbb{F}_{ki}[h](v),

\displaystyle(v^{j}\partial_{v^{k}}-v^{k}\partial_{v^{j}})\!\left(\frac{\widehat{v}^{\mu}}{v^{0}}\mathbb{F}_{\mu i}[h](v)\!\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\widehat{v}^{\mu}}{v^{0}}(v^{j}\partial_{v^{k}}-v^{k}\partial_{v^{j}})\mathbb{F}_{\mu i}[h](v)+\frac{\widehat{v}^{j}}{v^{0}}\mathbb{F}_{ki}[h](v)-\frac{\widehat{v}^{k}}{v^{0}}\mathbb{F}_{ji}[h](v).

The relation for the asymptotic Lorentz force then ensues from the first part of the statement as well as
\widehat{v}^{\mu}G_{\mu 0}=-\widehat{v}^{\ell}\widehat{v}^{\mu}G_{\mu\ell}
, which holds for any antisymmetric matrix
G_{\mu\nu}
. Finally, the pointwise estimates are given by Proposition
5.44
.

We then deduce, using the expression of the Lie derivative (
26
), the following identity.

Corollary 5.46
.

View as the
2
-form
(t,x)\mapsto\mathbb{F}[h](x)
, the asymptotic electromagnetic field verifies

\mathcal{L}_{\Omega_{ij}}\mathbb{F}[h]=\mathbb{F}\big[\widehat{\Omega}_{ij}h\big],\qquad\qquad 1\leq i<j\leq 3.

Let us mention, even if it will not be useful in this article, that as for the asymptotic Lorentz force, the structure of the modified coefficients
\mathscr{C}_{t,v}^{i}
is also preserved by differentiation (see
[
8
, Lemma
6.33
]
).

We end this subsection by proving a convergence result for
t^{2}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]
along
t\mapsto(t,x+t\widehat{v})
. For this, we will make use of the following lemma, which will allow us to transform
t-r
decay into time decay along timelike straight lines.

Lemma 5.47
.

Let
(x,v)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
. Then,

\forall\,0\leq t\leq 16\,\langle x\rangle\,\langle v\rangle^{2},\quad 1\leq 16\frac{\langle x\rangle\,\langle v\rangle^{2}}{\langle t\rangle},\qquad\qquad\forall\,t\geq 16\,\langle x\rangle|v^{0}|^{2},\qquad t-|x+t\widehat{v}|\geq\frac{t}{4\langle v\rangle^{2}}\geq 2t_{0}.

Proof.

The first inequality is straightforward. For the second one, use
1\leq t_{0}\leq 2
as well as

\forall\,t\geq 4|x|\,\langle v\rangle^{2},\qquad t\geq\frac{2|x|}{1-|\widehat{v}|},\qquad t-|x+t\widehat{v}|\geq t-\frac{1-|\widehat{v}|}{2}t-|\widehat{v}|t=t-\frac{1+|\widehat{v}|}{2}t\geq\frac{t}{4\langle v\rangle^{2}}.

Corollary 5.48
.

Let
|\gamma|\leq N_{0}
. Then, for all
(t,x,v)\in[t_{0},+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
,

\big|t^{2}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}\big(F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]\big)(t,x+t\widehat{v})-\mathbb{F}\big[\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}_{\infty}f_{\infty}](v)\big|\lesssim\langle t\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}}\,\langle x\rangle^{\frac{3}{2}}\,\langle v\rangle^{3}\,\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}].

Remark 5.49
.

By loosing more powers of
|x|
and
|v|
, we could improve the rate of convergence to
t^{-1}
.

Proof.

If
Z^{\gamma}
is composed by at least one translation
\partial_{x^{\lambda}}
, so that
\gamma_{T}\geq 1
, or the scaling
S
, the result follows from
\mathbb{F}\big[\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}_{\infty}f_{\infty}]=0
, the decay estimates provided by Proposition
5.19
and the previous Lemma
5.47
. Otherwise,
Z^{\gamma}
is only composed by boosts and rotations
\Omega_{\lambda k}
.

•

If
0\leq t\leq 16\langle x\rangle\,\langle v\rangle^{2}
, we perform the crude estimate

\big|t^{2}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}\big(F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]\big)(t,x+t\widehat{v})-\mathbb{F}\big[\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\gamma}f_{\infty}\big](v)\big|\lesssim(t+1)\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}],

obtained by controlling the first term through the first pointwise decay estimate of Proposition
5.19
and the second one by Proposition
5.44
. It remains to apply Lemma
5.47
.

•

Otherwise, using again Lemma
5.47
, we have
t-|x+t\widehat{v}|\geq\frac{t}{4\langle v\rangle^{2}}\geq 2t_{0}
, so that the precise estimate for
\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]
, for the interior of the light cone, given by Proposition
5.19
implies

\bigg|t^{2}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}\big(F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]\big)(t,x+t\widehat{v})-\mathbb{F}\big[\Omega_{v}^{\gamma}f_{\infty}\big]\bigg(\frac{\widecheck{x+t\widehat{v}}}{t}\bigg)\bigg|\lesssim\frac{\overline{E}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}]\,\langle v\rangle^{3}}{t^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

Note now that for all
|x|<t
,

\frac{\widecheck{\;x\;}}{t}=\frac{x}{\sqrt{t^{2}-|x|^{2}}},\qquad\left\langle\frac{\widecheck{\;x\;}}{t}\right\rangle=\frac{t}{\sqrt{t^{2}-|x|^{2}}},

so that, for any
\psi\in H^{1}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v},\mathbb{R})
and
1\leq\ell\leq 3
,

\partial_{x^{\ell}}\bigg(\psi\bigg(\frac{\widecheck{\;x\;}}{t}\bigg)\bigg)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{t^{2}-|x|^{2}}}\big[\partial_{v^{\ell}}\psi\big]\bigg(\frac{\widecheck{\;x\;}}{t}\bigg)+\frac{x^{\ell}x}{\sqrt{t^{2}-|x|^{2}}^{\,3}}\cdot\big[\nabla_{v}\psi\big]\bigg(\frac{\widecheck{\;x\;}}{t}\bigg).

We then deduce

\bigg|\nabla_{x}\bigg[\mathbb{F}\big[\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\gamma}f_{\infty}\big]\bigg(\frac{\widecheck{\;x\;}}{t}\bigg)\bigg]\bigg|\lesssim\frac{1}{t}\left(1+\frac{|x|^{2}}{t^{2}-|x|^{2}}\right)\bigg[v^{0}\nabla_{v}\mathbb{F}\big[\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\gamma}f_{\infty}\big]\bigg]\bigg(\frac{\widecheck{\;x\;}}{t}\bigg)\leq\frac{1}{t-|x|}\bigg[v^{0}\nabla_{v}\mathbb{F}\big[\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\gamma}f_{\infty}\big]\bigg]\bigg(\frac{\widecheck{\;x\;}}{t}\bigg).

Applying the divergence theorem and using the algebraic relations between the derivatives of
\mathbb{F}[f_{\infty}]
provided by Proposition
5.45
, we get

\bigg|\mathbb{F}\big[\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\gamma}f_{\infty}\big]\bigg(\frac{\widecheck{x+t\widehat{v}}}{t}\bigg)-\mathbb{F}\big[\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\gamma}f_{\infty}\big](v)\bigg|\leq\frac{|x|}{t}\sup_{|\xi|\leq|\gamma|+1}\sup_{\tau\in[0,1]}\frac{1}{t-|\tau x+t\widehat{v}|}\Big|\mathbb{F}\big[\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\xi}f_{\infty}\big]\Big|\bigg(\frac{\widecheck{\tau x+t\widehat{v}}}{t}\bigg).

(94)

Since
\|\mathbb{F}[\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\xi}f_{\infty}]\|_{L^{\infty}_{v}}\lesssim\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}]
by Proposition
5.45
, it remains to remark

t-|\tau x+t\widehat{v}|\geq\tau(t-|x+t\widehat{v}|)+(1-\tau)(t-t|\widehat{v}|)\gtrsim\tau t\,\langle v\rangle^{-2}+(1-\tau)t\,\langle v\rangle^{-2}=t\,\langle v\rangle^{-2}.

In fact, in the nonlinear problem, we will only be interested in the rate of convergence of the Lorentz force, which turns out to be stronger (see Proposition
5.58
below).

5.5 Study of the null properties of
F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]
and of its radiation field

In view of the weak decay estimates verified by
F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]
and by comparison with a generic solution to the vacuum Maxwell equations initially decaying as
r^{-2}
, one should, from a quick look, expect

•

F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]
to admit a radiation field along
\mathcal{I}^{+}
decaying merely as
\langle u\rangle^{-1}
.

•

Moreover, it is not clear if
r^{2}\rho(F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}])
and
r^{2}\sigma(F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}])
converge or not along null rays
r\mapsto(r+u,r\omega)
.

In fact, a more thorough analysis will allow us to obtain stronger results than expected. We first establish improved estimates for the good null components of the solution to the asymptotic Maxwell equations (
87
). Recall from (
21
) the definition of
\overline{F}
.

Proposition 5.50
.

Let
|\gamma|\leq N_{0}
and
|\xi|\leq N_{0}-1
. Then, for all
(t,x)\in[t_{0}+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
,

\big|\underline{\alpha}\big(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]\big)\big|(t,x)\lesssim\frac{\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}]}{\langle t+|x|\rangle^{2}}+\frac{\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}]}{\langle t+|x|\rangle\,\langle t-|x|\rangle^{2}}.

and

\big|\alpha\big(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\xi}}F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]\big)\big|(t,x)+\big|\rho\big(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\xi}}F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]\big)\big|(t,x)+\big|\sigma\big(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\xi}}F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]\big)\big|(t,x)\lesssim\frac{\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}]}{\langle t+|x|\rangle^{2}}

Moreover, we have improved estimates in the exterior of the light cone,

\forall\,|x|\geq t,\qquad\Big|\rho\Big(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\xi}}\big(F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]-\overline{F}\,\big)\Big)\Big|(t,x)+\Big|\sigma\Big(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\xi}}\big(F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]-\overline{F}\,\big)\Big)\Big|(t,x)\lesssim\frac{\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}]}{\langle t+|x|\rangle^{2}\,\langle t-|x|\rangle}.

Finally, if
|\beta|\leq N_{0}-2
, the best null component verifies

\forall\,(t,x)\in[t_{0}+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3},\qquad\big|\alpha\big(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\beta}}F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]\big)\big|(t,x)\lesssim\frac{\langle t-|x|\rangle+\log(\langle t\rangle)}{\langle t+|x|\rangle^{3}}\,\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}].

Proof.

Fix
(t,x)\in[t_{0},+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
and remark that the result is a consequence of Proposition
5.19
if
|x|\leq t/2+1
. Indeed, in this case,
\overline{F}(t,x)=0
and
\langle t-|x|\rangle\gtrsim\langle t+|x|\rangle
. Assume then that
|x|\geq t/2+1
and remark

\displaystyle\hskip-11.38109pt|\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}\overline{F}|(t,x)

\displaystyle\lesssim Q|x|^{-2-\gamma_{T}}\mathds{1}_{|x|\geq t+1},

(95)

\displaystyle\hskip-11.38109pt\big|\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}\big(F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]-\overline{F}\,\big)\big|(t,x)

\displaystyle\lesssim\langle t-|x|\rangle^{-2}\mathds{1}_{t>|x|}\,\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}]+\langle t+|x|\rangle^{-1}\,\langle t-|x|\rangle^{-2}\,\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}].

Indeed, the first estimate is given by Proposition
3.17
and the second one by (
92
), Proposition
3.18
as well as Proposition
5.41
. It implies the estimate for the null component
\underline{\alpha}
. Consider now
|\xi|\leq N_{0}-1
and let us denote by
\zeta
any of the null components
\alpha
,
\rho
or
\sigma
. Then, using

•

The null Maxwell equations (
56
) and (
58
)–(
59
),

•

Lemma
3.8
, allowing us to extract
r
decay from the angular derivatives
\not{\nabla}
,

•

Lemma
5.24
and Proposition
3.16
, giving that the source term of the Maxwell equations verified by
\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\xi}}F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]
(respectively
\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\xi}}\overline{F}
) decays as
t^{-3}\overline{E}_{N_{0}-1}[f_{\infty}]\mathds{1}_{t>|x|}
(respectively as
Q|x|^{-2}\mathds{1}_{|t-|x||\leq 2}
),

we get, as
|x|\geq t/2+1
, so that
|x|\gtrsim\langle t+|x|\rangle
,

\displaystyle\Big|\nabla_{\underline{L}}\zeta\Big(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\xi}}\!\big(F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]-\overline{F}\big)\Big)\Big|(t,x)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\overline{E}_{N_{0}-1}[f_{\infty}]}{\langle t+|x|\rangle^{3}}\mathds{1}_{t>|x|}+\frac{Q\,\mathds{1}_{|t-|x||\leq 2}}{\langle t+|x|\rangle^{2}}\!+\!\sum_{|\beta|\leq N_{0}}\frac{\big|\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\beta}}\!\big(F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]-\overline{F}\big)\big|(t,x)}{|x|},

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}]}{\langle t+|x|\rangle^{3}}\mathds{1}_{t>|x|}+\frac{\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}]}{\langle t+|x|\rangle^{2}\,\langle t-|x|\rangle^{2}}.

Consider now

\phi(u,\underline{u}):=\zeta\Big(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\xi}}\big(F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]-\overline{F}\big)\Big)\left(\frac{\underline{u}+u}{2},\frac{\underline{u}-u}{2}\omega\right),\qquad\qquad|u|\leq\underline{u},

(96)

and remark that, in order to integrate in
u
, along a null ray of constant
t+r
,

\left|\nabla_{\partial_{u}}\phi\right|(u,\underline{u})=\frac{1}{2}\left|\nabla_{\underline{L}}\zeta\Big(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\xi}}\big(F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]-\overline{F}\big)\Big)\right|\left(\frac{\underline{u}+u}{2},\frac{\underline{u}-u}{2}\omega\right)\lesssim\frac{\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}]}{\langle\underline{u}\rangle^{3}}\mathds{1}_{u>0}+\frac{\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}]}{\langle\underline{u}\rangle^{2}\,\langle u\rangle^{2}}.

Fix
t\geq t_{0}
,
r>0
,
\omega\in\mathbb{S}^{2}
and write

\left|\zeta\Big(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\xi}}\big(F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]-\overline{F}\big)\Big)\right|(t,r\omega)=|\phi|(t-r,t+r)=\bigg|\phi(2t_{0}-t-r,t+r)+\int_{u=2t_{0}-t-r}^{t-r}\nabla_{\partial_{u}}\phi(u,t+r)\mathrm{d}u\bigg|,

where the initial term is controlled by Proposition
5.18
as

|\phi(2t_{0}-t-r,t+r)|=\left|\zeta\Big(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\xi}}\big(F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]-\overline{F}\big)\Big)\right|\big(t_{0},(t+r-t_{0})\omega\big)\lesssim\langle t+r\rangle^{-3}\,\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}].

For the integral, we have

\displaystyle\int_{u=2t_{0}-t-r}^{t-r}|\nabla_{\partial_{u}}\phi(u,t+r)|\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}]}{\langle t+r\rangle^{3}}\int_{u=0}^{\max(0,t-r)}\mathrm{d}u+\frac{\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}]}{\langle t+r\rangle^{2}}\int_{u=2t_{0}-t-r}^{t-r}\frac{\mathrm{d}u}{\langle u\rangle^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\langle t+r\rangle^{-2}\,\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}],&\text{if $t\geq r$},\\
\langle t+r\rangle^{-2}\langle t-r\rangle^{-1}\,\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}],&\text{if $t\leq r$}.\end{array}\right.

Combining these last computations with (
95
) yields all the stated estimates for the good null components but the last one on
\alpha
. Consider then
|\beta|\leq N_{0}-2
and, as before, we use the null Maxwell equation (
56
), Lemma
3.8
as well as Lemma
5.24
and Proposition
3.16
. The differences allowing to a better estimate, compared to the case of
\rho
and
\sigma
, are that

•

in (
56
), the bad derivative
\underline{L}
of the null component
\alpha
is expressed in terms of good derivatives
\not{\nabla}
of the good components
\alpha
,
\rho
and
\sigma
as well as the angular components of the electromagnetic current.

•

Moreover, the angular components of
J^{\mathrm{asymp}}[h]
vanish and the angular component of the source term of the Maxwell equations satisfied by
\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\beta}}\overline{F}
decays as
Q|x|^{-3}\mathds{1}_{1\leq|x|-t\leq 2}
.

Using the estimates that we have just proved and
|x|\geq t/2+1
, we get

\displaystyle\Big|\nabla_{\underline{L}}\alpha\Big(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\beta}}\!\big(F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]-\overline{F}\,\big)\Big)\Big|(t,x)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{Q}{|x|^{3}}\mathds{1}_{|t-|x||\leq 2}+\sum_{|\xi|\leq N_{0}-1}\sum_{\zeta\in\{\alpha,\rho,\sigma\}}\frac{\big|\zeta\big(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\xi}}(F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]-\overline{F}\,)\big)\big|(t,x)}{|x|},

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}]}{\langle t+|x|\rangle^{3}}\mathds{1}_{t>|x|}+\frac{\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}]}{\langle t+|x|\rangle^{3}\,\langle t-|x|\rangle}.

It allows us to improve the estimate for
\nabla_{\partial_{u}}\phi
, in the case
\xi=\beta
and
\zeta=\alpha
, where the function
\phi
is introduced in (
96
). As a consequence, we have

\displaystyle\int_{u=2t_{0}-t-r}^{t-r}|\nabla_{\partial_{u}}\phi(u,t+r)|\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}]}{\langle t+r\rangle^{3}}\int_{u=0}^{\max(0,t-r)}\mathrm{d}u+\frac{\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}]}{\langle t+r\rangle^{3}}\int_{u=2t_{0}-t-r}^{t-r}\frac{\mathrm{d}u}{\langle u\rangle}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\langle t+r\rangle^{-3}\,(\langle t-r\rangle+\log(\langle t+r\rangle))\,\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}],&\text{if $t\geq r$},\\
\langle t+r\rangle^{-3}\log(\langle t+r\rangle)\,\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}],&\text{if $t\leq r$},\end{array}\right.

from which we deduce an improved estimate for
\phi
. Finally, in order to conclude the proof, recall from Proposition
3.16
that
|\alpha(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\beta}}\overline{F})|(t,x)\lesssim Q\langle t+r\rangle^{-3}\,\langle t-r\rangle
.

These estimates, together with Lemma
3.9
, allow us to control the weighted energy of
\nabla_{t,x}F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]
.

Corollary 5.51
.

Let
|\gamma|\leq N-1
. If
\gamma_{T}\geq 1
, we have
\mathcal{E}^{K}\big[\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]\big](t)\lesssim\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}]
for all
t\geq t_{0}
.

We now prove a result which will be useful in order to rewrite the spherical divergence of
\underline{\alpha}(F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}])
.

Lemma 5.52
.

Recall that
\Omega_{ij}
are vector fields on
\mathbb{S}^{2}
. For any
1
-form
\beta:T\mathbb{S}^{2}\to\mathbb{R}
on the sphere
\mathbb{S}^{2}
,

\not{\nabla}\cdot\beta=\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq 3}\mathcal{L}_{\Omega_{ij}}(\beta)_{\Omega_{ij}}.

Proof.

In a well-chosen spherical coordinate system
(\theta,\varphi)
, we have

\Omega_{12}=\partial_{\varphi},\qquad\Omega_{23}=-\sin\varphi\,\partial_{\theta}-\cot\theta\cos\varphi\,\partial_{\varphi},\qquad\Omega_{31}=\cos\varphi\,\partial_{\theta}-\cot\theta\sin\varphi\,\partial_{\varphi}.

Recall that,

\forall\,(\theta,\varphi)\in\,]0,\pi[\times]0,2\pi[\,,\qquad\not{\nabla}\cdot\beta(\theta,\varphi)=\partial_{\theta}\big(\beta_{e_{\theta}}\big)+\cot(\theta)\beta_{\theta}+\frac{1}{\sin(\theta)}\partial_{\varphi}\big(\beta_{e_{\varphi}}\big).

(97)

Remark now that

\forall\,1\leq i<j\leq 3,\qquad\mathcal{L}_{\Omega_{ij}}(\beta)_{\Omega_{ij}}=\Omega_{ij}\big(\beta_{\,\Omega_{ij}}\big)-\beta_{[\Omega_{ij},\Omega_{ij}]}=\Omega_{ij}\big(\beta_{\,\Omega_{ij}}\big).

As
(e_{\theta},e_{\varphi})=(\partial_{\theta},\partial_{\varphi}/\sin\theta)
, we have further

\displaystyle\Omega_{12}\big(\beta_{\Omega_{12}}\big)\!

\displaystyle=\sin(\theta)\partial_{\varphi}\big(\beta_{e_{\varphi}}\big),

\displaystyle\Omega_{23}\big(\beta_{\Omega_{23}}\big)\!

\displaystyle=\sin^{2}(\varphi)\partial_{\theta}\big(\beta_{e_{\theta}}\big)+\sin\varphi\cos\varphi\,\partial_{\theta}\big(\cos\theta\,\beta_{e_{\varphi}}\big)+\cot\theta\cos\varphi\,\partial_{\varphi}\big(\sin\varphi\,\beta_{e_{\theta}}\big)+\cos\theta\cos\varphi\,\partial_{\varphi}\big(\cos\varphi\,\beta_{e_{\varphi}}\big),

\displaystyle\Omega_{13}\big(\beta_{\Omega_{13}}\big)\!

\displaystyle=\cos^{2}(\varphi)\partial_{\theta}\big(\beta_{e_{\theta}}\big)-\cos\varphi\sin\varphi\,\partial_{\theta}\big(\cos\theta\,\beta_{e_{\varphi}}\big)-\cot\theta\sin\varphi\,\partial_{\varphi}\big(\cos\varphi\,\beta_{e_{\theta}}\big)+\cos\theta\sin\varphi\,\partial_{\varphi}\big(\sin\varphi\,\beta_{e_{\varphi}}\big).

Finally, one can check that the sum of the last three terms is equal to the right hand side of (
97
).

We are now able to prove convergence results for the null components of
F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]
.

Proposition 5.53
.

Let
|\gamma|\leq N_{0}+1
. Then, the following properties hold.

1.

\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]
has a radiation field
\underline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\gamma}[f_{\infty}]
along future null infinity
\mathcal{I}^{+}
and

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{u}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\langle u\rangle^{\frac{5}{2}}\left|\underline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\gamma}[f_{\infty}]\right|^{2}\!(u,\omega)\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle\lesssim\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}\big[f_{\infty}\big],\qquad|\gamma|\leq N_{0}+1,

\displaystyle\sup_{(u,\omega)\in\mathbb{R}_{u}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\langle u\rangle^{2}\left|\underline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\gamma}[f_{\infty}]\right|(u,\omega)

\displaystyle\lesssim\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}\big[f_{\infty}\big],\qquad|\gamma|\leq N_{0}.

Moreover, for any
|\xi|\leq N_{0}-2
, we have, for all
\underline{u}\geq t_{0}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{(u,\omega)\in\mathbb{R}_{u}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\left|\big(r\underline{\alpha}(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\xi}}F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}])\big)(u,\underline{u},\omega)-\underline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\xi}[f_{\infty}](u,\omega)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}\big[f_{\infty}\big]\,\langle\underline{u}\rangle^{-1}.

2.

For any
|\beta|\leq N_{0}-3
, there exists
\rho^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\beta}[f_{\infty}],\,\sigma^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\beta}[f_{\infty}]\in C^{0}\!\cap L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{u}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega})
such that, for all
\underline{u}\geq t_{0}
,

\displaystyle\forall\,|u|\leq\underline{u},\,\omega\in\mathbb{S}^{2}\!,\qquad\!

\displaystyle\left|\big(r^{2}\rho(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\beta}}F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}])\big)(u,\underline{u},\omega)-\rho^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\beta}[f_{\infty}](u,\omega)\right|\!\lesssim\frac{\langle u\rangle+\log(\langle\underline{u}\rangle)}{\langle\underline{u}\rangle}\,\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}\big[f_{\infty}\big],

\displaystyle\left|\big(r^{2}\sigma(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\beta}}F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}])\big)(u,\underline{u},\omega)-\sigma^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\beta}[f_{\infty}](u,\omega)\right|\!\lesssim\frac{\langle u\rangle+\log(\langle\underline{u}\rangle)}{\langle\underline{u}\rangle}\,\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}\big[f_{\infty}\big].

3.

If
|\gamma|=0
, we drop the subscript
\gamma
on
\underline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\gamma}[f_{\infty}]
,
\rho^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\gamma}[f_{\infty}]
and
\sigma^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\gamma}[f_{\infty}]
. Their regularity are

\underline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]\in H^{N_{0}+1}(\mathbb{R}_{u}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}),\qquad\rho^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}],\,\sigma^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]\in H^{N_{0}}_{\mathrm{loc}}\cap C^{N_{0}-1}_{b}(\mathbb{R}_{u}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}).

Moreover, if
|\gamma|\geq 1
,
\rho^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\gamma}[f_{\infty}]
(respectively
\sigma^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\gamma}[f_{\infty}]
) can be computed in terms of derivatives of
\underline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]
and
\rho^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]
(respectively
\sigma^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]
).

4.

For any
|\gamma|\leq N_{0}-3
,

2\partial_{u}\rho^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\gamma}[f_{\infty}]=-\not{\nabla}\cdot\underline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\gamma}[f_{\infty}],\qquad\qquad 2\partial_{u}\sigma^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\gamma}[f_{\infty}]=-\not{\nabla}\times\underline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\gamma}[f_{\infty}].

5.

The following asymptotic constraint equations hold. For all
\omega\in\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{u}}\not{\nabla}\cdot\underline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}](u,\omega)\mathrm{d}u=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f_{\infty}(x,v)-f_{\infty}(x,|v|\omega)\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle-\int_{\tau=0}^{+\infty}\frac{2\tau^{2}}{\langle\tau\rangle+\tau}\,\not{\nabla}\cdot\underline{\alpha}\big(\mathbb{F}[f_{\infty}]\big)(\tau\omega)\frac{\mathrm{d}\tau}{\langle\tau\rangle^{3}},

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{u}}\not{\nabla}\times\underline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}](u,\omega)\mathrm{d}u=

\displaystyle-\int_{\tau=0}^{+\infty}\frac{2\tau^{2}}{\langle\tau\rangle+\tau}\,\not{\nabla}\times\underline{\alpha}\big(\mathbb{F}[f_{\infty}]\big)(\tau\omega)\frac{\mathrm{d}\tau}{\langle\tau\rangle^{3}}.

6.

The functions
\rho^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]
and
\sigma^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]
admit limit as
u\to\pm\infty
. For all
\omega\in\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}
,

\lim_{u\to-\infty}\rho^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}](u,\omega)=\frac{Q}{4\pi},\qquad\qquad\lim_{u\to-\infty}\sigma^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}](u,\omega)=0.

The limit as
u\to+\infty
can then be computed using
4.
and
5.
.

Remark 5.54
.

One can use the constraint equations to derive similar relations for
\underline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\gamma}[f_{\infty}]
.

Proof.

We focus first on the points
1
and
2
. Let
|\gamma|\leq N_{0}+1
and
J^{\gamma}
be the source term, computed in Proposition
5.16
, in the Maxwell equations satisfied by
\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]
. Then, according to Proposition
5.18
and Lemma
5.24
,

\big\|\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F^{\mathrm{asymp}}(t_{0},\cdot)\big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})}+\sup_{t\geq t_{0}}\,\langle t\rangle^{\frac{3}{2}}\big\|J^{\gamma}(t,\cdot)\big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})}\lesssim\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}].

We then deduce from Proposition
4.3
the existence of the radiation field
\underline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\gamma}[f_{\infty}]
for
\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]
. Fix
|\xi|\leq N_{0}-2
and recall from Lemma
5.24
that
J^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{e_{A}}[\cdot]=0
for any angular component
A\in\{\theta,\,\varphi\}
. Hence, applying first Corollary
3.12
and then the previous Proposition
5.50
, we get

\left|\nabla_{L}\big(r\underline{\alpha}(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\xi}}F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}])\big)\right|(t,x)\lesssim\sum_{|\kappa|\leq N_{0}-1}\!\Big(\big|\rho(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\kappa}}F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}])\big|+\big|\sigma(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\kappa}}F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}])\big|\Big)(t,x)\lesssim\frac{\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}]}{\langle t+|x|\rangle^{2}}

as well as

\displaystyle\left|\nabla_{L}\big(r^{2}\rho(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\beta}}F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}])\big)\right|(t,x)+\left|\nabla_{L}\big(r^{2}\sigma(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\beta}}F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}])\big)\right|(t,x)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\lesssim|x|^{2}|J^{\beta}_{L}|(t,x)+\sum_{|\kappa|\leq N_{0}-2}|x|\big|\alpha(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\kappa}}F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}])\big|(t,x)\lesssim\frac{\langle t-|x|\rangle+\log(\langle t+|x|\rangle)}{\langle t+|x|\rangle^{2}}\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}].

Recall that
L=\partial_{t}+\partial_{r}=2\partial_{\underline{u}}
in the null coordinate system
(u,\underline{u},\theta,\varphi)
.

•

The uniform convergence for
r\underline{\alpha}(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\xi}}F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}])
is then obtained by integrating in
\tau
along
(\tau+u,\tau\omega)
. In other words, by integrating in
\underline{u}
, along
t-r=u
, for fixed
\omega\in\mathbb{S}^{2}
.

•

The same procedure provides the existence of the limits
\rho^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\beta}[f_{\infty}]
and
\sigma^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\beta}[f_{\infty}]
, as well as the stated rate of convergence. Moreover, these two functions belong to
L^{\infty}_{u,\omega}
since
r^{2}\rho(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\beta}}F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}])
and
r^{2}\sigma(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\beta}}F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}])
are uniformly bounded according to Proposition
5.50
.

It remains us to justify the bounds for
\underline{\alpha}_{\gamma}^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]
. We start with the pointwise one, so that we assume
|\gamma|\leq N_{0}
. According to Proposition
5.50
, we have, for any
U>0
and all
r\geq U^{2}
,

\sup_{|u|\leq U}\sup_{\omega\in\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\langle u\rangle^{2}\big|r\underline{\alpha}(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}])\big|(r+u,r\omega)\lesssim\sup_{|u|\leq U}\big(\langle u\rangle^{2}\,\langle r\rangle^{-1}+1\big)\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}]\leq 2\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}].

If
|\gamma|\leq N_{0}-2
, it implies the result. Otherwise, by Banach-Alaoglu theorem and the uniqueness of the limit in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}_{u}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega})
, there exists an increasing sequence
(r_{n})_{n\geq 0}
of integers such that

(u,\omega)\!\mapsto\langle u\rangle^{2}r\underline{\alpha}(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}])(r_{n}+u,r_{n}\omega)\xrightharpoonup[n\to+\infty]{*}(u,\omega)\!\mapsto\langle u\rangle^{2}\underline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\gamma}[f_{\infty}](u,\omega),\quad\text{in $L^{\infty}([-U,U]\times\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega})$,}

and, by the uniform boundedness principle, the weak-
∗
limit is bounded by
\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}]
. We proceed similarly for the
L^{2}_{u,\omega}
bound. Let
|\gamma|\leq N_{0}+1
and recall that
\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]=F^{\gamma,\mathrm{hom}}+F^{\gamma,\mathrm{inh}}
. Combining the pointwise decay estimate for
F^{\gamma,\mathrm{inh}}
, given by Proposition
5.44
, and the
L^{2}_{x}
estimate for
F^{\gamma,\mathrm{hom}}
, provided by Proposition
5.33
, one has, for any
U>0
and all
n\geq U^{2}
,

\int_{t=n}^{n+2U}\int_{|t-|x||\leq U}\langle t-|x|\rangle^{\frac{5}{2}}\left|\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]\right|^{2}(t,x)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\lesssim\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}]\int_{t=n}^{n+2U}\frac{\langle U\rangle^{\frac{7}{2}}}{\langle t\rangle^{2}}+1\mathrm{d}t\lesssim U\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}].

Consequently, there exists a sequence
(\underline{u}_{n})_{n\geq U^{2}}
of advanced times, such that, for all
n\geq U^{2}
,

2n+U\leq\underline{u}_{n}\leq 2n+3U,\qquad\int_{|u|\leq U}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\langle u\rangle^{\frac{5}{2}}\left|r^{2}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]\right|^{2}\left(\frac{\underline{u}_{n}+u}{2},\frac{\underline{u}_{n}-u}{2}\omega\right)\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\mathrm{d}u\lesssim\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}].

The bound on
\underline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\gamma}[f_{\infty}]
then follows from Banach-Alaoglu theorem, applied this time in
L^{2}([-U,U]\times\mathbb{S}^{2})
, and the uniform boundedness principle.

We now deal with the last four parts of the statement. Fix
|\gamma|\leq N_{0}-3
, introduce
G=\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]
in orter to lighten the notations and let us use the null cordinates
(u,\underline{u},\omega)
, where
u=t-r
and
\underline{u}=t+r
. The null Maxwell equations (
58
)–(
59
) provide

\underline{L}\big(r^{2}\rho(G)\big)+r\not{\nabla}\cdot r\underline{\alpha}(G)=r^{2}J^{\gamma}_{\underline{L}},\qquad\qquad\underline{L}\big(r^{2}\sigma(G)\big)+r\not{\nabla}\times r\underline{\alpha}(G)=0.

(98)

In view of the commutation formula of Proposition
5.16
and Lemma
5.24
, we have

\forall\,\omega\in\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega},\qquad\big|r^{2}J^{\gamma}_{\underline{L}}\big|(u,\underline{u},\omega)\lesssim\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}-3}[f_{\infty}]\,\langle\underline{u}\rangle^{-1}.

As
(re_{\theta},re_{\varphi})=(\partial_{\theta},\partial_{\varphi}/\sin(\theta))
and
\underline{L}=2\partial_{u}
, we have in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}_{u}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega})
,

\displaystyle\underline{L}\big(r^{2}\rho(G)\big)(\cdot,\underline{u},\cdot)

\displaystyle\xrightharpoonup[\underline{u}\to+\infty]{}2\partial_{u}\rho^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\gamma}[f_{\infty}],\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\underline{L}\big(r^{2}\sigma(G)\big)(\cdot,\underline{u},\cdot)

\displaystyle\xrightharpoonup[\underline{u}\to+\infty]{}2\partial_{u}\sigma^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\gamma}[f_{\infty}],

\displaystyle r\not{\nabla}\cdot r\underline{\alpha}(G)(\cdot,\underline{u},\cdot)

\displaystyle\xrightharpoonup[\underline{u}\to+\infty]{}\not{\nabla}\cdot\underline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\gamma}[f_{\infty}],\qquad\qquad\qquad\;r\not{\nabla}\times r\underline{\alpha}(G)(\cdot,\underline{u},\cdot)

\displaystyle\xrightharpoonup[\underline{u}\to+\infty]{}\not{\nabla}\times\underline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\gamma}[f_{\infty}].

We then deduce that, in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}_{u}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega})
and for any
|\gamma|\leq N_{0}-3
,

2\partial_{u}\rho^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\gamma}[f_{\infty}]+\not{\nabla}\cdot\underline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\gamma}[f_{\infty}]=0,\qquad\qquad 2\partial_{u}\sigma^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\gamma}[f_{\infty}]+\not{\nabla}\times\underline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\gamma}[f_{\infty}]=0.

(99)

We are now able to deduce several informations from these two equations.

•

Recall from Proposition
4.3
that
\underline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\gamma}[f_{\infty}]
can be expressed in terms of derivatives of order less than
|\gamma|
of
\underline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]
, so that
\underline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]\in H^{N_{0}+1}(\mathbb{R}_{u}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega})
.

•

Let
u\leq-2
and
\omega\in\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}
. Recall from Proposition
3.17
that
r^{2}\rho(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}\overline{F})(r+u,r\omega)
and
