r^{2}\sigma(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}\overline{F})(r+u,r\omega)
converge, as
r\to+\infty
, and that the limit is independent of
u\leq-2
. In the particular case
|\gamma|=0
, these two quantities are constants, respectively equal to
Q/4\pi
and
0
. Consequently, the improved estimates for the exterior of the light cone given by Proposition
5.50
imply

\rho^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\gamma}[f_{\infty}](u,\omega)=\Phi^{\rho}_{\gamma}(\omega)+O_{u\to-\infty}\big(|u|^{-1}\big),\qquad\qquad\sigma^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\gamma}[f_{\infty}](u,\omega)=\Phi^{\sigma}_{\gamma}(\omega)+O_{u\to-\infty}\big(|u|^{-1}\big),

where
\Phi^{\rho}_{\gamma}
and
\Phi^{\sigma}_{\gamma}
are smooth function on
\mathbb{S}^{2}
.

•

We then get, from (
99
) for the particular case
|\gamma|=0
, that for all
(u,\omega)\in\mathbb{R}_{u}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}
,

\displaystyle 2\rho^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}](u,\omega)

\displaystyle=\frac{Q}{2\pi}-\int_{\tau=-\infty}^{u}\not{\nabla}\cdot\underline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}](\tau,\omega)\mathrm{d}\tau,

\displaystyle 2\sigma^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}](u,\omega)

\displaystyle=-\int_{\tau=-\infty}^{u}\not{\nabla}\times\underline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}](\tau,\omega)\mathrm{d}\tau.

In view of the
H^{N_{0}+1}(\mathbb{R}_{u}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega})
regularity of
\underline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]
as well as its decay properties, we obtain from these two equations that
\rho^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}],\,\sigma^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]\in H^{N_{0}}_{\mathrm{loc}}\cap C^{N_{0}-1}_{b}(\mathbb{R}_{u}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega})
.

•

We also get from (
99
) that
\rho^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\gamma}[f_{\infty}](u,\omega)
and
\sigma^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\gamma}[f_{\infty}](u,\omega)
both converge, as
u\to+\infty
.

In order to conclude the proof, it then remains us to prove the constraint equations verified by the spherical divergence and the spherical curl of
\underline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]
. From now on,
G=F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]
. Fix further
\underline{u}\geq 2
,
\omega\in\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}
and integrate (
98
) in
u
, between
-\underline{u}+4
and
\underline{u}
. As
r=0
for
u=\underline{u}
and
(t,r)=(2,\underline{u}-2)
for
u=\underline{u}-4
, we get

\displaystyle-2(\underline{u}-2)^{2}\rho(G)\big(2,(\underline{u}-2)\omega\big)+\int_{u=-\underline{u}+4}^{\underline{u}}\Big(r\not{\nabla}\cdot r\underline{\alpha}(G)\Big)(u,\underline{u},\omega)\mathrm{d}u=\int_{u=-\underline{u}+4}^{\underline{u}}\big(r^{2}J^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\underline{L}}[f_{\infty}]\big)(u,\underline{u},\omega)\mathrm{d}u,

\displaystyle-2(\underline{u}-2)^{2}\sigma(G)\big(2,(\underline{u}-2)\omega\big)+\int_{u=-\underline{u}+4}^{\underline{u}}\Big(r\not{\nabla}\times r\underline{\alpha}(G)\Big)(u,\underline{u},\omega)\mathrm{d}u=0.

(100)

The main difficulty is that the assumptions of the dominated convergence theorem are not satisfied for the integrals involving
\underline{\alpha}(G)
. In fact, we will see that the conclusion of this theorem does not hold neither. Using the pointwise estimates of Proposition
5.50
for the exterior of the light cone, one has

\lim_{r\to+\infty}r^{2}\rho(G)(2,r\omega)=\lim_{r\to+\infty}r^{2}\rho(\overline{F})(2,r\omega)=\frac{Q}{4\pi},\qquad\lim_{r\to+\infty}r^{2}\sigma(G)(2,r\omega)=\lim_{r\to+\infty}r^{2}\sigma(\overline{F})(2,r\omega)=0.

Next, applying Lemma
5.25
to the function
h(z,v):=f_{\infty}(z,|v|\omega)
, we get

\displaystyle\int_{u=-\underline{u}+4}^{\underline{u}}\big(r^{2}J^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\underline{L}}[f_{\infty}]\big)(u,\underline{u},\omega)\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{4\pi}\int_{u=-\underline{u}+4}^{\underline{u}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\bar{\omega}}}J^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\underline{L}}[h]\left(\frac{\underline{u}+u}{2},\frac{\underline{u}-u}{2}\bar{\omega}\right)\frac{r^{2}}{2}\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\bar{\omega}}}\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f_{\infty}(z,|v|\omega)\mathrm{d}z\mathrm{d}v.

It remains us to treat one term in both equations of (
100
). We start with the one in the equation satisfied by
\rho(G)
and we will deal with the other term by duality. For this, rewrite the spherical divergence by applying Lemma
5.52
and by using that
\mathcal{L}_{\Omega_{ij}}
commute with the null decomposition (see Lemma
3.8
). For all
t\geq 2
,
r>0
and
\omega\in\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}
,

\displaystyle\Big(r\not{\nabla}\cdot r\underline{\alpha}(G)\Big)(u,\underline{u},\omega)

\displaystyle=\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq 3}r\underline{\alpha}(\mathcal{L}_{\Omega_{ij}}G)_{\Omega_{ij}^{\mathrm{resc}}}(u,\underline{u},\omega),\qquad\Omega_{ij}^{\mathrm{resc}}=\omega^{i}\partial_{x^{j}}-\omega^{j}\partial_{x^{i}}=(\omega^{i}\omega_{j}^{A}-\omega^{j}\omega_{i}^{A})e_{A},

\displaystyle\not{\nabla}\cdot\underline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}](u,\omega)

\displaystyle=\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq 3}\mathcal{L}_{\Omega_{ij}}\big(\underline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]\big)_{\Omega_{ij}}(u,\omega),\qquad\Omega_{ij}=(\omega^{i}\omega_{j}^{A}-\omega^{j}\omega_{i}^{A})e_{A}.

(101)

Fix now
1\leq i<j\leq 3
and let
|\kappa_{ij}|=1
such that
\Omega_{ij}=Z^{\kappa_{ij}}
. Recall from Definition
5.22
the decomposition
\mathcal{L}_{\Omega_{ij}}G=\mathcal{L}_{\Omega_{ij}}F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]=F^{\kappa_{ij},\mathrm{inh}}+F^{\kappa_{ij},\mathrm{hom}}
and from Proposition
5.39

F^{\kappa_{ij},\mathrm{inh}}=F^{\kappa_{ij},\mathrm{T}}-F^{\kappa_{ij},\mathrm{sph}}
. Then,

\underline{\alpha}\big(\mathcal{L}_{\Omega_{ij}}G\big)=\underline{\alpha}\big(F^{\kappa_{ij},\mathrm{T}})+\underline{\alpha}(F^{\kappa_{ij},\mathrm{sph}}+F^{\kappa_{ij},\mathrm{hom}}\big).

As
|F^{\kappa_{ij},T}|(t,x)\lesssim\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}]\,\langle t+|x|\rangle^{-2}
according to Proposition
5.41
, we get

\lim_{r\to+\infty}r\underline{\alpha}\big(F^{\kappa_{ij},\mathrm{sph}}+F^{\kappa_{ij},\mathrm{hom}}\big)(r+u,r\omega)=\underline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\kappa_{ij}}[f_{\infty}](u,\omega)=\mathcal{L}_{\Omega_{ij}}\underline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}](u,\omega),

where we used Proposition
4.3
in the last step. Recall from Proposition
3.16
that
\mathcal{L}_{\Omega_{ij}}\overline{F}=0
. Hence, by the pointwise decay estimates given by (
92
) and Proposition
5.41
,

r\big|\underline{\alpha}\big(F^{\kappa_{ij},\mathrm{sph}}+F^{\kappa_{ij},\mathrm{hom}}\big)\big|(r+u,r\omega)\lesssim r\big|F^{\kappa_{ij},\mathrm{sph}}\big|(r+u,r\omega)+r\big|F^{\kappa_{ij},\mathrm{hom}}-\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\kappa_{ij}}}\overline{F}\big|(r+u,r\omega)\lesssim\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}]\,\langle u\rangle^{-2}.

Applying the dominated convergence theorem as well as (
101
), we then get

\lim_{\underline{u}\to+\infty}\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq 3}\int_{u=-\underline{u}+4}^{\underline{u}}r\underline{\alpha}\big(F^{\kappa_{ij},\mathrm{sph}}+F^{\kappa_{ij},\mathrm{hom}}\big)_{\Omega_{ij}^{\mathrm{resc}}}(u,\underline{u},\omega)\mathrm{d}u=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{u}}\not{\nabla}\cdot\underline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}](u,\omega)\mathrm{d}u.

It remains us to prove that

\lim_{\underline{u}\to+\infty}\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq 3}\int_{u=-\underline{u}+4}^{\underline{u}}r\underline{\alpha}\big(F^{\kappa_{ij},\mathrm{T}}\big)_{\Omega_{ij}^{\mathrm{resc}}}(u,\underline{u},\omega)\mathrm{d}u=\int_{\tau=0}^{+\infty}\frac{2\tau^{2}}{\langle\tau\rangle+\tau}\,\not{\nabla}\cdot\underline{\alpha}\big(\mathbb{F}[f_{\infty}]\big)(\tau\omega)\frac{\mathrm{d}\tau}{\langle\tau\rangle^{3}}.

(102)

Note first that we can restrict the domain of integration of the integral in the left hand side to
\{4\leq u\leq\underline{u}\}
, since the remainder converge to
0
. Indeed, we have
|F^{\kappa_{ij},T}|(t,x)\lesssim\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}]\,\langle t+|x|\rangle^{-2}\mathds{1}_{t>|x|}
by Proposition
5.41
. Consequently, as
1\leq t_{0}\leq 2
, we have from Proposition
5.44
that

\forall\,\omega\in\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega},\;4\leq u\leq\underline{u},\qquad F^{\kappa_{ij},\mathrm{T}}_{\mu\nu}(u,\underline{u},\omega)=\frac{4}{(\underline{u}+u)^{2}}\mathbb{F}_{\mu\nu}\big[\,\widehat{\Omega}_{ij}f_{\infty}\big]\bigg(\frac{\widecheck{\,(\underline{u}-u)\omega\,}}{\underline{u}+u}\bigg),

where we used that
u=t-r
and
\underline{u}=t+r
. Moreover, in view of Corollary
5.46
, Lemma
3.8
and Lemma
5.52
,

\underline{\alpha}\big(\mathbb{F}\big[\widehat{\Omega}_{ij}f_{\infty}\big]\big)=\underline{\alpha}\big(\mathcal{L}_{\Omega_{ij}}\mathbb{F}[f_{\infty}]\big)=\mathcal{L}_{\Omega_{ij}}\underline{\alpha}\big(\mathbb{F}[f_{\infty}]\big),\qquad\qquad\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq 3}\underline{\alpha}\left(\mathbb{F}\big[\widehat{\Omega}_{ij}f_{\infty}\big]\right)_{\Omega_{ij}^{\mathrm{resc}}}=r\not{\nabla}\cdot\underline{\alpha}\big(\mathbb{F}[f_{\infty}]\big).

We then deduce that, using the notation
[rh](y)=|y|h(y)
,

\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq 3}\int_{u=4}^{\underline{u}}r\underline{\alpha}(F^{\kappa_{ij},\mathrm{T}})_{\Omega_{ij}^{\mathrm{resc}}}(u,\underline{u},\omega)\mathrm{d}u=2\int_{u=4}^{\underline{u}}\frac{\underline{u}-u}{(\underline{u}+u)^{2}}\Big[r\not{\nabla}\cdot\underline{\alpha}\big(\mathbb{F}[f_{\infty}]\big)\Big]\!\bigg(\frac{\widecheck{\,(\underline{u}-u)\omega\,}}{\underline{u}+u}\bigg)\mathrm{d}u.

As, on the domain of integration,
t^{2}F^{\kappa_{ij},T}(t,x)
is a function of
x/t
, we perform the contractor change of variables
p=u/\underline{u}
. It allows us to integrate over a subset of
\{t+r=1\}
instead of
\{t+r=\underline{u}\}
and to obtain

\lim_{\underline{u}\to+\infty}\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq 3}\int_{u=-\underline{u}+4}^{\underline{u}}r\underline{\alpha}\big(F^{\kappa_{ij},\mathrm{T}}\big)_{\Omega_{ij}^{\mathrm{resc}}}(u,\underline{u},\omega)\mathrm{d}u=2\int_{p=0}^{1}\frac{1-p}{(1+p)^{2}}\Big[r\not{\nabla}\cdot\underline{\alpha}\big(\mathbb{F}[f_{\infty}]\big)\Big]\!\bigg(\frac{\widecheck{\,(1-p)\omega\,}}{1+p}\bigg)\mathrm{d}\tau.

We finally obtain (
102
) by performing the following two change of variables,

q=\frac{1-p}{1+p},\quad\mathrm{d}q=\frac{-2}{(1+p)^{2}}\mathrm{d}p,\quad 1-p=\frac{2q}{1+q},\qquad\qquad\tau=\frac{q}{\sqrt{1-q^{2}}},\quad\frac{\mathrm{d}\tau}{\langle\tau\rangle^{3}}=\mathrm{d}q,\quad\frac{2q}{1+q}=\frac{2\tau}{\langle\tau\rangle+\tau}.

In order to deal with the spherical curl of
\underline{\alpha}(G)
, we relate it to the divergence of its Hodge dual. According to
[
19
, Equations
(3.9)
–
(3.9^{\prime})
]
, for any
2
-form
H_{\mu\nu}
, we have

{}^{*}\!\underline{\alpha}(H)_{e_{A}}:={\varepsilon_{A}}^{B}\underline{\alpha}(H)_{e_{B}},\qquad{}^{*}\!\underline{\alpha}(H)_{e_{A}}=\underline{\alpha}\big({}^{*}\!H\big)_{e_{A}},\qquad\qquad A\in\{\theta,\varphi\}.

(103)

It allows us to deduce that

\not{\nabla}\times\underline{\alpha}(G)=\varepsilon^{AB}\not{\nabla}_{e_{A}}\underline{\alpha}(G)_{e_{B}}=\not{\nabla}^{e_{A}}\underline{\alpha}\big({}^{*}\!G\big)_{e_{A}}=\not{\nabla}\cdot\underline{\alpha}\big({}^{*}\!G\big)

(104)

and we can apply the previous proof to
{}^{*}G={}^{*}F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]
. Indeed, the properties used on
F^{\kappa_{ij},\mathrm{T}}
,
F^{\kappa_{ij},\mathrm{sph}}
,
F^{\kappa_{ij},\mathrm{hom}}
and
\mathcal{L}_{\Omega_{ij}}\overline{F}
hold as well for their Hodge dual. Consequently, using (
103
)–(
104
), we obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{\underline{u}\to+\infty}\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq 3}\int_{u=-\underline{u}+4}^{\underline{u}}r\underline{\alpha}\big({}^{*}\!F^{\kappa_{ij},\mathrm{sph}}\!+{}^{*}\!F^{\kappa_{ij},\mathrm{hom}}\big)_{\Omega_{ij}^{\mathrm{resc}}}(u,\underline{u},\omega)\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{u}}\not{\nabla}\cdot{}^{*}\underline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}](u,\omega)\mathrm{d}u,

\displaystyle\lim_{\underline{u}\to+\infty}\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq 3}\int_{u=-\underline{u}+4}^{\underline{u}}r\underline{\alpha}\big({}^{*}\!F^{\kappa_{ij},\mathrm{T}}\big)_{\Omega_{ij}^{\mathrm{resc}}}(u,\underline{u},\omega)\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle=\int_{\tau=0}^{+\infty}\frac{2\tau^{2}}{\langle\tau\rangle+\tau}\,\not{\nabla}\cdot\underline{\alpha}\big({}^{*}\mathbb{F}[f_{\infty}]\big)(\tau\omega)\frac{\mathrm{d}\tau}{\langle\tau\rangle^{3}}

and

\not{\nabla}\cdot{}^{*}\underline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]=\not{\nabla}\times\underline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}],\qquad\qquad\not{\nabla}\cdot\underline{\alpha}\big({}^{*}\mathbb{F}[f_{\infty}]\big)=\not{\nabla}\times\underline{\alpha}\big(\mathbb{F}[f_{\infty}]\big).

We now improve the convergence estimate of Corollary
5.48
for the Lorentz force
\widehat{v}^{\mu}F^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\mu j}[f_{\infty}]
. It will allow us to slightly relax the velocity decay for
f_{\infty}
and to simplify the analysis of Section
7
. For this, our proof will require to exploit improved decay estimates for the null components of
F^{\mathrm{hom}}
in the interior of the light cone.

Lemma 5.55
.

For any
|\gamma|\leq N_{0}-1
, we have, for all
t\geq|x|+2
,

|\underline{\alpha}\big(F^{\gamma,\mathrm{hom}}\big)\big|(t,x)\lesssim\frac{\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}]}{t\,\langle t-|x|\rangle^{2}},\quad\qquad\big(|\alpha\big(F^{\gamma,\mathrm{hom}}\big)\big|+|\rho\big(F^{\gamma,\mathrm{hom}}\big)\big|+|\sigma\big(F^{\gamma,\mathrm{hom}}\big)\big|\big)(t,x)\lesssim\frac{\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}]}{t^{2}\,\langle t-|x|\rangle}.

Proof.

For the the null component
\underline{\alpha}
, it suffices to apply Proposition
5.30
. The key idea in order to improve the estimates satisfied by the components
\alpha
,
\rho
and
\sigma
consists in exploiting certain of the Maxwell equations, written in null coordinates. The problem is that
F^{\gamma,\mathrm{hom}}
is not solution solution to the Maxwell equations. However, one can prove that
F^{\gamma,\mathrm{hom}}
is solution to the vacuum Maxwell equations in the interior of a light cone. Fix then
|\gamma|\leq N_{0}-1
and remark that, since
\Box F^{\gamma,\mathrm{hom}}_{\mu\nu}=0
,

\Box\,\nabla^{\mu}F^{\gamma,\mathrm{hom}}_{\mu\nu}=0,\qquad\Box\,\nabla^{\mu}{}^{*}\!F^{\gamma,\mathrm{hom}}_{\mu\nu}=0,\qquad\nu\in\llbracket 0,3\rrbracket.

By definition, we have further, for any
\nu\in\llbracket 0,3\rrbracket
,

\displaystyle\nabla^{\mu}F^{\gamma,\mathrm{hom}}_{\mu\nu}(t_{0},\cdot)=\nabla^{\mu}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}(F)_{\mu\nu}(t_{0},\cdot)=J^{\gamma}_{\nu}(t_{0},\cdot),\qquad

\displaystyle\;\;\;\nabla^{\mu}{}^{*}\!F^{\gamma,\mathrm{hom}}_{\mu\nu}(t_{0},\cdot)=\nabla^{\mu}{}^{*}\!\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}(F)_{\mu\nu}(t_{0},\cdot)=0,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}

\displaystyle\nabla^{\mu}F^{\gamma,\mathrm{hom}}_{\mu\nu}(t_{0},\cdot)=\partial_{t}\nabla^{\mu}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}(F)_{\mu\nu}(t_{0},\cdot)=\partial_{t}J^{\gamma}_{\nu}(t_{0},\cdot),\qquad

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\nabla^{\mu}{}^{*}\!F^{\gamma,\mathrm{hom}}_{\mu\nu}(t_{0},\cdot)=\partial_{t}\nabla^{\mu}{}^{*}\!\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}(F)_{\mu\nu}(t_{0},\cdot)=0.

It implies that
\nabla^{\mu}{}^{*}\!F^{\gamma,\mathrm{hom}}_{\mu\nu}=0
on
[t_{0},+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
. Exploiting the wave equation, we get that all the derivatives
\partial_{t,x}^{\kappa}
, up to second order
|\kappa|\leq 2
, of
\nabla^{\mu}F^{\gamma,\mathrm{hom}}_{\mu\nu}(t_{0},\cdot)
are supported in
\{|x|\leq t_{0}\}
since this property holds true for
J^{\gamma}(t_{0},\cdot)
. As
t_{0}\leq 2
, we get
\nabla^{\mu}F^{\gamma,\mathrm{hom}}_{\mu\nu}=0
on
\{t\geq|x|+2\}
by Huygens’ principle.

Fix now
t\geq|x|+2
. If
t\geq 2|x|
,
t-|x|\geq t/2
and it suffices to apply Proposition
5.30
. Otherwise
t\leq 2|x|
, so that
|x|\gtrsim 1+t+|x|
and let
\zeta\in\{\alpha,\rho,\sigma\}
. Using the Maxwell equation (
56
), (
58
) or (
59
) and then the decay estimates given by Proposition
5.30
as well as Remark
5.31
,

\big|\nabla_{\underline{L}}\zeta\big(F^{\gamma,\mathrm{hom}}\big)|(t,x)\lesssim|x|^{-1}\sup_{|\xi|\leq 1}\big|\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\xi}}F^{\gamma,\mathrm{hom}}\big|(t,x)\lesssim\langle t+|x|\rangle^{-2}\langle t-|x|\rangle^{-2}\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}].

In the same spirit as in the proof of Proposition
5.50
, consider
\omega=x/x
and, for all
2\leq u\leq\underline{u}
,

\phi(u,\underline{u}):=\zeta\big(F^{\gamma,\mathrm{hom}}\big)\Big(\frac{\underline{u}+u}{2},\frac{\underline{u}-u}{2}\omega\Big),\qquad|\phi|(u,\underline{u})\lesssim\frac{\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}]}{\langle\underline{u}\rangle\,\langle u\rangle^{2}},\qquad|\partial_{u}\phi|(u,\underline{u})\lesssim\frac{\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}]}{\langle\underline{u}\rangle^{2}\langle u\rangle^{2}}.

Integrating in
u
, along the segment
u\mapsto(u,t+|x|)
with
t-|x|\leq u\leq t+|x|
, yields

\big|\zeta\big(F^{\gamma,\mathrm{hom}}\big)\big|(t,x)=|\phi|(t-|x|,t+|x|)\leq\big|\zeta\big(F^{\gamma,\mathrm{hom}}\big)\big|(t+|x|,0)+\langle t+|x|\rangle^{-2}\int_{u=t-|x|}^{t+|x|}\frac{\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}]}{\langle u\rangle^{2}}\mathrm{d}u,

which implies the result.

We now investigate the null structure of the Lorentz force.

Lemma 5.56
.

Let
H
be a
2
-form defined on
\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
. Then, for any
0\leq\nu\leq 3
and all
(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
,

\left|\widehat{v}^{\mu}H_{\mu\nu}(t,x)\right|\lesssim\left(|\alpha(H)|+|\rho(H)|+|\sigma(H)|\right)(t,x)+\big(\widehat{v}^{\underline{L}}(x)+|\widehat{\not{v}}(x)|\big)|\underline{\alpha}(H)|(t,x).

Proof.

Since
H
is a
2
-form and
\widehat{v}^{0}=1
, we have
\widehat{v}^{\mu}H_{\mu 0}=-\widehat{v}^{\mu}\widehat{v}^{j}H_{\mu j}
and it suffices to treat the case
\nu\in\llbracket 1,3\rrbracket
. In order to lighten the notations, we drop in this proof the dependence in
(t,x)
of the quantities that we will consider. We start by expanding
\widehat{v}^{\mu}H_{\mu j}
, for
1\leq j\leq 3
, according to the null frame
(\underline{L},L,e_{\theta},e_{\varphi})
. We get

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{v}^{\mu}H_{\mu j}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\widehat{v}^{L}H_{Lj}+\widehat{v}^{\underline{L}}H_{\underline{L}j}+\widehat{v}^{e_{\theta}}H_{e_{\theta}j}+\widehat{v}^{e_{\varphi}}H_{e_{\varphi}j}\right|.

(105)

As
\widehat{v}^{L}\leq 1
and
2\partial_{x^{j}}=\omega_{j}L-\omega_{j}\underline{L}+2\omega_{j}^{e_{A}}e_{A}
, with
\omega_{j}(x):=x_{j}/|x|
and
|\omega_{j}^{e_{A}}|\leq 1
, we obtain

\left|\widehat{v}^{\mu}H_{\mu j}\right|\lesssim|\alpha(H)|+\left(\widehat{v}^{\underline{L}}+|\omega_{j}|\right)|\rho(H)|+|\not{v}||\sigma(H)|+\big(\widehat{v}^{\underline{L}}+|\widehat{\not{v}}||\omega_{j}|\big)|\underline{\alpha}(H)|.

(106)

It remains to use
\widehat{v}^{\underline{L}}+|\omega_{j}|+|\widehat{\not{v}}|\lesssim 1
.

Remark 5.57
.

We will not need it in this article, but
(\delta_{i}^{j}-\widehat{v}^{j}\widehat{v}_{i})\widehat{v}^{\mu}H_{\mu j}(t,x)
enjoys better null properties than
\widehat{v}^{\mu}H_{\mu\nu}
. For this use (
106
) and observe that

\displaystyle|(\delta_{i}^{j}-\widehat{v}^{j}\widehat{v}_{i})\omega_{j}|

\displaystyle=|\omega_{i}-\widehat{v}_{i}+\widehat{v}_{i}(1-\widehat{v}^{j}\omega_{j})|\leq|\omega_{i}-\widehat{v}_{i}|+2\widehat{v}^{\underline{L}}\leq|\omega-\widehat{v}|+2\widehat{v}^{\underline{L}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(|\omega|^{2}+|\widehat{v}|^{2}-2\widehat{v}\cdot\omega\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+2\widehat{v}^{\underline{L}}\leq\sqrt{2}\left|1-\widehat{v}\cdot\omega\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}+2\widehat{v}^{\underline{L}}=\sqrt{2}\left|v^{\underline{L}}\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}+2\widehat{v}^{\underline{L}}\leq(2+\sqrt{2})|\widehat{v}^{\underline{L}}|^{\frac{1}{2}}.

We end this section by a convergence estimate for the Lorentz force.

Proposition 5.58
.

Let
|\gamma|\leq N_{0}-1
and
1\leq j\leq 3
. Then, for all
(t,x,v)\in[2,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
,

\Big|t^{2}\widehat{v}^{\mu}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}\big(F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]\big)_{\mu j}(t,x+t\widehat{v})-\widehat{v}^{\mu}\mathbb{F}_{\mu j}\big[\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}_{\infty}f_{\infty}\big](v)\Big|\lesssim\bigg(\frac{|\widehat{v}^{\underline{L}}(x+t\widehat{v})|^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\langle t-|x+t\widehat{v}|\rangle}+\frac{1}{t}\bigg)\langle x\rangle\,\langle v\rangle\,\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}].

Note that the right hand side is bounded by
t^{-\frac{1}{2}}\,\langle x\rangle^{\frac{3}{2}}\,\langle v\rangle\,\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}]
according to Lemma
2.9
.

Proof.

Start by applying Lemma
5.56
to
G:=\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}(F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}])
in order to get

t^{2}\big|\widehat{v}^{\mu}G_{\mu j}\big|(t,x+t\widehat{v})\lesssim t^{2}\!\big(|\alpha(G)|\!+\!|\rho(G)|\!+\!|\sigma(G)|\big)(t,x+t\widehat{v})+t^{2}\big(\widehat{v}^{\underline{L}}(x+t\widehat{v})+|\widehat{\not{v}}(x+t\widehat{v})|\big)|\underline{\alpha}(G)|(t,x+t\widehat{v}).

If
Z^{\gamma}
contains at least one translation
\partial_{x^{\lambda}}
, that is
\gamma_{T}\geq 1
, the pointwise decay estimates for the null components of
G
, given by Proposition
5.50
and Lemma
3.9
, provide

t^{2}\big|\widehat{v}^{\mu}G_{\mu j}\big|(t,x+t\widehat{v})\lesssim\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}]\left[\langle t-|x+t\widehat{v}|\rangle^{-1}+t\,\langle t-|x+t\widehat{v}|\rangle^{-2}\big(\widehat{v}^{\underline{L}}(x+t\widehat{v})+|\widehat{\not{v}}(x+t\widehat{v})|\big)\right].

It remains to use
\mathbb{F}\big[\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}_{\infty}f_{\infty}\big]=0
and to apply Lemma
2.9
, which gives

1\lesssim\langle v\rangle\big|\widehat{v}^{\underline{L}}\big|^{1/2},\qquad t\big(\widehat{v}^{\underline{L}}(x+t\widehat{v})+|\widehat{\not{v}}(x+t\widehat{v})|\big)\lesssim\langle x\rangle\,\max\big(1,t-|x-t\widehat{v}|\big).

(107)

Assume now
Z^{\gamma}
is only composed by homogeneous vector fields. We treat first the case
|x+t\widehat{v}|\geq t-2
. Then, as
\big|\mathbb{F}\big[\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}_{\infty}f_{\infty}\big]\big|(v)\lesssim\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}]
by Proposition
5.45
, the decay estimates of Proposition
5.50
yield

t^{2}\big|\widehat{v}^{\mu}G_{\mu j}\big|(t,x+t\widehat{v})+\big|\widehat{v}^{\mu}\mathbb{F}_{\mu j}\big[\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}_{\infty}f_{\infty}\big](v)\big|\lesssim\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}]\left[1+t\,\langle t-|x+t\widehat{v}|\rangle^{-1}\big(\widehat{v}^{\underline{L}}(x+t\widehat{v})+|\widehat{\not{v}}(x+t\widehat{v})|\big)\right].

The result follows from (
107
) as well as
|x|\geq|x+t\widehat{v}|-t\geq-2
. Finally, if
|x+t\widehat{v}|\leq t-2
, since
G=F^{\gamma,\mathrm{hom}}+F^{\gamma,\mathrm{inh}}
, we have, according to Proposition
5.44
,

t^{2}\widehat{v}^{\mu}G_{\mu j}(t,x+t\widehat{v})-\widehat{v}^{\mu}\mathbb{F}_{\mu j}\big[\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}_{\infty}f_{\infty}\big](v)=t^{2}\widehat{v}^{\mu}F^{\gamma,\mathrm{hom}}_{\mu j}(t,x+t\widehat{v})+\widehat{v}^{\mu}\mathbb{F}_{\mu j}\big[\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}_{\infty}f_{\infty}\big]\bigg(\frac{\widecheck{x+t\widehat{v}}}{t}\bigg)-\widehat{v}^{\mu}\mathbb{F}_{\mu j}\big[\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}_{\infty}f_{\infty}\big](v).

We can bound the last two terms on the right hand side by
\langle x\rangle\langle v\rangle\,t^{-1}\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}]
by interpolation. Indeed, they are both controlled by
\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}]
and their difference is bounded by
\langle x\rangle\,t^{-1}\min(1,\langle v\rangle^{2}t^{-1})\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}]
according to (
94
). It then remains us to control sufficiently well
t^{2}\widehat{v}^{\mu}F^{\gamma,\mathrm{hom}}_{\mu j}(t,x+t\widehat{v})
. For this, we apply Lemma
5.56
in order to get

\displaystyle t^{2}\big|\widehat{v}^{\mu}F^{\gamma,\mathrm{hom}}_{\mu j}\big|(t,x+t\widehat{v})

\displaystyle\lesssim t^{2}\!\big(|\alpha(F^{\gamma,\mathrm{hom}})|\!+\!|\rho(F^{\gamma,\mathrm{hom}})|\!+\!|\sigma(F^{\gamma,\mathrm{hom}})|\big)(t,x+t\widehat{v})

\displaystyle\quad+t^{2}\big(\widehat{v}^{\underline{L}}(x+t\widehat{v})+|\widehat{\not{v}}(x+t\widehat{v})|\big)\big|\underline{\alpha}(F^{\gamma,\mathrm{hom}})\big|(t,x+t\widehat{v}).

We then obtain from Lemma
5.55
and (
107
) that

t^{2}\big|\widehat{v}^{\mu}F^{\gamma,\mathrm{hom}}_{\mu j}\big|(t,x+t\widehat{v})\lesssim\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}]\,\langle t-|x+t\widehat{v}|\rangle^{-1}\langle x\rangle\lesssim\big|v^{\underline{L}}(x+t\widehat{v})\big|^{\frac{1}{2}}\langle t-|x+t\widehat{v}|\rangle^{-1}\langle x\rangle\,\langle v\rangle\,\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}].

Remark 5.59
.

Let us mention that a refined estimate could provide an even stronger convergence estimate for
t^{2}(\delta_{i}^{j}-\widehat{v}_{i}\widehat{v}^{j})\widehat{v}^{\mu}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}\big(F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]\big)_{\mu j}(t,x+t\widehat{v})
. In particular, exploiting Remark
5.57
, we could prove an estimate uniform in
v
. For this, it would also be important to prove that
\langle v\rangle^{2}\,|\alpha(\mathbb{F}[f_{\infty}])|(v)\lesssim\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}]
by exploiting the strong decay estimate satisfied by
\alpha(F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}])
.

6
Set up of the Picard iteration and strategy of the proof

We fix, for the rest of this paper, an initial time
T\geq 2
and two constants
\Lambda\geq 1
,
\epsilon>0
such that
\Lambda\geq\epsilon
. We al so consider
N\geq 8
, as well as a distribution function
f_{\infty}:\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\to\mathbb{R}
and a radiation field
\underline{\alpha}^{\infty}
satisfying the assumptions of Theorem
1.8
. We assume further

\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N+1}[f_{\infty}]=\sup_{|\kappa_{z}|+|\kappa_{v}|\leq N+1}\,\sup_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\langle z\rangle^{4+|\kappa_{z}|}\,\langle v\rangle^{7+3|\kappa_{v}|}\big|\partial_{z}^{\kappa_{z}}\partial_{v}^{\kappa_{v}}f_{\infty}\big|(z,v)\leq\sqrt{\epsilon},

(108)

so that the result of Section
5
holds for the order of regularity
N_{0}=N
. The field
F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]
will denote the solution to (
87
) with initial time
t_{0}=1
. This extra assumption is automatically verified if
N=8
but requires more decay in
v
for
f_{\infty}
when
N\geq 9
. It is only significant when
N
is large. It will allow us to work with a much more convenient functional space and to simplify two parts of the proof. We detail how to deal with the general case in Section
10
.

For convenience, and since a smallness assumption will be required on this quantity, we further impose
\epsilon\log^{-1}(T)\leq 1
. In particular, we then have according to Proposition
5.45
that
|\mathscr{C}_{t,v}|\lesssim\sqrt{\epsilon}\log(t)\lesssim\log^{2}(t)
, where
\mathscr{C}_{t,v}
is introduced in (
34
).

6.1 Sequence of approximate solutions

The proof of Theorem
1.8
is based on a Picard iteration. More precisely, we will prove that the following sequence
(f_{n},F^{n})_{n\geq 1}
is well-defined.

•

We consider first

f_{1}(t,x,v):=f_{\infty}(t,x-t\widehat{v},v),

so that
f_{1}
is the solution of the linear Vlasov equation
\mathbf{T}_{0}(f_{1})=\widehat{v}^{\mu}\partial_{x^{\mu}}f_{1}=0
satisfying
f_{1}(0,\cdot,\cdot)=f_{\infty}
.

•

Assume that we have constructed the distribution function
f_{n}:[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
, for
n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
. We define
F^{n}
as the unique solution to

\nabla^{\mu}F^{n}_{\mu\nu}=J(f_{n})_{\nu},\qquad\nabla^{\mu}{}^{*}\!F^{n}_{\mu\nu}=0,\qquad\qquad\lim_{r\to+\infty}r\underline{\alpha}(F^{n})(r+u,r\omega)=\underline{\alpha}^{\infty}(u,\omega).

(109)

•

Suppose that we have constructed the electromagnetic field
F^{n}
, defined on
[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
, for
n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
. Then, the distribution function
f_{n+1}
is defined as the unique function satisfying

\mathbf{T}_{F^{n}}(f_{n+1})=0,\qquad\qquad\lim_{t\to+\infty}f_{n+1}\big(t,x+t\widehat{v}+\mathscr{C}_{t,v},v\big)=f_{\infty}(z,v).

(110)

For this, we will have to justify that (
109
) and (
110
) both admit a unique classical solution. Then, we will prove that
(f_{n},F^{n})_{n\geq 1}
is a Cauchy sequence in a well-chosen Banach space.

Remark 6.1
.

(f_{1},F^{1})
is a solution to the linearised system (
6
)–(
8
) around the trivial solution.

6.2 Functional spaces

For
n\geq 1
, the function
f_{n}
will belong to the following set.

Definition 6.2
.

Let
D\geq 1
and
\mathbb{V}^{D,\epsilon}_{N}
be the set of the distribution functions
f:[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\to\mathbb{R}
such that
g(t,z,v):=f(t,x+t\widehat{v}+\mathscr{C}_{t,v},v)
verifies the bound

\mathbf{E}_{N}^{7,13}[g]:=\sup_{|\beta|\leq N}\sup_{t\geq T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\langle z\rangle^{14+2N-2\beta_{H}}\,\langle v\rangle^{26}\big|\widehat{Z}^{\beta}_{\infty}g(t,z,v)\big|^{2}\mathrm{d}z\mathrm{d}v\leq D\epsilon.

For the electromagnetic fields
F^{n}
, we will make use of the norms introduced in (
54
).

Definition 6.3
.

Let
D\geq 1
and
\mathbb{M}_{N}^{D,\Lambda}
be the set of the
2
-forms
F
defined on
[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
and verifying

\sup_{t\geq T}\mathcal{E}^{K}_{N-1}\big[F-F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]\big](t)+\,\sup_{|\gamma|=N-1}\,\sup_{t\geq T}\mathcal{E}^{K,1}\big[\nabla_{t,x}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}(F-F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}])\big](t)\leq D\Lambda.

We now derive the properties that we will use for the electromagnetic fields
F\in\mathbb{M}^{D,\Lambda}_{N}
. For this, we will make use of the following result, suggesting that the velocity average of a distribution function satisfying modified scattering still decays as
t^{-3}
.

Lemma 6.4
.

There exists an absolute constant
\delta>0
such that, if
\sqrt{\epsilon}T^{-1}\log(T)\leq\delta
, then

\forall\,(t,x)\in[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3},\qquad\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\frac{\mathrm{d}v}{\langle x-t\widehat{v}-\mathscr{C}_{t,v}\rangle^{3}\,\langle v\rangle^{5}}\lesssim\frac{\log(t)}{\langle t+|x|\rangle^{3}}.

Proof.

Let us justify that we can perform the change of variables
y=x-t\psi_{t}(v)
, where
\psi_{t}(v):=\widehat{v}+t^{-1}\mathscr{C}_{t,v}
.
Recall now from Lemma
2.10
that the Jacobian determinant of
v\mapsto\widehat{v}
is equal to
-\langle v\rangle^{-5}
. Moreover, as
|\mathbb{F}_{\mu\nu}[f_{\infty}](v)|+|v^{0}\partial_{v^{k}}\mathbb{F}_{\mu\nu}[f_{\infty}](v)|\lesssim\sqrt{\epsilon}
according to Proposition
5.45
, we have

|\mathscr{C}_{t,v}|\lesssim\sqrt{\epsilon}\log(t)\langle v\rangle^{-1},\qquad\qquad|\partial_{v^{k}}\mathscr{C}^{i}_{t,v}|=\log(t)\Big|\partial_{v^{k}}\Big(\,\frac{\delta^{j}_{i}-\widehat{v}_{i}\widehat{v}^{j}}{v^{0}}{\mathbb{F}_{\mu j}}[f_{\infty}](v)\Big)\Big|\lesssim\sqrt{\epsilon}\log(t)\langle v\rangle^{-2}.

The Jacobian determinant of
v\mapsto t^{-1}\mathscr{C}_{t,v}
is then bounded by
\epsilon^{\frac{3}{2}}t^{-3}\log^{3}(t)\langle v\rangle^{-6}
. Hence, there exists
\delta>0
such that, if
\sqrt{\epsilon}T^{-1}\log(T)\leq\delta
, then

\forall\,t\geq T,\qquad|\det\psi_{t}(v)|\geq\langle v\rangle^{-5}/2,\qquad|\psi_{t}(v)|\leq 2.

We then deduce that

\forall\,t\geq T,\qquad t^{3}\,\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\frac{\mathrm{d}v}{\langle x-t\widehat{v}-\mathscr{C}_{t,v}\rangle^{3}\,\langle v\rangle^{5}}\leq\int_{|y-x|\leq 2t}\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{\langle y\rangle^{3}}\leq 4\log(t).

It concludes the proof if
|x|\leq 3t
. Otherwise use that we have
|x-t\widehat{v}-\mathscr{C}_{t,v}|\leq|x|-t-\sqrt{\epsilon}\log(t)\leq|x|/3
.

Proposition 6.5
.

Let
F\in\mathbb{M}_{N}^{D,\epsilon}
. Then, the following pointwise decay estimates hold for any
|\gamma|\leq N-3
,

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69054pt\forall\,(t,x)\in[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3},\qquad\quad\qquad

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F\right|(t,x)\lesssim\sqrt{\Lambda}\langle t+|x|\rangle^{-1}\langle t-|x|\rangle^{-1},

(111)

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69054pt\forall\,(t,x)\in[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3},\quad\qquad\qquad

\displaystyle\big(|\alpha(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F)|+|\rho(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F)|+|\sigma(\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F)|\big)(t,x)\lesssim\sqrt{\Lambda}\,\langle t+|x|\rangle^{-2},

(112)

as well as, for all
(t,x,v)\in[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
and any
1\leq j\leq 3
,

\left|t^{2}\widehat{v}^{\mu}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}(F)_{\mu j}(t,x+t\widehat{v})-\widehat{v}^{\mu}\mathbb{F}_{\mu j}\big[\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\gamma}f_{\infty}\big](v)\right|\lesssim\sqrt{\Lambda}\bigg(\,\frac{|v^{\underline{L}}(x+t\widehat{v})|^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\langle t-|x+t\widehat{v}|\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}}+\frac{1}{t}\bigg)\langle x\rangle\,\langle v\rangle.

(113)

Moreover, we have for any
N-3\leq|\xi|\leq N-1
and
N-2\leq|\gamma|\leq N-1
,

\displaystyle\hskip-19.91692pt\forall\,t\geq T,\qquad

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\max(t-|x|,1)\,\langle t-|x|\rangle^{2}\big|\nabla_{t,x}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\xi}}F\big|^{2}(t,x)\mathrm{d}x\lesssim\Lambda\log^{2}(t),

(114)

\displaystyle\hskip-19.91692pt\forall\,t\geq T,\qquad

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\left|t^{2}\widehat{v}^{\mu}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}(F)_{\mu j}(t,x)-\widehat{v}^{\mu}\mathbb{F}_{\mu j}\big[\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\gamma}f_{\infty}\big](v)\right|^{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x}{\langle x-t\widehat{v}-\mathscr{C}_{t,v}\rangle^{7}\langle v\rangle^{13}}\lesssim\Lambda\frac{\log^{9}(t)}{t}.

(115)

Proof.

We recall, since we will often use it here, that
\epsilon\leq\Lambda
and
\Lambda\geq 1
. By applying Proposition
3.13
to
H:=\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}(F-F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}])
, for
|\gamma|\leq N-3
, we obtain that

\displaystyle|\underline{\alpha}(H)|(t,x)

\displaystyle\lesssim\langle t+|x|\rangle^{-1}\,\langle t-|x|\rangle^{-\frac{3}{2}}\left|\mathcal{E}^{K}_{N-1}\big[F-F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]\big](t)\right|^{\frac{1}{2}},

\displaystyle|\alpha(H)|(t,x)+|\rho(H)|(t,x)+|\sigma(H)|(t,x)

\displaystyle\lesssim\langle t+|x|\rangle^{-2}\,\langle t-|x|\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left|\mathcal{E}^{K}_{N-1}\big[F-F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]\big](t)\right|^{\frac{1}{2}},

for all
(t,x)\in[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
. Together with Proposition
5.50
, where the the null components of
\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]
are estimated, it yields (
111
)–(
112
). Recall now from Lemma
2.9
that
\langle t+|x|\rangle(\widehat{v}^{\underline{L}}+|\not{\widehat{v}}|)\lesssim\langle t-|x|\rangle\,\langle x-t\widehat{v}\rangle
and
1\lesssim\langle v\rangle|\widehat{v}^{\underline{L}}|^{1/2}
. By Lemma
5.56
, we then get

t^{2}\big|\widehat{v}^{\mu}H_{\mu j}\big|(t,x)\lesssim\big|v^{\underline{L}}(x)\big|^{\frac{1}{2}}\langle v\rangle\,t^{2}\big(|\alpha(H)|+|\rho(H)|+|\sigma(H)|\big)(t,x)+\big|v^{\underline{L}}(x)\big|^{\frac{1}{2}}\langle x-t\widehat{v}\rangle\,\langle v\rangle\frac{t^{2}\langle t-|x|\rangle}{\langle t+|x|\rangle}|\underline{\alpha}(H)|(t,x).

Using the estimates for the null components of
H
, we then deduce that

t^{2}\big|\widehat{v}^{\mu}H_{\mu j}\big|(t,x+t\widehat{v})\lesssim\sqrt{\Lambda}\big|v^{\underline{L}}(x+t\widehat{v})\big|^{\frac{1}{2}}\langle x\rangle\,\langle v\rangle\,\langle t-|x-t\widehat{v}|\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}},

so that (
113
) follows from the corresponding estimate for
\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]
, given by Proposition
5.58
, and
\epsilon\leq\Lambda
. Next we fix
N-3\leq|\xi|\leq N-1
and we remark that Proposition
5.19
implies

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\frac{\langle t-|x|\rangle^{3}}{\log^{2}(1+\langle t-|x|\rangle)}\big|\nabla_{t,x}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\xi}}F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]\big|^{2}(t,x)\mathrm{d}x\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\frac{\epsilon\,\mathrm{d}x}{\langle t+|x|\rangle^{2}\langle t-|x|\rangle\,\log^{2}(1+\langle t-|x|\rangle)}\lesssim\epsilon.

If
|\xi|=N-1
, we then obtain (
114
) by using the
L^{2}_{x}
estimate for the top order derivatives of
F\in\mathbb{M}^{D,\Lambda}_{N}
. Otherwise, applying Lemma
3.9
, in order to gain
t-r
decay through
\nabla_{t,x}
, we get

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\langle t-|x|\rangle^{4}\big|\nabla_{t,x}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\xi}}\big(F-F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]\big)\big|^{2}(t,x)\mathrm{d}x\lesssim\mathcal{E}^{K}_{N-1}\big[F-F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]\big](t)\leq D\Lambda.

(116)

Finally, using first Proposition
5.58
and then
|\mathscr{C}_{t,v}|\lesssim\sqrt{\epsilon}\log(t)\lesssim\log^{2}(t)
as well as the change of variables
y=x-t\widehat{v}-\mathscr{C}_{t,v}
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\left|t^{2}\widehat{v}^{\mu}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}(F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}])_{\mu j}(t,x)-\widehat{v}^{\mu}\mathbb{F}_{\mu j}\big[\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\gamma}f_{\infty}\big](v)\right|^{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x}{\langle x-t\widehat{v}-\mathscr{C}_{t,v}\rangle^{7}\langle v\rangle^{13}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\frac{\epsilon\,\langle x-t\widehat{v}\rangle^{3}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x}{t\,\langle x-t\widehat{v}-\mathscr{C}_{t,v}\rangle^{7}\langle v\rangle^{11}}\lesssim\frac{\epsilon\,\langle\log^{2}(t)\rangle^{3}}{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\frac{\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x}{\langle x-t\widehat{v}-\mathscr{C}_{t,v}\rangle^{4}\langle v\rangle^{11}}\lesssim\frac{\epsilon\log^{6}(t)}{t}.

Let
G:=F-F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]
and remark that Lemma
2.9
implies

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\frac{|t^{2}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}G|^{2}(t,x)\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x}{\langle x-t\widehat{v}-\mathscr{C}_{t,v}\rangle^{7}\langle v\rangle^{13}}\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\big|\max(t-|x|,1)\big|^{4}\left|\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}G\right|^{2}(t,x)\frac{\langle x-t\widehat{v}\rangle^{4}\,\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x}{\langle x-t\widehat{v}-\mathscr{C}_{t,v}\rangle^{7}\langle v\rangle^{5}}.

Using again
|\mathscr{C}_{t,v}|\lesssim\log^{2}(t)
and applying Lemma
6.4
, one obtains

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\frac{|t^{2}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}G|^{2}(t,x)\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x}{\langle x-t\widehat{v}-\mathscr{C}_{t,v}\rangle^{7}\langle v\rangle^{13}}\lesssim\log^{9}(t)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\big|\max(t-|x|,1)\big|^{4}\left|\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}G\right|^{2}(t,x)\frac{\mathrm{d}x}{\langle t+|x|\rangle^{3}}.

If
|\gamma|\leq N-1
, the right hand side is bounded by

\log^{9}(t)t^{-1}\mathcal{E}^{K}_{N-1}\big[F-F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]\big](t)\leq D\Lambda\log^{9}(t)t^{-1}.

Otherwise, there exists
|\kappa|=N-1
such that
Z^{\gamma}=ZZ^{\kappa}
. Then
|\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}G|\lesssim\langle t+r\rangle|\nabla_{t,x}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\kappa}}G|
and the right hand side of the previous inequality is this time bounded above by

\log^{9}(t)t^{-1}\mathcal{E}^{K,1}\big[\nabla_{t,x}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\kappa}}\big(F-F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]\big)\big](t)\leq D\Lambda\log^{9}(t)t^{-1}.

Let us finally prove an inequality which will be used several times in the next section.

Lemma 6.6
.

For all
(t,z,v)\in[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
, we have

\langle t+|\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}|\rangle\lesssim\log^{2}(t)\langle t-|\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}|\rangle\,\langle v\rangle^{2}\,\langle z\rangle.

Proof.

Apply Lemma
2.9
for
x=\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}(t,z,v)
and use
|\mathscr{C}_{t,v}|\lesssim\log^{2}(t)
, so that
\langle z-\mathscr{C}_{t,v}\rangle\lesssim\langle z\rangle\,\log^{2}(t)
.

6.3 The Picard iteration and strategy of the proof

The main part of the remainder of this article will be devoted to the proof of the following proposition.

Proposition 6.7
.

There exists
D\geq 1
and
B>0
, both depending only
N
, and an absolute constant
\overline{\varepsilon}>0
, such that the following properties hold. If
B_{\Lambda}\epsilon\,\log^{-1}(T)\leq\overline{\varepsilon}
, where
B_{\Lambda}:=\exp\big(\exp\big(B\sqrt{\Lambda}\big)\big)
, then

1.

F^{1}
is well defined and belongs to
\mathbb{M}_{N}^{D,\Lambda}
.

2.

For any
F\in\mathbb{M}_{N}^{D,\Lambda}
, there exists a unique function
f:[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\to\mathbb{R}
such that

\mathbf{T}_{F}(f)=0,\qquad\lim_{t\to+\infty}f(t,x+t\widehat{v}+\mathscr{C}_{t,v},v)=f_{\infty}(x,v),\qquad f\in\mathbb{V}^{B_{\Lambda},\epsilon}_{N}.

Moreover, there exists a unique classical solution
F^{\mathrm{new}}
to the Maxwell equations with asymptotic data

\nabla^{\mu}G_{\mu\nu}=J(f)_{\nu},\qquad\nabla^{\mu}{}^{*}\!G_{\mu\nu}=0,\qquad\lim_{r\to+\infty}r\underline{\alpha}(G)(r+u,r\omega)=\underline{\alpha}^{\infty}(u,\omega),

(117)

and
F^{\mathrm{new}}\in\mathbb{M}^{D,\Lambda}_{N}
.

This result allows us to uniquely define the sequence
(f_{n},F_{n})_{n\geq 1}\in(\mathbb{V}^{B_{\Lambda},\epsilon}_{N}\times\mathbb{M}^{D,\Lambda}_{N}\,)^{\mathbb{N}^{*}}
. Section
7
will be devoted to the analysis of the Vlasov equation and Section
8
to the asymptotic Cauchy problem (
117
) as well as the construction of
F^{1}
. Then, in Section
9
, we prove that
(f_{n},F_{n})_{n\geq 1}
is a Cauchy sequence in
L^{\infty}\big([T,+\infty[,L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})\times L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})\big)
, allowing us to conclude the proof of Theorem
1.8
if the additional assumption (
108
) holds. Finally, in Section
10
, we treat the general case. In this more general setting,
\mathbb{M}^{D,\Lambda}_{N}
is not necessarily well-defined so we need to consider a slightly different functional space. Two steps are then slightly more technical, the proof of Proposition
6.5
and part of the analysis of (
117
).

7
The Vlasov equation with asymptotic data

We fix, for all this section as well as the next one, Section
8
,
F\in\mathbb{M}_{N}^{D,\Lambda}
. When there is no risk of confusion, we will simply write
\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}
instead of
\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}(t,z,v)=z+t\widehat{v}+\mathscr{C}_{t,v}
, where the correction term is given in (
34
).

7.1 Preparatories

We start by proving results which will be useful in order to state the first and the higher order commutation formula. We will then be able to propagate regularity on the solution. In order to simplify the presentation of certain computations, we introduce the following notation.

Definition 7.1
.

Let
|\gamma|\leq N
,
1\leq i\leq 3
and consider

\Delta_{Z^{\gamma}}^{i}(t,z,v):=\frac{\widehat{v}^{\mu}}{v^{0}}\Big(t^{2}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}(F)_{\mu j}\big(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}(t,z,v)\big)-\mathbb{F}_{\mu j}\big[\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\gamma}f_{\infty}\big](v)\Big).

When
|\gamma|=0
, we simply write
\Delta^{i}
.

Remark 7.2
.

Since
\mathbb{F}_{\mu\nu}[\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\xi}f_{\infty}](v)=0
if the multi-index
\xi
is such that
Z^{\xi}
contains at least one translation
\partial_{x^{\lambda}}
or the scaling vector field
S
, we have, for any
|\gamma|\leq N-1
and
\lambda\in\llbracket 0,3\rrbracket
,

\Delta_{\partial_{x^{\lambda}}Z^{\gamma}}^{i}(t,z,v)=t^{2}\frac{\widehat{v}^{\mu}}{v^{0}}{\mathcal{L}_{\partial_{x^{\lambda}}Z^{\gamma}}(F)_{\mu}}^{j}\big(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}\big),\qquad\qquad\Delta_{SZ^{\gamma}}^{i}(t,z,v)=t^{2}\frac{\widehat{v}^{\mu}}{v^{0}}{\mathcal{L}_{SZ^{\gamma}}(F)_{\mu}}^{j}\big(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}\big).

The following results will be useful in order to commute the Vlasov equation.

Lemma 7.3
.

Let
G
be a sufficiently regular
2
-form defined on
[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
. Then, denoting
\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}(t,z,v)
by
\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}
, we have for all
(t,z,v)\in[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
,

\displaystyle\partial_{z^{k}}\big[v^{\mu}G_{\mu\ell}(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})\big]

\displaystyle=v^{\mu}\mathcal{L}_{\partial_{x^{k}}}(G)_{\mu\ell}(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}),

\displaystyle\partial_{t}^{\infty}\big[v^{\mu}G_{\mu\ell}(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})\big]

\displaystyle=v^{\mu}\mathcal{L}_{\partial_{t}}(G)_{\mu\ell}(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})+\partial_{t}\big(\mathscr{C}_{t,v}^{n}\big)v^{\mu}\mathcal{L}_{\partial_{x^{n}}}(G)_{\mu\ell}(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}),

\displaystyle S\big[v^{\mu}G_{\mu\ell}(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})\big]

\displaystyle=\Big[v^{\mu}\mathcal{L}_{S}(G)_{\mu\ell}-2v^{\mu}G_{\mu\ell}+t\partial_{t}\big(\mathscr{C}_{t,v}^{n}\big)v^{\mu}\mathcal{L}_{\partial_{x^{n}}}(G)_{\mu\ell}\Big](t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}),

\displaystyle\widehat{\Omega}_{ij}\big[v^{\mu}G_{\mu\ell}(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})\big]

\displaystyle=\left[v^{\mu}\mathcal{L}_{\Omega_{ij}}(G)_{\mu\ell}-\delta_{\ell}^{i}v^{\mu}G_{\mu j}+\delta_{\ell}^{j}v^{\mu}G_{\mu i}\right]\!(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})

\displaystyle\quad+\left[\mathscr{C}^{j}_{t,v}v^{\mu}\mathcal{L}_{\partial_{x^{i}}}(G)_{\mu\ell}-\mathscr{C}^{i}_{t,v}v^{\mu}\mathcal{L}_{\partial_{x^{j}}}(G)_{\mu\ell}+\widehat{\Omega}_{ij}\big(\mathscr{C}^{n}_{t,v}\big)v^{\mu}\mathcal{L}_{\partial_{x^{n}}}(G)_{\mu\ell}\right]\!(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}),

\displaystyle\widehat{\Omega}_{0k}^{\infty}\big[v^{\mu}G_{\mu\ell}(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})\big]

\displaystyle=\left[v^{\mu}\mathcal{L}_{\Omega_{0k}}(G)_{\mu\ell}+\delta_{\ell}^{k}\widehat{v}^{n}v^{\mu}G_{\mu n}-\mathscr{C}^{k}_{t,v}v^{\mu}\mathcal{L}_{\partial_{t}}(G)_{\mu\ell}+\widehat{\Omega}_{0k}^{\infty}\big(\mathscr{C}^{n}_{t,v}\big)v^{\mu}\mathcal{L}_{\partial_{x^{n}}}(G)_{\mu\ell}\right]\!(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}),

for any
1\leq i<j\leq 3
and
1\leq k,\,\ell\leq 3
.

Proof.

Since
\partial_{z^{i}}\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}^{j}(t,z,v)=\delta_{i}^{j}
and
\mathcal{L}_{\partial_{x^{\nu}}}=\nabla_{\partial_{x^{\nu}}}
, we have

\partial_{z^{k}}\big[G_{\mu\ell}\big(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}\big)\big]=\big[\partial_{x^{k}}G_{\mu\ell}\big]\big(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}\big)=\mathcal{L}_{\partial_{x^{k}}}(G)_{\mu\ell}\big(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}\big).

We get similarly the relation for
\partial_{t}^{\infty}=\partial_{t}-\widehat{v}\cdot\nabla_{z}
since

\partial_{t}\big[G_{\mu\ell}\big(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}\big)\big]=\big[\partial_{t}G_{\mu\ell}\big]\big(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}\big)+\widehat{v}^{n}\big[\partial_{x^{n}}G_{\mu\ell}\big]\big(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}\big)+\partial_{t}\mathscr{C}^{n}_{t,v}\big[\partial_{x^{n}}G_{\mu\ell}\big]\big(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}\big).

Applying the relations of Lemma
2.22
to the function
f(t,x,v)=G_{\mu\nu}(t,x)
, we get

\displaystyle S\big[G_{\mu\ell}(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})\big]\!

\displaystyle=\!\big[SG_{\mu\ell}\big](t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})-\big(\mathscr{C}^{\ell}_{t,v}-t\partial_{t}\mathscr{C}_{t,v}^{n}\big)\big[\partial_{x^{n}}G_{\mu\ell}\big](t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}),

\displaystyle\widehat{\Omega}_{ij}\big[G_{\mu\ell}(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})\big]\!

\displaystyle=\!\big[\Omega_{ij}G_{\mu\ell}\big](t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})-\mathscr{C}^{i}_{t,v}\big[\partial_{x^{j}}G_{\mu\ell}\big](t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})+\mathscr{C}^{j}_{t,v}\big[\partial_{x^{i}}G_{\mu\ell}\big](t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})+\widehat{\Omega}_{ij}\big(\mathscr{C}_{t,v}^{n}\big)\big[\partial_{x^{n}}G_{\mu\ell}\big](t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}),

\displaystyle\widehat{\Omega}_{0k}^{\infty}\big[G_{\mu\ell}(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})\big]\!

\displaystyle=\!\big[\Omega_{0k}G_{\mu\ell}\big](t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})-\mathscr{C}^{k}_{t,v}\big[\partial_{t}G_{\mu\ell}\big](t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})+\widehat{\Omega}_{0k}^{\infty}\big(\mathscr{C}_{t,v}^{n}\big)\big[\partial_{x^{n}}G_{\mu\ell}\big](t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}).

To conclude the proof for the case of the scaling vector field
S
, we use

\mathcal{L}_{S}(G)_{\mu\ell}=S\!\left(G_{\mu\ell}\right)+2G_{\mu\ell},\qquad\qquad S(v^{\mu})=0.

For the rotations, remark that

\mathcal{L}_{\Omega_{ij}}(G)_{\mu\ell}=\Omega_{ij}\!\left(G_{\mu\ell}\right)+\delta_{\mu}^{i}G_{j\ell}-\delta_{\mu}^{j}G_{i\ell}+\delta_{\ell}^{i}G_{\mu j}-\delta_{\ell}^{j}G_{\mu i},\qquad\big(v^{i}\partial_{v^{j}}-v^{j}\partial_{v^{i}}\big)(v^{\mu})G_{\mu\ell}=v^{i}G_{j\ell}-v^{j}G_{i\ell}.

Finally, for the Lorentz boosts, use

\mathcal{L}_{\Omega_{0k}}(G)_{\mu\ell}=\Omega_{0k}\!\left(G_{\mu\ell}\right)+\delta_{\mu}^{0}G_{k\ell}+\delta_{\mu}^{k}G_{0\ell}+\delta_{\ell}^{0}G_{\mu k}+\delta_{\ell}^{k}G_{\mu 0},\qquad v^{0}\partial_{v^{k}}(v^{\mu})G_{\mu\ell}=v^{k}G_{0\ell}+v^{0}G_{k\ell}

and
\widehat{v}^{\mu}G_{\mu 0}=-\widehat{v}^{\mu}G_{\mu n}\widehat{v}^{n}
since
G
is a
2
-form and
\widehat{v}^{0}=1
.

Lemma 7.4
.

Let
|\gamma|\leq N-1
,
1\leq i<j\leq 3
and
1\leq k,\,\ell\leq 3
. We have, for all
(t,z,v)\in[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
,

\displaystyle\partial_{z^{k}}\Big[t^{-1}\Delta^{\ell}_{Z^{\gamma}}(t,z,v)\Big]

\displaystyle=t^{-1}\Delta^{\ell}_{\partial_{x^{k}}Z^{\gamma}}(t,z,v),

\displaystyle\partial_{t}^{\infty}\Big[t^{-1}\Delta^{\ell}_{Z^{\gamma}}(t,z,v)\Big]

\displaystyle=t^{-1}\Delta^{\ell}_{\partial_{t}Z^{\gamma}}(t,z,v)-t^{-2}\Delta^{\ell}_{Z^{\gamma}}(t,z,v)+2\frac{\widehat{v}^{\mu}}{v^{0}}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}(F)_{\mu\ell}(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})

\displaystyle\quad+t\partial_{t}\big(\mathscr{C}_{t,v}^{n}\big)t^{-2}\Delta^{\ell}_{\partial_{x^{n}}Z^{\gamma}}(t,z,v),

\displaystyle S\Big[t^{-1}\Delta^{\ell}_{Z^{\gamma}}(t,z,v)\Big]

\displaystyle=t^{-1}\Delta^{\ell}_{SZ^{\gamma}}(t,z,v)-t^{-1}\Delta^{\ell}_{Z^{\gamma}}(t,z,v)+t\partial_{t}\big(\mathscr{C}_{t,v}^{n}\big)t^{-1}\Delta^{\ell}_{\partial_{x^{n}}Z^{\gamma}}(t,z,v),

\displaystyle\widehat{\Omega}_{ij}\Big[t^{-1}\Delta^{\ell}_{Z^{\gamma}}(t,z,v)\Big]

\displaystyle=t^{-1}\Delta^{\ell}_{\Omega_{ij}Z^{\gamma}}(t,z,v)-\delta_{n}^{i}t^{-1}\Delta^{j}_{Z^{\gamma}}(t,z,v)+\delta_{n}^{j}t^{-1}\Delta^{i}_{Z^{\gamma}}(t,z,v)

\displaystyle\quad+\mathscr{C}^{j}_{t,v}t^{-1}\Delta^{\ell}_{\partial_{x^{i}}Z^{\gamma}}(t,z,v)-\mathscr{C}^{i}_{t,v}t^{-1}\Delta^{\ell}_{\partial_{x^{j}}Z^{\gamma}}(t,z,v)+\widehat{\Omega}_{ij}\big(\mathscr{C}^{n}_{t,v}\big)t^{-1}\Delta^{\ell}_{\partial_{x^{n}}Z^{\gamma}}(t,z,v),

\displaystyle\widehat{\Omega}_{0k}^{\infty}\Big[t^{-1}\Delta^{\ell}_{Z^{\gamma}}(t,z,v)\Big]

\displaystyle=t^{-1}\Delta^{\ell}_{\Omega_{0k}Z^{\gamma}}(t,z,v)-\widehat{v}^{k}t^{-1}\Delta^{\ell}_{Z^{\gamma}}(t,z,v)+\delta_{\ell}^{k}\widehat{v}_{n}t^{-1}\Delta^{n}_{Z^{\gamma}}(t,z,v)+z^{k}t^{-2}\Delta^{\ell}_{Z^{\gamma}}(t,z,v)

\displaystyle\quad-\mathscr{C}^{k}_{t,v}t^{-1}\Delta^{\ell}_{\partial_{t}Z^{\gamma}}(t,z,v)+\widehat{\Omega}_{0k}^{\infty}\big(\mathscr{C}^{n}_{t,v}\big)t^{-1}\Delta^{\ell}_{\partial_{x^{n}}Z^{\gamma}}(t,z,v).

Proof.

For the translations
\partial_{z^{k}}
, the scaling vector field
S
and the rotations
\widehat{\Omega}_{ij}
, it suffices to apply the previous Lemma
7.3
to
G=\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F
, Proposition
5.45
and to use

\partial_{z^{k}}(t)=\widehat{\Omega}_{ij}(t)=0,\qquad S(t)=t,\qquad\partial_{z^{k}}(v^{0})=S(v^{0})=\widehat{\Omega}_{ij}(v^{0})=0.

For
\partial_{t}^{\infty}=\partial_{t}-\widehat{v}\cdot\nabla_{z}
and the Lorentz boosts, use Lemma
7.3
, Proposition
5.45
as well as

t^{-1}\partial_{t}^{\infty}\!\big(t^{2}|v^{0}|^{-2}\big)\!=2|v^{0}|^{-2},\quad\partial_{t}^{\infty}\!\big(t^{-1}\big)=-t^{-2},\quad t^{-1}\widehat{\Omega}^{\infty}_{0k}\big(t^{2}|v^{0}|^{-2}\big)=2z^{k}|v^{0}|^{-2},\quad\widehat{\Omega}^{\infty}_{0k}\big(t^{-1}\big)=-z^{k}t^{-2}-\widehat{v}^{k}t^{-1}.

As
[\partial_{z^{k}},\partial_{z^{i}}]=[\partial_{z^{k}},v^{0}\partial_{v^{i}}]=0
, we get, by applying Lemmata
7.3
and
7.4
, for
|\gamma|=0
, the first order commutation formula for the
z
derivatives.

Lemma 7.5
.

We have, for any
k\in\llbracket 1,3\rrbracket
,

[\mathbf{T}_{F}^{\infty},\partial_{z^{k}}]=\frac{\delta_{j}^{i}-\widehat{v}^{i}\widehat{v}_{j}}{tv^{0}}\Delta_{\partial_{x^{k}}}^{j}(t,z,v)\partial_{z^{i}}-\frac{\widehat{v}^{\mu}}{v^{0}}{\mathcal{L}_{\partial_{x^{k}}}(F)_{\mu}}^{j}(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})\left(v^{0}\partial_{v^{j}}-v^{0}\partial_{v^{j}}(\mathscr{C}^{i}_{t,v})\partial_{z^{i}}\right)\!.

The cases of the other derivatives are more involved to compute. For this reason, we will directly state the higher order commutation formula. Moreover, as
v^{0}\partial_{v^{i}}
are not part of our set of commutators, we need to rewrite them as

v^{0}\partial_{v^{k}}=\widehat{\Omega}^{\infty}_{0k}-z^{k}\partial_{t}^{\infty}-\widehat{v}^{k}S+\widehat{v}^{k}z^{i}\partial_{z^{i}}.

(118)

7.2 Commutation formula

It will be convenient to introduce the schematic notation
P_{p,q}(\mathscr{C})
in order to denote any quantity of the form

P_{p,q}(\mathscr{C}):=\prod_{1\leq k\leq p}\big(v^{0}\partial_{v}\big)^{\kappa_{k}}(t\partial_{t})^{n_{k}}\!\left(\mathscr{C}^{i_{k}}\right),\qquad(p,q)\in\mathbb{N}^{2},\quad 1\leq i_{k}\leq 3,\quad q=\sum_{1\leq k\leq p}|\kappa_{k}|+n_{k}.

By convention, we set
P_{0,q}(\mathscr{C})=1
for
p=0
. Note that these quantities does not generate strong divergent terms since, in view of Proposition
5.45
and
\epsilon\log^{-1}(T)\leq 1
, we have

\hskip-42.67912pt\forall\,(t,v)\in[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v},\qquad\qquad\big|P_{p,q}(\mathscr{C})\big|(t,v)\lesssim\epsilon^{\frac{p}{2}}|v^{0}|^{-p}\log^{p}(t)\leq\log^{2p}(t).

(119)

In order to state the commutation formula in a concise form, we will use the notation
\partial_{z^{0}}\!=\partial_{t}^{\infty}=\partial_{t}-\widehat{v}\cdot\nabla_{x}
. Recall further that, for
\widehat{Z}^{\beta}_{\infty}\in\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{S}^{\infty}
,
\beta_{H}
denotes the number of homogeneous vector fields, that is
\widehat{\Omega}_{ij}
,
\widehat{\Omega}^{\infty}_{0k}
and
S
, composing
\widehat{Z}^{\beta}
. The quantity
\beta_{T}=|\beta|-\beta_{H}
is the number of translations
\partial_{z^{\mu}}
.

Proposition 7.6
.

Let
\beta
be a multi-index such that
|\beta|\leq N
. Then,
[\mathbf{T}^{\infty}_{F},\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\beta}]
can be written as a linear combination, with coefficients which are polynomials in
(\widehat{v}^{1},\widehat{v}^{2},\widehat{v}^{3})
, of the following five families of error terms.

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\bullet\;\,\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\kappa}\mathbf{T}_{F}^{\infty},

(Err-0)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\bullet\;\,R\!\left(z\right)P_{p,q}(\mathscr{C})\,t^{-1-m}\Delta^{i}_{Z^{\gamma}}(t,z,v)\,\partial_{z^{\nu}}\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\kappa},\qquad

\displaystyle\nu\in\llbracket 0,3\rrbracket,,

(Err-1)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\bullet\;\,R\!\left(z\right)P_{p,q}(\mathscr{C})\,t^{-m}\,\frac{\widehat{v}^{\mu}}{v^{0}}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}(F)_{\mu i}(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})\,\widehat{Z}_{\infty}\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\kappa},\qquad\qquad

\displaystyle\widehat{Z}_{\infty}\in\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{S}^{\infty},

(Err-2)

where
i\in\llbracket 1,3\rrbracket
,
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
R
is a polynomial in
z=(z^{j})_{1\leq j\leq 3}
of degree
\deg R
,

\deg R+\kappa_{H}\leq\beta_{H},\qquad\qquad\deg R\leq m,\qquad\qquad|\gamma|+|\kappa|\leq|\beta|,\qquad\qquad|\kappa|\leq|\beta|-1,\qquad\qquad p,\,q\leq|\beta|+1.

Moreover, for the terms (
Err-1
)–(
Err-2
), we have the following extra condition. If
\deg R+\kappa_{H}=\beta_{H}
, then

•

either
m\geq 1

•

or
\gamma_{T}\geq 1
and
p=0
.

Remark 7.7
.

The extra condition provides more decay in
t-|\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}|
, if
\gamma_{T}\geq 1
, or in
t
, if
m\geq 1
.

Proof.

The proof is based on an induction and in order to avoid any confusion, we will say that the error terms (
Err-0
)–(
Err-2
) written in the statement of the proposition are associated to the derivative
\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\beta}
. The starting point consists in writting the Vlasov operator as follows

\hskip-14.22636pt\mathbf{T}_{F}^{\infty}=\partial_{t}-\frac{\delta_{j}^{i}-\widehat{v}_{j}\widehat{v}^{i}}{v^{0}}t^{-1}\Delta^{j}\partial_{z^{i}}+\frac{\widehat{v}^{\mu}}{v^{0}}{F_{\mu}}^{j}(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})\left(\widehat{\Omega}^{\infty}_{0j}-z_{j}\partial_{t}^{\infty}-\widehat{v}_{j}S+\widehat{v}_{j}z^{i}\partial_{z^{i}}-v^{0}\partial_{v^{j}}\mathscr{C}^{i}(t,v)\partial_{z^{i}}\right),

(120)

where we rewrite the
v
-derivatives using (
118
). We will further need the following relations,

\displaystyle[\partial_{z^{k}},\partial_{z^{i}}]=0,\qquad[\partial_{z^{k}},\partial_{t}^{\infty}]=0,\qquad[\partial_{z^{k}},S]=\partial_{z^{k}},\qquad[\partial_{z^{k}},\widehat{\Omega}^{\infty}_{0k}]=\partial_{t}^{\infty},\qquad[\partial_{z^{k}},\widehat{\Omega}_{ij}]=\delta_{k}^{i}\partial_{z^{j}}-\delta^{j}_{k}\partial_{z^{i}},

\displaystyle[\partial_{t}^{\infty},\partial_{z^{i}}]=0,\qquad\,[\partial_{t}^{\infty},\partial_{t}^{\infty}]=0,\qquad\,[\partial_{t}^{\infty},S]=\partial_{t}^{\infty},\qquad\,[\partial_{t}^{\infty},\widehat{\Omega}^{\infty}_{0k}]=\partial_{z^{k}},\qquad[\partial_{t}^{\infty},\widehat{\Omega}_{ij}]=0.

(121)

We will also make use of

\displaystyle\partial_{t}^{\infty}(t)=1,\qquad

\displaystyle\partial_{z^{k}}(t)=0,\qquad\quad S(t)=t,\qquad\quad\widehat{\Omega}_{ij}(t)=0,\qquad\qquad\qquad\;\;\;\widehat{\Omega}^{\infty}_{0k}(t)=z^{k}+t\widehat{v}^{k},

\displaystyle\partial_{t}^{\infty}(z^{\ell})=-\widehat{v}^{\ell},\qquad

\displaystyle\partial_{z^{k}}(z^{\ell})=\delta_{k}^{\ell},\qquad S(z^{\ell})=z^{\ell},\qquad\widehat{\Omega}_{ij}(z^{\ell})=\delta^{\ell}_{j}z^{i}-\delta_{i}^{\ell}z^{j},\qquad\,\widehat{\Omega}^{\infty}_{0k}(z^{\ell})=-z^{k}\widehat{v}^{\ell},

\displaystyle\partial_{t}^{\infty}(v^{\mu})=0,\qquad

\displaystyle\partial_{z^{k}}(v^{\mu})=0,\qquad\;S(v^{\mu})=0,\qquad\;\widehat{\Omega}_{ij}(v^{\mu})=\delta^{\ell}_{j}v^{i}-\delta_{i}^{\ell}v^{j},\qquad\widehat{\Omega}^{\infty}_{0k}(v^{\mu})=\delta_{k}^{\mu}v^{0}+\delta_{0}^{\mu}v^{k}.

Next, given a quantity of the form
P_{p,q}(\mathscr{C})
, we have the schematic relations

\displaystyle\partial_{z^{k}}P_{p,q}(\mathscr{C})=0,\qquad\partial^{\infty}_{t}P_{p,q}(\mathscr{C})=\partial_{t}P_{p,q}(\mathscr{C})=t^{-1}P_{p,q+1}(\mathscr{C}),\qquad SP_{p,q}(\mathscr{C})\!=t\partial_{t}P_{p,q}(\mathscr{C})=P_{p,q+1}(\mathscr{C}),

\displaystyle\widehat{\Omega}_{ij}P_{p,q}(\mathscr{C})=\big(v^{i}\partial_{v^{j}}-v^{j}\partial_{v^{i}}\big)P_{p,q}(\mathscr{C})=\widehat{v}^{i}P_{p,q+1}(\mathscr{C})+\widehat{v}^{j}P_{p,q+1}(\mathscr{C}),

\displaystyle\widehat{\Omega}_{0k}^{\infty}P_{p,q}(\mathscr{C})=v^{0}\partial_{v^{k}}P_{p,q}(\mathscr{C})+z^{k}\partial_{t}P_{p,q}(\mathscr{C})+\widehat{v}^{k}t\partial_{t}P_{p,q}(\mathscr{C})=P_{p,q+1}(\mathscr{C})+z^{k}t^{-1}P_{p,q+1}(\mathscr{C})+\widehat{v}^{k}P_{p,q+1}(\mathscr{C}).

Finally, in order to commute with the scaling vector field
S
or
\widehat{\Omega}^{\infty}_{0k}
, we will use

[\partial_{t},t\partial_{t}]=\partial_{t}=\mathbf{T}_{F}^{\infty}+\frac{\delta_{j}^{i}-\widehat{v}_{j}\widehat{v}^{i}}{v^{0}}t^{-1}\Delta^{j}\partial_{z^{i}}-\frac{\widehat{v}^{\mu}}{v^{0}}{F_{\mu}}^{j}(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})\left(\widehat{\Omega}^{\infty}_{0j}-z_{j}\partial_{t}^{\infty}-\widehat{v}_{j}S+\widehat{v}_{j}z^{i}\partial_{z^{i}}-v^{0}\partial_{v^{j}}\mathscr{C}^{i}(t,v)\partial_{z^{i}}\right).

The first order commutation formula then follows from these relations as well as Lemmata
7.3
–
7.4
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
such that the statement holds for any multi-index
|\xi|=n
and consider
\widehat{Z}_{\infty}\in\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{S}^{\infty}
as well as
|\beta|=n
. We use then the general relation

[\mathbf{T}^{\infty}_{F},\widehat{Z}_{\infty}\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\beta}]=[\mathbf{T}^{\infty}_{F},\widehat{Z}_{\infty}]\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\beta}+\widehat{Z}_{\infty}[\mathbf{T}^{\infty}_{F},\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\beta}].

Appealing to the first order commutation formula, we can write
[\mathbf{T}^{\infty}_{F},\widehat{Z}_{\infty}]\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\beta}
as a linear combination of terms of type (
Err-1
)–(
Err-2
), corresponding to the derivative
\widehat{Z}_{\infty}\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\beta}
, and, if
\widehat{Z}_{\infty}=S
or
\widehat{Z}_{\infty}=\widehat{\Omega}^{\infty}_{0k}
,
\mathbf{T}_{F}^{\infty}\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\beta}
. We deal with the last term by writting
\mathbf{T}_{F}^{\infty}\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\beta}=\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\beta}\mathbf{T}_{F}^{\infty}+[\mathbf{T}_{F}^{\infty},\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\beta}]
and then by using the induction hypothesis. The other term,
\widehat{Z}_{\infty}[\mathbf{T}^{\infty}_{F},\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\beta}]
, can be treated by combining the induction hypothesis with the following property.

Let
0\leq\lambda\leq 2
and
\mathbf{E}^{\beta}_{\lambda}
be an error term of the form (Err-
\lambda
), associated to
\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\beta}
. Then,

•

if
(\widehat{Z}_{\infty},\lambda)\neq(\partial_{t}^{\infty},1)
,
\widehat{Z}_{\infty}\mathbf{E}^{\beta}_{\lambda}
is a linear combination of terms of type (Err-
\lambda
) associated to
\widehat{Z}_{\infty}\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\beta}
.

•

\partial_{t}^{\infty}\mathbf{E}^{\beta}_{1}
is a linear combination of terms of type (
Err-1
)–(
Err-2
), associated to
\partial_{t}^{\infty}\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\beta}
.

This can be observed by combining again the relations written in the beginning of the proof with Lemmata
7.3
–
7.4
. The crucial part consists in observing that the extra condition is preserved. For this, introduce
|\xi|=|\beta|+1
such that
\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\xi}=\widehat{Z}_{\infty}\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\beta}
and make the next observations. Once we apply
\widehat{Z}_{\infty}
to an error term
E^{\beta}_{\lambda}
,
\lambda\in\{1,2\}
, we obtain error terms
E^{\xi}_{j}
,
j\in\{1,2\}
, such that

•

if
\widehat{Z}_{\infty}\in\{\partial_{t}^{\infty},\partial_{z^{1}},\,\partial_{z^{2}},\,\partial_{z^{3}}\}
, then
\xi_{H}=\beta_{H}
and
\deg R
as well as
\kappa_{H}
does not strictly increase. The quantities
\gamma_{T}
and
m
does not strictly decrease. Moreover, according to Lemmata
7.3
–
7.4
, if
p
strictly increases, then
m
increases by
1
.

•

Otherwise
\widehat{Z}_{\infty}
is an homogeneous vector field and
\xi_{H}=\beta_{H}+1
. As before, none of the quantities
\gamma_{T}
and
m
strictly decrease. Moreover,
\deg R+\kappa_{H}
increases at most by
1
, in which case either
p
does not stricly increase or
m
increases by
1
.

It is for the purpose of exploiting the hierarchies related to the condition
\deg_{z}\!R+\kappa_{H}\leq\beta_{H}
that we will propagate weighted norms such as the ones of Definition
6.2
. For this we will apply the next result, which is a consequence of Proposition
7.6
.

Corollary 7.8
.

Let
(M_{z},M_{v})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
and
h
be a sufficiently regular solution to
\mathbf{T}_{F}^{\infty}(h)=0
. Then, for any
|\beta|\leq N
, we can bound
\big[\mathbf{T}_{F},\langle v\rangle^{M_{v}}\,\langle z\rangle^{M_{z}-\beta_{H}}\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\beta}\big](h)
by a linear combination of the following terms,

\displaystyle\;\bullet\;\,\frac{\log^{2N+2}(t)}{\langle z\rangle\,v^{0}\,t}\left|\widehat{v}^{\mu}\Big(t^{2}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}(F)_{\mu i}(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})-\mathbb{F}_{\mu i}\big[\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\gamma}f_{\infty}\big](v)\Big)\right|\left|\langle v\rangle^{M_{v}}\,\langle z\rangle^{M_{z}-\kappa_{H}}\,\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\kappa}h\right|,

(T-1)

\displaystyle\;\bullet\;\,\frac{\langle t+|z|\rangle}{v^{0}}\Big|\widehat{v}^{\mu}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}(F)_{\mu i}(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})\Big|\left|\langle v\rangle^{M_{v}}\,\langle z\rangle^{M_{z}-\kappa_{H}}\,\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\kappa}h\right|,\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad Z^{\gamma}=\partial_{x^{\nu}}Z^{\xi}

(T-2)

\displaystyle\;\bullet\;\log^{2N+2}(t)\frac{\langle t+|z|\rangle}{v^{0}\,t}\Big|\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})\Big|\left|\langle v\rangle^{M_{v}}\,\langle z\rangle^{M_{z}-\kappa_{H}}\,\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\kappa}h\right|,

(T-3)

\displaystyle\;\bullet\;\,t^{-\frac{7}{4}}\left|\langle v\rangle^{M_{v}}\,\langle z\rangle^{M_{z}-\kappa_{H}}\,\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\kappa}h\right|,

(T-4)

where
1\leq i\leq 3
, and
|\gamma|+|\kappa|\leq|\beta|+1
and
|\gamma|,\,|\kappa|\leq|\beta|
.

Proof.

Let us first reduce the proof to the case
M_{v}=M_{z}=0
. Note that

\displaystyle\big|\big[\mathbf{T}^{\infty}_{F},\langle v\rangle^{M_{v}}\,\langle z\rangle^{M_{z}-\beta_{H}}\,\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\beta}\big](h)\big|

\displaystyle\leq(|M_{z}|+N)\big|z_{i}\mathbf{T}_{F}^{\infty}(z^{i})\big|\langle v\rangle^{M_{v}}\,\langle z\rangle^{M_{z}-\beta_{H}-2}\big|\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\beta}h\big|

\displaystyle\quad+|M_{v}|\big|\mathbf{T}_{F}^{\infty}(\langle v\rangle)\big|\langle v\rangle^{M_{v}-1}\,\langle z\rangle^{M_{z}-\beta_{H}}\big|\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\beta}h\big|

\displaystyle\quad+\langle v\rangle^{M_{v}}\,\langle z\rangle^{M_{v}}\,\langle z\rangle^{-\beta_{H}}\big|\big[\mathbf{T}^{\infty}_{F},\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\beta}\big](h)\big|.

Recall now from (
119
) that
v^{0}|\partial_{v^{j}}\mathscr{C}^{i}_{t,v}|\lesssim\sqrt{\epsilon}\log(t)
and remark, in view of the expression (
39
) of
\mathbf{T}_{F}^{\infty}
, that

\displaystyle\mathbf{T}_{F}^{\infty}(\langle v\rangle)

\displaystyle=\widehat{v}_{i}\mathbf{T}_{F}^{\infty}(v^{i})=\widehat{v}^{\mu}\widehat{v}^{i}F_{\mu i}(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}),

\displaystyle\mathbf{T}_{F}^{\infty}(z^{i})

\displaystyle=-\frac{\delta_{j}^{i}-\widehat{v}_{j}\widehat{v}^{i}}{tv^{0}}\widehat{v}^{\mu}\Big(t^{2}{F_{\mu}}^{j}(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})-{\mathbb{F}_{\mu}}^{j}[f_{\infty}](v)\Big)-\frac{\widehat{v}^{\mu}}{v^{0}}{F_{\mu}}^{j}(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})v^{0}\partial_{v^{j}}\mathscr{C}^{i}_{t,v}.

The first term on the right hand side of the commutator estimate can then be bounded by quantities of type (
T-1
) and (
T-3
). We can control the second one by terms of the form (
T-3
). Next, in order to deal with the last term on the right hand side, it suffices to prove the result for
M_{v}=M_{z}=0
. For this, we apply the previous Proposition
7.6
. As
\mathbf{T}^{\infty}_{F}(h)=0
, the terms (
Err-0
) vanish. In view of the estimate (
119
) of the correction coefficients, the error terms (
Err-1
)–(
Err-2
) can be bounded by quantities of the form

\displaystyle\mathbf{A}_{1}

\displaystyle:=\log^{2p}(t)\,t^{-1-m}|\Delta^{i}_{Z^{\gamma}}|(t,z,v)\langle z\rangle^{-\beta_{H}+\deg R}\,\big|\partial_{z^{\nu}}\widehat{Z}^{\kappa}h\big|,\qquad

\displaystyle\nu\in\llbracket 0,3\rrbracket,

\displaystyle\mathbf{A}_{2}

\displaystyle:=\log^{2p}(t)\,t^{-m}\left|\frac{\widehat{v}^{\mu}}{v^{0}}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}(F)_{\mu i}(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})\right|\langle z\rangle^{-\beta_{H}+\deg R}\,\big|\widehat{Z}_{\infty}\widehat{Z}^{\kappa}h\big|,\qquad\qquad

\displaystyle\widehat{Z}_{\infty}\in\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{S}^{\infty},

where

\deg R+\kappa_{H}\leq\beta_{H},\qquad i\in\llbracket 1,3\rrbracket,\qquad m\in\mathbb{N},\qquad p\leq|\beta|+1,\qquad|\gamma|+|\kappa|\leq|\beta|,\qquad|\kappa|\leq|\beta|-1

and
\deg R+\kappa_{H}=\beta_{H}
implies

•

m\geq 1

•

or
\gamma_{T}\geq 1
and
p=0
.

Note that, for any
Z\in\mathbb{K}
and
0\leq\nu\leq 3
, we have
[\partial_{x^{\nu}},Z]=0
or
[\partial_{x^{\nu}},Z]=\pm\partial_{x^{\lambda}}
for a certain
0\leq\lambda\leq 3
. Hence, if
\gamma_{T}\geq 1
, we can assume without loss of generality that
Z^{\gamma}=\partial_{x^{\nu}}Z^{\zeta}
.

In order to deal with
\mathbf{A}_{2}
, we stress that there exists
|\xi|=|\kappa|+1
such that
\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\xi}=\widehat{Z}_{\infty}\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\kappa}
and
\xi_{H}\leq\kappa_{H}+1
.

•

If
-\beta_{H}+\deg R\leq-\kappa_{H}-1\leq-\xi_{H}
,
\mathbf{A}_{2}
is less than a term of type (
T-3
).

•

Otherwise
-\beta_{H}+\deg R=-\kappa_{H}
, so that
\langle z\rangle^{-\beta_{H}+\deg R}\leq\langle z\rangle\,\langle z\rangle^{-\xi_{H}}
. If
p\geq 1
, we have
m\geq 1
and
\mathbf{A}_{2}
can be bounded by (
T-3
). Finally, for the case
p=0
, we necessarily have
\gamma_{T}\geq 1
and
\mathbf{A}_{2}
can be controlled by quantities of the form (
T-2
).

For
\mathbf{A}_{1}
, we introduce
|\xi|=|\kappa|+1
verifying
\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\xi}=\partial_{z^{\nu}}\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\kappa}
, so that
\xi_{H}=\kappa_{H}
. Then,

•

if
\deg R+\kappa_{H}\leq\beta_{H}-1
, we have
\langle z\rangle^{-\beta_{H}+\deg R}\leq\langle z\rangle^{-1}\,\langle z\rangle^{-\xi_{H}}
and
\mathbf{A}_{1}
is bounded above by (
T-1
).

•

If
m\geq 1
, we have

\mathbf{A}_{1}\lesssim\frac{\log^{2p}(t)}{v^{0}t^{2}}\left(t^{2}\big|\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F\big|(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})+\big|\mathbb{F}\big[\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\gamma}f_{\infty}\big]\big|(v)\right)\langle z\rangle^{-\xi_{H}}\,\big|\widehat{Z}^{\xi}h\big|.

Since
\big|\mathbb{F}\big[\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\gamma}f_{\infty}\big]\big|(v)\lesssim\sqrt{\epsilon}\lesssim\log(t)
by Proposition
5.45
and
\log^{2p+1}(t)\lesssim t^{1/4}
,
\mathbf{A}_{1}
is controlled by terms of the form (
T-3
)–(
T-4
).

•

Otherwise
\deg R+\kappa_{H}=\beta_{H}
and
m=0
, so that
\gamma_{T}\geq 1
and
p=0
. We then have

\mathbb{F}\big[\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\gamma}f_{\infty}\big]=0,\qquad t^{-1}|\Delta_{Z^{\gamma}}^{i}|(t,z,v)\lesssim\sup_{1\leq k\leq 3}\,\frac{t}{v^{0}}\big|\widehat{v}^{\mu}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}(F)_{\mu k}(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})\big|,\qquad\gamma_{T}\geq 1,

and
\mathbf{A}_{1}
is bounded by terms of type (
T-2
).

One can check in the commutation formula of Corollary
7.8
that the error terms containing a high order derivative of
F
carry a low order derivative of
h
. Consequently, we can expect to close the energy estimates by bounding first a well-chosen weighted
L^{2}
norm of the lower order derivatives of
h
and by controlling then a weaker norm of the higher order derivatives
\widehat{Z}^{\beta}_{\infty}h
,
|\beta|\geq N-2
.

More concretely, in the terms (
T-1
)–(
T-3
), when
|\gamma|\geq N-2
the distribution function is allowed to absorb more powers of
\langle z\rangle
and
v^{0}
. Since we have a weaker control of
\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F
at the top order
|\gamma|=N
, we unify the treatment of (
T-2
)–(
T-3
) in the following way.

Remark 7.9
.

If
|\gamma|\geq N-2
, the terms (
T-2
)–(
T-3
) are bounded by a sum of quantities of the form

\log^{2N+4}(t)\frac{\langle t+|\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}|\rangle\,\langle z\rangle\,\langle v\rangle}{t}\langle t-|\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}|\rangle\big|\nabla_{t,x}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\xi}}F\big|(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})\left|\langle v\rangle^{M_{v}}\,\langle z\rangle^{M_{z}-\kappa_{H}}\,\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\kappa}h\right|,

where
|\xi|+|\kappa|\leq N
and
N-3\leq|\xi|\leq N-1
.

Proof.

Remark that if
Z^{\gamma}=ZZ^{\xi}
, then
|\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F|\lesssim\langle t+r\rangle|\nabla_{t,x}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\xi}}F|
. It then remains us to observe

\frac{\langle t+|z|\rangle}{v^{0}}+\frac{\langle t+|z|\rangle\,\langle t+|\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}|\rangle}{tv^{0}}\lesssim\frac{\langle t+|\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}|\rangle^{2}}{tv^{0}}\lesssim\frac{\langle t+|\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}|\rangle}{t}\langle t-|\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}|\rangle\,\langle z\rangle\,\langle v\rangle\log^{2}(t).

The first inequality ensues from
|z|\lesssim|\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}|+t+|\mathscr{C}_{t,v}|\lesssim t+|\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}|
, where we used
|\mathscr{C}_{t,v}|\lesssim\epsilon\log(t)\lesssim\log^{2}(t)
. For the second one, it suffices to apply Lemma
2.9
for
x=\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}=z+t\widehat{v}+\mathscr{C}_{t,v}
.

Finally, we end this subsection by writing an other consequence of Proposition
7.6
. We will use it in order to prove a strong convergence estimate for the spatial average of
\widehat{Z}^{\beta}f
and derive the asymptotic behavior of the source term of the Maxwell equations. The idea is that we can get rid of the worst error terms (
T-1
) in the previous commutation formula by performing integration by parts in
z
.

Corollary 7.10
.

Let
h
be a sufficiently regular solution to
\mathbf{T}_{F}^{\infty}(h)=0
and
|\beta|\leq N-1
. Then,

\partial_{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}\widehat{Z}^{\beta}_{\infty}h(t,z,v)\mathrm{d}z

can be bounded by a linear combination of terms of the form

\displaystyle\;\bullet\;\,\log^{2N}(t)\frac{1}{v^{0}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}t\Big|\nabla_{t,x}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F\Big|(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})\left|\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\kappa}h\right|(t,z,v)\mathrm{d}z,

(SA1)

\displaystyle\;\bullet\;\,\log^{2N}(t)\frac{1}{v^{0}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}\Big|\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F\Big|(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})\left|\langle z\rangle^{N-\kappa_{H}}\,\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\kappa}h\right|(t,z,v)\mathrm{d}z,

(SA2)

\displaystyle\;\bullet\;\,\log^{2N+1}(t)\frac{1}{v^{0}t^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}\langle z\rangle^{N-\kappa_{H}}\,\left|\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\kappa}h\right|(t,z,v)\mathrm{d}z,

(SA3)

where
|\gamma|+|\kappa|\leq N
and
|\gamma|\leq N-1
.

Proof.

We use (
120
) in order to write

\partial_{t}\widehat{Z}^{\beta}_{\infty}h=\mathbf{T}_{F}^{\infty}\big(\widehat{Z}^{\beta}_{\infty}h\big)+\frac{\delta_{j}^{i}-\widehat{v}_{j}\widehat{v}^{i}}{v^{0}t}\Delta^{j}\partial_{z^{i}}\widehat{Z}^{\beta}_{\infty}h-\frac{\widehat{v}^{\mu}}{v^{0}}{F_{\mu}}^{j}(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})\!\left(\widehat{\Omega}^{\infty}_{0j}\!-z_{j}\partial_{t}^{\infty}\!-\widehat{v}_{j}S+\widehat{v}_{j}z^{i}\partial_{z^{i}}-v^{0}\partial_{v^{j}}\mathscr{C}^{i}_{t,v}\partial_{z^{i}}\!\right)\!\big(\widehat{Z}^{\beta}_{\infty}h\big).

As
\mathbf{T}_{F}^{\infty}(h)=0
, we can then express
\partial_{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}\widehat{Z}^{\beta}_{\infty}h(t,z,v)\mathrm{d}z
as a combination, with coefficients which are polynomials in
\widehat{v}
, of quantities of the form

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\bullet\;\,\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}R\!\left(z\right)P_{p,q}(\mathscr{C})\,t^{-1-m}\Delta^{i}_{Z^{\gamma}}(t,z,v)\,\partial_{z^{\nu}}\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\xi}h(t,z,v)\mathrm{d}z,\qquad

\displaystyle\nu\in\llbracket 0,3\rrbracket,

(122)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\bullet\;\,\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}R\!\left(z\right)P_{p,q}(\mathscr{C})\,t^{-m}\,\frac{\widehat{v}^{\mu}}{v^{0}}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}(F)_{\mu i}(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})\,\widehat{Z}_{\infty}\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\xi}h(t,z,v)\mathrm{d}z,\qquad\qquad

\displaystyle\widehat{Z}_{\infty}\in\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{S}^{\infty},

(123)

where
i\in\llbracket 1,3\rrbracket
,
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
R
is a polynomial in
z=(z^{j})_{1\leq j\leq 3}
of degree
\deg R
,

\deg R+\xi_{H}\leq\beta_{H},\qquad\qquad\deg R\leq m,\qquad\qquad|\gamma|+|\xi|\leq|\beta|,\qquad\qquad p,\,q\leq|\beta|+1.

Note then that
\deg R\leq N-1-\xi_{H}
and recall from (
119
) that
|P_{p,q}(\mathscr{C})|\lesssim\log^{2p}(t)
. One can then control (
123
) by (
SA2
), where
\kappa
verifies
|\kappa|=|\xi|+1
and
\xi_{H}\leq\kappa_{H}+1
. For (
122
), if
\nu=0
, we write

\partial_{z^{0}}=\partial_{t}^{\infty}=\partial_{t}-\widehat{v}\cdot\nabla_{z}=t^{-1}S-t^{-1}z\cdot\nabla_{z}-\widehat{v}\cdot\nabla_{z}.

Hence, we are lead to bound

\displaystyle\hskip-25.60747pt\log^{2p}(t)\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}t^{-1}\Delta^{i}_{Z^{\gamma}}(t,z,v)\,\partial_{z^{j}}\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\xi}h(t,z,v)\mathrm{d}z\bigg|,\qquad

\displaystyle j\in\llbracket 1,3\rrbracket,

(124)

\displaystyle\hskip-25.60747pt\log^{2p}(t)\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}R\!\left(z\right)t^{-2}\Delta^{i}_{Z^{\gamma}}(t,z,v)\,\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\kappa}h(t,z,v)\mathrm{d}z\bigg|,\qquad

\displaystyle\deg R+\kappa_{H}\leq\beta_{H}+1,\quad|\kappa|\leq|\beta|+1-|\gamma|.

(125)

By performing an integration by parts, we get

\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}t^{-1}\Delta^{i}_{Z^{\gamma}}(t,z,v)\,\partial_{z^{j}}\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\xi}(t,z,v)\mathrm{d}z\bigg|\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}t\big|\nabla_{\partial_{x^{j}}}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F\big|(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})\,\big|\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\xi}h\big|(t,z,v)\mathrm{d}z.

Next, in order to control (
125
) by quantities of the form (
SA2
)–(
SA3
), we simply write

\big|R\!\left(z\right)t^{-2}\Delta^{i}_{Z^{\gamma}}(t,z,v)\,\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\kappa}h(t,z,v)\big|\lesssim\Big(\big|\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F\big|(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})+t^{-2}\big|\mathbb{F}\big[\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}_{\infty}f_{\infty}\big]\big|(v)\Big)\langle z\rangle^{N-\kappa_{H}}\big|\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\kappa}h(t,z,v)\big|

and we use
\big|\mathbb{F}\big[\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}_{\infty}f_{\infty}\big]\big|(v)\lesssim\sqrt{\epsilon}\lesssim\log(t)
.

It allows to derive the following simpler estimate. For this, recall from Definition
6.2
the energy
\mathbf{E}^{7,14}_{N}[\cdot]
.

Corollary 7.11
.

Consider
h
a sufficiently regular solution to
\mathbf{T}_{F}^{\infty}(h)=0
. For any
|\beta|\leq N-1
and almost all
(t,v)\in[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
,

\displaystyle\bigg|\partial_{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}\widehat{Z}^{\beta}_{\infty}h(t,z,v)\mathrm{d}z\bigg|\lesssim\sum_{|\kappa|\leq N}

\displaystyle\,\frac{\sqrt{\Lambda}\log^{2N+4}(t)}{t^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}\langle v\rangle^{3}\langle z\rangle^{N+2-\kappa_{H}}\,\left|\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\kappa}g\right|(t,z,v)\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle+\log^{2N+2}(t)\big|\mathbf{E}^{7,13}_{N}[h]\big|^{\frac{1}{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}\frac{\langle t-|\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}|\rangle}{\langle z\rangle^{4}\,\langle v\rangle^{12}}\left|\nabla_{t,x}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\kappa}}F\right|(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})\mathrm{d}z.

Proof.

This result is a consequence of Corollary
7.10
,
\Lambda\geq 1
, and the following properties.

•

Lemma
6.6
and the pointwise decay estimates (
111
), together with Lemma
3.9
, give, for any
|\gamma|\leq N-4
,

\displaystyle t\big|\nabla_{t,x}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F\big|(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\Lambda}\langle t-|\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}|\rangle^{-2}\lesssim\sqrt{\Lambda}\log^{4}(t)t^{-2}\langle z\rangle^{2}\,\langle v\rangle^{4},

\displaystyle\big|\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F\big|(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\Lambda}\,t^{-1}\,\langle t-|\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}|\rangle^{-1}\lesssim\sqrt{\Lambda}\log^{2}(t)t^{-2}\langle z\rangle\,\langle v\rangle^{2}.

•

If
N-3\leq\gamma\leq N-1
, we write
|\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F|\lesssim\langle t+r\rangle|\nabla_{t,x}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\kappa}}F|
, where
\kappa
is such that
Z^{\gamma}=ZZ^{\kappa}
. Moreover, by Lemma
6.6
,

\langle t+|\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}|\rangle\big|\nabla_{t,x}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F\big|(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})\lesssim\log^{2}(t)\,\langle z\rangle\,\langle v\rangle^{2}\,\langle t-|\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}|\rangle\big|\nabla_{t,x}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F\big|(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}).

•

Finally, according to the Sobolev embedding
H^{2}_{z,v}\hookrightarrow L^{\infty}_{z,v}
, we have, for all
|\kappa|\leq 3\leq N-5
,

\langle z\rangle^{5+N-\kappa_{H}}\langle v\rangle^{13}\big|\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\kappa}h\big|(t,z,v)\lesssim\big|\mathbf{E}^{5,13}_{N-3}[h]\big|^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\big|\mathbf{E}^{7,13}_{N}[h]\big|^{\frac{1}{2}}.

7.3 Estimates of the error terms

We now perform estimates which will allow us to control the terms (
T-1
)–(
T-3
). We start by considering the cases where the electromagnetic field is differentiated at most
N-3
times, so that it can be estimated pointwise and we have access to improved estimates on its good null components.

Proposition 7.12
.

Let
i\in\llbracket 1,3\rrbracket
,
|\gamma|\leq N-3
. Then, for all
(t,z,v)\in[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
,

\displaystyle\frac{\log^{2N+2}(t)}{v^{0}\langle z\rangle\,t}\Big|t^{2}\widehat{v}^{\mu}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}(F)_{\mu i}(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})-\widehat{v}^{\mu}\mathbb{F}_{\mu i}\big[\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}_{\infty}f_{\infty}\big](v)\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{\Lambda}}{t^{\frac{4}{3}}}+\frac{\sqrt{\Lambda}\,\widehat{v}^{\underline{L}}(\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})}{\langle t-|\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}|\rangle^{\frac{4}{3}}},

\displaystyle\log^{2N+2}(t)\frac{\langle t+|z|\rangle}{v^{0}\,t}\big|\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}(F)(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{\Lambda}}{t^{\frac{7}{4}}}+\frac{\sqrt{\Lambda}\,\widehat{v}^{\underline{L}}(\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})}{\langle t-|\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}|\rangle^{2}}.

If
0\leq\nu\leq 3
and
|\xi|\leq N-4
, there holds

\frac{\langle t+|z|\rangle}{v^{0}}\Big|\widehat{v}^{\mu}\mathcal{L}_{\partial_{x^{\nu}}Z^{\xi}}(F)_{\mu i}\Big|(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{\Lambda}}{t^{2}}+\frac{\sqrt{\Lambda}\,\widehat{v}^{\underline{L}}(\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})}{\langle t-|\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}|\rangle^{2}}.

Proof.

For the first estimate, start by using the convergence estimate (
113
) for
x=z+\mathscr{C}_{t,v}
. Since
\langle z+\mathscr{C}_{t,v}\rangle\lesssim\log^{2}(t)\,\langle z\rangle
, we get
