\displaystyle\log^{2N+2}(t)\frac{1}{v^{0}\langle z\rangle\,t}\Big|t^{2}\widehat{v}^{\mu}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}(F)_{\mu i}(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})-\widehat{v}^{\mu}\mathbb{F}_{\mu i}\big[\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}_{\infty}f_{\infty}\big](v)\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\Lambda}\log^{2N+4}(t)\bigg(\,\frac{|v^{\underline{L}}(\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})|^{\frac{1}{2}}}{t\,\langle t-|\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}|\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}}+\frac{1}{t^{2}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\Lambda}\big|\widehat{v}^{\underline{L}}(\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})\big|^{\frac{1}{2}}\,t^{-\frac{5}{6}}\langle t-|\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}|\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}}+\sqrt{\Lambda}\,t^{-\frac{7}{4}}.

If
|\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}|\geq 2t
, it implies the stated estimate. Otherwise
t^{-1/6}\leq\langle t-|\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}|\rangle^{-1/6}
and it then suffices to use the inequality
2ab\leq a^{2}+b^{2}
. Next, using first

t+|z|\leq 2t+|\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}|+|\mathscr{C}_{t,v}|\lesssim t+|\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}|,\qquad\qquad 1\leq 2v^{0}|\widehat{v}^{\underline{L}}|^{1/2},

which is given by Lemma
2.9
, as well as the pointwise decay estimate (
111
), we obtain

\log^{2N+2}(t)\frac{\langle t+|z|\rangle}{v^{0}\,t}\big|\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}(F)(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})\big|\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{\Lambda}\big|\widehat{v}^{\underline{L}}(\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})\big|^{\frac{1}{2}}\log^{2N+2}(t)}{t\,\langle t-|\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}|\rangle}\leq\frac{\sqrt{\Lambda}\log^{4N+4}(t)}{t^{2}}+\frac{\Lambda\,\widehat{v}^{\underline{L}}(\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})}{\langle t-|\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}|\rangle^{2}},

which implies the second inequality. Finally, for the last one, we start by expanding
\widehat{v}^{\mu}\mathcal{L}_{\partial_{x^{\nu}}Z^{\xi}}(F)_{\mu i}
according to the null frame
(\underline{L},L,e_{\theta},e_{\varphi})
. More precisely, Lemmata
2.9
and
5.56
imply

\left|\widehat{v}^{\mu}\mathcal{L}_{\partial_{x^{\nu}}Z^{\xi}}(F)_{\mu i}\right|\lesssim v^{0}\big|\widehat{v}^{\underline{L}}\big|^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(|\alpha(\mathcal{L}_{\partial_{x^{\nu}}Z^{\xi}}F)|+|\rho(\mathcal{L}_{\partial_{x^{\nu}}Z^{\xi}}F)|+|\sigma(\mathcal{L}_{\partial_{x^{\nu}}Z^{\xi}}F)|\big)+v^{0}\widehat{v}^{\underline{L}}|\underline{\alpha}(\mathcal{L}_{\partial_{x^{\nu}}Z^{\xi}}F)|.

Recall from Lemma
3.9
that we have improved decay estimates for the quantities on the right hand side since
\partial_{x^{\nu}}Z^{\xi}
contains a translation. Hence, using the pointwise decay estimates (
112
) for the null components of
\mathcal{L}_{\partial_{x^{\nu}}Z^{\gamma}}F
, we get

\frac{\langle t+|z|\rangle}{v^{0}}\Big|\widehat{v}^{\mu}\mathcal{L}_{\partial_{x^{\nu}}Z^{\xi}}(F)_{\mu i}(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})\Big|\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{\Lambda}\big|v^{\underline{L}}(\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})\big|^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\langle t+|\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}|\rangle\,\langle t-|\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}|\rangle}+\frac{\sqrt{\Lambda}v^{\underline{L}}(\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})}{\langle t-|\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}|\rangle^{2}}.

It remains to use again the inequality
2ab\leq a^{2}+b^{2}
.

We now prove estimates which will allow us to deal with the error terms where the electromagnetic field is differentiated too many times in order to be controlled pointwise. For simplicity, even if the estimates that we have at our disposal on
F
are worse at the top order, we will unify the analysis of the cases
N-2\leq|\gamma|\leq N
. In fact, it will be convenient to use the coordinate system
(t,x,v)=(t,z-t\widehat{v}-\mathscr{C}_{t,v},v)
in the energy estimates.

Proposition 7.13
.

Let
1\leq i\leq 3
,
N-2\leq|\gamma|\leq N
and
N-3\leq\xi\leq N-1
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\frac{1}{t^{2}}\Big|t^{2}\widehat{v}^{\mu}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}(F)_{\mu i}(t,x)-\widehat{v}^{\mu}\mathbb{F}_{\mu i}\big[\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}f_{\infty}\big](v)\Big|^{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x}{\langle x-t\widehat{v}-\mathscr{C}_{t,v}\rangle^{7}\,\langle v\rangle^{13}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\Lambda\log^{9}(t)}{t^{3}},

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\frac{\langle t+|x|\rangle^{2}\,}{t^{2}}\langle t-|x|\rangle^{2}\big|\nabla_{t,x}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\xi}}F\big|^{2}(t,x)\frac{\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x}{\langle x-t\widehat{v}-\mathscr{C}_{t,v}\rangle^{4}\,\langle v\rangle^{7}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\Lambda\log^{5}(t)}{t^{4}}.

Proof.

The first estimate is a direct consequence of (
115
). For the second one, using first first Lemma
2.9
and then
|\mathscr{C}_{t,v}|\lesssim\log^{2}(t)
as well as Lemma
6.4
, we have

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\frac{\mathrm{d}v}{\langle x-t\widehat{v}-\mathscr{C}_{t_{v}}\rangle^{4}\,\langle v\rangle^{7}}\lesssim\frac{\max(t-|x|,1)}{\langle t+|x|\rangle}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\frac{\langle x-t\widehat{v}\rangle\,\mathrm{d}v}{\langle x-t\widehat{v}-\mathscr{C}_{t_{v}}\rangle^{4}\,\langle v\rangle^{5}}\lesssim\frac{\max(t-|x|,1)}{\langle t+|x|\rangle^{4}}\log^{3}(t).

It remains to use the
L^{2}_{x}
estimate (
114
).

7.4 Well-posedness for the Vlasov equation with asymptotic data

The purpose of this subsection is to solve the asymptotic Cauchy problem

\begin{cases}\,\mathbf{T}_{F}^{\infty}(h)(t,z,v)=0,\qquad&(t,z,v)\in[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v},\\
\underset{t\to+\infty}{\lim}\,h(t,z,v)=h_{\infty}(z,v),\quad&(z,v)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v},\end{cases}

(126)

for a sufficiently regular asymptotic data
h_{\infty}:\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\to\mathbb{R}
. For this, in order to perform energy estimates and motivated by the commutation formula of Corollary
7.8
, we introduce (the square of) hierarchised weighted energy norms. We define

\overline{h}(t,x,v):=h(t,x-t\widehat{v}-\mathscr{C}_{t,v},v),\qquad\qquad\mathbf{T}_{F}\big(\,\overline{h}\,\big)=\overline{\mathbf{T}_{F}^{\infty}(h)}=0,

which simply corresponds to the change of coordinates
(t,z,v)=(t,x+t\widehat{v}+\mathscr{C}_{t,v},v)
, so that
\overline{\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}}(t,x,v)=x
.

Definition 7.14
.

Let
M\in\mathbb{N}
,
(N_{z},N_{v})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
as well as
h:[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\to\mathbb{R}
and
h_{\infty}:\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\to\mathbb{R}
be two sufficiently regular functions. Then, we consider

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{M}^{N_{z},N_{v}}[h](t,u):=\sup_{|\kappa|\leq M}\,\overline{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\;\overline{\langle z\rangle^{N_{z}+M-\kappa_{H}}\,\langle v\rangle^{N_{v}}\big|\widehat{Z}^{\kappa}_{\infty}h\big|}\;\Big](t,u),\qquad t\geq T,\,u\in\mathbb{R},

where
\overline{\mathbb{E}}[\cdot]
is introduced in (
46
), as well as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{M}^{N_{v},N_{z}}[h_{\infty}]:=\sup_{|\beta|\leq M}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\langle z\rangle^{2N_{z}+2M-2\beta_{H}}\,\langle v\rangle^{2N_{v}}\big|\widehat{Z}^{\beta}_{\infty}h_{\infty}\big|^{2}(z,v)\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}z,

which is equivalent to
\sup_{|\kappa|\leq M}\,\|\langle z\rangle^{N_{z}+N-|\kappa_{v}|}\,\langle v\rangle^{N_{v}+|\kappa_{v}|}\partial_{v}^{\kappa_{v}}\partial_{z}^{\kappa_{z}}h_{\infty}\|_{L^{2}_{z,v}}^{2}
.

We are now able to prove the following existence and uniqueness result for (
126
). We could consider less regular initial data but it would not be useful for the purpose of this paper.

Proposition 7.15
.

Let
(N_{z},N_{v})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
and
h_{\infty}:\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\to\mathbb{R}
be such that
\mathbb{E}_{N}^{N_{z},N_{v}}[h_{\infty}]<+\infty
. Then, there exists a unique global and classical solution
h:[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\to\mathbb{R}
to the asymptotic Cauchy problem (
126
). Moreover, there exists a constant
B_{N}^{N_{z},N_{v}}>0
, such that

\sup_{t\geq T}\,\sup_{u\in\mathbb{R}}\,\mathbb{E}_{N-3}^{N_{z},N_{v}}[h](t,u)+\sup_{t\geq T}\,\sup_{u\in\mathbb{R}}\,\mathbb{E}^{N_{z}-3,N_{v}-6}_{N}[h](t,u)\leq\mathbb{E}_{N}^{N_{z},N_{v}}[h_{\infty}]\exp\left[\exp\left(B_{N}^{N_{z},N_{v}}\,\sqrt{\Lambda}\right)\right].

(127)

The proof of Proposition
7.15
is divided in three parts. We derive first a priori estimates for solutions to the Vlasov equation with asymptotic data. Then, we prove, by studying the characteristics of the operator
\mathbf{T}_{F}^{\infty}
, that for any
h_{\infty}\in C^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})
, there exists a global
C^{1}
solution to (
126
). Finally, by combining these results and by a standard approximation argument, we are able to conclude the proof of Proposition
7.15
.

7.4.1 A priori estimates

We start by proving a two variables version of Gronwall’s inequality.

Lemma 7.16
.

Let
\Psi\geq 0
and three continous function

\psi_{1}\in L^{1}([T,+\infty[,\mathbb{R}_{+}),\qquad\psi_{2}\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{u},\mathbb{R}_{+}),\qquad H:[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}_{u}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}.

Assume further that
H(t_{1},u)\geq H(t_{2},u)
for all
t_{1}\leq t_{2}
and
u\in\mathbb{R}
as well as

\forall\,(t,u)\in[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}_{u},\qquad H(t,u)\leq\Psi+\int_{\tau=t}^{+\infty}\psi_{1}(\tau)H(\tau,u)\mathrm{d}s+\int_{U=u}^{+\infty}\psi_{2}(U)H(t,U)\mathrm{d}U.

Then,

\forall\,(t,u)\in[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}_{u},\qquad H(t,u)\leq\Psi\exp\left(\|\psi_{1}\|_{L^{1}_{t}}+e^{\|\psi_{1}\|_{L^{1}_{t}}}\|\psi_{2}\|_{L^{1}_{u}}\right).

Proof.

Fix
u\in\mathbb{R}_{u}
. As
t\mapsto\int_{U=u}^{+\infty}\psi_{2}(U)H(t,U)\mathrm{d}u
is non-increasing, we have, by applying Gronwall’s inequality to
t\mapsto H(t,u)
,

\displaystyle\forall\,t\geq T,\qquad H(t,u)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\Psi+\int_{U=u}^{+\infty}\psi_{2}(U)H(t,U)\mathrm{d}U\right)\exp\left(\int_{\tau=t}^{+\infty}\psi_{1}(\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau\right)

\displaystyle\leq\Psi e^{\|\psi_{1}\|_{L^{1}_{t}}}+\int_{U=u}^{+\infty}e^{\|\psi_{1}\|_{L^{1}_{t}}}\psi_{2}(\tau)H(t,U)\mathrm{d}u.

Since this inequality holds for all
u\in\mathbb{R}_{u}
, we can this time fix
t\geq T
and apply Gronwall’s inequality to the function
u\mapsto H(t,u)
.

We are now able to prove the next result.

Proposition 7.17
.

Consider an asymptotic data
h_{\infty}:\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\to\mathbb{R}
and
h:[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\to\mathbb{R}
. Assume that
h
is a solution to (
126
) satisfying

\mathbb{E}_{N}^{N_{z},N_{v}}[h_{\infty}]<+\infty,\qquad\qquad\sup_{t\geq T}\,\sup_{u\in\mathbb{R}}\,\mathbb{E}_{N-3}^{N_{z},N_{v}}[h](t,u)+\sup_{t\geq T}\,\sup_{u\in\mathbb{R}}\,\mathbb{E}^{N_{z}-3,N_{v}-6}_{N}[h](t,u)<+\infty.

Then,
h
verifies the estimate (
127
).

Proof.

In order to lighten the presentation, we introduce the weighted derivatives of
h
that we have to bound in order to control the lower order energy norm
\mathbb{E}^{N_{z},N_{v}}_{N-3}[h]
,

h^{\kappa}_{\mathrm{low}}(t,z,v):=\langle v\rangle^{N_{v}}\,\langle z\rangle^{N_{z}+N-\kappa_{H}}\widehat{Z}^{\kappa}_{\infty}h(t,z,v),\qquad\overline{h^{\kappa}_{\mathrm{low}}}(t,x,v)=h^{\kappa}_{\mathrm{low}}(t,x-t\widehat{v}-\mathscr{C}_{t,v},v),\qquad|\kappa|\leq N-3.

Let
t_{0}\geq T
and
u_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
. According to the energy estimate of Proposition
3.2
, we have

\mathbb{E}_{N-3}^{N_{z},N_{v}}[h](t_{0},u_{0})\leq\mathbb{E}_{N}^{N_{z},N_{v}}[h_{\infty}]+\sup_{|\beta|\leq N-3}\,\int_{t=t_{0}}^{+\infty}\int_{|x|\leq t-u_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\bigg|\,\overline{\mathbf{T}_{F}^{\infty}\big(|h^{\beta}_{\mathrm{low}}|^{2}\big)^{\mathstrut}}\,\bigg|(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t.

Since
\mathbf{T}_{F}^{\infty}(h)=0
and
|\beta|\leq N-3
, the integrands on the right hand side of the previous inequality can be estimated by applying the commutation formula of Corollary
7.8
and Proposition
7.12
. It yields, since
\overline{\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}}(t,x,v)=x
, to

\mathbb{E}_{N-3}^{N_{z},N_{v}}[h](t_{0},u_{0})-\mathbb{E}_{N}^{N_{z},N_{v}}[h_{\infty}]\lesssim\sup_{|\beta|,\,|\kappa|\leq N-3}\,\int_{t=t_{0}}^{+\infty}\!\int_{|x|\leq t-u_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\bigg(\frac{\sqrt{\Lambda}}{t^{\frac{4}{3}}}+\frac{\sqrt{\Lambda}\widehat{v}^{\underline{L}}(x)}{\langle t-|x|\rangle^{\frac{4}{3}}}\bigg)\Big|\overline{h^{\kappa}_{\mathrm{low}}\,h^{\beta}_{\mathrm{low}}}\Big|(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t.

Since
2h^{\kappa}_{\mathrm{low}}\,h^{\beta}_{\mathrm{low}}\leq|h^{\kappa}_{\mathrm{low}}|^{2}+|h^{\beta}_{\mathrm{low}}|^{2}
, we finally get

\mathbb{E}_{N-3}^{N_{z},N_{v}}[h](t_{0},u_{0})-\mathbb{E}_{N}^{N_{z},N_{v}}[h_{\infty}]\lesssim\sup_{|\kappa|\leq N-3}\mathcal{T}_{t_{0},u_{0}}\big[|h^{\kappa}_{\mathrm{low}}|^{2}\big]+\mathcal{U}_{t_{0},u_{0}}\big[|h^{\kappa}_{\mathrm{low}}|^{2}\big],

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{t_{0},u_{0}}\big[|h^{\kappa}_{\mathrm{low}}|^{2}\big]

\displaystyle:=\int_{t=t_{0}}^{+\infty}\!\int_{|x|\leq t-u_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\frac{\sqrt{\Lambda}}{t^{\frac{4}{3}}}\Big|\overline{h^{\kappa}_{\mathrm{low}}}\Big|^{2}(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\leq\int_{t=t_{0}}^{+\infty}\frac{\sqrt{\Lambda}}{t^{\frac{4}{3}}}\,\mathbb{E}_{N-3}^{N_{z},N_{v}}[h](t,u_{0})\mathrm{d}t,

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}_{t_{0},u_{0}}\big[|h^{\kappa}_{\mathrm{low}}|^{2}\big]

\displaystyle:=\int_{t=t_{0}}^{+\infty}\!\int_{|x|\leq t-u_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\frac{\sqrt{\Lambda}\widehat{v}^{\underline{L}}(x)}{\langle t-|x|\rangle^{\frac{4}{3}}}\Big|\overline{h^{\kappa}_{\mathrm{low}}}\Big|^{2}(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t.

For
\mathcal{U}_{t_{0},u_{0}}[|h^{\kappa}_{\mathrm{low}}|^{2}]
, we perform the change of variables
(u,\underline{u},\omega)=(t-|x|,t+|x|,x/|x|)
, so that

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}_{t_{0},u_{0}}\big[|h^{\kappa}_{\mathrm{low}}|^{2}\big]

\displaystyle=\int_{u\geq u_{0}}\int_{\underline{u}\geq\max(u,2t_{0}-u)}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\frac{\sqrt{\Lambda}\widehat{v}^{\underline{L}}(\omega)}{\langle u\rangle^{\frac{4}{3}}}\Big|\overline{h^{\kappa}_{\mathrm{low}}}\Big|^{2}\Big(\frac{\underline{u}+u}{2},\frac{\underline{u}-u}{2}\omega\Big)\mathrm{d}v\frac{r^{2}}{2}\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\mathrm{d}\underline{u}\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle=\int_{u\geq u_{0}}\frac{\sqrt{\Lambda}}{\sqrt{2}\langle u\rangle^{\frac{4}{3}}}\int_{C_{u}^{t_{0}}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\widehat{v}^{\underline{L}}\Big|\overline{h^{\kappa}_{\mathrm{low}}}\Big|^{2}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}\mu_{C_{u}}\mathrm{d}u\leq\int_{u\geq u_{0}}\frac{\sqrt{\Lambda}}{\sqrt{2}\langle u\rangle^{\frac{4}{3}}}\,\mathbb{E}_{N-3}^{N_{z},N_{v}}[h](t_{0},u)\mathrm{d}u.

We then deduce that, for all
(t_{0},u_{0})\in[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}_{u}
,

\mathbb{E}_{N-3}^{N_{z},N_{v}}[h](t_{0},u_{0})-\mathbb{E}_{N}^{N_{z},N_{v}}[h_{\infty}]\lesssim\int_{t=t_{0}}^{+\infty}\frac{\sqrt{\Lambda}}{t^{\frac{4}{3}}}\,\mathbb{E}_{N-3}^{N_{z},N_{v}}[h](t,u_{0})\mathrm{d}t+\int_{u\geq u_{0}}\frac{\sqrt{\Lambda}}{\langle u\rangle^{\frac{4}{3}}}\,\mathbb{E}_{N-3}^{N_{z},N_{v}}[h](t_{0},u)\mathrm{d}u.

The estimate for
\mathbb{E}_{N-3}^{N_{z},N_{v}}[h]
then ensues from Lemma
7.16
, applied to
H(t,u)=\sup_{\tau\geq t}\mathbb{E}^{N_{z},N_{v}}_{N-3}[h](\tau,u)
. We now turn to the higher order energy norm. We then introduce the notation

h^{\kappa}_{\mathrm{high}}:=\langle v\rangle^{N_{v}-6}\,\langle z\rangle^{N_{z}-3+N-\kappa_{H}}\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\kappa}h(t,z,v),\qquad\overline{h^{\kappa}_{\mathrm{high}}}(t,x,v)=h^{\kappa}_{\mathrm{high}}(t,x-t\widehat{v}-\mathscr{C}_{t,v},v),\qquad|\kappa|\leq N.

As before, we fix
t_{0}\geq T
,
u_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
and we apply the energy inequality of Proposition
3.2
. We claim that it yields to

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{N}^{N_{z}-3,N_{v}-6}[h](t_{0},u_{0})-\mathbb{E}_{N}^{N_{z},N_{v}}[h_{\infty}]\lesssim\!\sum_{|\beta|\leq N}

\displaystyle\sum_{|\kappa|\leq N}\mathcal{T}_{t_{0},u_{0}}\big[h^{\kappa}_{\mathrm{high}}\,h^{\beta}_{\mathrm{high}}\big]+\mathcal{U}_{t_{0},u_{0}}\big[h^{\kappa}_{\mathrm{high}}\,h^{\beta}_{\mathrm{high}}\big]

\displaystyle\!+\!\sum_{N-2\leq|\gamma|\leq N}\,\sum_{|\kappa|\leq N+1-|\gamma|}\mathcal{I}^{\,\gamma,\kappa,\beta}_{t_{0},u_{0}}+\sum_{N-3\leq|\xi|\leq N-1}\,\sum_{|\kappa|\leq N-|\xi|}\mathcal{J}^{\,\xi,\kappa,\beta}_{t_{0},u_{0}},

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}^{\,\gamma,\kappa,\beta}_{t_{0},u_{0}}

\displaystyle:=\int_{t=t_{0}}^{+\infty}\!\int_{|x|\leq t-u_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\Big|t^{2}\widehat{v}^{\mu}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}(F)_{\mu j}(t,x)-\widehat{v}^{\mu}\mathbb{F}_{\mu j}\big[\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}_{\infty}f_{\infty}\big](v)\Big|\,\Big|\overline{h^{\kappa}_{\mathrm{high}}\,h^{\beta}_{\mathrm{high}}}\Big|(t,x,v)\frac{\log^{2N+2}(t)\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t}{\langle v\rangle\,\langle x-t\widehat{v}-\mathscr{C}_{t,v}\rangle\,t},

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}^{\,\xi,\kappa,\beta}_{t_{0},u_{0}}

\displaystyle:=\int_{t=t_{0}}^{+\infty}\!\int_{|x|\leq t-u_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\frac{\log^{2N+4}(t)\,\langle t+|x|\rangle}{t}\langle t-|x|\rangle\Big|\nabla_{t,x}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\xi}}(F)\Big|(t,x)\Big|\overline{\langle z\rangle\,\langle v\rangle\,h^{\kappa}_{\mathrm{high}}\,h^{\beta}_{\mathrm{high}}}\Big|(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t.

Indeed, we bound
\mathbf{T}_{F}^{\infty}(|h^{\beta}_{\mathrm{high}}|^{2})
by applying the commutation formula of Corollary
7.8
, for any
|\beta|\leq N
.

•

The error terms of type (
T-1
)–(
T-3
) give rise, when
N-2\leq|\gamma|\leq N
and according to Remark
7.9
, to
\mathcal{I}^{\,\gamma,\kappa,\beta}_{t_{0},u_{0}}
and
\mathcal{J}^{\,\xi,\kappa,\beta}_{t_{0},u_{0}}
.

•

Otherwise
|\gamma|\leq N-3
and they can be controlled by
\mathcal{T}_{t_{0},u_{0}}[h^{\kappa}_{\mathrm{high}}\,h^{\beta}_{\mathrm{high}}]+\mathcal{U}_{t_{0},u_{0}}[h^{\kappa}_{\mathrm{high}}\,h^{\beta}_{\mathrm{high}}]
according to Proposition
7.12
.

•

Finally the source terms (
T-4
) are bounded by
\mathcal{T}_{t_{0},u_{0}}[h^{\kappa}_{\mathrm{high}}\,h^{\beta}_{\mathrm{high}}]
.

Arguing as previously, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{|\beta|,|\kappa|\leq N}\mathcal{T}_{t_{0},u_{0}}\big[h^{\kappa}_{\mathrm{high}}\,h^{\beta}_{\mathrm{high}}\big]

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{|\kappa|\leq N}\mathcal{T}_{t_{0},u_{0}}\big[|h^{\kappa}_{\mathrm{high}}|^{2}\big]\lesssim\int_{t=t_{0}}^{+\infty}\frac{\sqrt{\Lambda}}{t^{\frac{4}{3}}}\mathbb{E}^{N_{z}-5,N_{v}-11}_{N}[h](t,u_{0})\mathrm{d}t,

\displaystyle\sum_{|\beta|,|\kappa|\leq N}\mathcal{U}_{t_{0},u_{0}}\big[h^{\kappa}_{\mathrm{high}}\,h^{\beta}_{\mathrm{high}}\big]

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{|\kappa|\leq N}\mathcal{U}_{t_{0},u_{0}}\big[|h^{\kappa}_{\mathrm{high}}|^{2}\big]\lesssim\int_{u\geq u_{0}}\frac{\sqrt{\Lambda}}{2\langle u\rangle^{\frac{4}{3}}}\,\mathbb{E}^{N_{z}-5,N_{v}-11}_{N}[h](t,u_{0})\mathrm{d}u.

Fix now
|\beta|\leq N
,
|\kappa|\leq 3
,
N-2\leq|\gamma|\leq N
and
N-3\leq|\xi|\leq N-1
. The Cauchy-Schwarz inequality in
(x,v)
gives

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}^{\,\gamma,\kappa,\beta}_{t_{0},u_{0}}\leq\int_{t=t_{0}}^{+\infty}\bigg(\,

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\Big|t^{2}\widehat{v}^{\mu}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}(F)_{\mu j}(t,x)-\widehat{v}^{\mu}\mathbb{F}_{\mu j}\big[\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\gamma}f_{\infty}\big](v)\Big|^{2}\frac{\log^{4N+4}(t)\mathrm{d}v\,\mathrm{d}x}{t^{2}\langle x-t\widehat{v}-\mathscr{C}_{t,v}\rangle^{7}\,\langle v\rangle^{13}}\bigg|^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\times\bigg|\int_{|x|\leq t-u_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\Big|\overline{\langle z\rangle^{\frac{5}{2}}\,\langle v\rangle^{\frac{11}{2}}h^{\kappa}_{\mathrm{high}}\,h^{\beta}_{\mathrm{high}}}\Big|^{2}(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}v\bigg|^{\frac{1}{2}}\,\bigg)\mathrm{d}t

as well as

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}^{\,\xi,\kappa,\beta}_{t_{0},u_{0}}\leq\int_{t=t_{0}}^{+\infty}\bigg(

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\frac{\langle t+|x|\rangle^{2}}{t^{2}}\langle t-|x|\rangle^{2}\Big|\nabla_{t,x}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\xi}}(F)\Big|^{2}\!(t,x)\,\frac{\log^{4N+8}(t)\mathrm{d}v\,\mathrm{d}x}{\langle x-t\widehat{v}-\mathscr{C}_{t,v}\rangle^{4}\,\langle v\rangle^{7}}\bigg|^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\times\bigg|\int_{|x|\leq t-u_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\Big|\overline{\langle z\rangle^{3}\,\langle v\rangle^{\frac{9}{2}}h^{\kappa}_{\mathrm{high}}\,h^{\beta}_{\mathrm{high}}}\Big|^{2}(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}v\bigg|^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg)\mathrm{d}t.

Applying Proposition
7.13
, we obtain,

\mathcal{I}^{\,\gamma,\kappa,\beta}_{t_{0},u_{0}}+\mathcal{J}^{\,\gamma,\kappa,\beta}_{t_{0},u_{0}}\lesssim\int_{t=t_{0}}^{+\infty}\frac{\sqrt{\Lambda}\log^{2N+7}(t)}{t^{\frac{3}{2}}}\bigg|\int_{|x|\leq t-u_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\Big|\overline{\langle z\rangle^{3}\,\langle v\rangle^{6}h^{\kappa}_{\mathrm{high}}\,h^{\beta}_{\mathrm{high}}}\Big|^{2}(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}v\bigg|^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathrm{d}t.

Note, as
|\kappa|\leq 3\leq N-3
, that
\langle v\rangle^{6}\,\langle z\rangle^{3}h^{\kappa}_{\mathrm{high}}=h^{\kappa}_{\mathrm{low}}
. Since we have further
|\kappa|\leq 3\leq N-5
, we can in fact use that
H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})
is continously embedded in
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})
in order to get

\displaystyle\int_{|x|\leq t-u_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\Big|\overline{\langle z\rangle^{3}\,\langle v\rangle^{6}h^{\kappa}_{\mathrm{high}}\,h^{\beta}_{\mathrm{high}}}\Big|^{2}(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{u\in\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{E}^{N_{z},N_{v}}_{N-3}[h](t,u)\int_{|x|\leq t-u_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\Big|\overline{h^{\beta}_{\mathrm{high}}}\Big|^{2}(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{u\in\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{E}^{N_{z},N_{v}}_{N-3}[h](t,u)\,\mathbb{E}^{N_{z}-3,N_{v}-6}_{N}[h](t,u_{0}).

By combining the previous estimates and using
2ab\leq a^{2}+b^{2}
as well as
\log^{2N+7}(t)\lesssim t^{1/4}
, we finally get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{N}^{N_{z}-3,N_{v}-6}[h](t_{0},u_{0})-\mathbb{E}_{N}^{N_{z},N_{v}}[

\displaystyle h_{\infty}]\lesssim\int_{t=t_{0}}^{+\infty}\frac{\sqrt{\Lambda}}{t^{\frac{5}{4}}}\,\sup_{\tau\geq T}\,\sup_{u\in\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{E}_{N-3}^{N_{z},N_{v}}[h](\tau,u)\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle+\int_{t=t_{0}}^{+\infty}\frac{\sqrt{\Lambda}}{t^{\frac{5}{4}}}\,\mathbb{E}_{N}^{N_{z}-3,N_{v}-6}[h](t,u_{0})\mathrm{d}t+\int_{u\geq u_{0}}\frac{\sqrt{\Lambda}}{\langle u\rangle^{\frac{4}{3}}}\,\mathbb{E}_{N}^{N_{z}-3,N_{v}-6}[h](t_{0},u)\mathrm{d}u.

According to the first part of the proof, there exists
\widetilde{D}=\widetilde{D}^{N_{z},N_{v}}_{N}>0
such that

\int_{t=t_{0}}^{+\infty}\frac{\Lambda}{t^{\frac{5}{4}}}\,\sup_{\tau\geq T}\,\sup_{u\in\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{E}_{N-3}^{N_{z},N_{v}}[h](\tau,u)\mathrm{d}t\leq 4\Lambda\exp\left(e^{\widetilde{D}\Lambda}\right)\mathbb{E}^{N_{z},N_{v}}_{N}[h_{\infty}]\leq 4\exp\left(e^{\Lambda+\widetilde{D}\Lambda}\right)\,\mathbb{E}^{N_{z},N_{v}}_{N}[h_{\infty}].

In order to conclude the proof, it remains to apply Lemma
7.16
to
H(t,u)=\sup_{\tau\geq t}\mathbb{E}_{N}^{N_{z}-3,N_{v}-6}[h](\tau,u)
, with
\Psi=\big(1+C\exp\big(e^{\Lambda+\widetilde{D}\Lambda}\big)\big)\mathbb{E}^{N_{z},N_{v}}_{N}[h_{\infty}]
, where
C>0
depends only on
(N,N_{z},N_{v})
.

7.4.2 Study of the characteristics of
\mathbf{T}_{F}^{\infty}

The purpose of this section is to prove that the characteristics of the operator
\mathbf{T}^{\infty}_{F}
are defined globally and converge as time approaches infinity. For this, it is convenient to introduce

a^{i}_{z}:(t,z,v)\mapsto-\frac{\delta_{j}^{i}-\widehat{v}_{j}\widehat{v}^{i}}{tv^{0}}\widehat{v}^{\mu}\left(t^{2}{F_{\mu}}^{j}(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})-{\mathbb{F}_{\mu}}^{j}[f_{\infty}](v)\right)-\frac{\widehat{v}^{\mu}}{v^{0}}{F_{\mu}}^{j}(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})v^{0}\partial_{v^{j}}\mathscr{C}^{j}_{t,v}=\mathbf{T}_{F}^{\infty}(z^{i}),

so that
\mathbf{T}_{F}^{\infty}=\partial_{t}+a^{j}_{z}(t,z,v)\partial_{z^{j}}+\widehat{v}^{\mu}{F_{\mu}}^{j}\big(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}(t,z,v)\big)\partial_{v^{j}}
.

Definition 7.18
.

For any
(t_{0},z,v)\in[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
, we denote by
t\mapsto\big(\mathbf{Z}(t,t_{0},z,v),\mathbf{V}(t,t_{0},z,v)\big)
the unique solution to the Cauchy problem

\dot{\mathbf{Z}}^{i}=a_{z}^{i}(t,\mathbf{Z},\mathbf{V}),\qquad\dot{\mathbf{V}}^{i}=\widehat{\mathbf{V}}^{\mu}{F_{\mu}}^{i}\big(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}(t,\mathbf{Z},\mathbf{V})\big),\qquad\big(\mathbf{Z}(t_{0},t_{0},z,v),\mathbf{V}(t_{0},t_{0},z,v)\big)=(z,v).

(128)

A characteristic of the operator
\mathbf{T}_{F}^{\infty}
will refer to any such function.

Remark 7.19
.

Let
(t_{0},z,v)\in[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
,
\mathbf{Z}_{t}:=\mathbf{Z}(t,t_{0},z,v)
and
\mathbf{V}_{t}:=\mathbf{V}(t,t_{0},z,v)
. The characteristic, in the coordinate system
(t,x,v)
, of the Vlasov operator
\mathbf{T}_{F}
which is equal to
(z+t_{0}\widehat{v}+\mathscr{C}_{t_{0},v},v)
at time
t_{0}
is

t\mapsto(\mathbf{X}_{t},\mathbf{V}_{t}),\qquad\qquad\mathbf{X}_{t}:=\mathbf{Z}_{t}+t\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{t}+\mathscr{C}_{t,\mathbf{V}_{t}}=\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}\big(t,\mathbf{Z}_{t},\mathbf{V}_{t}\big).

In particular,
\dot{\mathbf{X}}_{t}=\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{t}
.

Our goal then consists in proving that
(t_{0},z,v)\mapsto(\mathbf{Z},\mathbf{V})(t^{\prime},t_{0},z,v)
converges locally in
L^{\infty}_{t_{0},z,v}
as
t^{\prime}\to+\infty
and that the limit belongs to
C^{1}([T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})
. Our analysis will rely on the following result.

Lemma 7.20
.

Let
(t_{0},z,v)\in[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
,
\mathbf{Z}_{t}:=\mathbf{Z}(t,t_{0},z,v)
as well as
\mathbf{V}_{t}:=\mathbf{V}(t,t_{0},z,v)
. Then,

\mathcal{I}(t_{0},z,v):=\int_{t=T}^{+\infty}\frac{\mathbf{V}^{\underline{L}}_{t}\big(\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}(t,\mathbf{Z}_{t},\mathbf{V}_{t})\big)}{\langle t-|\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}(t,\mathbf{Z}_{t},\mathbf{V}_{t})|\rangle^{\frac{4}{3}}}\leq\int_{u=-\infty}^{+\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}u}{2\langle u\rangle^{\frac{4}{3}}}\leq 5.

Proof.

In order to bound the first integral by the second one, we perform the change of variables
u=t-|\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}(t,\mathbf{Z}_{t},\mathbf{V}_{t})|
. In other words, we parameterise the characteristic by
u
instead of
t
. Note that in the coordinate system
(u,x,v)
, the linear Vlasov operator reads
2\widehat{v}^{\underline{L}}\partial_{u}+\widehat{v}\cdot\nabla_{x}
. In our case, in view of Remark
7.19
, we have

\partial_{t}(u)=1-\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{t}\cdot\frac{\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}(t,\mathbf{Z}_{t},\mathbf{V}_{t})}{|\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}(t,\mathbf{Z}_{t},\mathbf{V}_{t})|}=2\widehat{\mathbf{V}}^{\underline{L}}_{t}\big(\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}(t,\mathbf{Z}_{t},\mathbf{V}_{t})\big).

Then, the second integral can be easily estimated.

We are now able to prove that the characteristics converge locally in
L^{\infty}_{z,v}
as
t\to+\infty
.

Lemma 7.21
.

There exists an absolute constant
C>0
such that the following statement holds. For all
(t_{0},z,v)\in[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
, there exists
\mathbf{Z}(\infty,t_{0},z,v)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}
and
\mathbf{V}(\infty,t_{0},z,v)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
verifying

\lim_{t\to+\infty}\big(\mathbf{Z},\mathbf{V}\big)(t,t_{0},z,v)=\big(\mathbf{Z},\mathbf{V}\big)(\infty,t_{0},z,v).

Moreover, we have

\forall\,T\leq t\leq\infty,\qquad\quad\big|\mathbf{V}(t,t_{0},z,v)-v\big|\leq C\sqrt{\Lambda},\qquad\big|\mathbf{Z}(t,t_{0},z,v)-z\big|\leq e^{C\sqrt{\Lambda}}\langle z\rangle

and
(\mathbf{Z},\mathbf{V})(\infty,t_{0},z,v)\to(z,v)
as
t_{0}\to+\infty
, for all
(z,v)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
.

Proof.

Let
1\leq i\leq 3
. Note first the inequality
|\langle y\rangle-\langle x\rangle|\leq|x-y|
, which holds for all
x,\,y\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
and which will be used many times during this proof. Next, Proposition
7.12
provides

\left|\mathbf{T}_{F}^{\infty}(v^{i})\right|(t,z,v)=\left|\widehat{v}^{\mu}F_{\mu i}(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})\right|\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{\Lambda}}{t^{\frac{7}{4}}}+\frac{\sqrt{\Lambda}v^{\underline{L}}(\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})}{\langle t-|\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}|\rangle^{2}}.

Fix
(t_{0},z,v)\in[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
and let
\mathbf{Z}_{t}:=\mathbf{Z}(t,t_{0},z,v)
as well as
\mathbf{V}_{t}:=\mathbf{V}(t,t_{0},z,v)
. By Duhamel’s principle we have, for all
t\geq T
,

\mathbf{V}^{i}_{t}-\mathbf{V}^{i}_{t_{0}}=\mathbf{V}^{i}_{t}-v^{i}=\int_{s=t_{0}}^{t}\mathbf{T}_{F}^{\infty}(v^{i})(s,\mathbf{Z}_{s},\mathbf{V}_{s})\mathrm{d}s.

(129)

Moreover, for all
T\leq\tau_{1}\leq\tau_{2}
,

\int_{s=\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\left|\mathbf{T}_{F}^{\infty}(v^{i})(s,\mathbf{Z}_{s},\mathbf{V}_{s})\right|\mathrm{d}s\lesssim\sqrt{\Lambda}\int_{s=\tau_{1}}^{+\infty}\frac{1}{s^{\frac{7}{4}}}+\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{V}}^{\underline{L}}(\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}(s,\mathbf{Z}_{s},\mathbf{V}_{s})}{\langle s-|\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}(s,\mathbf{Z}_{s},\mathbf{V}_{s})|\rangle^{2}}\mathrm{d}s\leq\sqrt{\Lambda}\big(2+\,\mathcal{I}(t_{0},z,v)\big)\lesssim\sqrt{\Lambda},

where
\mathcal{I}(t_{0},z,v)
is defined and bounded by
5
in Lemma
7.20
. We then deduce from the last estimate and (
129
) that
\mathbf{V}^{i}_{t}
converges to
\mathbf{V}^{i}_{\infty}
, as
t\to+\infty
, and then that
|\mathbf{V}^{i}_{t}-\mathbf{V}^{i}_{t_{0}}|\lesssim\sqrt{\Lambda}
for all
t_{0}\geq T
and
T\leq t\leq\infty
.

Next, we obtain from Proposition
7.12
,

\left|\mathbf{T}_{F}^{\infty}(z^{i})\right|=\bigg|-\frac{\delta_{j}^{i}-\widehat{v}_{j}\widehat{v}^{i}}{tv^{0}}\widehat{v}^{\mu}\left(t^{2}{F_{\mu}}^{j}(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})-{\mathbb{F}_{\mu}}^{j}[f_{\infty}](v)\right)-\frac{\widehat{v}^{\mu}}{v^{0}}{F_{\mu}}^{j}(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})v^{0}\partial_{v^{j}}\mathscr{C}^{i}_{t,v}\bigg|\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{\Lambda}}{t^{\frac{4}{3}}}\langle z\rangle+\frac{\sqrt{\Lambda}\,\widehat{v}^{\underline{L}}(\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})}{\langle t-|\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}|\rangle^{\frac{4}{3}}}\langle z\rangle.

Fix again
(t_{0},z,v)\in[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
. By Dumamel’s principle, we have, for all
t\geq T
,

\mathbf{Z}^{i}_{t}-z^{i}=\int_{s=t_{0}}^{t}\mathbf{T}_{F}^{\infty}(z^{i})(s,\mathbf{Z}_{s},\mathbf{V}_{s})\mathrm{d}s,\qquad\qquad\langle\mathbf{Z}_{t}\rangle\leq\langle z\rangle+\int_{s=\min(t_{0},t)}^{\max(t_{0},t)}\big|\mathbf{T}_{F}^{\infty}\big(\langle z\rangle\big)\big|(s,\mathbf{Z}_{s},\mathbf{V}_{s})\mathrm{d}s.

(130)

As
|\mathbf{T}_{F}(\langle z\rangle)|\leq\langle z\rangle^{-1}|z_{i}||\mathbf{T}_{F}^{\infty}(z^{i})|
, we get, for all
t\geq T
,

\langle\mathbf{Z}_{t}\rangle-\langle z\rangle\lesssim\int_{s=\min(t_{0},t)}^{\max(t_{0},t)}\frac{\sqrt{\Lambda}}{s^{\frac{4}{3}}}\langle\mathbf{Z}_{s}\rangle+\frac{\sqrt{\Lambda}\,\widehat{v}^{\underline{L}}\big(\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}(s,\mathbf{Z}_{s},\mathbf{V}_{s})\big)}{\langle t-|\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}(s,\mathbf{Z}_{s},\mathbf{V}_{s})|\rangle^{\frac{4}{3}}}\,\langle\mathbf{Z}_{s}\rangle\,\mathrm{d}s.

Applying first Gronwall’s inequality and then Lemma
7.20
, we obtain that there exists a constant
C_{0}>0
such that

\forall\,t\geq T,\qquad\langle\mathbf{Z}_{t}\rangle\leq\langle z\rangle\exp\big(3C_{0}\sqrt{\Lambda}+C_{0}\sqrt{\Lambda}\,\mathcal{I}(t_{0},z,v)\big)\leq\langle z\rangle\exp\big(8C_{0}\sqrt{\Lambda}\,\big).

It implies, applying again Lemma
7.20
, that for all
T\leq\tau_{1}\leq\tau_{2}
,

\int_{s=\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\left|\mathbf{T}_{F}(z^{i})\right|(s,\mathbf{Z}_{s},\mathbf{V}_{s})\mathrm{d}s\lesssim\sqrt{\Lambda}\,e^{8C_{0}\sqrt{\Lambda}}\langle z\rangle\left(\int_{s=\tau_{1}}^{+\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}s}{s^{\frac{4}{3}}}+\mathcal{I}(t_{0},z,v)\right)\leq 8\sqrt{\Lambda}\,e^{8C_{0}\sqrt{\Lambda}}\langle z\rangle.

Thus, from the last estimate and (
130
), we get that
\mathbf{Z}^{i}_{t}
converges, as
t\to+\infty
, and that the estimate
|\mathbf{Z}^{i}_{t}-z^{i}|\lesssim\sqrt{\Lambda}\,e^{8C_{0}\sqrt{\Lambda}}\langle z\rangle
holds for all
t_{0}\leq t\leq\infty
. Then, we bound the constant using
ae^{c}\leq e^{a+c}
. Finally, for the convergence of
(\mathbf{Z},\mathbf{V})(\infty,t_{0},z,v)
as
t_{0}\to+\infty
, we use the relations (
129
)–(
130
) for
t=+\infty
and that the right hand sides go to zero as
t_{0}\to+\infty
.

We now prove that the characteristics as well their first order derivatives converge as
t_{0}\to+\infty
. Inverting the limit will provide the
C^{1}
regularity of
(\mathbf{Z},\mathbf{V})(\infty,t_{0},\cdot,\cdot)
. For this, we will make use of the next result.

Lemma 7.22
.

Let
d\geq 1
and, for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
\psi_{n}\in C^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d})
be an invertible function such that

•

(\psi_{n})
converges pointwise to
\psi:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
and, for any compact
K\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\sup_{n}\sup_{K}|\nabla\psi_{n}|<+\infty
.

•

(\psi_{n}^{-1})
converges pointwise to
\overline{\psi}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
as
n\to+\infty
.

Then,
\psi
is invertible and
\overline{\psi}=\psi^{-1}
.

Proof.

For all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
, we have

\big|\psi(\overline{\psi}(x))-x\big|=\big|\psi\big(\overline{\psi}(x)\big)-\psi_{n}\big(\psi^{-1}_{n}(x)\big)\big|=\big|\psi\big(\overline{\psi}(x)\big)-\psi_{n}\big(\overline{\psi}(x)\big)\big|+\big|\psi_{n}\big(\overline{\psi}(x)\big)-\psi_{n}\big(\psi^{-1}_{n}(x)\big)\big|.

By assumption,
\psi\big(\overline{\psi}(x)\big)-\psi_{n}\big(\overline{\psi}(x)\big)\to 0
as
n\to+\infty
. Since
(\psi_{n}^{-1}(x))_{n}
is bounded and converges to
\overline{\psi}(x)
, the mean value theorem implies that the second term on the right hand side goes to
0
as
n\to+\infty
.

For any
t_{0}\geq T
, since
(z,v)\mapsto(\mathbf{Z},\mathbf{V})(t_{0},t_{0},z,v)=(z,v)
, we have, for any
1\leq i,\,j\leq 3
,

\displaystyle\partial_{z^{j}}\mathbf{Z}^{i}(t_{0},t_{0},z,v)=\partial_{v^{j}}\mathbf{V}^{i}(t_{0},t_{0},z,v)=\delta^{i}_{j},\qquad\qquad\partial_{v^{j}}\mathbf{Z}^{i}(t_{0},t_{0},z,v)=\partial_{z^{j}}\mathbf{V}^{i}(t_{0},t_{0},z,v)=0.

(131)

Proposition 7.23
.

Let
t\in[T,+\infty[
. Then,

•

(z,v)\mapsto(\mathbf{Z},\mathbf{V})(t,t_{0},z,v)
converges to a
C^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})
function
(\mathbf{Z},\mathbf{V})(t,\infty,\cdot,\cdot)
, as
t_{0}\to+\infty
, which is the inverse of
(\mathbf{Z},\mathbf{V})(\infty,t,\cdot,\cdot)
.

•

There exists a constant
D_{\Lambda}>0
such that, for all
T\leq t_{0}\leq+\infty
,
(z,v)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
and
1\leq i,\,j\leq 3
,

\displaystyle\big|\partial_{z^{j}}\mathbf{Z}^{i}(t,t_{0},z,v)-\delta_{j}^{i}\big|+\big|\partial_{z^{j}}\mathbf{V}^{i}(t,t_{0},z,v)\big|

\displaystyle\leq e^{D_{\Lambda}\langle v\rangle\,\langle z\rangle^{3}},

\displaystyle\langle v\rangle\,\big|\partial_{v^{j}}\mathbf{Z}^{i}(t,t_{0},z,v)\big|+\langle v\rangle\,\big|\partial_{v^{j}}\mathbf{V}^{i}(t,t_{0},z,v)-\delta_{j}^{i}\big|

\displaystyle\leq e^{D_{\Lambda}\langle v\rangle\,\langle z\rangle^{3}}.

Proof.

Let
\tau\geq T
,
1\leq i\leq 3
and
h=\mathbf{Z}^{i}(\tau,\cdot,\cdot,\cdot)
or
h=\mathbf{V}^{i}(\tau,\cdot,\cdot,\cdot)
. By definition of the characteristics,
h
is solution to the Vlasov equation
\mathbf{T}_{F}^{\infty}(h)=0
. The strategy is the following.

1.

First, we prove that the
(z,v)
-derivatives of
h
converge in
C^{1}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})
, as
t_{0}\to+\infty
.

2.

Then, according to Lemma
7.21
,
t_{0}\mapsto(\mathbf{Z},\mathbf{V})(\tau,t_{0},0,0)
is bounded. Hence, there exists a sequence of time
(s_{n})
, with
s_{n}\to+\infty
, such that
(\mathbf{Z},\mathbf{V})(\tau,s_{n},0,0)
converges as
n\to+\infty
. Together with
1.
, we get that
(\mathbf{Z},\mathbf{V})(\tau,s_{n},z,v)
converges for all
(z,v)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
. Applying Lemma
7.22
, with
d=6
, to

\psi_{n}=(\mathbf{Z},\mathbf{V})(\tau,s_{n},\cdot,\cdot),\qquad\psi_{n}^{-1}=(\mathbf{Z},\mathbf{V})(s_{n},\tau,\cdot,\cdot),

we obtain that
(z,v)\mapsto(\mathbf{Z},\mathbf{V})(\infty,\tau,z,v)
is invertible and that its inverse, denoted
(\mathbf{Z},\mathbf{V})(\tau,\infty,z,v)
, is the limit of
(\mathbf{Z},\mathbf{V})(\tau,s_{n},z,v)
. Finally, since
t_{0}\mapsto(\mathbf{Z},\mathbf{V})(\tau,t_{0},z,v)
is bounded and has a unique subsequential limit, it converges to
(\mathbf{Z},\mathbf{V})(\tau,\infty,z,v)
as
t_{0}\to+\infty
.

In order to prove the first step as well as the estimates for the derivatives of
h
, remark that in view of (
131
) and

v^{0}\partial_{v^{k}}=\widehat{\Omega}_{0k}^{\infty}+z^{k}\partial_{t}^{\infty}+\widehat{v}^{k}S-\widehat{v}^{k}z\nabla_{z},\qquad\partial_{z^{k}}\in\widehat{\mathbb{P}}^{\infty}_{S},

it suffices to prove that
\widehat{Z}_{\infty}h(t_{0},\cdot,\cdot)
converges in
C^{1}_{\mathrm{loc}}
, as
t_{0}\to+\infty
, for all
\widehat{Z}_{\infty}\in\widehat{\mathbb{P}}^{\infty}_{S}
. According to the commutation formula of Corollary
7.8
and Proposition
7.12
, there holds

\sup_{\widehat{Z}_{\infty}\in\widehat{\mathbb{P}}^{\infty}_{S}}\big|\mathbf{T}_{F}^{\infty}\big(\widehat{Z}_{\infty}h\big)\big|(t,z,v)\lesssim\bigg(\,\frac{\sqrt{\Lambda}}{t^{\frac{4}{3}}}+\frac{\sqrt{\Lambda}\,\widehat{v}^{\underline{L}}(\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})}{\langle t-|\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}|\rangle^{\frac{4}{3}}}\bigg)\langle z\rangle\,\sup_{\widehat{Z}_{\infty}\in\widehat{\mathbb{P}}^{\infty}_{S}}\big|\widehat{Z}_{\infty}h\big|(t,z,v)\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{\Lambda}}{t^{\frac{5}{4}}}\langle v\rangle\,\langle z\rangle^{3}\sup_{\widehat{Z}_{\infty}\in\widehat{\mathbb{P}}^{\infty}_{S}}\big|\widehat{Z}_{\infty}h\big|(t,z,v),

where, in the last step, we applied Lemma
6.6
. Fix
(z,v)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
and let
\mathbf{Z}_{s}:=\mathbf{Z}(s,t_{0},z,v)
,
\mathbf{V}_{s}:=\mathbf{V}(s,t_{0},z,v)
as well as

\Phi(s):=\,\sup_{\widehat{Z}_{\infty}\in\widehat{\mathbb{P}}^{\infty}_{S}}\big|\widehat{Z}_{\infty}h\big|(s,\mathbf{Z}_{s},\mathbf{V}_{s}).

By Duhamel’s principle, and since
\mathbf{T}^{\infty}_{F}(|g|)=\frac{g}{|g|}\mathbf{T}_{F}^{\infty}(g)
in
W^{1,1}_{\mathrm{loc}}
, we have

\forall t\geq T,\qquad|\Phi(t_{0})-\Phi(\tau)|\lesssim\int_{s\in[(\tau,t_{0})]}\frac{\Lambda}{s^{\frac{5}{4}}}\langle\mathbf{V}_{s}\rangle\,\langle\mathbf{Z}_{s}\rangle^{3}\Phi(s)\mathrm{d}s,\qquad[(\tau,t_{0})]:=[\min(\tau,t_{0}),\max(\tau,t_{0})].

By Lemma
7.21
,
\langle\mathbf{V}_{s}\rangle\,\,\langle\mathbf{Z}_{s}\rangle^{3}\lesssim C_{\Lambda}\,\langle v\rangle\,\langle z\rangle^{3}
, for a certain constant
C_{\Lambda}>0
. We then deduce from Gronwall’s inequality that there exists
C^{1}_{\Lambda},\,C^{2}_{\Lambda}>0
such that

\forall\,t\geq T,\qquad\sup_{s\in[(\tau,t_{0})]}\Phi(s)\leq\Phi(\tau)\exp\left(C^{1}_{\Lambda}\langle v\rangle\,\langle z\rangle^{3}\right)\lesssim\big(\langle v\rangle+\langle z\rangle\big)\exp\left(C^{1}_{\Lambda}\langle v\rangle\,\langle z\rangle^{3}\right)\lesssim\exp\left(C^{2}_{\Lambda}\langle v\rangle\,\langle z\rangle^{3}\right),

where we estimated
\Phi(\tau)
by exploiting (
131
). Using Duhamel principle once again, we obtain that

\forall\,t_{0}\geq T,\qquad\big|\widehat{Z}_{\infty}h(t_{0},z,v)-\widehat{Z}_{\infty}h(\tau,z,v)\big|\lesssim\int_{s\in[(\tau,t_{0})]}\frac{\sqrt{\Lambda}}{s^{\frac{5}{4}}}\,\langle\mathbf{V}_{s}\rangle\,\langle\mathbf{Z}_{s}\rangle^{3}\Phi(s)\mathrm{d}s\lesssim\exp\left(\,\overline{D}_{\Lambda}\langle v\rangle\,\langle z\rangle^{3}\right),

which concludes the proof.

Although certain of the estimates that have just derived could be significantly improved, they are sufficient in order to prove regularity results for the limit of the characteristics.

Proposition 7.24
.

For all
t_{0}\in[T,+\infty[
,
(z,v)\mapsto(\mathbf{Z},\mathbf{V})(\infty,t_{0},z,v)
is a
C^{1}
-diffeomorphism from
\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
onto itself. Moreover,
(\mathbf{Z},\mathbf{V})(\infty,\cdot,\cdot,\cdot)\in C^{1}([T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})
.

Proof.

Let, for
(t,t_{0})\in[T,+\infty[\times[T,+\infty]
,
J_{t,t_{0}}
be the Jacobian determinant of the map
\Phi_{t,t_{0}}:(z,v)\mapsto(\mathbf{Z},\mathbf{V})(t,t_{0},z,v)
. Then, Proposition
7.23
implies that
\Phi_{t,t_{0}}\in C^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})
and

\forall\,(t,t_{0},z,v)\in[T,+\infty[^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v},\qquad\qquad|J_{t,t_{0}}|(z,v)\leq 6!\,e^{6D_{\Lambda}\,\langle v\rangle\,\langle z\rangle^{3}}.

(132)

Since
\Phi_{t_{0},t}\circ\Phi_{t,t_{0}}=\mathrm{id}
for all
(t,t_{0})\in[T,+\infty[^{2}
, we get from the previous estimate that, for all
t\geq T
,

|J_{t,t_{0}}|^{-1}(z,v)=|J_{t_{0},t}|\big(\mathbf{Z}(t,t_{0},z,v),\mathbf{V}(t,t_{0},z,v)\big)\leq 6!\,\exp\left(6D_{\Lambda}\,\langle\mathbf{V}(t,t_{0},z,v)\rangle\,\langle\mathbf{Z}(t,t_{0},z,v)\rangle^{3}\right).

We then deduce from (
132
) and Lemma
7.21
that

\exists\,\overline{D}_{\Lambda}>0,\;\forall\,t\geq T,\;\forall\,(z,v)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v},\qquad e^{-\overline{D}_{\Lambda}\,\langle v\rangle\,\langle z\rangle^{3}}\lesssim|J_{t,t_{0}}|(z,v)\lesssim e^{\overline{D}_{\Lambda}\,\langle v\rangle\,\langle z\rangle^{3}}.

Since these inequalities necessarily hold for
t_{0}=+\infty
, we obtain from the inverse function theorem that
\Phi_{t,\infty}:(z,v)\mapsto(\mathbf{Z},\mathbf{V})(t,\infty,z,v)
is a local
C^{1}
-diffeomorphism. As we already know from Proposition
7.23
that it is invertible,
\Phi_{t,\infty}
is in fact a global diffeomorphism. Finally, since
(t_{0},z,v)\mapsto(\mathbf{Z},\mathbf{V})(t,t_{0},z,v)
is solution to the Vlasov equation
\mathbf{T}_{F}^{\infty}(h)=0
for all
t\geq T
, the same holds for the limit
(\mathbf{Z},\mathbf{V})(\infty,\cdot,\cdot,\cdot)
in the space of distributions. It implies that
\partial_{t}(\mathbf{Z},\mathbf{V})(\infty,\cdot,\cdot,\cdot)
is continous, which concludes the proof.

7.4.3 Proof of Proposition
7.15

Let
h_{\infty}:\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\to\mathbb{R}
such that
\mathbb{E}_{N}^{N_{z},N_{v}}[h_{\infty}]<+\infty
. Assume that
h\in C^{1}\big([T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\big)
is a solution to the asymptotic Cauchy problem (
126
) and fix
(z,v)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
. By Duhamel principle, we have, for all
(t,t_{0})\in[T,+\infty[^{\,2}
,

h(t,z,v)\!=\!h\big(t_{0},\mathbf{Z}(t_{0},t,z,v),\mathbf{V}(t_{0},t,z,v)\big)-h\big(t_{0},\mathbf{Z}(\infty,t,z,v),\mathbf{V}(\infty,t,z,v)\big)+h\big(t_{0},\mathbf{Z}(\infty,t,z,v),\mathbf{V}(\infty,t,z,v)\big).

By assumption, the last term on the right hand side converges to
h_{\infty}\big(\mathbf{Z}(\infty,t,z,v),\mathbf{V}(\infty,t,z,v)\big)
as
t_{0}\to+\infty
. By the mean value theorem, we have

\displaystyle|h(t_{0},\mathbf{Z}(t_{0},t,z,v),\mathbf{V}(t_{0},t,z,v))-h(t_{0},\mathbf{Z}(\infty,t,z,v),\mathbf{V}(\infty,t,z,v))|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\lesssim\|\nabla_{z,v}h\|_{L^{\infty}_{t,z,v}}\big(|\mathbf{Z}(t_{0},t,z,v)-\mathbf{Z}(\infty,t,z,v)|+|\mathbf{V}(t_{0},t,z,v)-\mathbf{V}(\infty,t,z,v)|\big)

and, according to Lemma
7.21
, the right hand side goes to
0
as
t_{0}\to+\infty
. Hence, we have

\forall\,(t,z,v)\in[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v},\qquad h(t,z,v):=h_{\infty}\big(\mathbf{Z}(\infty,t,z,v),\mathbf{V}(\infty,t,z,v)\big),

(133)

so that uniqueness holds in the functional space
C^{1}\big([T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\big)
. Conversally, let
h
be defined by (
133
). By Lemma
7.21
and Proposition
7.24
, it is a
C^{1}
solution to the asymptotic Cauchy problem (
126
). Let
\psi:\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
be a mollifier and

\psi_{n}(z,v):=n^{6}\psi(nz,nv),\qquad h_{n}:=h\ast\psi_{n},\qquad\qquad n\in\mathbb{N}^{*},

so that
h_{n}
is a
C^{\infty}
solution to (
126
) with asymptotic data
h_{\infty}\ast\psi_{n}
.

1.

Assume first that
h_{\infty}
is compactly supported. By Lemma
7.21
,
h_{n}(t,\cdot,\cdot)
is then compactly supported as well for all
t\geq T
. We can then apply Proposition
7.17
to
h_{n}
and derive the estimates stated in Proposition
7.15
, for the energy norms of
h
, by letting
n\to+\infty
.

2.

Let
\chi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R},\mathbb{R}_{+})
be a cutoff function such that
\chi(s)=1
for all
s\leq 1/4
and
\chi(s)=0
for all
s\geq 1/2
. Consider then the solution
h_{n}
to the Vlasov equation arising from the asymptotic data
(z,v)\mapsto h_{\infty}(z,v)\chi(|z,v|n^{-1})
. We can then apply the previous step to
h_{n}
and derive the expected estimates for
h
by passing to the limit as
n\to+\infty
.

7.5 Construction of the solution and the
L^{2}_{x}
estimates

According to Proposition
7.15
, we can define
g
as the unique global solution to (
126
) with asymptotic data
f_{\infty}
. Furthermore, Proposition
7.15
applied for the parameters
(N_{z},N_{v})=(10,19)
implies that there exists
B>0
, depending only on
(N,D)
, such that

\mathbf{E}_{N-3}^{10,19}[g]+\mathbf{E}_{N}^{7,13}[g]\leq\mathbb{E}_{N}^{10,19}[f_{\infty}]\exp\big(\exp\big(B\sqrt{\Lambda}\,\big)\big)\leq\epsilon B_{\Lambda},

(134)

where
B_{\Lambda}:=\exp\big(\exp\big(B\sqrt{\Lambda}\,\big)\big)
. In particular, we get
g\in\mathbb{V}^{B_{\Lambda},\epsilon}_{N}
.

Remark 7.25
.

Similarly, in view of the assumption (
24
) verified by
f_{\infty}
, there exists
B^{\prime}>0
depending only on
N
such that

\mathbf{E}_{N}^{8,15}[g]\leq\mathbb{E}_{N}^{11,21}[f_{\infty}]\exp\big(\exp\big(B^{\prime}\sqrt{\Lambda}\,\big)\big)\lesssim\epsilon\exp\big(\exp\big(B^{\prime}\sqrt{\Lambda}\,\big)\big).

This stronger bound is important in order to control sufficiently well the initial data in the perspective of constructing the scattering map in Theorem
1.12
. However, we do not need it in order to prove the existence of the solution
(f,F)
to the Vlasov-Maxwell system stated by Theorem
1.8
.

We conclude this section by deriving decay estimates for
\int_{v}\widehat{Z}^{\beta}f\mathrm{d}v
, where
f(t,x,v)=g(t,x-t\widehat{v}-\mathscr{C}_{t,v},v)
. For this, we will apply Proposition
2.13
to
\widehat{Z}^{\beta}f
. It motivates us to relate
\widehat{Z}^{\beta}f
with
\widehat{Z}^{\kappa}_{\infty}g
and to prove the convergence of the spatial averages of
\widehat{Z}^{\kappa}_{\infty}g
, which is the purpose of the next result.

Proposition 7.26
.

For any
|\beta|\leq N-1
, we have

\forall\,t\geq T,\qquad\bigg\||v^{0}|^{8}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}\widehat{Z}^{\beta}_{\infty}g(t,z,\cdot)-\widehat{Z}^{\beta}_{\infty}f_{\infty}(z,\cdot)\mathrm{d}z\bigg\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}\lesssim\sqrt{\epsilon\Lambda B_{\Lambda}}\,\frac{\log^{3N}(t)}{t}.

Proof.

We will use that for any sufficiently regular function
\psi:[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
, we have, according to Minkowski integral inequality,

\forall\,T\leq t_{1}\leq t_{2},\qquad\big\|\psi(t_{2},\cdot)-\psi(t_{1},\cdot)\big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}=\bigg\|\int_{t=t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\partial_{t}\psi(t,\cdot)\mathrm{d}t\bigg\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}\!\leq\int_{t=t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\big\|\partial_{t}\psi(t,\cdot)\big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}\mathrm{d}t.

Applying first Corollary
7.11
and then the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality in
z
, we get, as
z\mapsto\langle z\rangle^{-2}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z})
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\langle v\rangle^{16}\bigg|\partial_{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}\widehat{Z}^{\beta}_{\infty}h(t,z,v)\mathrm{d}z\bigg|^{2}\mathrm{d}v\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{|\kappa|\leq N}\,\frac{\Lambda\log^{5N}(t)}{t^{4}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\langle v\rangle^{22}\langle z\rangle^{8+2N-2\kappa_{H}}\,\big|\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\kappa}g\big|^{2}(t,z,v)\mathrm{d}z\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle+\log^{4N+4}(t)\,\mathbf{E}_{N}^{7,13}[g]\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\langle t-|\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}|\rangle^{2}\left|\nabla_{t,x}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\kappa}}F\right|^{2}(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})\frac{\mathrm{d}z\mathrm{d}v}{\langle z\rangle^{4}\,\langle v\rangle^{8}}.

The
L^{2}
bound (
134
) for
g
and Proposition
7.13
imply the result.

We now relate the derivatives of
f
to the ones of
g
.

Lemma 7.27
.

Let
|\beta|\leq N
. Then, we have the
L^{2}_{x,v}
bound

\forall\,t\geq T,\qquad\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\langle x-t\widehat{v}\rangle^{14}\,\langle v\rangle^{26}\big|\widehat{Z}^{\beta}f\big|^{2}(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}v\lesssim\epsilon B_{\Lambda}\log^{6N}(t).

For any
|\kappa|\leq N-2
, we have the
L^{\infty}_{x,v}
bound

\forall\,t\geq T,\qquad\sup_{(x,v)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\langle x-t\widehat{v}\rangle^{7}\,\langle v\rangle^{13}\big|\widehat{Z}^{\beta}f\big|(t,x,v)\lesssim\sqrt{\epsilon B_{\Lambda}}\log^{3N}(t).

If
|\beta|\leq N-1
, we have

\forall\,t\geq T,\qquad\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\langle v\rangle^{16}\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\widehat{Z}^{\beta}f(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}x-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}\widehat{Z}^{\beta}_{\infty}f_{\infty}(z,v)\mathrm{d}z\bigg|^{2}\mathrm{d}v\lesssim\epsilon\Lambda B_{\Lambda}\frac{\log^{6N}(t)}{t^{2}}.

Proof.

By iterating Lemma
2.22
and by exploiting the commutation relations (
121
) between
\partial_{t}^{\infty}
and
\partial_{z^{i}}
with
Z_{\infty}\in\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{S}^{\infty}
, we obtain the following result. For any
|\beta|\leq N
, we can write

\big(\widehat{Z}^{\beta}f\big)(t,z+t\widehat{v}+\mathscr{C}_{t,v},v)-\widehat{Z}^{\beta}_{\infty}g(t,z,v)

as a combination, with coefficients which are polynomials in
\widehat{v}
, of terms of the form

t^{-m}P_{p,q}(\mathscr{C})\big(\partial_{t}^{\infty}\big)^{n}\partial_{z}^{\alpha_{z}}\widehat{Z}^{\kappa}_{\infty}g(t,z,v),\qquad n+|\alpha_{z}|+|\kappa|\leq|\beta|,\quad n+|\alpha_{z}|\geq 1,\quad 0\leq p\leq m+|\beta|\leq 2|\beta|,\quad q\leq|\beta|.

By (
119
), we have
|t^{-m}P_{p,q}(\mathscr{C})|\lesssim t^{-m}\log^{2p}(t)\lesssim 1
if
m\geq 1
and
|t^{-m}P_{p,q}(\mathscr{C})|\lesssim\log^{2N}(t)
otherwise. Hence,

\langle z+\mathscr{C}_{t,v}\rangle^{7}\,\langle v\rangle^{13}\big|\big(\widehat{Z}^{\beta}f\big)(t,z+t\widehat{v}+\mathscr{C}_{t,v},v)\big|\lesssim\log^{2N+7}(t)\sup_{|\xi|\leq|\beta|}\langle z\rangle^{7}\,\langle v\rangle^{13}\big|\widehat{Z}^{\xi}_{\infty}g\big|(t,z,v).

The
L^{2}_{x,v}
estimates then follows from the boundedness in
L^{2}_{z,v}
of the derivatives of
g
given by (
134
). The
L^{\infty}_{x,v}
ones follow from the Sobolev embedding
H^{2}_{z,v}\hookrightarrow L^{\infty}_{z,v}
. For the spatial average, by integration by parts in
z
, we have

\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\widehat{Z}^{\beta}f(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}x-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}\widehat{Z}^{\beta}_{\infty}g(t,z,v)\mathrm{d}z\bigg|\lesssim\sum_{1\leq n\leq|\beta|}\sum_{|\kappa|\leq|\beta|-n}\log^{2N}(t)\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}\big(\partial_{t}^{\infty}\big)^{n}\widehat{Z}^{\kappa}_{\infty}g(t,z,v)\mathrm{d}z\bigg|.

Then, write
t\partial_{t}^{\infty}=S-z\cdot\nabla_{z}+\widehat{v}\cdot\nabla_{z}
and perform again integration by parts, so that the result follows from Proposition
7.26
as well as (
134
).

We are finally able to deduce the following
L^{2}_{x}
estimates for the velocity averages of
f
.

Corollary 7.28
.

For any
|\beta|\leq N-1
and
\mu\in\llbracket 0,3\rrbracket
, we have

\forall\,t\geq T,\qquad\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\langle t+|x|\rangle^{5}\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\widehat{v}_{\mu}\widehat{Z}^{\beta}f(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}v-J^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\mu}\big[Z_{\infty}^{\beta}f_{\infty}\big](t,x)\bigg|^{2}\mathrm{d}x\lesssim\epsilon\Lambda B_{\Lambda}\log^{6N}(t).

Remark 7.29
.

Recall that if
\beta_{T}\geq 1
, so that
\widehat{Z}^{\beta}
contains at least one translation, then
J^{\mathrm{asymp}}[Z_{\infty}^{\beta}f_{\infty}]=0
.

In fact, this result is a direct consequence of the following one, applied for
\mathbf{q}(\omega,v)=\widehat{v}_{\mu}
, requiring more decay in
v
but which will allow us to deal with the top order derivatives, where a parameter
\omega\in\mathbb{S}^{2}
will naturally appear.

Corollary 7.30
.

Let
\mathbf{q}:\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\to\mathbb{R}
be a smooth function such that
|\mathbf{q}|(\cdot,v)+|\nabla_{v}\mathbf{q}|(\cdot,v)\lesssim\langle v\rangle^{3}
and
|\beta|\leq N-1
. We define

\rho^{\beta}\big[f,f_{\infty};\mathbf{q}\big](t,x,\omega):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\mathbf{q}(\omega,v)\widehat{Z}^{\beta}f(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}v-\mathds{1}_{|x|<t}\,\frac{1}{t^{3}}\mathbf{q}\bigg(\omega,\frac{\widecheck{\;x\;}}{t}\bigg)\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}\Big[|v^{0}|^{5}\widehat{Z}^{\beta}_{\infty}f_{\infty}\Big]\bigg(z,\frac{\widecheck{\;x\;}}{t}\bigg)\mathrm{d}z.

There holds, uniformly in
\omega\in\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}
,

\forall\,t\geq T,\qquad\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\langle t+|x|\rangle^{5}\Big|\rho^{\beta}\big[f,f_{\infty};\mathbf{q}\big](t,x,\omega)\Big|^{2}\mathrm{d}x\lesssim\epsilon\Lambda B_{\Lambda}\log^{6N}(t).

Proof.

Apply Proposition
2.13
, to the distribution function
\mathbf{q}(\omega,v)\widehat{Z}^{\beta}f
and for all
\omega\in\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}
, together with Lemma
7.27
. Then, remark that, by the change of variables
x=t\widehat{v}
(recall Lemma
2.10
) and Lemma
7.27
,

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\langle t+|x|\rangle^{5}\bigg|\frac{\mathds{1}_{|x|<t}}{t^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{y}}\Big[|v^{0}|^{5}\widehat{Z}^{\beta}f\Big]\bigg(t,y,\frac{\widecheck{\;x\;}}{t}\bigg)-\Big[|v^{0}|^{5}\widehat{Z}^{\beta}_{\infty}f_{\infty}\Big]\bigg(y,\frac{\widecheck{\;x\;}}{t}\bigg)\mathrm{d}y\bigg|^{2}\mathrm{d}x\lesssim\epsilon\Lambda B_{\Lambda}\log^{6N}(t).

In order to deal with the top order derivatives or with quadratic terms, we will make use of the next estimates.

Corollary 7.31
.

For any
|\beta|\leq N
, we have

\forall\,t\geq T,\qquad\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\langle t+|x|\rangle^{3}\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\langle x-t\widehat{v}\rangle^{3}\,\langle v\rangle^{9}\big|\widehat{Z}^{\beta}f(t,x,v)\big|\mathrm{d}v\bigg|^{2}\mathrm{d}x\lesssim\epsilon B_{\Lambda}\log^{6N}(t).

For any
|\kappa|\leq N-2
, there holds

\forall\,(t,x)\in[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3},\qquad\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\langle x-t\widehat{v}\rangle\,\langle v\rangle^{5}\big|\widehat{Z}^{\kappa}f\big|(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}v\lesssim\sqrt{\epsilon B_{\Lambda}}\,\frac{|\max(t-|x|,1)|^{\frac{3}{2}}}{\langle t+|x|\rangle^{\frac{9}{2}}}\log^{6N}(t).

Proof.

For the
L^{2}_{x}
estimate, apply Proposition
2.11
to
\langle x-t\widehat{v}\rangle^{3}\,\langle v\rangle^{9}\,\widehat{Z}^{\beta}f(t,x,v)
and use then Lemma
7.27
. For the
L^{\infty}_{x}
one, we apply Corollary
2.12
to
\langle x-t\widehat{v}\rangle\,\langle v\rangle^{5}\widehat{Z}^{\kappa}f
and we use again Lemma
7.27
.

Finally, let us mention that we can derive a convergence estimate for
g
.

Proposition 7.32
.

For all
t\geq T
, we have

\|g(t,\cdot,\cdot)-f_{\infty}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}\lesssim\sqrt{\epsilon\Lambda B_{\Lambda}}\,t^{-\frac{1}{2}}\log^{3}(t).

Proof.

Following the proof of Proposition
7.12
and using Lemma
6.6
, one can derive

|\partial_{t}g|(t,z,v)=|\partial_{t}g-\mathbf{T}^{\infty}_{F}(g)|(t,z,v)\lesssim\sqrt{\Lambda}t^{-\frac{3}{2}}\log^{3}(t)\sup_{|\beta|=1}\,\langle z\rangle^{\frac{3}{2}}\,\langle v\rangle^{2}\big|\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\beta}g\big|(t,z,v),

which, in view of the weighted
L^{\infty}_{z,v}
bounds on the lower order derivatives of
g
, implies the result.

8
Construction of the electromagnetic field

In this section, we study the asymptotic Cauchy problem

\nabla^{\mu}G_{\mu\nu}=J(f)_{\nu},\qquad\nabla^{\mu}{}^{*}\!G_{\mu\nu}=0,\qquad\qquad\lim_{r\to+\infty}r\underline{\alpha}(G)(r+u,r\omega)=\underline{\alpha}^{\infty}(u,\omega),

(135)

where
f
is the distribution function constructed in Section
7
.

Remark 8.1
.

Given a source term
J
and an
L^{2}_{u,\omega}
radiation field
\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}
, the asymptotic Cauchy problem for the Maxwell equations admits at most one solution in
L^{\infty}([T,+\infty[,L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))
according to Theorem
4.20
.

Our goal is to prove the next result, where we recall from Definition
6.3
the set
\mathbb{M}^{D,\Lambda}_{N}
.

Proposition 8.2
.

There exists a unique classical solution
F^{\mathrm{new}}
to (
135
) on
[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
. Furthermore, if
\Lambda^{2}B_{\Lambda}\epsilon\log^{-1}T
is small enough, we have

F^{\mathrm{new}}\in\mathbb{M}^{D,\Lambda}_{N}
.

Remark 8.3
.

We deduce from
F^{\mathrm{new}}\in\mathbb{M}^{D,\Lambda}_{N}
as well as Propositions
4.5
and
5.53
the convergence estimate

\forall\,(t,r,\omega)\in[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{S}^{2},\qquad\qquad\big|\underline{\alpha}\big(F^{\mathrm{new}}\big)(t,r\omega)-\underline{\alpha}^{\infty}(t-r,\omega)\big|\lesssim\sqrt{\Lambda}\langle t+r\rangle^{-1}.

In fact, a part of the proof of Proposition
8.2
will also allow us to construct
F^{1}
, the first electromagnetic field of the sequence of approximate solutions.

Proposition 8.4
.

If
f=f_{1}
, there exists a unique classical solution
F^{1}
to (
135
) on
[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
. Moreover, if
\Lambda B_{\Lambda}\epsilon\log^{-1}T
is small enough, we have

\mathcal{E}^{K}_{N}\big[F^{1}-F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]\big](t)\leq D\Lambda
for all
t\geq T
, so that
F^{1}\in\mathbb{M}^{D,\Lambda}_{N}
.

Our study of (
135
) is divided in three parts.

1.

We start by proving that there exists a unique classical solution
F_{\mathrm{new}}
to (
135
), which further belongs to
W^{k,\infty}([T,+\infty[,H^{N-k}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))
for all
0\leq k\leq N
.

2.

Then, we prove that the lower order derivatives verify
\mathcal{E}^{K}_{N-1}\big[F^{\mathrm{new}}-F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]\big]\leq\frac{D}{2}\Lambda
on
[T,+\infty[
.

3.

Finally, we deal with the top order derivatives by using the Glassey-Strauss decomposition of the electromagnetic fields, providing
F^{\mathrm{new}}\in\mathbb{M}^{D,\Lambda}_{N}
.

Remark 8.5
.

The first step could be avoided but it allows us to estimate all the derivatives of
F^{\mathrm{new}}
separately. Otherwise, we would have to prove a higher order version of Proposition
4.9
taking moreover into account the difficulties related to the top order derivatives.

The properties of
f
that we will exploit to deal with
1.
are the estimates of Corollary
7.31
, which turn out to be satisfied by
f_{1}
as well (see Corollary
2.17
). For
2.
, we will use further the estimates, for the derivatives of
f
up to order
N-1
, given by Corollary
7.28
. According to Corollary
2.17
, the derivatives of
f_{1}
, up to order
N
, verify the same estimates. Hence, in Sections
8.1
–
8.2
, we also allow
f
to denote
f_{1}
, in which case
N\!-\!1
has to be formally replaced by
N
, so that the results proved there will imply Proposition
8.4
.

8.1 Well-posedness for the Maxwell equations with asymptotic data

Proposition 8.6
.

There exists a unique solution
F^{\mathrm{new}}
to (
135
), defined on
[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
, which further verifies

\forall\,|\gamma|\leq N,\qquad\qquad\sup_{t\geq T}\big\|\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F^{\mathrm{new}}(t,\cdot)\big\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}<+\infty.

Proof.

Let
H
be the unique soluton to

\nabla^{\mu}H_{\mu\nu}=J(f)_{\nu},\quad\;\nabla^{\mu}{}^{*}\!H_{\mu\nu}=0,\qquad\quad\nabla_{\partial_{x^{i}}}H_{0j}(T,\cdot)=\nabla_{\partial_{x^{j}}}H_{0i}(T,\cdot),\quad\;\nabla_{\partial_{x^{i}}}{}^{*}\!H_{0j}(T,\cdot)=\nabla_{\partial_{x^{j}}}{}^{*}\!H_{0i}(T,\cdot).

1\leq i,\,j\leq 3
. Note then that
\nabla^{\mu}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}(H)_{\mu\nu}=J(\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}f)_{\nu}
by Proposition
2.8
. According to Proposition
3.14
, Lemma
7.27
and Corollary
7.28
, we have

\forall\,|\gamma|\leq N,\qquad\big\|\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}H(T,\cdot)\big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\sup_{t\geq T}\big\|\langle t-|\cdot|\rangle^{\frac{3}{2}}J\big(\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}f\big)(t,\cdot)\big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}<+\infty.

By the general scattering result of Proposition
4.3
, applied to
\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}H
for any
|\gamma|\leq N-1
,
H
admits a radiation field
\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}
along future null infinity, which satisfies

\forall\,|\gamma|\leq N,\qquad\big\|\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}_{\gamma}\big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{u}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega})}<+\infty.

Consequently, in view of the assumptions on
\underline{\alpha}^{\infty}
, the radiation field
\underline{\alpha}^{\infty}_{\gamma}-\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}_{\gamma}
is bounded as well in
L^{2}_{u,\omega}
for any
|\gamma|\leq N
. By Theorem
4.20
, there exists a unique
2
-form
H^{\mathrm{vac}}
verifying

\nabla^{\mu}H^{\mathrm{vac}}_{\mu\nu}=0,\quad\nabla^{\mu}{}^{*}\!H_{\mu\nu}^{\mathrm{vac}}=0;\qquad\qquad\forall\,|\gamma|\leq N,\;\forall\,t\in\mathbb{R}_{+},\quad 2\big\|\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}H(t,\cdot)\big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}=\big\|\underline{\alpha}^{\infty}_{\gamma}-\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}_{\gamma}\big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{u}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega})},

and
\underline{\alpha}^{\infty}\!-\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}
is the radiation field of
H^{\mathrm{vac}}
along
\mathcal{I}^{+}
. Thus, the result holds and
F^{\mathrm{new}}:=H\!+H^{\mathrm{vac}}
.

8.2 Improved estimates for the lower order derivatives of the electromagnetic field

We refine now our estimates on
\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F^{\mathrm{new}}
, for
|\gamma|\leq N-1
. More precisely, we prove here the next result.

Proposition 8.7
.

If
D
is chosen large enough and if
\Lambda B_{\Lambda}\epsilon\log^{-1}T
is small enough, then

\forall\,t\geq T,\qquad\qquad\mathcal{E}^{K}_{N-1}\big[F^{\mathrm{new}}-F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]\big](t)\leq\frac{D}{2}\Lambda.

Let
|\kappa|\leq N-1
. If
Z^{\kappa}
is only composed by homogeneous vector fields
\Omega_{0k}
,
\Omega_{ij}
and
S
, then, according to the commutation formula given by Proposition
5.16
, we have

\nabla^{\mu}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\kappa}}(F^{\mathrm{new}}-F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}])_{\mu\nu}=J(\widehat{Z}^{\kappa}f)_{\nu}-J^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\nu}[\widehat{Z}^{\kappa}_{\infty}f_{\infty}],\qquad\qquad\nabla^{\mu}{}^{*}\!\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\kappa}}(F^{\mathrm{new}}-F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}])_{\mu\nu}=0.

If
\kappa_{T}\geq 1
,
J(\widehat{Z}^{\kappa}f)
and the source term of the commuted asymptotic Maxwell equations are strongly decaying so that we will be able to estimate both fields separately. Recall further that
J^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\nu}[\widehat{Z}^{\kappa}_{\infty}f_{\infty}]=0
in that case. This motivates the first step of the proof, which consists in proving that, for any
|\gamma|\leq N-1
, there exists an electromagnetic field
G^{\gamma}
such that

\hskip-17.07164pt\nabla^{\mu}G_{\mu\nu}^{\gamma}=J(\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}f)_{\nu}-J^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\nu}\big[\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}_{\infty}f_{\infty}\big],\qquad\nabla^{\mu}{}^{*}\!G_{\mu\nu}^{\gamma}=0;\qquad\qquad\sup_{t\geq T}\mathcal{E}^{K}[G^{\gamma}](t)\lesssim\epsilon T^{-1/2}\Lambda B_{\Lambda}

(136)

and its radiation field along future null infinity vanishes identically. For this, it suffices to check that we can apply Proposition
4.9
for
\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}=0
, the source term
J:=J(\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}f)_{\nu}-J^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\nu}[\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}_{\infty}f_{\infty}]
,
a=0
,
\delta=1/4
and
B_{\mathrm{source}}\lesssim\epsilon T^{-1/2}\Lambda B_{\Lambda}
. In view of the strong
L^{2}_{x}
decay estimates satisfied by
J
given by Corollary
7.28
and Proposition
4.11
, we have indeed, for all
n\geq T
,

\forall\,t\geq T,\qquad\mathcal{E}^{K}\big[S(J,n)\big](t)\lesssim\langle t\rangle^{-\frac{1}{4}}\epsilon T^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda B_{\Lambda},

so that the assumption (
84
) is verified. Let us now prove the following result, which will also be useful for the treatment of the top order derivatives.

Proposition 8.8
.

There exists a solution
G^{\mathrm{vac}}
to the vacuum Maxwell equations such that, for any
|\gamma|\leq N
,

\forall\,t\in\mathbb{R}_{+},\quad\qquad\mathcal{E}^{K}\big[\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}G^{\mathrm{vac}}\big](t)=\big\|\underline{\alpha}^{\infty}_{\gamma}-\underline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\gamma}[f_{\infty}]\big\|^{2}_{\mathcal{S}^{K}_{\mathcal{I}^{+}}}\lesssim\Lambda,

and
\underline{\alpha}^{\infty}_{\gamma}-\underline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\gamma}[f_{\infty}]
is the radiation field of
\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}G^{\mathrm{vac}}
along future null infinity
\mathcal{I}^{+}
.

Proof.

Recall from Proposition
5.53
the properties satisfied by the radiation field
\underline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]
of
F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]
, which imply, for all
|\gamma|\leq N
,

\underline{\alpha}^{\infty}_{\gamma}-\underline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\gamma}[f_{\infty}]\in\mathcal{S}^{K}_{\mathcal{I}^{+}},\qquad\qquad\qquad\big\|\underline{\alpha}^{\infty}_{\gamma}-\underline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\gamma}[f_{\infty}]\big\|^{2}_{\mathcal{S}^{K}_{\mathcal{I}^{+}}}\lesssim\Lambda+\epsilon\leq 2\Lambda.

Consequently,
\underline{\alpha}^{\infty}-\underline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]\in\mathcal{S}^{\partial_{t},N}_{\mathcal{I}^{+}}
and Theorem
4.20
implies that there exists a unique solution
G^{\mathrm{vac}}
to the vacuum Maxwell equations such that

\forall\,|\gamma|=N,\qquad\quad\|\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}G^{\mathrm{vac}}(0,\cdot)\|_{\mathcal{S}^{\partial_{t},N}_{\{t=0\}}}=\big\|\underline{\alpha}^{\infty}_{\gamma}-\underline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\gamma}[f_{\infty}]\big\|_{\mathcal{S}^{\partial_{t},N}_{\mathcal{I}^{+}}}

and
\underline{\alpha}^{\infty}-\underline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]
is the radiation field of
G^{\mathrm{vac}}
along
\mathcal{I}^{+}
. By Proposition
4.3
,
\underline{\alpha}^{\infty}_{\gamma}-\underline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\gamma}[f_{\infty}]
is the radiation field of
\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}G^{\mathrm{vac}}
for any
|\gamma|\leq N
. The improved energy estimate given in the statement then follows from various applications of Theorem
4.20
, this time in the energy spaces
S^{K}_{\{t=0\}}
and
S^{K}_{\mathcal{I}^{+}}
, as well as the conservation of
\mathcal{E}^{K}[\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}G^{\mathrm{vac}}]
over time.

We are now able to control the weakly decaying derivatives. Fix then
|\gamma|\leq N-1
such that
\gamma_{T}=0
, that is
Z^{\gamma}
is only composed by homogeneous vector fields. By uniqueness considerations for the asymptotic Cauchy problem, we have
\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F^{\mathrm{new}}=\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}G^{\mathrm{vac}}+G^{\gamma}+\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]
. Thus,

\forall\,t\geq T,\qquad\qquad\mathcal{E}^{K}\big[\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}\big(F^{\mathrm{new}}-F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]\big)\big](t)\lesssim\Lambda+\epsilon T^{-1/2}\Lambda B_{\Lambda}

(137)

according to Proposition
8.8
and (
136
). Otherwise
\gamma_{T}\geq 1
so
J^{\mathrm{asymp}}[\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\gamma}f_{\infty}]=0
and the source terms of the Maxwell equations verified by
G^{\gamma}
and
\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F^{\mathrm{new}}
correspond. It leads us to consider a different solution to the vacuum Maxwell equations.

Proposition 8.9
.

There exists a unique solution
M^{\mathrm{vac}}
to the vacuum Maxwell equations such that,

•

for any
|\gamma|\leq N
and all
t\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
, we have
2\|\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}M^{\mathrm{vac}}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}=\|\underline{\alpha}_{\gamma}^{\infty}\|_{L^{2}_{u,\omega}}
,

•

\underline{\alpha}_{\gamma}^{\infty}
is the radiation field of
\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}M^{\mathrm{vac}}
along future null infinity
\mathcal{I}^{+}
,

•

if
\gamma_{T}\geq 1
, then
\mathcal{E}^{K}\big[\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}M^{\mathrm{vac}}\big](t)=\big\|\underline{\alpha}^{\infty}_{\gamma}\big\|^{2}_{\mathcal{S}^{K}_{\mathcal{I}^{+}}}\lesssim\Lambda
for all
t\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
.

Proof.

For the first two points, we use the property of the operator
\mathscr{F}^{+}|_{\mathcal{S}^{\partial_{t},N}_{\{t=0\}}}
given by Theorem
4.20
and the assumption (
25
) on
\underline{\alpha}^{\infty}
. For the last part, fix
|\gamma|\leq N
such that
\gamma_{T}\geq 1
and recall Definition
4.4
. Then,

\int_{\mathbb{R}_{u}}\underline{\alpha}^{\infty}_{\gamma}(u,\cdot)\mathrm{d}u=0,

so that, using again (
25
), we have
\underline{\alpha}^{\infty}_{\gamma}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{I}^{+}}^{K}
. It then remains to apply once again Theorem
4.20
and to use that
\mathcal{E}^{K}[\cdot]
is conserved for any sufficiently regular solution to the free Maxwell equations.

We then deduce that
\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F^{\mathrm{new}}=\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}M^{\mathrm{vac}}+G^{\gamma}
as well as

\forall\,t\geq T,\qquad\qquad\mathcal{E}^{K}\big[\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F^{\mathrm{new}}\big](t)\lesssim\Lambda+\epsilon T^{-1/2}\Lambda B_{\Lambda}.

(138)

Finally, by Corollary
5.51
, we have
\mathcal{E}^{K}\big[\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]\big]\lesssim\epsilon\leq\Lambda
. Thus, (
137
) is in fact verified for any
|\gamma|\leq N
and Proposition
8.7
holds.

8.3 The top order derivatives

We fix, for all this section,
|\gamma|\leq N-1
. In order to control sufficiently well
\nabla_{\partial_{x^{k}}}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F^{\mathrm{new}}
, for
|\gamma|=N-1
, through Proposition
4.9
, we will be interested in the first order spatial derivatives of the following solution to the Maxwell equations. Let
\tau_{0}\geq 4T
and
G^{\tau_{0}}
be uniquely defined as the solution to

\nabla^{\mu}G_{\mu\nu}^{\tau_{0}}=\chi\big(t/\tau_{0}\big)\Big[J\big(\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}f\big)_{\nu}-J^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\nu}\big[\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}_{\infty}f_{\infty}\big]\Big],\qquad\nabla^{\mu}{}^{*}\!G_{\mu\nu}^{\tau_{0}}=0,\qquad\qquad G^{\tau_{0}}(\tau_{0},\cdot)=0,

where
\chi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R},[0,1])
verifies
\chi(s)=1
for all
s\leq 1/4
and
\chi(s)=0
for all
s\geq 1/2
. Then, we will control the time derivative of
\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F^{\mathrm{new}}
through the Maxwell equations (see for instance (
2
)–(
3
)). For the purpose of controlling the spatial derivatives of
G^{\tau_{0}}
, we first determine their Glassey-Strauss decomposition, allowing for a gain of regularity which can be compared to the elliptic regularity in the context of the Vlasov-Poisson system.

8.3.1 Glassey-Strauss decomposition of the derivatives of the field

We fix
\tau_{0}\geq 4T
and we simplify the notations by dropping the superscript
\tau_{0}
of
G^{\tau_{0}}
. The decomposition of
\nabla_{\partial_{x^{k}}}G
that we will use requires to introduce several integral kernels. We point out that, apart for one of them, the only information that we will need on these kernels is an upper bound, so that their precise expression are only useful for the statement of the decomposition of the derivatives of
G
.

Definition 8.10
.

For any
(i,j)\in\llbracket 1,3\rrbracket^{2}
, we define the next smooth functions of the variables
(\omega,v)\in\mathbb{S}_{\omega}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
,

\mathbf{w}_{0i}(\omega,v)=-\mathbf{w}_{i0}(\omega,v):=\omega_{i}+\widehat{v}_{i},\qquad\mathbf{w}_{ij}(\omega,v):=\omega_{i}\widehat{v}_{j}-\omega_{j}\widehat{v}_{i}.

Let further, for any
k\in\llbracket 1,3\rrbracket
and
0\leq\mu,\,\nu\leq 3
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{a}_{\mu\nu}^{k}(\omega,v)

\displaystyle:=-3\frac{\mathbf{w}_{\mu\nu}(\omega,v)\omega_{k}}{\langle v\rangle^{4}(1+\omega\cdot\widehat{v})^{4}}-3\frac{\mathbf{w}_{\mu\nu}(\omega,v)\widehat{v}_{k}}{\langle v\rangle^{2}(1+\omega\cdot\widehat{v})^{3}}+\frac{\delta_{k\mu}\widehat{v}_{\nu}-\delta_{k\nu}\widehat{v}_{\mu}}{\langle v\rangle^{2}(1+\omega\cdot\widehat{v})^{2}},

\displaystyle\mathbf{b}^{k}_{\mu\nu}(\omega,v)

\displaystyle:=3\frac{\mathbf{w}_{\mu\nu}(\omega,v)\omega_{k}}{\langle v\rangle^{2}(1+\omega\cdot\widehat{v})^{3}}-2\frac{\mathbf{w}_{\mu\nu}(\omega,v)\widehat{v}_{k}}{(1+\omega\cdot\widehat{v})^{2}}-\frac{\delta_{k\mu}\widehat{v}_{\nu}-\delta_{k\nu}\widehat{v}_{\mu}}{1+\omega\cdot\widehat{v}},

\displaystyle\mathbf{c}^{k}_{\mu\nu}(\omega,v)

\displaystyle:=\frac{\omega_{k}\mathbf{w}_{\mu\nu}(\omega,v)}{(1+\omega\cdot\widehat{v})^{2}},\qquad\qquad\qquad\mathbf{d}^{k}_{\mu\nu}(\omega,v):=\frac{\omega_{k}\mathbf{w}_{\mu\nu}(\omega,v)}{\langle v\rangle^{2}(1+\omega\cdot\widehat{v})^{3}}.

All these quantities are antisymmetric in
(\mu,\nu)
.

Now, according to
[
8
, Corollary
5.8
]
, the following estimates hold.

Lemma 8.11
.

For any
1\leq k\leq 3
, we have,

\forall\,v\in\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v},\qquad\Big(\big|\mathbf{a}^{k}\big|+\big|\nabla_{v}\mathbf{a}^{k}\big|+\big|\nabla_{v}\mathbf{b}^{k}\big|+\big|\mathbf{c}^{k}\big|+\big|\nabla_{v}\mathbf{c}^{k}\big|+\big|\nabla_{v}\nabla_{v}\mathbf{c}^{k}\big|+\big|\mathbf{d}^{k}\big|+\big|\nabla_{v}\mathbf{d}^{k}\big|\Big)(\cdot,v)\lesssim\langle v\rangle^{3}.

Recall the quantities
\rho^{\beta}\big[f_{n},f_{\infty};\mathbf{q}\big](t,x,\omega)
introduced in Corollary
7.30
. We further lighten the notations by denoting the Lorentz force as

K^{j}(t,x,v):=\widehat{v}^{\mu}{F_{\mu}}^{j}(t,x),\qquad\qquad K_{\xi}^{j}(t,x,v):=\widehat{v}^{\mu}{\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\xi}}(F)_{\mu}}^{j}(t,x),\qquad 1\leq j\leq 3,\quad|\xi|\leq N.

(139)

Proposition 8.12
.

Let
1\leq k\leq 3
. Then, on
[T,\tau_{0}]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}
,
\nabla_{\partial_{x^{k}}}G
can be written as

4\pi\nabla_{\partial_{x^{k}}}G=G^{\mathrm{ver}}_{k}+G^{\chi^{\prime}}_{k}+G^{\chi^{\prime\prime}}_{k}+G^{TT}_{k}+G^{TS}_{k}+G^{SS}_{k},

where the six
2
-forms are defined as follows. Fix indices
0\leq\mu,\nu\leq 3
and let us lighten the presentation by using, in the integrals below, the shorthands

\omega:=\frac{y-x}{|y-x|},\qquad\qquad t_{y}:=t+|y-x|.

We have linear quantities,

•

the vertex term

G^{\mathrm{ver}}_{k,\,\mu\nu}(t,x):=\int_{\sigma\in\mathbb{S}^{2}}\chi\left(\frac{t}{\tau_{0}}\right)\rho^{\gamma}_{k}\big[f,f_{\infty};\mathbf{d}_{\mu\nu}\big](t,x,\sigma)\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}.

•

The most singular term,

G^{TT}_{k,\,\mu\nu}(t,x):=\int_{|y-x|\leq\tau_{0}-t}\chi\left(\frac{t+|y-x|}{\tau_{0}}\right)\rho^{\gamma}_{k}\big[f,f_{\infty};\mathbf{a}_{\mu\nu}\big](t+|y-x|,y,\omega)\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{|y-x|^{3}}

where the crucial identity
\int_{|\sigma|=1}\mathbf{a}_{\mu\nu}^{k}(\sigma,\widehat{v})\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}=0
holds for all
v\in\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
.

•

The terms arising from the cutoff function,

\displaystyle G^{\chi^{\prime}}_{k,\,\mu\nu}(t,x):=\frac{1}{\tau_{0}}\int_{|y-x|\leq\tau_{0}-t}\chi^{\prime}\left(\frac{t+|y-x|}{\tau_{0}}\right)\rho^{\gamma}_{k}\big[f,f_{\infty};\mathbf{b}_{\mu\nu}\big](t+|y-x|,y,\omega)\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{|y-x|^{2}},

\displaystyle G^{\chi^{\prime\prime}}_{k,\,\mu\nu}(t,x):=-\frac{1}{\tau_{0}^{2}}\int_{|y-x|\leq\tau_{0}-t}\chi^{\prime\prime}\left(\frac{t+|y-x|}{\tau_{0}}\right)\rho^{\gamma}_{k}\big[f,f_{\infty};\mathbf{c}_{\mu\nu}\big](t+|y-x|,y,\omega)\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{|y-x|}.

Next, we have the nonlinear quantities
G^{TS}_{k}
and
G^{SS}_{k}
. There exists integers
N^{\gamma}_{\xi,\beta},\,N^{\gamma}_{\xi,\kappa,\beta}\in\mathbb{Z}
such that

G^{\,TS}_{k,\,\mu\nu}(t,x):=-\sum_{|\xi|+|\beta|\leq|\gamma|}N^{\gamma}_{\xi,\kappa}\int_{|y-x|\leq\tau_{0}-t}\chi\left(\frac{t_{y}}{\tau_{0}}\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\nabla_{v}\mathbf{b}_{\mu\nu}^{k}(\omega,v)\cdot\big(\widehat{Z}^{\beta}f\,K_{\xi}\big)(t_{y},y,v)\frac{\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}y}{|y-x|^{2}}

and
G^{SS}_{k}
is the sum of the four following terms,

\displaystyle G^{SS,I}_{k,\,\mu\nu}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{|\xi|+|\kappa|+|\beta|\leq|\gamma|}N^{\gamma}_{\xi,\kappa,\beta}\,\int_{|y-x|\leq\tau_{0}-t}\chi\left(\frac{t_{y}}{\tau_{0}}\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\left[\nabla_{v}\big(\nabla_{v}\mathbf{c}_{\mu\nu}^{k}(\omega,\cdot)\cdot K_{\xi}\big)\cdot K_{\kappa}\widehat{Z}^{\beta}f\right](t_{y},y,v)\frac{\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}y}{|y-x|},

\displaystyle G^{SS,II}_{k,\,\mu\nu}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{|\xi|+|\beta|\leq|\gamma|}N^{\gamma}_{\xi,\beta}\,\int_{|y-x|\leq\tau_{0}-t}\chi\left(\frac{t_{y}}{\tau_{0}}\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\nabla_{v}\mathbf{c}_{\mu\nu}^{k}(\omega,v)\cdot\big(\mathbf{T}_{0}(K_{\xi})\widehat{Z}^{\beta}f\big)(t_{y},y,v)\frac{\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}y}{|y-x|},

\displaystyle G^{SS,III}_{k,\,\mu\nu}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{|\xi|+|\beta|\leq|\gamma|}N^{\gamma}_{\xi,\beta}\,\int_{|y-x|\leq\tau_{0}-t}\chi\left(\frac{t_{y}}{\tau_{0}}\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\mathbf{c}_{\mu\nu}^{k}(\omega,v)\frac{\delta_{j}^{i}-\widehat{v}^{i}\widehat{v}_{j}}{v^{0}}\partial_{x^{i}}\big(K_{\xi}^{j}\widehat{Z}^{\beta}f\big)(t_{y},y,v)\frac{\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}y}{|y-x|},

\displaystyle G^{SS,IV}_{k,\,\mu\nu}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{|\xi|+|\beta|\leq|\gamma|}\frac{N^{\gamma}_{\xi,\beta}}{\tau_{0}}\int_{|y-x|\leq\tau_{0}-t}\chi^{\prime}\left(\frac{t_{y}}{\tau_{0}}\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\nabla_{v}\mathbf{c}_{\mu\nu}^{k}(\omega,v)\cdot\big(\widehat{Z}^{\beta}f\,K_{\xi}\big)(t_{y},y,v)\frac{\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}y}{|y-x|}.

Proof.

Let
k\in\llbracket 1,3\rrbracket
,
|\gamma|\leq N-1
and consider a sufficiently regular function
h:[T,\tau_{0}]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\to\mathbb{R}
as well as
H
satisfying

\nabla^{\mu}H_{\mu\nu}=\chi\big(t/\tau_{0}\big)J(h)_{\nu},\qquad\nabla^{\mu}{}^{*}\!H_{\mu\nu}=0,\qquad\qquad H(\tau_{0},\cdot)=0.

In order to fit with the framework of Glassey-Strauss, recall further the electric part
E^{i}:=H_{0i}
and the magnetic part
B^{i}=-\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon^{ijk}H_{jk}
of
H
. We apply
5
5

5

See also the original version of the result,
[
32
, Theorem
4
]
.

[
28
, Theorem
5.4.1
]
to
(\chi(t/\tau_{0})h,E,B)
, where we denote abusively
(t,x,v)\mapsto\chi(t/\tau_{0})
by
\chi(t/\tau_{0})
. This yields, as
H
and its derivatives vanish at
t=\tau_{0}
,

\nabla_{\partial_{x^{k}}}H_{\mu\nu}=H^{\mathrm{ver}}_{k,\,\mu\nu}+H^{TT}_{k,\,\mu\nu}+\bar{A}^{\,TS}_{k,\,\mu\nu}+\bar{A}^{\,SS}_{k,\,\mu\nu},

where

\displaystyle H^{\mathrm{ver}}_{k,\,\mu\nu}(t,x)

\displaystyle:=\int_{\sigma\in\mathbb{S}^{2}}\chi\left(\frac{t}{\tau_{0}}\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\mathbf{d}^{k}_{\mu\nu}(\sigma,v)h(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}},

\displaystyle H^{TT}_{k,\,\mu\nu}(t,x)

\displaystyle:=\int_{|y-x|\leq\tau_{0}-t}\chi\left(\frac{t+|y-x|}{\tau_{0}}\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\mathbf{a}^{k}_{\mu\nu}(\omega,v)h(t+|y-x|,y,v)\frac{\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}y}{|y-x|^{3}},

\displaystyle\bar{A}^{\,TS}_{k,\,\mu\nu}

\displaystyle:=\int_{|y-x|\leq\tau_{0}-t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\mathbf{b}^{k}_{\mu\nu}(\omega,v)\mathbf{T}_{0}\big(\chi(t/\tau_{0})h\big)(t+|y-x|,y,v)\frac{\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}y}{|y-x|^{2}},

\displaystyle\bar{A}^{\,SS}_{k,\,\mu\nu}

\displaystyle:=-\int_{|y-x|\leq\tau_{0}-t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\mathbf{c}^{k}_{\mu\nu}(\omega,v)\mathbf{T}_{0}\mathbf{T}_{0}\big(\chi(t/\tau_{0})h\big)(t+|y-x|,y,v)\frac{\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}y}{|y-x|},

as well as
\int_{|\sigma|=1}\mathbf{a}^{k}_{\mu\nu}(\sigma,\widehat{v})\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}=0
. We now apply this result for

h:=\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}f(t,x,v)-h_{n},\qquad\qquad h_{n}(t,x,v):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}\big[|v^{0}|^{5}\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\gamma}f_{\infty}\Big](z,v)\mathrm{d}z\psi_{n}(x-t\widehat{v}),

where
(\psi_{n})_{n\geq 1}
is a mollifier. Then, recall from Section
5.1
that
(h_{n})_{n\geq 1}
converges to
f^{\mathrm{sing}}\big[\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\gamma}f_{\infty}\big]
as
n\to\infty
and the corresponding convergence properties of its velocity averages (see in particular Lemma
5.6
). As
\mathbf{T}_{0}(h_{n})=0
, we obtain by letting
n\to+\infty
,

H^{\mathrm{ver}}_{k}=G^{\mathrm{ver}}_{k},\qquad H^{TT}_{k}=G^{TT}_{k},\qquad\bar{A}^{\,TS}_{k,\,\mu\nu}=G^{\chi^{\prime}}_{k}+A^{\,TS}_{k,\,\mu\nu},\qquad\bar{A}^{\,SS}_{k,\,\mu\nu}=G^{\chi^{\prime\prime}}_{k}+A^{\,SS}_{k,\,\mu\nu},

with, using the shorthand
t_{y}=t+|y-x|
,

\displaystyle A^{\,TS}_{k,\,\mu\nu}

\displaystyle:=\int_{|y-x|\leq\tau_{0}-t}\chi\left(\frac{t_{y}}{\tau_{0}}\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\mathbf{b}^{k}_{\mu\nu}(\omega,v)\mathbf{T}_{0}\big(\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}f\big)(t_{y},y,v)\frac{\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}y}{|y-x|^{2}},

\displaystyle A^{\,SS}_{k,\,\mu\nu}

\displaystyle:=-\int_{|y-x|\leq\tau_{0}-t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\mathbf{c}^{k}_{\mu\nu}(\omega,v)\bigg[\chi\left(\frac{t_{y}}{\tau_{0}}\right)\mathbf{T}_{0}\mathbf{T}_{0}\big(\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}f\big)(t_{y},y,v)+\frac{1}{\tau_{0}}\chi^{\prime}\!\left(\frac{t_{y}}{\tau_{0}}\right)\mathbf{T}_{0}\big(\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}f\big)(t_{y},y,v)\bigg]\frac{\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}y}{|y-x|}.

Recall now that the Lorentz force (
139
