) is divergence free, that is
\nabla_{v}\cdot K_{\xi}=0
for any
|\xi|\leq N
. The commutation formula of Proposition
2.8
then yields

\displaystyle\mathbf{T}_{0}\big(\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}f\big)=-\nabla_{v}\cdot\big(K\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}f\big)+\sum_{|\beta|\leq|\gamma|-1}\sum_{|\xi|+|\beta|\leq|\gamma|}C^{\gamma}_{\xi,\beta}\,\nabla_{v}\cdot\big(K_{\xi}\,\widehat{Z}^{\beta}f\big).

(140)

We set
N^{\gamma}_{\xi,\beta}=-1
if
|\xi|+|\beta|=0
and
N^{\gamma}_{\xi,\beta}=C^{\gamma}_{\xi,\beta}
otherwise. We get
A^{\,TS}_{k}=G^{\,TS}_{k}
by performing integration by parts in
v
. Similarly, the part of
A^{\,SS}_{k}
related to
\chi^{\prime}
gives rise to
G^{\,SS,IV}_{k}
. In order to deal with the last part of
A^{\,SS}_{k}
, we observe
[\mathbf{T}_{0},\partial_{v^{j}}]=-\partial_{v^{j}}(\widehat{v}^{i})\partial_{x^{i}}
, so that

\displaystyle\mathbf{T}_{0}\mathbf{T}_{0}\big(\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}f\big)=\sum_{|\xi|+|\beta|\leq|\gamma|}N^{\gamma}_{\xi,\beta}\bigg[\nabla_{v}\cdot\Big(K_{\xi}\,\mathbf{T}_{0}(\widehat{Z}^{\beta}f)\Big)+\nabla_{v}\cdot\Big(\mathbf{T}_{0}\big(K_{\xi}\big)\,\widehat{Z}^{\beta}f\Big)-\frac{\delta_{j}^{i}-\widehat{v}^{i}\widehat{v}_{j}}{v^{0}}\partial_{x^{i}}\Big(K_{\xi}^{j}\,\widehat{Z}^{\beta}f\Big)\bigg].

The last term generates
G^{SS,III}_{k}
and the second one yields, by integration by parts in
v
,
G^{SS,II}_{k}
. Finally, we transform the first term using (
140
) for
\gamma=\beta
. Performing twice integration by parts in
v
provides the quantity
G^{SS,I}_{k}
, for certain integers
N^{\gamma}_{\xi,\kappa,\beta}
.

8.3.2 Estimates for the derivatives of
G

Our analysis of the terms of the Glassey-Strauss decomposition of
\nabla_{\partial_{x^{k}}}G
will rely on the following result. Recall the shorthand
t_{y}:=t+|y-x|
.

Lemma 8.13
.

Let
B>0
and
:\psi:[T,\tau_{0}]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}\to\mathbb{R}
be a function such that

\forall\,(s,\omega)\in[T,\tau_{0}]\times\mathbb{S}^{2},\qquad\qquad\big\|\langle s+|\cdot|\rangle^{\frac{5}{2}}\,\psi(s,\cdot,\omega)\big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})}^{2}\leq B\,\langle s\rangle^{\frac{1}{8}}.

Let further
p\in\{1,2,3\}
as well as
b_{p}
such that
b_{1}=b_{2}=0
and
b_{3}=1
. Then, for all
t\in[T,\tau_{0}]
,

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\langle t+|x|\rangle^{4}\bigg|\int_{b_{p}\leq|y-x|\leq\tau_{0}-t}\psi\bigg(t_{y},y,\frac{y-x}{|y-x|}\bigg)\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{\langle t_{y}\rangle^{3-p}\,|y-x|^{p}}\bigg|^{2}\mathrm{d}x\lesssim\frac{B}{\sqrt{t}}.

Proof.

Let us denote
\frac{y-x}{|y-x|}
by
\overline{\omega}
. The Cauchy-Schwarz inequality in
y
gives

\bigg|\int_{b_{p}\leq|y-x|\leq\tau_{0}-t}\psi(t_{y},y,\overline{\omega})\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{\langle t_{y}\rangle^{3-p}\,|y-x|^{p}}\bigg|^{2}\leq\int_{|y-x|\leq\tau_{0}-t}\langle t_{y}+|y|\rangle^{5}\big|\psi(t_{y},y,\overline{\omega})\big|^{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{\langle t_{y}\rangle^{3-p}\,|y-x|^{p+\frac{1}{4}}}\,\mathcal{Q}^{p}_{t,x},

where

\mathcal{Q}^{p}_{t,x}:=\int_{b_{p}\leq|y-x|\leq\tau_{0}-t}\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{\langle t+|y-x|+|y|\rangle^{5}\,\langle t+|y-x|\rangle^{3-p}\,|y-x|^{p-\frac{1}{4}}}.

Now, we use
|y|/2\geq|x|/2-|y-x|/2
and we parameterize the domain of integration, which is a cone, by
(s,\omega)\mapsto(t+s,x+s\omega)
. As
s^{2}\langle t+s\rangle^{p-3}s^{-p+1/4}\lesssim\langle s\rangle^{-3/4}
for
s\geq b_{p}
, one obtains

\mathcal{Q}^{p}_{t,x}\leq 4\pi\int_{s=0}^{\tau_{0}-t}\frac{\mathrm{d}s}{\langle t+|x|+s\rangle^{5}\langle s\rangle^{\frac{3}{4}}}\lesssim\frac{1}{\langle t+|x|\rangle^{4+\frac{3}{4}}}\lesssim\frac{1}{\langle t+|x|\rangle^{4}\,t^{\frac{3}{4}}}.

Next, we parameterize again the cone by
(s,\omega)\mapsto(t+s,x+s\omega)
. Hence, applying Fubini’s theorem and performing then the change of variables
X=x+s\omega
, we get

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\int_{b_{p}\leq|y-x|\leq\tau_{0}-t}\langle t_{y}+|y|\rangle^{5}\big|\psi(t_{y},y,\overline{\omega})\big|^{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{\langle t_{y}\rangle^{3-p}\,|y-x|^{p+\frac{1}{4}}}\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=\int_{s=b_{p}}^{\tau_{0}-t}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{X}}\langle t+s+|X|\rangle^{5}\big|\psi(t+s,X,\omega)\big|^{2}\mathrm{d}X\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\frac{\mathrm{d}s}{\langle s\rangle\,|s|^{\frac{1}{4}}}\leq 4\pi B\int_{s=b_{p}}^{\tau_{0}-t}\frac{\langle t+s\rangle^{\frac{1}{8}}\mathrm{d}s}{\langle s\rangle\,|s|^{\frac{1}{4}}}\lesssim B\,t^{\frac{1}{8}}.

We start by dealing with the linear terms in the decomposition of
\nabla_{\partial_{x^{k}}}G
.

Proposition 8.14
.

Let
1\leq k\leq 3
. Then,

\forall\,t\in[T,\tau_{0}],\qquad\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\langle t+|x|\rangle^{4}\Big(\big|G^{\mathrm{ver}}_{k}\big|^{2}+\big|G^{\chi^{\prime}}_{k}\big|^{2}+\big|G^{\chi^{\prime\prime}}_{k}\big|^{2}+\big|G^{TT}_{k}\big|^{2}\Big)(t,x)\mathrm{d}x\lesssim\frac{\epsilon\Lambda B_{\Lambda}}{\sqrt{t}}.

Proof.

The estimate for
G^{\mathrm{ver}}_{k}
simply follows from the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality in the variables
\sigma\in\mathbb{S}^{2}
and the
L^{2}_{x}
decay estimate of Corollary
7.30
. For the other terms, in order to apply the previous Lemma
8.13
, we will use that

\forall\,(s,\omega)\in[T,\tau_{0}]\times\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega},\qquad\big\|\langle s+|\cdot|\rangle^{\frac{5}{2}}\,\rho^{\gamma}_{k}[f,f_{\infty};\mathbf{q}_{\mu\nu}](s,\cdot,\omega)\big\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}\lesssim\epsilon\Lambda B_{\Lambda}\,\langle s\rangle^{\frac{1}{8}},

for any integral kernel
\mathbf{q}\in\{\mathbf{a},\mathbf{b},\mathbf{c}\}
. This ensues from Corollary
7.30
. We treat
G^{\chi^{\prime}}_{k}
as well as
G^{\chi^{\prime\prime}}_{k}
by noticing that
\tau_{0}^{-1}\lesssim\langle t_{y}\rangle^{-1}
on the domain of integration and by applying Lemma
8.13
, for
p=1
and
p=2
. In order to deal with the singular factor
|y-x|^{-3}
of the integrand in the term
G^{TT}_{k}
, we write

\displaystyle G^{TT}_{k}(t,x)

\displaystyle=G^{TT,[0,1]}_{k}(t,x)+G^{TT,[1,\tau_{0}-t]}_{k}(t,x),

\displaystyle G^{TT,[a,b]}_{k}(t,x)

\displaystyle:=\int_{a\leq|y-x|\leq b}\chi\left(\frac{t_{y}}{\tau_{0}}\right)\rho^{\gamma}_{k}\big[f,f_{\infty};\mathbf{a}_{\mu\nu}\big](t_{y},y,\omega)\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{|y-x|^{3}}.

Note that we can assume
\tau_{0}-t\geq 1
since the integrand vanishes if
\tau_{0}-t\leq 1
in view of the support of
\chi
and
\tau_{0}\geq 4
. Then,
G^{TT,[1,\tau_{0}-t]}_{k}(t,x)
can be estimated by applying again Lemma
8.13
, for
p=3
. Next, since
\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\sigma}}\mathbf{a}^{k}_{\mu\nu}(\sigma,\cdot)\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\sigma}}=0
, there holds

\big|G^{TT,[0,1]}_{k}\big|(t,x)\leq\int_{s=0}^{1}\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\rho^{\gamma}_{k}\big[f,f_{\infty};\mathbf{a}_{\mu\nu}\big](t+s,x+s\omega,\omega)-\rho^{\gamma}_{k}\big[f_{n},f_{\infty};\mathbf{a}_{\mu\nu}\big](t+s,x,\omega)\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\bigg|\frac{\mathrm{d}s}{s}.

We apply the fundamental theorem of calculus
\tau\mapsto\rho^{\gamma}_{k}\big[f,f_{\infty};\mathbf{a}_{\mu\nu}\big](t+s,x+\tau s\omega,\omega)
in order to compensate the singular factor
s^{-1}
. We get

\displaystyle\big|G^{TT,[0,1]}_{k}\big|^{2}(t,x)

\displaystyle\leq\bigg|\int_{s=0}^{1}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\int_{\tau=0}^{1}\Big|\nabla_{x}\rho^{\gamma}_{k}\big[f,f_{\infty};\mathbf{a}_{\mu\nu}\big](t+s,x+\tau\omega,\omega)\Big|\mathrm{d}\tau\frac{s|\omega|\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\mathrm{d}s}{s}\bigg|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 4\pi\int_{s=0}^{1}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\int_{\tau=0}^{1}\Big|\nabla_{x}\rho^{\gamma}_{k}\big[f,f_{\infty};\mathbf{a}_{\mu\nu}\big](t+s,x+\tau\omega,\omega)\Big|^{2}\mathrm{d}\tau\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\mathrm{d}s.

As
\langle t+|x|\rangle\lesssim\langle t+s+|x+\tau\omega|\rangle
for all
(s,\tau)\in[0,1]^{2}
, we obtain from Fubini’s theorem and the change of variables
X=x+\tau\omega
,

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\langle t+|x|\rangle^{4}\big|G^{TT,[0,1]}_{k}\big|^{2}(t,x)\mathrm{d}x\lesssim\sup_{(s,\omega)\in[0,1]\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{X}}\langle t+s+|X|\rangle^{4}\Big|\nabla_{x}\rho^{\gamma}_{k}\big[f,f_{\infty};\mathbf{a}_{\mu\nu}\big](t+s,X,\omega)\Big|^{2}\mathrm{d}X.

It turns out that each of the two terms defining
\nabla_{x}\rho^{\gamma}_{k}\big[f,f_{\infty};\mathbf{a}_{\mu\nu}\big]
are decaying sufficiently fast in order to bound them separatly. Consider
T\leq\tau\leq\tau_{0}
,
X\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
,
\omega\in\mathbb{S}^{2}
as well as
1\leq i\leq 3
and remark that

\Big|\partial_{x^{i}}\rho^{\gamma}_{k}\big[f,f_{\infty};\mathbf{a}_{\mu\nu}\big]\Big|(\tau,X,\omega)\leq\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\mathbf{a}_{\mu\nu}^{k}(\omega,v)\partial_{x^{i}}\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}f(\tau,X,v)\mathrm{d}v\bigg|+\bigg|\partial_{x^{i}}J^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{0}\big[\mathbf{a}_{\mu\nu}^{k}(\omega,\cdot)\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}_{\infty}f_{\infty}\big](\tau,X)\bigg|.

Since
|\mathbf{a}^{k}_{\mu\nu}(\cdot,v)|+|v^{0}\nabla_{v}\mathbf{a}^{k}_{\mu\nu}(\cdot,v)|\lesssim\langle v\rangle^{4}
, we get by applying Corollary
2.19
,

\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\mathbf{a}_{\mu\nu}^{k}(\omega,v)\partial_{x^{i}}\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}f(\tau,X,v)\mathrm{d}v\bigg|\lesssim\sum_{|\beta|\leq|\gamma|+1}\frac{1}{\langle\tau+|X|\rangle}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\langle v\rangle^{6}\,\langle X-\tau\widehat{v}\rangle\,\big|\widehat{Z}^{\beta}f\big|(\tau,X,v)\mathrm{d}v.

Similarly, by Lemma
5.13
and the assumptions on
f_{\infty}
, we have

\bigg|\partial_{x^{i}}J^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{0}\big[\mathbf{a}_{\mu\nu}^{k}(\omega,\cdot)\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}_{\infty}f_{\infty}\big](\tau,X)\bigg|\lesssim\frac{1}{\tau^{4}}\mathds{1}_{|X|<\tau}\sqrt{\epsilon}\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\langle\tau+|X|\rangle^{4}}.

Consequently,

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\langle t+|x|\rangle^{4}\big|G^{TT,[0,1]}_{k}\big|^{2}(t,x)\mathrm{d}x\lesssim\sup_{s\in[0,1]}\sup_{|\beta|\leq N}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{X}}\langle t+s+|X|\rangle^{2}\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\langle v\rangle^{6}\,\langle X-\tau\widehat{v}\rangle\,\big|\widehat{Z}^{\beta}f\big|(\tau,X,v)\mathrm{d}v\bigg|^{2}\mathrm{d}X+\frac{\epsilon}{\langle t\rangle}

and it remains to use the
L^{2}_{x}
estimate of Corollary
7.31
.

We now focus on the nonlinear terms.

Proposition 8.15
.

For any
1\leq k\leq 3
, we have

\forall\,t\in[T,\tau_{0}],\qquad\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\langle t+|x|\rangle^{4}\big|G^{\,TS}_{k}(t,x)\big|^{2}\mathrm{d}x\lesssim\frac{\epsilon\Lambda B_{\Lambda}}{\sqrt{t}}.

Proof.

Recall from Lemma
8.11
that
|\nabla_{v}\mathbf{b}^{k}|(\cdot,v)\lesssim\langle v\rangle^{3}
. As
|\chi|\leq 1
,
\big|G^{\,TS}_{k}(t,x)\big|
is bounded by a linear combination of the following terms,

\mathfrak{B}^{\xi,\beta}_{t,x}:=\int_{|y-x|\leq\tau_{0}-t}\left|\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\xi}}F\right|(t_{y},y)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\langle v\rangle^{3}\big|\widehat{Z}^{\beta}f\big|(t_{y},y,v)\mathrm{d}v\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{|y-x|^{2}},

where
|\xi|+|\beta|\leq|\gamma|
. Assume first that
|\xi|\leq N-3
, so that, by (
111
) and Lemma
2.9
,

\left|\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\xi}}F\right|(t_{y},y)\lesssim\sqrt{\Lambda}\,\langle t_{y}+|y|\rangle^{-1}\,\langle t_{y}-|y|\rangle^{-1}\lesssim\sqrt{\Lambda}\,\langle t_{y}+|y|\rangle^{-2}\,\langle y-t_{y}\widehat{v}\rangle\,\langle v\rangle^{2}.

We then deduce, by combining the previous Lemma
8.13
with the
L^{2}_{x}
estimate of Corollary
7.31
that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\langle t+|x|

\displaystyle\rangle^{4}\big|\mathfrak{B}^{\xi,\beta}_{t,x}\big|^{2}\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\langle t+|x|\rangle^{4}\bigg|\int_{|y-x|\leq\tau_{0}-t}\frac{\sqrt{\Lambda}}{\langle t_{y}+|y|\rangle}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\langle v\rangle^{5}\langle y-t_{y}\widehat{v}\rangle\big|\widehat{Z}^{\beta}f\big|(t_{y},y,v)\mathrm{d}v\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{\langle t_{y}\rangle\,|y-x|^{2}}\bigg|^{2}\mathrm{d}x\lesssim\frac{\epsilon\Lambda B_{\Lambda}}{\langle t\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

Assume now that
|\xi|\geq N-2
, so that
|\beta|\leq 1
and, according to Corollary
7.31
,

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\langle v\rangle^{3}\big|\widehat{Z}^{\beta}f\big|(t_{y},y,v)\mathrm{d}v\lesssim\sqrt{\epsilon B_{\Lambda}}\,\frac{|\max(t_{y}-|y|,1)|^{\frac{3}{2}}}{\langle t_{y}+|y|\rangle^{\frac{9}{2}}}\log^{6N}(t_{y})\lesssim\sqrt{\epsilon B_{\Lambda}}\,\frac{|\max(t_{y}-|y|,1)|^{\frac{3}{2}}}{\langle t_{y}+|y|\rangle^{\frac{9}{2}}}\langle t_{y}\rangle^{\frac{1}{10}}.

Then, with
\psi(s,z):=\langle s\rangle^{\frac{1}{10}}|\max(s-|z|,1)|^{\frac{3}{2}}\,\langle s+|z|\rangle^{-\frac{7}{2}}\left|\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\xi}}F\right|(s,z)
, we have

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\langle t+|x|\rangle^{4}\big|\mathfrak{B}^{\xi,\beta}_{t,x}\big|^{2}\mathrm{d}x\lesssim\epsilon B_{\Lambda}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\langle t+|x|\rangle^{4}\bigg|\int_{|y-x|\leq\tau_{0}-t}\psi(t_{y},y)\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{\langle t_{y}\rangle\,|y-x|^{2}}\bigg|^{2}\mathrm{d}x.

It then remains to apply Lemma
8.13
and to prove

\sup_{T\leq s\leq\tau_{0}}\big\|\langle s+|\cdot|\rangle^{\frac{5}{2}}\,\psi(s,\cdot)\big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})}^{2}\lesssim\Lambda\langle s\rangle^{\frac{1}{8}}.

For this, note that
\langle s+|z|\rangle^{-1}|\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\xi}}F|(s,z)\lesssim\sup_{Z\in\mathbb{K}}|\nabla_{t,x}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\kappa}}F|(s,z)
, where
Z^{\xi}=ZZ^{\kappa}
, and use (
114
).

Finally, we deal with
G^{SS}_{k}
.

Proposition 8.16
.

For any
1\leq k\leq 3
, we have

\forall\,t\in[T,\tau_{0}],\qquad\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\langle t+|x|\rangle^{4}\big|G^{\,SS}_{k}(t,x)\big|^{2}\mathrm{d}x\lesssim\frac{\epsilon\Lambda^{2}B_{\Lambda}}{\sqrt{t}}.

Proof.

Let us first prove that
G^{\,SS}_{k}(t,x)
can be bounded by a linear combination of terms of the form

\sqrt{\Lambda}\int_{|y-x|\leq\tau_{0}-t}\Big(\langle t_{y}\rangle\big|\nabla_{t,x}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\xi}}F(t_{y},y)\big|+\big|\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\xi}}F(t_{y},y)\big|\Big)\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\langle v\rangle^{5}\,\langle y-t_{y}\widehat{v}\rangle\,\big|\widehat{Z}^{\beta}f\big|(t_{y},y,v)\mathrm{d}v\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{\langle t_{y}\rangle\,|y-x|},

where
|\xi|+|\beta|\leq N
and
|\xi|\leq N-1
. Indeed, all the integral kernels and their derivatives are bounded by
\langle v\rangle^{3}
according to Lemma
8.11
. Moreover,

•

we control
G^{\,SS,IV}_{k}(t,x)
by using
\tau_{0}^{-1}\leq t_{y}^{-1}
.

•

For
G^{\,SS,I}_{k}(t,x)
, we remark that
|\xi|+|\kappa|\leq N-1
implies that either
K_{\xi}(t_{y},y,v)
or
K_{\kappa}(t_{y},y,v)
can be estimated pointwise by
\sqrt{\Lambda}\,\langle t_{y}\rangle^{-1}
.

•

We control
G^{\,SS,II}_{k}(t,x)
as well as the terms obtained when
\partial_{x^{i}}
is applied to the Lorentz force in
G^{\,SS,III}_{k}(t,x)
by simply using the triangle inequality for integrals.

•

Finally, when
\partial_{x^{i}}
is applied to the distribution function in
G^{\,SS,III}_{k}(t,x)
, we use Corollary
2.19
.

Fix now
|\xi|+|\beta|\leq N
and assume first that
|\xi|\leq N-4
. Then, using first the pointwise decay estimates (
111
) together with Lemma
3.9
and then Lemma
2.9
,

\langle t_{y}\rangle\,\left|\nabla_{t,x}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\xi}}F\right|(t_{y},y)+\left|\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\xi}}F\right|(t_{y},y)\lesssim\sqrt{\Lambda}\,\langle t_{y}-|y|\rangle^{-2}\lesssim\sqrt{\Lambda}\,\langle t_{y}+|y|\rangle^{-2}\,\langle y-t_{y}\widehat{v}\rangle^{2}\,\langle v\rangle^{4}.

Otherwise we write
|\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\xi}}F|(t_{y},y)\lesssim\langle t_{y}+|y|\rangle|\nabla_{t,x}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\kappa}}F|(t_{y},y)
for
Z^{\xi}=ZZ^{\kappa}
. Moreover, we have
|\beta|\leq 3
so Corollary
7.31
provides

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\langle v\rangle^{5}\,\langle y-t_{y}\widehat{v}\rangle\,\big|\widehat{Z}^{\beta}f\big|(t_{y},y,v)\mathrm{d}v\lesssim\sqrt{\epsilon B_{\Lambda}}\,\frac{|\max(t_{y}-|y|,1)|^{\frac{3}{2}}}{\langle t_{y}+|y|\rangle^{\frac{9}{2}}}\langle t_{y}\rangle^{\frac{1}{10}}.

Consequently, we have

\displaystyle\big|G^{\,SS}_{k}(t,x)\big|\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{|\beta|\leq N}\int_{|y-x|\leq\tau_{0}-t}\frac{\Lambda}{\langle t_{y}+|y|\rangle}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\langle v\rangle^{9}\,\langle y-t_{y}\widehat{v}\rangle^{3}\,\big|\widehat{Z}^{\beta}f\big|(t_{y},y,v)\mathrm{d}v\frac{\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}x}{\langle t_{y}\rangle^{2}\,|y-x|}

\displaystyle+\sum_{|\xi|\leq N-1}\sqrt{\epsilon\Lambda B_{\Lambda}}\int_{|y-x|\leq\tau_{0}-t}\langle t_{y}\rangle^{\frac{1}{10}}\frac{|\max(t_{y}-|y|,1)|^{\frac{3}{2}}}{\langle t_{y}+|y|\rangle^{\frac{5}{2}}}\big|\nabla_{t,x}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\xi}}F\big|(t_{y},y)\frac{\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}x}{\langle t_{y}\rangle^{2}|y-x|}.

It remains to use Lemma
8.13
as well as the
L^{2}_{x}
estimates given by Corollary
7.31
and (
114
).

8.3.3 Improved estimates for the top order derivatives

In order to conclude the proof of Proposition
8.2
, it remains to establish that, if
D
is chosen large enough and if
\Lambda^{2}B_{\Lambda}\epsilon\log^{-1}T
is small enough, then

\hskip-25.60747pt\forall\,|\gamma|=N-1,\qquad\qquad\sup_{t\geq T}\,\mathcal{E}^{K,1}\big[\nabla_{\partial_{x^{\lambda}}}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}\big(F^{\mathrm{new}}-F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]\big)\big](t)\leq\frac{D\Lambda}{8},

(141)

for any
0\leq\lambda\leq 3
. We first deal with the case
\lambda\neq 0
and we then fix
|\gamma|=N-1
as well as
1\leq k\leq 3
. Hence, Combining the Glassey-Strauss decomposition of
G^{n}
given by Proposition
8.12
with Propositions
8.14
–
8.16
, we obtain

\forall\,n\geq 4T,\;\forall\,t\geq T,\qquad\mathcal{E}^{K,1}\big[\nabla_{\partial_{x^{k}}}G^{n}\big](t)\lesssim\epsilon T^{-\frac{1}{4}}\Lambda^{2}B_{\Lambda}\langle t\rangle^{-\frac{1}{4}}.

We claim there exists
G^{\gamma,k}
such that

\hskip-17.07164pt\nabla^{\mu}G_{\mu\nu}^{\gamma,k}=\partial_{x^{k}}J(\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}f)_{\nu}-\partial_{x^{k}}J^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\nu}\big[\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}_{\infty}f_{\infty}\big],\qquad\nabla^{\mu}{}^{*}\!G_{\mu\nu}^{\gamma,k}=0;\qquad\quad\sup_{t\geq T}\mathcal{E}^{K,1}[G^{\gamma,k}](t)\lesssim\frac{\epsilon\Lambda^{2}B_{\Lambda}}{T^{\frac{1}{4}}}

(142)

and its radiation field along
\mathcal{I}^{+}
is identically zero. This is obtained by applying Proposition
4.9
for
\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}=0
, the source term
J:=\partial_{x^{k}}J(\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}f)_{\nu}-\partial_{x^{k}}J^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\nu}[\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}_{\infty}f_{\infty}]
,
a=1
,
\delta=1/4
and
B_{\mathrm{source}}\lesssim\epsilon T^{-1/4}\Lambda^{2}B_{\Lambda}
. For this, note that
S(J,n)=\nabla_{\partial_{x^{k}}}G^{n}
, so that (
84
) is verified.

Recall from Propositions
8.8
–
8.9
the solutions to the vacuum Maxwell equations
G^{\mathrm{vac}}
and
M^{\mathrm{vac}}
. Consider first the case
\gamma_{T}=0
, so that

\nabla_{\partial_{x^{k}}}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F^{\mathrm{new}}=G^{\gamma,k}+\nabla_{\partial_{x^{k}}}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}G^{\mathrm{vac}}+\nabla_{\partial_{x^{k}}}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]

and (
141
) holds for this multi-index
\gamma
and
\lambda=k
in view of Proposition
8.8
, Lemma
3.9
as well as (
142
). Otherwise
\gamma_{T}\geq 1
, so
J^{\mathrm{asymp}}[\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\gamma}f_{\infty}]=0
and
\nabla_{\partial_{x^{k}}}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F^{\mathrm{new}}=G^{\gamma,k}+\nabla_{\partial_{x^{k}}}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}M^{\mathrm{vac}}
. Proposition
8.9
, combined with Lemma
3.9
, and (
142
) then imply

\forall\,t\geq T,\qquad\qquad\mathcal{E}^{K,1}\big[\nabla_{\partial_{x^{k}}}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F^{\mathrm{new}}\big](t)\lesssim\Lambda+\epsilon T^{-\frac{1}{4}}\Lambda^{2}B_{\Lambda}.

(143)

Finally, Lemma
3.9
and Corollary
5.51
imply that
\mathcal{E}^{K,1}\big[\nabla_{\partial_{x^{k}}}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]\big](t)\lesssim\epsilon
if
\gamma_{T}\geq 1
, so that (
141
) holds for
\lambda=k
in this case as well.

It remains to treat the case
\lambda=0
. For this, note that if
H
is solution to the Maxwell equations with source term
J
, that is
\nabla^{\mu}H_{\mu\nu}=J_{\nu}
and
\nabla^{\mu}{}^{*}\!H_{\mu\nu}=0
, then

\hskip-17.07164pt\partial_{t}H_{0i}=\partial_{x^{1}}H_{1i}+\partial_{x^{2}}H_{2i}+\partial_{x^{3}}H_{3i}-J_{i},\qquad\partial_{t}H_{ij}=\partial_{x^{i}}H_{0j}+\partial_{x^{j}}H_{i0},\qquad\qquad 1\leq i,\,j\leq 3.

(144)

We then estimate the time derivative by using the commutation formula of Proposition
5.16
and by controlling the source term through Corollary
7.28
if
\gamma_{T}=0
or Corollaries
2.19
and
7.31
if
\gamma_{T}\geq 1
.

9
Proof of Theorem
1.8

The main part of this section will be devoted to the proof of the following result. For simplicity, given
C\in\mathbb{R}
, we will denote
\exp\big(\exp\big(C\sqrt{\Lambda}\,\big)\big)
by
C_{\Lambda}
. Recall the constants
\overline{\varepsilon}
and
B_{\Lambda}
given by Proposition
6.7
.

Lemma 9.1
.

There exists
0<\varepsilon_{0}\leq\overline{\varepsilon}
and
C\geq B
, which depends only on
N
, such that the next statement holds. If
\epsilon\log^{-1}(T)C_{\Lambda}\leq\varepsilon_{0}
, then the sequence
(f_{n},F^{n})_{n\geq 1}
is Cauchy in
L^{\infty}\big([T,+\infty[,L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})\times L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})\big)
.

Before proving it, let us show that, together with the results of the previous sections, it implies Theorem
1.8
except the uniqueness statement. Indeed, we obtain from Lemma
9.1
that there exists a distribution function
f:[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\to\mathbb{R}
and a
2
-form
F
, defined on
[T,+\infty[
, such that

f_{n}\xrightarrow[n\to+\infty]{}f\qquad\text{in $L^{\infty}\big([T,+\infty[,L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})\big)$},\qquad\qquad F^{n}\xrightarrow[n\to+\infty]{}F\qquad\text{in $L^{\infty}\big([T,+\infty[,L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})\big)$.}

We then deduce, in view of the definition (
109
)–(
110
) of
(f_{n},F^{n})_{n\geq 1}
,

\mathbf{T}_{F}(f)=0\qquad\text{in $\mathcal{D}\big(]T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\big)$},\qquad\qquad\nabla^{\mu}F_{\mu\nu}=J(f)_{\nu},\quad\nabla^{\mu}{}^{*}\!F_{\mu\nu}=0\qquad\text{in $\mathcal{D}\big(]T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\big)$},

so that
(f,F)
is a weak solution to the Vlasov-Maxwell system (
1
)–(
3
). Since
(F^{n})_{n\geq 1}
is a bounded sequence in
\mathbb{M}^{D,\Lambda}_{N}
, we have for any
|\gamma|=N-1
and all
t\geq T
,

\mathcal{E}_{N-1}^{K}\big[F-F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]\big](t)+\mathcal{E}^{K,1}\big[\nabla_{t,x}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}\big(F-F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]\big)\big](t)\leq D\Lambda.

Let
g(t,z,v):=f(t,z+t\widehat{v}+\mathscr{C}_{t,v},v)
. By Remark
7.25
, if we choose
C\geq B^{\prime}
, there holds, for all
t\geq T
,

\displaystyle\sup_{|\beta|\leq N}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\langle z\rangle^{16+2N-2\beta_{H}}\langle v\rangle^{30}\big|\widehat{Z}^{\beta}_{\infty}g(t,z,v)\big|^{2}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon\exp\big(\exp\big(B^{\prime}\sqrt{\Lambda}\,\big)\big)\leq C_{\Lambda}\epsilon.

Consequently,
(f,F)
is a classical solution to the Vlasov-Maxwell system. By interpolating we also have convergence in stronger topologies so that, by Proposition
7.32
and Remark
8.3
,

\lim_{t\to+\infty}g(t,z,v)=f_{\infty}(z,v),\qquad\qquad\qquad\lim_{r\to+\infty}r\underline{\alpha}(F)(t+u,r\omega)=\underline{\alpha}^{\infty}(u,\omega).

(145)

Hence, we have modified scattering for
f
, along the correction of the linear trajectories
t\mapsto(x+t\widehat{v}+\mathscr{C}_{t,v},v)
, to
f_{\infty}
. Moreover,
\underline{\alpha}^{\infty}
is the radiation field along future null infinity
\mathcal{I}^{+}
of the electromagnetic field
F
.

Remark 9.2
.

Since
F\in\mathbb{M}^{D,\Lambda}_{N}
and
(f,F)
is a solution to the Vlasov-Maxwell system with asymptotic data
(f_{\infty},\underline{\alpha}^{\infty})
, all the results of Sections
7
–
8
apply to
(f,F)
. In particular, Proposition
7.32
and Remark
8.3
provide a uniform rate of convergence for (
145
).

9.1 The approximate solutions form a Cauchy sequence

Since the arguments are very similar to part of those used during the last two Sections
7
–
8
, we will be more sketchy. Consider, for
n\geq 1
,

\delta_{\mathrm{Vlasov}}^{n}:=\mathbf{E}^{7,13}_{1}[g_{n+1}-g_{n}],\qquad\qquad\delta_{\mathrm{Maxwell}}^{n}:=\sup_{t\geq T}\mathcal{E}^{K}\big[F^{n+1}-F^{n}\big](t)+\sup_{t\geq T}\mathcal{E}^{K,1}\big[\nabla_{t,x}\big(F^{n+1}-F^{n}\big)\big](t)

and let us control first
g_{n+1}-g_{n}
.

Proposition 9.3
.

For all
n\geq 2
, we have
\delta_{\mathrm{Vlasov}}^{n}\lesssim\epsilon T^{-\frac{1}{4}}C_{\Lambda}\delta_{\mathrm{Maxwell}}^{n-1}
, where
C>0
depends only on
N
, and

\forall\,t\geq T,\qquad\bigg\||v^{0}|^{8}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}\big[g_{n+1}-g_{n}\big](t,z,\cdot)\mathrm{d}z\bigg\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon\Lambda C_{\Lambda}}\log^{4}(t)}{T^{\frac{1}{8}}t}\sqrt{\delta_{\mathrm{Maxwell}}^{n-1}}.

Proof.

Let
n\geq 2
. Following the proof of the estimate (
115
), one can obtain

\hskip-22.76219pt\forall\,t\geq T,\qquad\quad\sup_{|\gamma|\leq 1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}t^{4}\left|\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}(F^{n}-F^{n-1})\right|^{2}(t,x)\frac{\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x}{\langle x-t\widehat{v}-\mathscr{C}_{t,v}\rangle^{7}\langle v\rangle^{13}}\lesssim\frac{\log^{9}(t)}{t}\delta_{\mathrm{Maxwell}}^{n-1}.

(146)

Let
|\beta|\leq 1
. Then, since
\mathbf{T}_{F^{n}}^{\infty}(g_{n+1})=\mathbf{T}^{\infty}_{F^{n-1}}(g_{n})=0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{T}_{F^{n}}^{\infty}\Big[\langle v\rangle^{13}\,\langle z\rangle^{8-\beta_{H}}\,\widehat{Z}^{\beta}_{\infty}

\displaystyle\big(g_{n+1}-g_{n}\big)\Big]=\mathbf{T}_{F^{n}}^{\infty}\big(\langle v\rangle^{13}\,\langle z\rangle^{8-\beta_{H}}\big)\widehat{Z}^{\beta}_{\infty}\big(g_{n+1}-g_{n}\big)

\displaystyle+\langle v\rangle^{13}\,\langle z\rangle^{8-\beta_{H}}\,\big[\mathbf{T}^{\infty}_{F^{n}},\widehat{Z}^{\beta}_{\infty}\big]\big(g_{n+1}-g_{n}\big)+\langle v\rangle^{13}\,\langle z\rangle^{8-\beta_{H}}\widehat{Z}^{\beta}\big(\mathbf{T}_{F^{n}}^{\infty}(g_{n})-\mathbf{T}^{\infty}_{F^{n-1}}(g_{n})\big),

where
\big[\mathbf{T}^{\infty}_{F^{n}},\widehat{Z}^{\beta}_{\infty}\big]=0
if
|\beta|=0
. According to Corollary
7.8
and Proposition
7.12
,

\displaystyle\big|\mathbf{T}_{F^{n}}\big(\langle v\rangle^{13}\,\langle z\rangle^{8-\beta_{H}}\big)\widehat{Z}^{\beta}_{\infty}\big(g_{n+1}-g_{n}\big)+\langle v\rangle^{13}\,\langle z\rangle^{8-\beta_{H}}\,\big[\mathbf{T}^{\infty}_{F^{n}},\widehat{Z}^{\beta}_{\infty}\big]\big(g_{n+1}-g_{n}\big)\big|(t,z,v)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\lesssim\!\Big(\sqrt{\Lambda}t^{-\frac{4}{3}}+\sqrt{\Lambda}\,\widehat{v}^{\underline{L}}(\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})\,\langle t-|\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}}|\rangle^{-\frac{4}{3}}\Big)\sup_{|\kappa|\leq 1}\langle v\rangle^{13}\,\langle z\rangle^{8-\kappa_{H}}\big|\widehat{Z}^{\kappa}_{\infty}\big(g_{n+1}-g_{n}\big)\big|(t,z,v).

Moreover, we have

\mathbf{T}_{F^{n}}^{\infty}-\mathbf{T}^{\infty}_{F^{n-1}}=\frac{\delta_{j}^{i}-\widehat{v}_{j}\widehat{v}^{i}}{tv^{0}}\widehat{v}^{\mu}t^{2}{\big[F^{n-1}-F^{n}\big]_{\mu}}^{j}(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})\partial_{z^{i}}+\frac{\widehat{v}^{\mu}}{v^{0}}{\big[F^{n}-F^{n-1}\big]_{\mu}}^{j}(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})\left(v^{0}\partial_{v^{j}}-v^{0}\partial_{v^{j}}\mathscr{C}^{i}_{t,v}\partial_{z^{i}}\right).

Now, applying Lemmata
7.3
–
7.4
to
F=F^{n}-F^{n-1}
and
f_{\infty}=0
, one can derive
6
6

6

Alternatively, one can apply Lemma
7.4
to
F^{n}
as well as
F^{n-1}
and consider the difference of the resulting quantities.

\displaystyle\big|\langle v\rangle^{13}\,\langle z\rangle^{8-\beta_{H}}\widehat{Z}^{\beta}\big(\mathbf{T}_{F^{n}}^{\infty}(g_{n})-

\displaystyle\mathbf{T}^{\infty}_{F^{n-1}}(g_{n})\big)\big|\lesssim\frac{1}{v^{0}t}\sup_{|\xi|,|\kappa|\leq 1}t^{2}\big|\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\xi}}\big(F^{n}-F^{n-1}\big)\big|(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})\,\big|\,\langle v\rangle^{13}\langle z\rangle^{9-\kappa_{H}}\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\kappa}g_{n}\big|,

where we used
|v^{0}\partial_{v^{j}}\mathscr{C}_{t,v}^{i}|\lesssim\log^{2}(t)\lesssim t
. Next, as
H^{2}_{z,v}\hookrightarrow L^{\infty}_{z,v}
, we have
\big|\,\langle v\rangle^{19}\langle z\rangle^{13-\kappa_{H}}\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\kappa}g_{n}\big|\lesssim\sqrt{\epsilon B_{\Lambda}}
in view of the bound (
134
). Consequently, using (
146
) and performing
L^{2}_{z,v}
estimates as in Proposition
7.17
, we get, since
\widehat{Z}^{\beta}_{\infty}(g_{n+1}-g_{n})(t,\cdot,\cdot)\to 0
as
t\to+\infty
by construction,

\delta^{n}_{\mathrm{Vlasov}}\leq\sup_{t\geq T}\,\sup_{u\in\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{E}^{7,13}_{1}[g_{n+1}-g_{n}](t,u)\lesssim\frac{\epsilon B_{\Lambda}}{T^{\frac{1}{4}}}C^{\prime}_{\Lambda}\delta_{\mathrm{Maxwell}}^{n-1}=\frac{\epsilon C_{\Lambda}}{T^{\frac{1}{4}}}\delta_{\mathrm{Maxwell}}^{n-1},\qquad C^{\prime},\,C>0.

For the
L^{2}_{v}
decay estimate of the spatial average of
g_{n+1}-g_{n}
, note first that it goes to
0
as
t\to+\infty
. Then, we use the relation
\mathbf{T}_{F^{n}}(g_{n+1}-g_{n})=(\mathbf{T}_{F^{n-1}}-\mathbf{T}^{\infty}_{F^{n}})(g_{n})
and integration by parts in
z
, as the ones performed in Corollary
7.10
. Using also
|\,\langle v\rangle^{16}\langle z\rangle^{7}\widehat{Z}_{\infty}^{\kappa}g_{n}|\lesssim\sqrt{\epsilon B_{\Lambda}}
given by (
134
), (
111
) as well as Lemmata
3.9
and
6.6
, we get

\displaystyle\bigg|\partial_{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}\big(g_{n+1}-g_{n}\big)(t,z,v)\mathrm{d}z\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{\Lambda}\log^{4}(t)}{t^{2}}\sup_{|\kappa|\leq 1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}\langle z\rangle^{2}\,\langle v\rangle^{3}\,\big|\widehat{Z}^{\kappa}_{\infty}\big(g_{n+1}-g_{n}\big)\big|(t,z,v)\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon B_{\Lambda}}\log^{2}(t)}{t^{2}}\sup_{|\xi|\leq 1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}t^{2}\big|\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\xi}}\big(F^{n}-F^{n-1}\big)\big|(t,\mathrm{X}_{\mathscr{C}})\frac{\mathrm{d}z}{\langle z\rangle^{6}\langle v\rangle^{15}}.

We then obtain the convergence estimate by following the proof of Proposition
7.26
and by using (
146
) as well as the estimate obtained for
\delta_{\mathrm{Vlasov}}^{n}
.

We control now
F^{n+1}-F^{n}
.

Proposition 9.4
.

For all
n\geq 2
and
t\geq T
, we have

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathrm{Maxwell}}^{n}\lesssim\epsilon T^{-\frac{1}{4}}\Lambda^{2}C_{\Lambda}\delta_{\mathrm{Maxwell}}^{n-1}.

Proof.

Let
\mathbf{q}:\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\to\mathbb{R}
such that
|\mathbf{q}|(\cdot,v)+|\nabla_{v}\mathbf{q}|(\cdot,v)\lesssim|v^{0}|^{3}
. We have from Proposition
2.13
, applied to
\mathbf{q}(\omega,v)(f_{n+1}-f_{n})
, and the previous Proposition
9.3
,

\forall\,t\geq T,\qquad\;\sup_{\omega\in\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\,\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\langle t+|x|\rangle^{5}\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\mathbf{q}(\omega,v)\big(f_{n+1}-f_{n}\big)(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}v\bigg|^{2}\mathrm{d}x\lesssim\epsilon T^{-\frac{1}{4}}\Lambda C_{\Lambda}\log^{8}(t)\delta_{\mathrm{Maxwell}}^{n-1}.

Similarly, according to Proposition
2.11
, we have for any
|\beta|\leq 1
,

\forall\,t\geq T,\qquad\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\langle t+|x|\rangle^{3}\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\langle x-t\widehat{v}\rangle^{3}\,\langle v\rangle^{9}\big|\widehat{Z}^{\beta}(f_{n+1}-f_{n})\big|(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}v\bigg|^{2}\mathrm{d}x\lesssim\epsilon T^{-\frac{1}{4}}C_{\Lambda}\log^{12}(t)\delta_{\mathrm{Maxwell}}^{n-1}.

We refer to the proof of Corollary
7.30
for more details. Consider now, for
\tau_{0}\geq 4T
,

\displaystyle\nabla^{\mu}G^{\tau_{0}}_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=\chi\big(t/\tau_{0}\big)J\big(f_{n+1}-f_{n}\big)_{\nu},\qquad

\displaystyle\nabla^{\mu}{}^{*}\!G_{\mu\nu}^{\tau_{0}}=0,\qquad G(\tau_{0},\cdot)={}^{*}\!G(\tau_{0},\cdot)=0.

Bounding
\log^{8}(t)
by
t^{1/4}
, we obtain from Proposition
4.11
that

\forall\,t\geq T,\qquad\mathcal{E}^{K}\big[G^{\tau_{0}}\big](t)\lesssim\epsilon T^{-\frac{1}{4}}\Lambda C_{\Lambda}\delta^{n-1}_{\mathrm{Maxwell}}\,\langle t\rangle^{-\frac{3}{4}}.

Hence, since the radiation field of
F^{n+1}-F^{n}
vanishes, we have
\mathcal{E}^{K}[F^{n+1}-F^{n}]\lesssim\epsilon T^{-1/4}\Lambda C_{\Lambda}\delta^{n-1}_{\mathrm{Maxwell}}
on
[T,+\infty[
according to Proposition
4.9
. Following the analysis carried out in Section
8.3
and using the pointwise decay estimates given by (
111
), for
F^{n+1}
and
F^{n}
, as well as Corollary
7.31
, for
f_{n+1}
and
f_{n}
, we get

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geq T}\,\langle t\rangle^{\frac{1}{4}}\,\mathcal{E}^{K,1}\big[\nabla_{\partial_{x^{k}}}G^{\tau_{0}}\big](t)\lesssim\epsilon T^{-\frac{1}{4}}\Lambda^{2}C_{\Lambda}\delta_{\mathrm{Maxwell}}^{n-1},

for any
1\leq k\leq 3
. We then obtain from Proposition
4.9
the stated estimates for the spatial derivatives of
F^{n+1}-F^{n}
. The time derivative can be handled by exploiting (
144
).

Hence, if
\epsilon T^{-1/4}\Lambda^{2}C_{\Lambda}
is small enough, there exists
0\leq\eta<1
such that
\delta^{n}_{\mathrm{Maxwell}}+\delta^{n}_{\mathrm{Vlasov}}\leq\eta^{n}\delta^{1}_{\mathrm{Maxwell}}
for all
n\geq 2
. By considering a slightly larger constant
C
, this implies Lemma
9.1
.

9.2 Uniqueness of the solution

To conclude the proof of Theorem
1.8
, it remains us to prove that
(f,F)
is the only solution to the Vlasov-Maxwell system with scattering data
(f_{\infty},\underline{\alpha}^{\infty})
such that

\mathbf{E}_{8}^{7,13}[g]+\sup_{t\geq T}\mathcal{E}^{K}_{7}\big[F-F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]\big](t)+\sup_{|\gamma|=7}\,\sup_{t\geq T}\mathcal{E}^{K,1}\big[\nabla_{t,x}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}\big(F-F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]\big)\big](t)<+\infty.

(Uniq)

Let
(h,H)
be a solution to the same asymptotic Cauchy problem and verifying the bound (
Uniq
). Since local well-posedness hold,
(h,H)=(f,F)
is a consequence of the next result.

Lemma 9.5
.

There exists
T^{\prime}\geq T
such that
(h,H)=(f,F)
on
[T^{\prime},+\infty[
.

Proof.

Let
\mathbf{h}(t,z,v):=h(t,z+t\widehat{v}+\mathscr{C}_{t,v},v)
. By assumption, there exists
\Lambda^{\prime}\geq 1
such that

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}_{8}^{7,13}[\mathbf{h}]+\sup_{t>T}\mathcal{E}^{K}\big[H-F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]\big](t)+\sup_{|\gamma|=7}\,\sup_{t\geq T}\mathcal{E}^{K,1}\big[\nabla_{t,x}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}\big(H-F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]\big)\big](t)

\displaystyle\leq D\Lambda^{\prime},

that is
F\in\mathbb{M}^{D,\Lambda^{\prime}}_{8}
. We can assume, without loss of generality, that
\Lambda\leq\Lambda^{\prime}
. Consider now
T^{\prime}\geq T
such that
\epsilon\log^{-1}(T^{\prime})C_{\Lambda^{\prime}}\leq\varepsilon_{0}
, where the constants
C_{\Lambda^{\prime}}
and
\varepsilon_{0}
are given by Lemma
9.1
. Applying Proposition
9.3
to, with a slight abuse of notations,

(f_{n+1},F^{n})=(h,H),\qquad(f_{n},F^{n-1})=(f,F),\qquad T=T^{\prime},\qquad\Lambda=\Lambda^{\prime},

and Proposition
9.4
to
(f_{n+1},F^{n+1})=(h,H)
and
(f_{n},F^{n})=(f,F)
, we get
(h,H)=(f,F)
on
[T^{\prime},+\infty[
.

10
Improved velocity decay assumptions on
f_{\infty}
when the regularity order
N
is large

We explain here how to get rid of the extra hypothesis (
108
), which is relevant as soon as
N\geq 9
but only significant for
N\gg 1
. This assumption (
108
) ensures that all the derivatives of
F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]
are well-defined up to order
N
.

10.1 Commutation properties of
\mathbb{K}
with
F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[\cdot]

The idea, if
\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]
cannot be controlled sufficiently well, consists in considering
F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}_{\infty}f_{\infty}]
, which requires less decay in
v
on
f_{\infty}
in order to be well-defined. To check it, compare
\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{|\gamma|+1}[f_{\infty}]
with
\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{1}[\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}_{\infty}f_{\infty}]
. We then estimate the difference of this two fields.

Proposition 10.1
.

Let
1\leq|\gamma|\leq N
such that
\gamma_{T}=0
. If
\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{N+1}[f_{\infty}]<+\infty
, we have

\forall\,(t,x)\in[t_{0},+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3},\qquad\big|\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}\big(F^{\mathrm{asymp}}\big[f_{\infty}\big]\big)-F^{\mathrm{asymp}}\big[\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}_{\infty}f_{\infty}\big]-\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}(\,\overline{F}\,)\big|(t,x)\lesssim\frac{\overline{E}_{N+1}[f_{\infty}]}{\langle t+|x|\rangle\,\langle t-|x|\rangle^{2}}.

Proof.

This estimate is related to the asymptotic expansion of their initial data. More precisely, we have

\forall\,1\leq|\gamma|\leq N+1,\qquad\qquad Q^{\gamma}:=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}_{\infty}f_{\infty}(z,v)\mathrm{d}z\mathrm{d}v=0.

These identities are obtained by integration by parts, for
|\gamma|=1
, and a direct induction for the general case. Thus, the stated estimate ensues from the following observations.

•

The inhomogeneous part, according to Definition
5.22
, of
F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}_{\infty}f_{\infty}]
and
\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]
are equal.

•

Up to terms decaying as
\overline{E}_{N_{0}+1}[f_{\infty}]\,\langle t+r\rangle^{-1}\,\langle t-r\rangle^{-2}
, the difference of their homogeneous part is equal to
\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}\overline{F}
. This is a consequence of the analysis carried out in Section
5.3.3
(see in particular (
92
)).

Thus, although
F^{\mathrm{asymp}}\big[\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}_{\infty}f_{\infty}\big]
captures the asymptotic behavior of
\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]
in the interior of the light cone
|x|<t
, and then along timelike trajectories, it fails to do so in the exterior region. Fortunately, this failure can be quantified by an electromagnetic field that we know well,
\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}\overline{F}
, which is a derivative of the pure charge part of
F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]
.

10.2 Alternative functional space for the electromagnetic fields

Motivated by the previous discussion, we introduce the following sets of
2
-forms.

Definition 10.2
.

Let
D\geq 1
and
\widetilde{\mathbb{M}}_{N}^{D,\Lambda}
be the set of the
2
-forms
F
defined on
[T,+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
and verifying

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geq T}\mathcal{E}^{K}_{2}\big[F-F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[f_{\infty}]\big](t)

\displaystyle\leq D\Lambda,

(147)

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geq T}\mathcal{E}^{K}_{2}\big[\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}\big(F-\overline{F}\big)-F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[Z_{\infty}^{\gamma}f_{\infty}]\big](t)

\displaystyle\leq D\Lambda,\qquad\qquad 1\leq|\gamma|\leq N-3

(148)

\displaystyle\hskip-45.5244pt\sup_{t\geq T}\mathcal{E}^{K,1}\big[\nabla_{t,x}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}\big(F-\overline{F}\big)-\nabla_{t,x}F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[Z_{\infty}^{\gamma}f_{\infty}]\big](t)

\displaystyle\leq D\Lambda,\qquad\qquad|\gamma|=N-1.

(149)

Then the following remarks are importants.

•

Controlling two derivatives of
\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}(F-\overline{F}\,)-F^{\mathrm{asymp}}[Z_{\infty}^{\gamma}f_{\infty}]
is important in order to derive pointwise decay estimates.

•

The assumptions of Theorem
1.8
satisfied by
f_{\infty}
imply that
\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{3}[Z_{\infty}^{\gamma}f_{\infty}]\lesssim\epsilon
for all
|\gamma|\leq N-3
, so that the results of Section
5
can be applied to the distribution function
Z_{\infty}^{\gamma}f_{\infty}
and for
N_{0}=2
.

•

Similarly,
\overline{\mathbb{E}}_{2}[Z_{\infty}^{\xi}f_{\infty}]\leq\epsilon
for all
|\xi|\leq N-1
. In particular, we can apply Proposition
5.19
as well as the first part of Proposition
5.53
to
Z_{\infty}^{\xi}f_{\infty}
and for
N_{0}=1
.

•

For all
F\in\widetilde{\mathbb{M}}_{N}^{D,\Lambda}
, the five estimates (
111
)–(
115
) hold. This can be obtained by using the estimates satisfied by
\overline{F}
and its derivatives, which are given by Proposition
3.16
, and by following the proof of Proposition
6.5
.

10.3 Modifications in the proof of Theorem
1.8

The extra assumption (
108
) was not directly exploited in Section
7
, where the analysis of the Vlasov equation were carried out. Similarly, since we proved that
(f_{n},F^{n})_{n\geq 1}
is a Cauchy sequence in a space of low regularity, we did not use (
108
) neither
7
7

7

More precisely, we only used it through the estimates (
111
)–(
115
), which hold as well in this setting.
in Section
9
. In addition to Proposition
6.5
, already mentioned, the analysis of Section
8
has to be modified as well. More precisely, we need to prove that
(F^{n})_{n\geq 1}
is a bounded sequence in
\widetilde{\mathbb{M}}_{N}^{D,\Lambda}
. For this, we will use the next result which ensues from Proposition
3.17
.

Lemma 10.3
.

For any multi-index
\gamma
, the radiation field of
\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}\overline{F}
along future null infinity vanishes identically. Moreover, for any
0\leq\mu,\,\nu\leq 3
,
r^{2}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}(\,\overline{F}\,)_{\mu\nu}(r+u,r\omega)
converges as
r\to+\infty
in
L^{\infty}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{R}_{u}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega})
.

By keeping the notations of Section
8
, we have to prove
F^{\mathrm{new}}\in\widetilde{\mathbb{M}}_{N}^{D,\Lambda}
for
D
chosen large enough and if
\epsilon\log^{-1}T
small enough, which is a stronger statement than
F^{\mathrm{new}}\in\mathbb{M}_{N}^{D,\Lambda}
. Note now that the condition (
147
) holds by the results of Section
8.2
. Note that Proposition
3.16
and Lemma
3.9
imply, for any multi-index
\kappa
such that
\kappa_{T}\geq 1
,

\forall\,t\in\mathbb{R}_{+},\qquad\qquad\mathcal{E}^{K,1}\big[\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\kappa}}\overline{F}\big](t)\lesssim\epsilon.

Since
F^{\mathrm{asymp}}\big[\widehat{Z}^{\kappa}_{\infty}f_{\infty}\big]=0
for such multi-indices
\kappa
, we obtain from (
138
) and (
143
) that (
148
)–(
149
) hold for any
\gamma
verifying
\gamma_{T}\geq 1
. Fix now
1\leq|\gamma|\leq N-3
as well as
|\xi|=N-1
such that
\gamma_{T}=\xi_{T}=0
and let us prove that (
148
) as well as (
149
) hold for them. Recall the fields
G^{\gamma}
and
G^{\xi,k}
, which were introduced in (
136
) and (
142
). Compared with the analysis performed in Section
8
, we will need to consider slightly different decompositions of the derivatives of
F^{\mathrm{new}}
. More precisely, let us prove that

1.

for any
|\kappa|\leq 2
, there exists an electromagnetic fields
\overline{G}^{\kappa,\gamma}
such that

\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\kappa}Z^{\gamma}}F^{\mathrm{new}}\!=\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\kappa}}\big(F^{\mathrm{asym}}\big[\widehat{Z}^{\gamma}_{\infty}f_{\infty}]+\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\gamma}}\overline{F}\,\big)+\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\kappa}}G^{\gamma,\mathrm{vac}}+G^{\kappa\gamma}+\overline{G}^{\kappa,\gamma},\qquad\sup_{t\geq T}\mathcal{E}^{K}\big[\,\overline{G}^{\kappa,\gamma}\big](t)\lesssim\epsilon T^{-\frac{1}{2}},

where, for any
|\beta|\leq N-1
and
1\leq k\leq 3
,
G^{\beta,\mathrm{vac}}
is the unique solution to the vacuum Maxwell equations with radiation field
\underline{\alpha}^{\infty}_{\beta}-\underline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{asymp}}_{\beta}\big[\widehat{Z}^{\beta}_{\infty}f_{\infty}]
. Moreover,
G^{\kappa\gamma}=G^{\zeta}
, where
Z^{\kappa}Z^{\gamma}=Z^{\zeta}
.

2.

For any
|\xi|=N-1
, there exists
\overline{G}^{\xi,k}
such that

\nabla_{\partial_{x^{k}}}\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\xi}}F^{\mathrm{new}}\!=\nabla_{\partial_{x^{k}}}\big(F^{\mathrm{asymp}}\big[\widehat{Z}^{\xi}_{\infty}f_{\infty}\big]+\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\xi}}\overline{F}\,\big)+\nabla_{\partial_{x^{k}}}G^{\xi,\mathrm{vac}}+G^{\xi,k}+\overline{G}^{\xi,k}\!,\quad\;\sup_{t\geq T}\mathcal{E}^{K}\big[\,\overline{G}^{\xi,k}\big](t)\lesssim\epsilon T^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

Note then that according to Theorem
4.20
and Lemma
3.9
,

\forall\,t\geq T,\qquad\mathcal{E}^{K}\big[\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\kappa}}G^{\gamma,\mathrm{vac}}\big](t)+\mathcal{E}^{K,1}\big[\nabla_{\partial_{x^{k}}}G^{\xi,\mathrm{vac}}\big](t)\lesssim\Lambda,\qquad\qquad|\kappa|\leq 2.

If
1.
and
2.
hold true, (
148
)–(
149
) would be verified in view of the estimates derived for
G^{\gamma}
and
G^{\xi,k}
in Section
8
. For simplicity, we denote by
\overline{J}^{\beta}
the source term of Maxwell equations verified by
\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\beta}}\overline{F}
. Let us justify that, for any multi-index
\beta
, we can define
\overline{G}^{\beta}
as the unique finite energy electromagnetic field, with identically zero radiation fields, such that

\nabla^{\mu}\overline{G}_{\mu\nu}^{\beta}=-\overline{J}^{\,\beta}_{\nu},\qquad\qquad\nabla^{\mu}{}^{*}\!\overline{G}_{\mu\nu}^{\beta}=0.

Moreover, it turns out that
\mathcal{E}^{K}\big[\overline{G}^{\beta}\big]\lesssim\epsilon T^{-1/2}
on
\mathbb{R}_{+}
. For this, we use Proposition
4.9
, applied for
\underline{\alpha}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}=0
,
\delta=1/2
and
B_{\mathrm{source}}=\epsilon T^{-1/2}
, together with the next result.

Lemma 10.4
.

For any
|\kappa|\leq N
,
a\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
and all
n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
, we have, uniformly in
a
,

\forall\,t\in[0,n],\qquad\mathcal{E}^{K,a}\big[S(\,\overline{J}^{\kappa}\!,n)\big](t)\lesssim\epsilon\,\langle t\rangle^{-1},

where
S(\,\overline{J}^{\kappa}\!,n)
is introduced in Definition
4.7
.

Proof.

Let
n\geq 1
,
|\kappa|\leq N
,
H:=S(\overline{J}^{\kappa},n)
and remark that we only need to consider the case
a=0
. Indeed,
H
is supported in the exterior of the light cone by finite speed of propagation. Note then, according to Proposition
3.7
and since
H(n,\cdot)=0
,

\forall\,t\geq 0,\qquad\qquad\mathcal{E}^{K}[H](t)+\sup_{u\in\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{F}^{K}[H]_{t}^{n}(u)\leq 8\int_{\tau=t}^{n}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\big|K^{\mu}H_{\mu\nu}\,\overline{J}^{\kappa,\nu}\big|(\tau,x)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}\tau.

By expanding the integrand according to the null frame
(\underline{L},L,e_{\theta},e_{\varphi})
, we obtain, exactly as in (
85
),

\displaystyle 2\big|K^{\mu}H_{\mu\nu}\,\overline{J}^{\kappa,\nu}\big|

\displaystyle\leq\langle t-r\rangle^{2}|\rho(H)|\big|\overline{J}^{\kappa}_{\underline{L}}\big|+\langle t+r\rangle^{2}|\rho(H)|\big|\overline{J}^{\kappa}_{L}\big|+\langle t-r\rangle^{2}|\underline{\alpha}(H)|\big|\not{\overline{J}}^{\kappa}\big|+\langle t+r\rangle^{2}|\alpha(H)|\big|\not{\overline{J}}^{\kappa}\big|,

where
\not{\overline{J}}^{\kappa}
is the angular part of
\overline{J}^{\kappa}
. According to Lemma
2.3
,
\overline{J}^{\kappa}
is a linear combination of
\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\beta}}\overline{J}
. Thus, by Proposition
3.16
,

\displaystyle\big|K^{\mu}H_{\mu\nu}\,\overline{J}^{\kappa,\nu}\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\epsilon}\,\langle t+r\rangle^{-2}|\rho(H)|\mathds{1}_{1\leq r-t\leq 2}+\sqrt{\epsilon}\,\langle t+r\rangle^{-3}|\underline{\alpha}(H)|\mathds{1}_{1\leq r-t\leq 2}+\sqrt{\epsilon}\,\langle t+r\rangle^{-1}|\alpha(H)|\mathds{1}_{1\leq r-t\leq 2}.

Now, by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality in
x
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\tau=t}^{n}\int_{1\leq|x|-t\leq 2}\frac{|\rho(H)|(\tau,x)}{\langle\tau+|x|\rangle^{2}}+\frac{|\underline{\alpha}(H)|(\tau,x)}{\langle\tau+|x|\rangle^{3}}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{[t,n]}\big|\mathcal{E}^{K}[H]\big|^{\frac{1}{2}}\int_{\tau=t}^{n}\bigg|\int_{|r-t|\leq 2}\frac{r^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r}{\langle t+r\rangle^{6}}\bigg|^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\lesssim\langle t\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sup_{[t,n]}\big|\mathcal{E}^{K}[H]\big|^{\frac{1}{2}}.

For the other term, we perform the change of variables
(u,\underline{u},\omega)=(\tau-|x|,\tau+|x|,x/|x|)
and we apply the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality in
(\underline{u},\omega)
. We obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\tau=t}^{n}\int_{1\leq|x|-t\leq 2}\frac{|\alpha(H)|(\tau,x)}{\langle\tau+|x|\rangle}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\leq\int_{u=-1}^{-2}\int_{\underline{u}=2t-u}^{2n-u}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\frac{|\alpha(H)|(u,\underline{u},\omega)}{\langle\underline{u}\rangle}\,r^{2}\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\mathrm{d}\underline{u}\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{\mathbb{R}}\big|\mathcal{F}^{K}[H]_{t}^{n}\big|^{\frac{1}{2}}\int_{u=-1}^{-2}\bigg|\int_{\underline{u}=2t-u}^{2n-u}\frac{r^{2}\mathrm{d}\underline{u}}{\langle\underline{u}\rangle^{4}}\bigg|^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathrm{d}u\lesssim\langle t\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sup_{\mathbb{R}}\big|\mathcal{F}^{K}[H]_{t}^{n}\big|^{\frac{1}{2}}.

We finally get the result by combining the previous inequalities.

Then, we set
\overline{G}^{\kappa,\gamma}\!:=\overline{G}^{\kappa\gamma}
and, for
\beta
such that
\partial_{x^{k}}Z^{\xi}=Z^{\beta}
,
\overline{G}^{\xi,k}\!:=\overline{G}^{\beta}
. Since uniqueness holds for the asymptotic Cauchy problem for the Maxwell equations, the properties
1.
and
2.
hold, which concludes the proof of Theorem
1.8
.

Appendix A
Elliptic estimates

We prove here classical estimates for solutions to the Poisson equation, which are in particular relevant in order to control initial electromagnetic fields.

Lemma A.1
.

Let
\psi:\mathbb{R}^{3}\to\mathbb{R}
be a sufficiently regular function and
\phi
be the unique function in
H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
such that
\Delta\,\phi=\psi
. Then,

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\left|\nabla\phi(x)\right|^{2}\mathrm{d}x\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\langle x\rangle^{\frac{5}{2}}|\psi(x)|^{2}\mathrm{d}x.

Moreover, if
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\psi=0
, we have

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\langle x\rangle^{\frac{5}{2}}\left|\nabla\phi(x)\right|^{2}\mathrm{d}x\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\langle x\rangle^{6}|\psi(x)|^{2}\mathrm{d}x,\qquad\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\langle x\rangle^{\frac{9}{2}}\left|\nabla^{2}\phi(x)\right|^{2}\mathrm{d}x\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\langle x\rangle^{6}|\psi(x)|^{2}\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\langle x\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}|\phi(x)|^{2}\mathrm{d}x.

as well as

\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{3}}\langle x\rangle^{3}\left|\nabla\phi(x)\right|\lesssim\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{3}}\,\langle y\rangle^{5}|\psi(y)|.

Remark A.2
.

We refer to the proof of Proposition
3.15
in order to deal with the case
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\psi=Q\neq 0
.

Proof.

Using the Green function of the Laplacian, we have, for almost all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
,

4\pi\nabla\phi(x)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{y}}\frac{x-y}{|x-y|^{3}}\psi(y)\mathrm{d}y=\int_{|x-y|\leq\frac{|x|}{2}}\frac{x-y}{|x-y|^{3}}\psi(y)\mathrm{d}y+\int_{|x-y|\geq\frac{|x|}{2}}\frac{x-y}{|x-y|^{3}}\psi(y)\mathrm{d}y.

(150)

We start by the unweighted
L^{2}
estimate, for which we use Young’s convolution inequality. For this, we need to be careful since the Kernel does not belong to any
L^{p}
space. We proceed as follows,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{y}}\frac{x-y}{|x-y|^{3}}\psi(y)\mathrm{d}y\bigg|^{2}\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{y}}\frac{\mathds{1}_{|x-y|\leq 1}}{|x-y|^{2}}|\psi(y)|\mathrm{d}y\bigg|^{2}\mathrm{d}x+\int_{|x|\leq 1}\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{y}}\frac{\mathds{1}_{|x-y|\geq 1}}{|x-y|^{2}}|\psi(y)|\mathrm{d}y\bigg|^{2}\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}\!\mathds{1}_{|z|\leq 1}\frac{\mathrm{d}z}{|z|^{2}}\|\psi\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\bigg|^{2}+\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}\!\mathds{1}_{|z|\geq 1}\frac{\mathrm{d}z}{|z|^{\frac{10}{3}}}\bigg|^{\frac{6}{5}}\|\psi\|^{2}_{L^{10/9}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})}\!\lesssim\|\psi\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\psi\|^{2}_{L^{10/9}}.

Then, we apply Hölder inequality in order to get
\|\psi\|_{L^{10/9}}\lesssim\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{\frac{5}{4}}\psi\|_{L^{2}}
. We now focus on the weighted
L^{\infty}
estimate and we treat first the region
\{|x|\leq 1\}
. We have

\forall\,|x|\leq 1,\qquad|\nabla\phi|(x)\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{y}}\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{\langle y\rangle^{2}|x-y|^{2}}\mathrm{d}y\,\sup_{z\in\mathbb{R}^{3}}\,\langle z\rangle^{2}|\psi(z)|\lesssim\sup_{z\in\mathbb{R}^{3}}\,\langle z\rangle^{2}|\psi(z)|.

Fix now
|x|\geq 1
and use (
150
) in order to decompose
\nabla\phi(x)
into three parts,

\nabla\phi(x)=\int_{|x-y|\leq\frac{|x|}{2}}\frac{x-y}{|x-y|^{3}}\psi(y)\mathrm{d}y+\frac{x}{|x|^{3}}\int_{|x-y|\geq\frac{|x|}{2}}\psi(y)\mathrm{d}y+\int_{|x-y|\geq\frac{|x|}{2}}\frac{(x-y)|x|^{3}-x|x-y|^{3}}{|x|^{3}|x-y|^{3}}\psi(y)\mathrm{d}y.

Then, as
|x-y|\leq|x|/2
implies
\langle y\rangle\geq|x|/2
, we have

\bigg|\int_{|x-y|\leq\frac{|x|}{2}}\frac{x-y}{|x-y|^{3}}\psi(y)\mathrm{d}y\bigg|\lesssim\frac{1}{|x|^{4}}\int_{|x-y|\leq\frac{|x|}{2}}\frac{1}{|x-y|^{2}}\langle y\rangle^{4}\left|\psi(y)\right|\mathrm{d}y\lesssim\frac{1}{|x|^{3}}\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{3}}\,\langle y\rangle^{4}|\psi(y)|.

Next, since the average of
\psi
vanishes by assumption, we have

\bigg|\frac{x}{|x|^{3}}\int_{|x-y|\geq\frac{|x|}{2}}\psi(y)\mathrm{d}y\bigg|=\bigg|\frac{x}{|x|^{3}}\int_{|x-y|\leq\frac{|x|}{2}}\psi(y)\mathrm{d}y\bigg|\lesssim\frac{1}{|x|^{6}}\int_{|x-y|\leq\frac{|x|}{2}}\langle y\rangle^{4}|\psi(y)|\mathrm{d}y\lesssim\frac{1}{|x|^{3}}\sup_{z\in\mathbb{R}^{3}}\,\langle z\rangle^{4}|\psi(z)|.

For the last integral, note first that for
a=x-y
,

|a|x|^{3}-x|a|^{3}|=|(a-x)|x|^{3}+x(|x|^{3}-|a|^{3})|\lesssim|a-x||x|(|a|^{2}+|x|^{2})=|y||x|(|x-y|^{2}+|x|^{2}).

(151)

As
|y-x|\geq|x|/2
on the domain of integration, we get

\int_{|x-y|\geq\frac{|x|}{2}}\frac{|(x-y)|x|^{3}-x|x-y|^{3}|}{|x|^{3}|x-y|^{3}}\big|\psi(y)\big|\mathrm{d}y\lesssim\int_{|x-y|\geq\frac{|x|}{2}}\frac{|y||\psi(y)|}{|x|^{2}|x-y|}\mathrm{d}y\lesssim\frac{1}{|x|^{3}}\int_{|x-y|\geq\frac{|x|}{2}}\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{\langle y\rangle^{4}}\sup_{z\in\mathbb{R}^{3}}\,\langle z\rangle^{5}|\psi(z)|.

Since the integral on the right hand side has a finite value, this concludes the proof of the
L^{\infty}
estimate. For the weighted
L^{2}
estimate, we consider again these three terms. It suffices to deal with the region
\{|x|\geq 1\}
since we already treated the complement one. By decomposing the Kernel as
1/|z|^{2}\mathds{1}_{|z|\leq 1}+1/|z|^{2}\mathds{1}_{|z|>1}
and by applying Young’s convolution inequality

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{|x|\geq 1}\langle x\rangle^{\frac{5}{2}}\bigg|\int_{|x-y|\leq\frac{\langle x\rangle}{2}}\frac{x-y}{|x-y|^{3}}\psi(y)\mathrm{d}y\bigg|^{2}\mathrm{d}x\bigg|^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\bigg|\int_{|x-y|\leq\frac{|x|}{2}}\frac{1}{|x-y|^{2}}\langle y\rangle^{\frac{5}{4}}\left|\psi(y)\right|\mathrm{d}y\bigg|^{2}\mathrm{d}x\bigg|^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}\mathds{1}_{|z|\leq 1}\frac{\mathrm{d}z}{|z|^{2}}\big\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{\frac{5}{4}}\,\psi\big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}}\mathds{1}_{|z|\geq 1}\frac{\mathrm{d}z}{|z|^{4}}\bigg|^{\frac{1}{2}}\big\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{\frac{5}{4}}\,\psi\big\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\lesssim\big\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{\frac{5}{4}}\,\psi\big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\big\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{\frac{5}{4}}\,\psi\big\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

For the second term, we use again that the average of
\psi
vanishes and then that
|y|\geq|x|/2
on the domain of integration in order to get

\int_{|x|\geq 1}\langle x\rangle^{\frac{5}{2}}\bigg|\frac{x}{|x|^{3}}\int_{|x-y|\geq\frac{|x|}{2}}\psi(y)\mathrm{d}y\bigg|^{2}\mathrm{d}x\lesssim\int_{|x|\geq 1}\frac{1}{|x|^{\frac{7}{2}}}\bigg|\int_{|x-y|\leq\frac{|x|}{2}}\langle y\rangle\left|\psi(y)\right|\mathrm{d}y\bigg|^{2}\mathrm{d}x\lesssim\big\|\langle\cdot\rangle\,\psi\big\|^{2}_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

Finally, for the last term, we use (
151
) and then that
|x-y|\gtrsim|x|
on the domain of integration. It yields

\int_{|x|\geq 1}\langle x\rangle^{\frac{5}{2}}\bigg|\int_{|x-y|\geq\frac{|x|}{2}}\frac{(x-y)|x|^{3}-x|x-y|^{3}}{|x|^{3}|x-y|^{3}}\psi(y)\mathrm{d}y\bigg|^{2}\mathrm{d}x\lesssim\int_{|x|\geq 1}\frac{1}{|x|^{\frac{7}{2}}}\bigg|\int_{|x-y|\geq\frac{|x|}{2}}\langle y\rangle|\psi(y)|\mathrm{d}y\bigg|^{2}\mathrm{d}x\lesssim\big\|\langle\cdot\rangle\,\psi\big\|^{2}_{L^{1}}

and the result ensues from
\big\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{5/4}\,\psi\big\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\lesssim\big\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{3}\,\psi\big\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}
. For the second order derivatives, we remark that

\Delta\big(\langle x\rangle^{\frac{9}{4}}\phi\big)=\langle x\rangle^{\frac{9}{4}}\psi+\frac{9}{2}\langle x\rangle^{\frac{1}{4}}x^{i}\partial_{x^{i}}\phi+\frac{27}{4}\langle x\rangle^{\frac{1}{4}}\phi+\frac{9}{16}|x|^{2}\langle x\rangle^{-\frac{7}{4}}\phi.

By elliptic regularity, we then get

\big\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{\frac{9}{4}}\nabla^{2}\phi\big\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\big\|\nabla^{2}\big(\langle\cdot\rangle^{\frac{9}{4}}\phi\big)\big\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\big\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{\frac{5}{4}}\nabla\phi\big\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\big\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{\frac{1}{4}}\phi\big\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\big\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{\frac{9}{4}}\psi\big\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\big\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{\frac{5}{4}}\nabla\phi\big\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\big\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{\frac{1}{4}}\phi\big\|_{L^{2}_{x}}.

We are now able to state higher order statements. For this, it will be convenient to exploit the following ordered set of vector fields,

\mathbb{Q}:=\{\partial_{x^{k}},\,\Omega_{ij},\,S_{x}\,|\,1\leq k\leq 3,\,1\leq i,\,j\leq 3\}=\{\Gamma^{\ell}\,|\,1\leq\ell\leq 7\},\qquad S_{x}:=x^{\ell}\partial_{x^{\ell}}.

which verify
\mathbb{Q}\subset\mathbb{K}
on
\{t=0\}
as well as the following commutation relations,

\forall\,\Gamma\in\mathbb{Q}\setminus{S_{x}},\quad[\Delta,\Gamma]=0,\qquad\qquad[\Delta,S_{x}]=2\Delta.

(152)

If
\gamma\in\llbracket 1,7\rrbracket^{p}
, we define the operator
\Gamma^{\gamma}\in\mathbb{Q}^{|\gamma|}
as
\Gamma^{\gamma_{1}}\dots\Gamma^{\gamma_{p}}
. Note that these weighted derivatives capture decay in
x
. Indeed, in view of the relations
|x|^{2}\partial_{x^{j}}=x^{j}S_{x}+x^{i}\Omega_{ij}
, for any sufficiently regular function
\Phi
,

\sup_{|\gamma|\leq N}|\nabla\Gamma^{\gamma}\Phi|(x)\lesssim\sup_{|\kappa|\leq N}\langle x\rangle^{|\kappa|}|\nabla\partial_{x}^{\kappa}\Phi|(x)\lesssim\sup_{|\gamma|\leq N}|\nabla\Gamma^{\gamma}\Phi|(x).

(153)

Lemma A.3
.

Let
N\geq 0
,
t_{0}\geq 0
,
f:[t_{0},+\infty[\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\to\mathbb{R}
be a sufficiently regular function and
\phi
be the unique
H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
solution to
\Delta\,\phi=\int_{v}f(t_{0},\cdot,v)\mathrm{d}v
. Then,

\forall\,|\kappa|\leq N,\qquad\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\langle x\rangle^{2|\kappa|}\left|\nabla\partial_{x}^{\kappa}\phi(x)\right|^{2}\mathrm{d}x\lesssim_{t_{0}}\sup_{|\beta|\leq N}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\langle x\rangle^{\frac{5}{2}}\langle v\rangle^{4}\big|\widehat{Z}^{\beta}f(t_{0},x,v)\big|^{2}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x.

Proof.

Apply first Lemma
A.1
to
\Gamma^{\gamma}\phi
, for any
|\gamma|\leq N
, together with the commutation relations (
152
). Then, we use (
153
) for
p=1
and we remark that, by integration by parts in
v
and since
S_{x}=S-t_{0}\partial_{t}
,

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\langle x\rangle^{\frac{5}{2}}\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\Gamma^{\xi}f(t_{0},x,v)\mathrm{d}v\bigg|^{2}\mathrm{d}x\lesssim\langle t_{0}\rangle^{N}\sup_{|\beta|\leq N}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\langle x\rangle^{\frac{5}{2}}\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\widehat{Z}^{\beta}f(t_{0},x,v)\mathrm{d}v\bigg|^{2}\mathrm{d}x.

It remains to use the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality in
v
.

Lemma A.4
.

Let
N\geq 0
,
\psi:\mathbb{R}^{3}\to\mathbb{R}
be a sufficiently regular function supported in
\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\,|\,|x|\leq 3\}
and such that
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\psi=0
. Let further
\phi
be the unique
H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
solution to
\Delta\,\phi=\psi
. Then, for any
|\kappa|\leq N
,

\forall\,x\in\mathbb{R}^{3},\qquad\langle x\rangle^{3+|\kappa|}\left|\nabla\partial_{x}^{\kappa}\phi(x)\right|\lesssim\big\|\psi\big\|_{W^{N,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

If
N\geq 1
, we have, for any
|\kappa|\leq N+1
,

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}\langle x\rangle^{\frac{5}{2}+2|\kappa|}\left|\nabla\partial_{x}^{\kappa}\phi(x)\right|^{2}\mathrm{d}x\lesssim\big\|\psi\big\|_{H^{N}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

Proof.

By integration by parts and an induction, we have

\forall\,|\gamma|\leq N,\qquad\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Gamma^{\gamma}\psi=0.

Apply then Lemma
A.1
to
\Gamma^{\gamma}\phi
, for any
|\gamma|\leq N
, together with the commutation relations (
152
). Since
\psi
is compactly supported, one obtains both the
L^{\infty}_{x}
and the
L^{2}_{x}
estimates, up to
|\kappa|\leq N
, by using (
153
). For the record, we in particular have

\forall\,|\beta|\leq N,\qquad\big\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{\frac{5}{4}}\Gamma^{\beta}\phi\big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})}\lesssim\big\|\psi\big\|_{H^{N}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

Assume now that
N\geq 1
and remark that

\forall\,|\kappa|=N+1,\qquad\langle x\rangle^{|\kappa|}\left|\nabla\partial_{x}^{\kappa}\phi(x)\right|\lesssim\sup_{|\gamma|\leq N+1}\left|\nabla\Gamma^{\gamma}\phi(x)\right|\lesssim\sup_{|\gamma|=N}\langle x\rangle\left|\nabla^{2}\Gamma^{\gamma}\phi(x)\right|+\sup_{|\beta|\leq N}\left|\nabla\Gamma^{\beta}\phi(x)\right|.

We have already proved the required weighted
L^{2}
estimate for the second term on the right hand side. Consider then
|\gamma|=N
,
|\xi|=N-1
and
\Gamma\in\mathbb{Q}
such that
\Gamma^{\gamma}=\Gamma\Gamma^{\xi}
. As
\Gamma
is either a translation
\partial_{x^{i}}
or a homogeneous vector field, we have

\langle x\rangle^{\frac{1}{4}}\left|\Gamma^{\gamma}\phi(x)\right|\leq\langle x\rangle^{\frac{5}{4}}\left|\nabla\Gamma^{\xi}\phi(x)\right|,

so that we can control
\langle\cdot\rangle^{\frac{9}{4}}\left|\nabla^{2}\Gamma^{\gamma}\phi\right|
in
L^{2}
by applying once again from Lemma
A.1
and (
152
).
